# The Teaching Core of the Kephalaia

*Extracted from the Coptic Kephalaia of the Teacher (Gardner, Brill 1995).*
*Later editorial layers — hagiographic frame, pastoral instructions,*
*and explicit Christian overlay — have been removed. Mixed passages*
*have been repaired to recover the teaching content. The text is*
*presented in its original chapter order.*

---

**Chapters analyzed**: 123
**Chapters with core content**: 101
**Core text words**: ~48,839

## Chapter 0
### Introduction

*Not a proper kephalaion but a framing/justification sequence for the entire work. The oldest teaching layer survives as a compressed cosmogonic survey in paragraphs 1-9, mapping the full cosmic drama from primordial separation through final reintegration. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system — realm structures, cosmic agents, cosmic processes. Heavy lacunae throughout. Paragraphs 10-23 are entirely institutional: Mani asserting scriptural authority, narrating earlier apostles (Jesus, Zarathustra, Buddha), and commanding disciples to preserve his oral teaching — the editorial justification for compiling the Kephalaia. Editorial fatigue is strong (0.67), with substrate vocabulary disappearing completely in the second half while institutional vocabulary doubles. The cosmogonic substrate is genuine but presented in highly compressed summary form, likely an editorial condensation of fuller substrate teaching found in later chapters.*

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Extremely fragmentary. The frame elements ('he has unveiled,' 'his disciples') wrap what appears to be a cosmogonic substrate fragment about the primordial separation and the realm of light. 'Word in the beginning' and 'concerning the separation' are ambiguous — could be meta-discourse about the teaching topic or cosmological description. Retained in extraction given the heavy lacunae make finer discrimination impossible.
- ¶2: Substrate score 6.38 — highest non-trivial score for this paragraph. Describes the realm of Darkness: its root, its nature, its distance from the light. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system (dark realm → its properties). Heavily lacunose but the surviving fragments are purely cosmological description.
- ¶3: Clear editorial voice intrusion: 'which I have written for you' is unmistakably Mani's first person. The substrate teaching describes the eternal separation of the two principles and the Darkness's aggression — both sides internal to the cosmic system. 'Desiring that it would reign over an essence not its own' is a key substrate formula describing the fundamental cosmic violation. Darkness and Light capitalized as personified agents ('has made war,' 'desiring').
- ¶4: Emanation of the First Man — a core substrate narrative. 'The power came forth from [the Father]' describes cosmic emanation; the First Man arms himself with 'the armour of light' and confronts the enmity. Process-oriented, impersonal, both sides within the cosmic system. The 'crucified it' language is notable — may be older than its Christian overlay.
- ¶5: Substrate score 1.92. Construction of the universe by the Living Spirit — systematic cosmological process description. 'Spread out the universe,' 'set in order,' 'the wheel of the course,' 'constructed the foundation' — all cosmic architecture vocabulary. 'The garments' likely refers to the cosmic garments/vestments that are a substrate concept. Both sides of every mapping are cosmic.
- ¶6: Highest substrate score in chapter (9.46). Classic cosmological architecture: three earths, four mountains, three vessels — numbered cosmic structures systematically enumerated. 'A bond for the evil powers' describes the universe's function within the cosmic system. Both sides of every mapping internal to the cosmic architecture. The numbered structures (three earths, four mountains, three vessels) are the hallmark of substrate enumeration.
- ¶7: Highest substrate score in chapter (17.65). Despite being only 17 words, this is a dense substrate summary: the Third Ambassador's cosmic function (purification of light by his image, universal redemption). Named cosmic agent performing cosmic process. Both sides internal to the system.
- ¶8: Substrate eschatology: the final purification, separation, and reintegration of the divine. 'The fire blazes,' 'he becomes purifier and goes up to his essence' — cosmic process. 'The darkness is gathered in to a bond' — final binding. 'A single God comes to be over the totality' — the restored divine unity. 'You find no opponent' is teaching voice (impersonal second person), not exhortation — it describes the eschatological cosmic state. Both sides of every mapping remain within the cosmic system. The 'You' address is part of the Persian pedagogical tradition.
- ¶9: Very brief substrate fragment about the primordial mixture of light and darkness. 'Mixed and joined with one another' is the substrate description of the cosmic mixture — a core concept that the entire cosmogonic drama addresses. Both sides internal to the system.
- ¶10: Clear institutional layer: Mani enumerating his canonical scriptures (The Great Gospel, Treasury of the Life, The Treatise, The One of the Mysteries, The Writing for the Parthians, Epistles, Psalms, Prayers). 'These three lessons are the measure of all wisdom' — Mani's truth-claim. Institutional score 13.68 — highest in first half. This is the pivot point from cosmogonic substrate to institutional framing.
- ¶11: Institutional score 7.27. Mani's self-assertion: 'Every writer, if he reveals these three great lessons: that one is the writer of truth. Also, every teacher...' First-person truth-claim extending the canonical framework. The 'three great lessons' structure is Mani's own organizational category.
- ¶12: Describes what the 'first great lesson' contains. Some cosmological terms survive in the fragments ('the twelve,' 'the doors that are opened,' 'the four corners') but they are REFERENCED as topics within Mani's lesson structure, not systematically expounded as substrate teaching. 'My pains' and 'my afflictions' are autobiographical — Mani's first person. The cosmological terms are subordinated to the institutional framework.
- ¶13: Flagged as probable editorial extension (high institutional vocabulary 12.3 with register shift). Mani urging disciples to write down his oral teaching — the direct justification for the Kephalaia as a sub-canonical text. 'The teachers,' 'the leaders,' 'the elect and the catechumens' — institutional hierarchy vocabulary. 'The questions that you have asked me' — explicit reference to the Q&A format that will structure the Kephalaia.
- ¶14: Imperative command to preserve teaching: 'You must remember them and write them; gather them in different places.' Institutional prescription — text-preservation directive. Part of the Kephalaia justification sequence.
- ¶15: Institutional score 3.42. Mani describing his differentiated teaching to different audiences: 'The elect [...] psychic wisdom [...] The suckling babes, according to their ability.' 'The sects and the heresies' — reference to contemporary religious landscape, but used here in Mani's biographical narrative rather than as a cosmic→contemporary mapping. Institutional self-description.
- ¶16: Apostolic succession narrative: 'the apostles [...] the earliest fathers past unveiled it in their scriptures.' Mani positioning himself within a lineage. 'The church' — institutional vocabulary. 'In each place, in each city, in each land' — biographical description of mission scope.
- ¶17: Historical narrative about Jesus: 'At the time that Jesus trod [...] the land of the west [...] his parables [...] the signs and wonders.' Apostolic succession — establishing that earlier apostles taught truth but did not write their own scriptures. 'My beloved ones' — direct address to community. Biographical/historical, not cosmological.
- ¶18: Institutional score 5.71. Historical narrative about Zarathustra: 'he came to Persia, up to Hystaspes the king [...] Zarathustra (did not) write books. Rather, his disciples who came after him, they remembered; they wrote.' Apostolic succession pattern: earlier apostle → did not write → disciples wrote after him → teachings adulterated. Specific historical proper noun (Hystaspes).
- ¶19: Highest institutional score in chapter (23.68). Buddha narrative following the same apostolic pattern: 'He chose his churches, and perfected his churches [...] he did not write his wisdom in books. His disciples, who came after him, are the ones who remembered.' 'Churches' used anachronistically for Buddhist sangha — institutional vocabulary imposed on the narrative.
- ¶20: Institutional argument for written scriptures: 'the fathers of righteousness did not write their wisdom in books [...] their righteousness and their church will pass away from the world.' 'Lead astray,' 'adulterate,' 'mix' — vocabulary of textual corruption. This is the editorial argument that justifies Mani's canonical project and, by extension, the Kephalaia.
- ¶21: Institutional score 3.95. Mani's command to write: 'unveiled for you in my scriptures [...] the wisdom and the interpretation [...] you write after me, so that [...] it leads you not astray!' Institutional prescription with protective rationale. 'The commandments' — institutional vocabulary.
- ¶22: Final institutional command: 'remember! And write a little something from the great wisdom that you have heard from me.' Direct address to community with imperative voice. Content is about text preservation, not spiritual exhortation — the voice is exhortatory but the purpose is institutional (preserve the teaching). 'City after city, in each land separately' echoes paragraph 16.
- ¶23: Devotional closure: 'When you write and are amazed by them [...] enlighten greatly; and they shall give benefit and make free [...] of the truth.' Promises spiritual benefit from the act of preservation. This is the wrapper that closes the institutional sequence with a devotional seal. Brief and formulaic.

## Chapter 2
### The Second, concerning the Parable of the Tree.

*This chapter is heavily overlaid with editorial material. The first half (¶1-11) is almost entirely dialogue frame, Gospel citation, and anti-sectarian polemic — the editors use Jesus' parable as a proof-text and argue against 'the sects' using NT examples (Judas, Paul, the date palm). The substrate teaching emerges in the second half with the five-fold faculty mapping: consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind → cosmic beings/locations for both the good tree (ascending: holy church → Pillar of Glory → First Man → Third Ambassador → the Father) and the bad tree (descending: law of death → transmigration → gehenna → vessel → the lump). This systematic faculty-realm correspondence is the oldest layer. The chapter closes with a dualistic creedal assertion (the two trees did not arise from one another) wrapped in exhortation. The editorial fatigue pattern is confirmed: substrate vocabulary increases in the second half while frame/institutional vocabulary drifts upward. The 'taste' mappings (good tree → holy church/catechumens; bad tree → the sects) are editorial applications that reach outside the cosmic system into contemporary institutions.*

... the intellectuals that are cloaked upon its body [ ... ] this good tree. They are the light elements that are mingled, being compounded in the totality.

it has five limbs. [They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind.] Its consideration is the holy church. [Its counsel] is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. [Its insight is the Fir]st Man who dwells in the ship of living waters. Its thought is the Third Ambassador [who dwells in] the ship of living fire, that shines in [ ... ] Also, the mind is the Father who dwells in greatness, who is perfect in the aeons of light.

It is consideration that shall [ ... ] they shall be raised up [ ... ] is counsel; they shall be raised up to insight, which is the First Man who dwells in the ship of the night; from insight they shall rise up to thought, which is the Ambassador who dwells in the ship of the day. And he too, great glorious thought, shall bring them in to mind; which is the Father, the God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory.

[ ... ] bad [ ... ] happen in [ ... ] from [ ... ] also [ ... ] the way that it is [ ... ] the good tree [ ... ] of the fire [ ... ] they are the ruling-power [ ... ] heaven and the wheel [ ... ] they are the five fleshes that walk [ ... ] fruit [ ... te]aching as of error, the mystery that [ ... wh]ich Satan appointed in the world.

The bad tree has five limbs. They are consideration, counsel, insight, thought, mind. Its consideration is the law of death. Its counsel is transmigration [ ... ] in type after type. Also, its insight is the furnaces of fire [ ... ge]henna; which is full of smoke. Thought [ ... ] vessel. Its mind is the [ ... ] the lump, the last bond, the [ ... ], they that Satan cast away [ ... ] the great [ ... ] and they fall [ ... ] the Darkness [ ... ] all [ ... ] and they take away [ ... ] are swallowed in to the lump, and they are bound [ ... ] the fruit of Darkness, the one that they [ ... ] for ever and ever [ ... ] his face [ ... ] will arrive at that place; and they have no repentance therein.

[ ... ] which lies in its good earth [ ... life] and light; as death and Darkness do not exist [ ... ] Also, the bad tree is Matter [ ... wh]ich occurs in its bad earth [ ... ] Darkness and death.

He understands that they did not arise out of one another, nor did they come from one another. They did not come from one! The person who can separate them out may go up to the aeon of light

bad fruit did not arise from it [ ... ]; but whoever will not separate out [ ... ] from one another, he falls to the land of Darkness [ ... ] until he arrives at the [ ... ]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Purely formulaic opening marker. Introduces the chapter as the first question. No cosmological content.
- ¶2: Dialogue frame with Gospel citation (Matthew 7:17-20 parallel). 'As it is written in the Gospel, he says' — citation formula marks this as editorial. The parable entered this text via NT citation, not as pre-Mani substrate. 'We beseech you, our master' is standard disciple-question formula.
- ¶3: Continuation of disciple question. 'It is written [...] scriptures' — further citation framework. 'Mystery by mystery' — pedagogical formula. No cosmological substance to extract.
- ¶4: 'The sects' — one side of the discussion reaches into the contemporary religious landscape. The editors present alternative interpretations of the parable. 'Composite earth' appears but within the sects' reported reasoning, not as substrate teaching.
- ¶5: Opens with full dialogue frame attribution ('Then speaks our master Manichaios, the apostle of greatness'). Substantive content discusses the sects' interpretations and introduces the date palm example — editorial polemic against literal readings. No substrate teaching extractable.
- ¶6: Very fragmentary. Continues the date palm discussion — fibres, branches, dust, sweet/bad qualities. This is editorial reasoning about the mixed nature of physical trees, arguing against literal identification. Not cosmological mapping.
- ¶7: NT narrative proof-text: Judas counted among the twelve, then Satan entered him. 'It is written about him' — citation formula. Used to illustrate the mixture principle through a scriptural example. The mixture doctrine is cosmological but the vehicle (Judas narrative) is editorial.
- ¶8: Continuation of Judas example. 'First they called him a good man' — editorial illustration of good-to-bad transformation in a composite being.
- ¶9: Paul example: persecutor → apostle (bad-to-good). 'It is written about Paul' — citation formula. 'The mingling' and 'mingled in all things' briefly touch cosmological vocabulary but function within the editorial argument about mixture in composite beings. The principle of mingling is substrate; its illustration through Paul is editorial.
- ¶10: Transitional paragraph: rejects 'the sects' explanation, promises Mani's true revelation. 'The saviour preached in the parable' — citation frame. 'What are these two trees?' — the question is cosmological but embedded in editorial transition and polemic.
- ¶11: Very fragmentary. 'How I will reveal to you' — first-person frame promise. 'The Father' and 'the Pillar' are cosmic entities but too fragmentary to extract as coherent teaching. Transitional passage leading into the substrate proper.
- ¶12: High substrate vocabulary (37.93). 'Intellectuals cloaked upon its body' — faculty-body correspondence, both sides within cosmic system. 'Light elements mingled, compounded in the totality' — systematic description of cosmic mixture. Clear oldest layer. Impersonal, descriptive voice.
- ¶13: Seam flag confirmed. Maps the tree's 'fruit' to 'Jesus the Splendour, the father of all the apostles' and its 'taste' to 'the holy church, in her teachers and her [...] the catechumens.' While Jesus the Splendour is a cosmic figure in Manichaean cosmology, 'father of all the apostles' crosses into institutional territory, and 'the holy church, teachers, catechumens' is clearly institutional application — one side of the mapping reaches into the organized church.
- ¶14: Minimal fragment with bridge connective 'Behold, this is.' Summary/demonstrative statement. Too brief to classify with confidence but the connective pattern is editorial.
- ¶15: Core five-fold faculty mapping: intellectual faculties → cosmic beings/locations. Ship of living waters = moon ship; ship of living fire = sun ship. This is systematic correspondential teaching — domain mapped onto domain. The ascending hierarchy (church → Pillar → First Man → Ambassador → Father) matches the ascent sequence in ¶17. High substrate score (43.82) confirms.
- ¶16: Seam flag confirmed. Very high institutional vocabulary (29.73): 'alms', 'catechumens', 'purified in the holy church.' This is editorial extension applying the ascending faculty teaching to Manichaean church practices — the process of purification through alms-giving. One side of the mapping reaches into institutional practice.
- ¶17: Pure substrate: the ascending sequence through five faculties mapped to cosmic beings and locations. Ship of the night = moon, ship of the day = sun. The process is systematic and impersonal — it describes HOW souls ascend through the cosmic hierarchy. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'The God of truth, the great Mind of all the aeons of glory' — cosmological titles for the Father. No frame, citation, or editorial markers.
- ¶18: Summary/transition echoing Gospel parable language: 'This is the good tree that shall give good fruit.' Connects the substrate teaching back to the proof-text framework. 'The entire life and light for ever' has cosmological resonance but functions as editorial summary. 'It is blessed' is possibly exhortative.
- ¶19: Very fragmentary but identifiable vocabulary is cosmological: 'the ruling-power', 'heaven and the wheel', 'the five fleshes' (the five types of beings in the kingdom of Darkness), 'Satan appointed in the world' (cosmic narrative of Satan establishing error in the physical cosmos). Describes the bad tree's cosmic nature. Both sides of identifiable mappings stay within the cosmic system. Substrate score 9.64 despite fragmentation.
- ¶20: Maps bad tree's taste to 'these evil people, the sects [...] bound in law after law, they and their teachers [...] the law of death.' One side of the mapping reaches into the contemporary religious landscape — 'the sects' and 'their teachers' are the editor's identified opponents. Parallel to ¶13's institutional mapping of the good tree's taste. 'The law of death' and 'souls of death' are cosmological terms used to characterize contemporary groups.
- ¶21: Editorial summary: 'the one that our master called' — dialogue frame attribution; 'in the scriptures' — citation frame; 'all the sects know it' — polemic. Connects the teaching back to the Gospel parable and polemicizes against the sects.
- ¶22: Core five-fold faculty mapping for the bad tree, exact negative counterpart to ¶15. Descending hierarchy: law of death → transmigration → gehenna/furnaces → vessel → the lump (the last bond). The cosmic eschatology (swallowed into the lump, bound in the fruit of Darkness, no repentance) describes terminal cosmic destiny. Both sides of the faculty-realm mappings stay within the cosmic system. Substrate score 10.06.
- ¶23: Summary frame: 'this is the explanation of the bad tree, as I have explained to you.' First-person retrospective. Very fragmentary. No extractable cosmological content.
- ¶24: Dualistic cosmological identification: good tree in good earth (life, light; death and Darkness absent) vs. bad tree = Matter in bad earth (Darkness, death). This is the semi-creedal substrate assertion Gardner identifies. 'The bad tree is Matter' — direct ontological identification, both sides within the cosmic system. Exhortation score 6.85 reflects the 'my beloved ones' address.
- ¶25: Core dualistic creedal assertion: 'they did not arise out of one another, nor did they come from one another. They did not come from one!' — this is the absolute dualism teaching. The two principles are eternally separate, not derived from each other. 'May go up to the aeon of light' — eschatological promise tied to understanding. The teaching IS the creedal core; the beatitude wrapper is editorial devotional closure.
- ¶26: Continuation of dualistic teaching: 'bad fruit did not arise from it' — cosmological assertion about the good tree's nature. 'He falls to the land of Darkness' — cosmic eschatological consequence. Both sides within the cosmic system. Low substrate score (4.35) but the identifiable content is cosmological.
- ¶27: Pure exhortation closure. 'Blessed is whoever can believe!' — beatitude formula. 'For ever and ever' — devotional closing formula. Seam flag confirmed: this extends the preceding teaching sequence with devotional application. High exhortation score (36.36).

## Chapter 3
### The Interpretation of Happiness, Wisdom and Power; what they signify.

*The oldest teaching layer is a four-fold descending iteration mapping happiness, wisdom, and power to cosmic beings at four levels: (1) the Father/land of light, (2) the sun ship, (3) the moon ship, (4) the elements. Each iteration follows the same triadic structure (happiness=sovereign being, wisdom=cognitive/generative being, power=the gods/angels at that level). This is pure cosmos→cosmos mapping. An editor extended this to a fifth level (the holy church, ¶9) by mimicking the pattern syntax — detected by bridge connective 'Now, moreover' and institutional vocabulary. The chapter's editorial fatigue score confirms significant institutional drift in the second half (+4.77). The summary in ¶10 integrates the editorial extension ('five churches') and closes with a beatitude. Paragraphs 1-4 are frame/setup with the topic question in ¶2 being substantive.*

[What] are these thr[ee things] that in the world are called 'happine[ss', 'wisdom]', and 'power'?

[The happin]ess of the glorious one is the Father, the God of [truth, who] is established in the great land of [light]. ⟨p.24⟩ His glorious wis[d]om is his Great Spirit that [ ... ] below, which flows through all his aeons, and t[h]ey float therein. His great power is all the gods, the rich ones and the angels who were summoned from him as they [ ... ] t]hey that are called aeons [ ... ]

[ ... ] which is called] the sun [ ... the ship of] living fire, [ ... the Third Amba]ssador, the second greatne[ss ... The] glorious [happiness] is the [Living] Spirit, [ ... , the wisdom] is the Mother of Lif[e ... and great power] is [all] the gods, [the] rich o]nes and the angels who are within the ship.

Again, [h]appiness, wisdom and power exist in the s[hip of liv]ing waters [ ... ] the happiness [ ... ] the Mind of the Father. Also, wisdom [is the Vir]gi]n of [Li]ght. And the power that is [i]n the ship is [all] the go[ds], the [ri]ch ones and the angels who are established i[n it].

Again, these three exist in the elements: happiness, wis[d]o]m and power. The happiness is [the Pillar of Glory], the Perfect Man. Wisdom is the [five sons of the] Living Spirit; and great powe[r is ... the fi]ve sons of the First Man [ ... who are encl]osed and compounded in the totality, that [ ... ] while he supports the totality.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening. '[Once aga]in the enlightener speaks to his disciples' is the stereotyped frame formula. 'assembly of the church' adds institutional coloring.
- ¶2: The substantive question defines the teaching topic (three cosmic principles: happiness, wisdom, power) and generates the four-fold iteration that follows. The rhetoric about worldly boasting and passing away is editorial setup voice. Heavy lacunae obscure some content.
- ¶3: Heavily damaged. Surviving content is entirely rhetorical/editorial: 'People boast of them. They praise...' continues the worldly contrast rhetoric; 'as I have told you' is self-referential back-reference. No cosmological mapping survives. Functions as editorial bridge between the topic question and the systematic teaching.
- ¶4: Entirely first-person pedagogical framing: 'I will teach you of another happiness... listen that I may reveal to you.' The concept of 'another' (true/cosmic) happiness/wisdom/power is implicit in the teaching structure but here it is expressed in the teacher's instructional voice, not in systematic mapping. The topic was already established in ¶2.
- ¶5: FIRST ITERATION — Land of Light / the Father. Happiness=the Father in the land of light; Wisdom=the Great Spirit flowing through the aeons; Power=the gods, rich ones, and angels summoned from him. Pure cosmos→cosmos mapping. Both sides internal: sovereign being → cosmic attribute → emanated beings. The triadic structure (sovereign/cognitive/collective) repeats at each level.
- ¶6: SECOND ITERATION — The Sun / Ship of Living Fire. Heavily damaged but structure clear: Happiness=Living Spirit; Wisdom=Mother of Life; Power=gods, rich ones, angels within the ship. The Third Ambassador is identified with 'the second greatness.' Same triadic pattern descends one cosmic level.
- ¶7: THIRD ITERATION — The Moon / Ship of Living Waters. Happiness=Mind of the Father; Wisdom=Virgin of Light; Power=gods, rich ones, angels established in the ship. 'Again' is an iteration marker integral to the teaching structure. The triadic pattern continues its descent.
- ¶8: FOURTH ITERATION — The Elements / Mixed World. Happiness=Pillar of Glory/Perfect Man; Wisdom=five sons of Living Spirit; Power=five sons of First Man. This is the lowest cosmic level: beings 'enclosed and compounded in the totality.' The substrate vocabulary 'enclosed and compounded' describes the mixture condition. 'Supports the totality' may refer to the Pillar of Glory's cosmological function.
- ¶9: EDITORIAL SEAM — confirmed. 'Now, moreover' is the bridge connective that extends the four-fold cosmic iteration to a fifth level: the holy church. The pattern syntax is mimicked exactly (happiness=X, wisdom=Y, power=Z) but Mapping Side B now reaches into the editor's institutional world: 'Apostle of Light who has been sent', 'leaders and teachers who travel in the holy church proclaiming wisdom and truth', 'elect, virgins, continent, catechumens.' Every identification is institutional. The preceding four iterations map cosmos→cosmos; this one maps cosmos→church hierarchy. The entire paragraph is one editorial act.
- ¶10: Devotional closure. 'Blessed, therefore, is every one who will know them' is the standard beatitude formula. The summary 'five happinesses, five wisdoms, five powers in the five churches' integrates the editorial extension (¶9) into the count — 'five' rather than 'four' reflects the editor's addition. 'Five churches' applies institutional framing to all cosmic levels. The summary + beatitude form a single editorial closure act. Note: it is possible the original teaching had a fifth cosmic level that the editor replaced with the church, but this cannot be reconstructed from surviving text.

## Chapter 4
### Concerning the Four Great Days that have come forth from one another, together with the Four Nights

*The oldest teaching layer is dominant. A four-fold day/night schema maps cosmic entities to twelve-hour structures. The substrate is clean through the first three positions of each sequence. The fourth positions (day 4 = Jesus the Splendour, night 4 = law of Sin) show editorial bridging toward institutional identifications ('his church,' 'the twelve sects,' 'the old man'). This is a classic pattern: the editor extends a complete cosmic schema into the contemporary world at the terminal position. The substrate pentadic structure (five elements, five sense organs, five spirits) underlies the twelve-fold systematization, which Gardner rightly notes is 'secondary' — yet the cosmic correspondences within each position (zodiacal signs as Matter's thrones, five parts above/below, heat and cold as father and mother) are genuinely substratic. Editorial fatigue is moderate: substrate vocabulary drops from 34.09 to 8.62 in the second half while editorial_bridge rises from 0.0 to 2.39.*

Four great days have come forth from one another; they were evoked out of one another!

The first great day is the Father, the God of truth, the fir[st ... ... m]idst the aeons of his greatness, in his [l]iving kingdom. The twelve hours of this great day are the twelve great rich Gods of greatness. These, who are the first evocations that he evoked (to mirror) his greatness, he spread out to the four climes, three by three before his face.

The second day is the Third Ambassador, the one who dwells in the light ship. His twelve hours are the twelve virgins that he evoked in his greatness.

[The third day is the Pillar of Glory, the great porter that is greater than] all [the po]rters; the one that supports all [ ... ], those above and those below. Its twelve hours are the five sons of the First Man; the five sons of the Living Spirit, who support all the weights of the universe; together with the summons and the obedience, which are counted to their ten brethren. These are the twelve light hours of the third day.

The fourth day is Jesus the Splendour. His twelve hours are the twelve wisdoms, which are his [light] h[o]u[rs].

These are the four great days that have come forth from one another, which were evoked out of one another.

⟨p.26⟩ [ ... ]: For just as four day[s ex]ist, s[o] too four nights exist.

The first night is the land of Darkness. It has twelve black [shadows] therein, which are its dark h[o]ur[s]. The twelve shadows of the first night are the five elements of the land of Darkness, [which] poured forth from its five sense organs; and [ ... ] which [ ... ] in its [fi]ve elements and its five [ ... ]; the five [sp]irits that dwell in its elements [ ... ]. These are the twelve shadows and spirits of the first night.

The second night is Matter, the sculptress [ ... ... ] who has sculpted [ ... ] and the entire ruling-power that is in the worl[ds of Darkness]. She has fashioned it in five sense organs, five [male] and five female, two per world; together with the fire and the lust [that] dwell in men and women, inflaming [them] inward to one another. These are the twelve spirits of this second night.

Indeed she, this Matter, the thought of death, ga[ve] the King of the realms of Darkness and his powers strength for the war and the fight, against the aeons of greatness. Yet, he was caught! They laid a snare for him through the Fi[rst M]an. [He] was ta[ken] t[o ... ] through the powers [ ... He] was brought up from the land of death and s[et ... ab]ove and below in the [whole] universe; in the five par[t]s in the heavens above, as well as the five [parts] in the earths below; together with the heat and the cold that are the father and the mother, their fire and their l[u]st.

This is the second night that is begotten from the first night. They laid a snare for her through the First M[a]n. She was brought up by the hand of the Living Spirit, and set in this composite univers[e ... ] he[r], above and below. The [upper side is the sense] organs of males; the lower side [ ... ] fe[mal]es.

⟨p.27⟩ The thir[d night is the five] worlds of flesh [ ... five] male, and five female; that were begotten from the powers [abo]ve. They have fallen upon the earth, being displayed in wh[at is d]ry and what is moist; together with the fire and the lust [that d]we[l]l in them, which drives them inward to one another.

[Mat]te[r] herself, the thought of death, is the [mother] of them all; as among them she is named 'the night'. [The worlds] of male and female fleshes, together with the fire and the l[ust], shall [ ... ] twelve hours of [ ... ... ] night. Again, [it] too, the third night that is in the worlds of fl[e]sh, was begotten from the second night; which the Living [Spir]it crucified in the uni[verse ... , ab]ove and below in heaven [ ... ... ].

The fourth night is the law of Sin, which is the dark spirit who speaks in the twelve spirits. They are the nakednesses, the twelve zodiacal signs of Matter. They are her thrones.

the hours of this fourth night are the twelve e[vi]l spirits; the [ ... ] which [are ... ... ... ... di]fferentiate the day and night [ ... ... of] death.

This is the fourth night [ ... ] that have come forth of one another. One has [ ... ... ] just like the days of lig[ht ... ] they manifested one another. So als[o ... ] they did arise out of one another.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Standard dialogue frame opening wrapping a substantive cosmological declaration. The teaching statement defines the schema (four days, evoked from one another) and belongs to the oldest layer.
- ¶2: Pure degree-mapping: first day → Father → twelve hours → twelve rich Gods → four climes (3×4=12). 'Evocations that he evoked' is characteristic substrate emanation vocabulary. The spatial distribution 'three by three before his face' is systematic cosmological architecture. All mappings stay within the cosmic system.
- ¶3: Very high substrate density (75.0). Clean degree-mapping: second day → Third Ambassador → light ship (cosmic geography) → twelve virgins. 'Evoked in his greatness' repeats the emanation formula from ¶2.
- ¶4: Heavily restored opening (brackets) but pattern is clear: third day → Pillar of Glory → twelve hours composed of two pentads + a dyad (5+5+2=12). The summation formula 'These are the twelve light hours of the third day' closes the teaching unit. The pentadic sub-structure (five sons × 2) reflects the substrate's native five-fold organization being fitted into the twelve-fold schema.
- ¶5: Institutional vocabulary fires (20.83). The pattern demands a locating clause for the fourth entity; the substrate likely had a cosmic location that was replaced with 'his church.' The twelve wisdoms as hours is substratic — purely cosmic mapping.
- ¶6: The summary statement ('These are the four great days...') is a substrate closure paralleling ¶16 for the nights. The beatitude is standard devotional wrapper. The bridge connective flag ('These are the') is a false positive — this is a demonstrative summary, not an editorial seam.
- ¶7: Transitional teaching statement linking the two halves of the schema. The lacuna with colon '[ ... ]:' likely contained a dialogue attribution, but since it is unreadable, the visible text is preserved as substrate. The structural parallel (four days → four nights) is the teaching's own architecture.
- ¶8: Perfect substrate pattern: first night → land of Darkness → twelve shadows composed of pentadic sub-structures (five elements + five sense organs + five spirits? — damaged but structure is clear). 'Poured forth from its five sense organs' is cosmic emanation vocabulary. The pentadic architecture mirrors the day sequence.
- ¶9: Matter as 'sculptress' is a cosmic agent designation — describes what she IS and does within the system. The five-fold gender pairing (five male, five female, 'two per world') is systematic cosmological architecture. 'Fire and lust dwelling in men and women' stays within the cosmic body-system — describes cosmic forces operating in the body-cosmos, not contemporary moral application.
- ¶10: Cosmic narrative: the war, the First Man as snare, the five-fold distribution above and below. 'The heat and the cold that are the father and the mother' is a cosmic correspondence — thermal polarity mapped to generative polarity. All mappings stay within the cosmic system. Low substrate score (10.71) is because the vocabulary is narrative rather than terminological, but the content is entirely cosmic-to-cosmic.
- ¶11: Continues the cosmic narrative of the second night. 'Begotten from the first night' echoes the schema's evocation-from-one-another principle. The upper/lower mapping to male/female sense organs is body-cosmos correspondence. 'Composite universe' is substrate cosmological terminology.
- ¶12: Third night → five worlds of flesh. Five male/five female continues the pentadic gender-pairing of ¶9. 'Dry and moist' is an elemental polarity mapping. 'Fire and lust driving them inward to one another' repeats the cosmic force description from ¶9 — systematic correspondence, not moral application.
- ¶13: Matter as 'the night' is a cosmic identity mapping — she IS the principle of darkness. 'Mother of them all' maps Matter's generative role in the cosmic system. 'The Living Spirit crucified in the universe' is cosmic process vocabulary — not a Christological reference but a description of how Matter was bound in the cosmic structure. Heavy lacunae but the teaching skeleton is visible.
- ¶14: Editorial_bridge score peaks at 15.69. The substrate core maps fourth night → law of Sin → twelve spirits → zodiacal signs → Matter's thrones. This is cosmic-to-cosmic (spirits → zodiac → Matter). The editorial layer then identifies these spirits with 'the twelve sects' and Matter's display with 'the old man' — both reaching into the contemporary religious world. The seam falls after 'her thrones.'
- ¶15: Editorial_bridge at 10.64, institutional at 4.26. The substrate element (twelve evil spirits as hours, differentiating day and night) follows the pattern of ¶¶8-9. The editorial additions ('the old man,' 'the sects') are syntactically inserted into the substrate frame as appositives and possessives. Heavy lacunae make precise extraction difficult.
- ¶16: Substrate summary/closure paralleling ¶6 for the days. Despite heavy lacunae, the surviving text restates the schema's foundational principle: arising out of one another, manifesting one another. All vocabulary scores at 0.0 — no editorial layer vocabulary detected. The 'just like the days of light' explicitly links the night closure to the day sequence.
- ¶17: 'Blessed is he who will know them, and separate them' — standard beatitude/devotional closure formula. Matches the exhortation pattern identified across the corpus. 'Separate them' may reflect the teaching's content (separating days from nights, light from darkness) but the vehicle is the compilers' devotional wrapper, not the substrate's systematic voice.

## Chapter 5
### Concerning Four Hunters of Light and Four of Darkness

*The substrate is a well-preserved four-fold parallel enumeration mapping cosmic agents to hunter correspondences (net, ship, sea). Both light and dark series follow the same structural template. The substrate is dominant in the light hunters (except P4 where 'his holy church' intrudes) and in the first three dark hunters. The fourth dark hunter (P11) is almost entirely overwritten by editorial bridge material identifying 'every sect' and 'erroneous teaching' in the contemporary world. Two exhortation beatitudes close each series. The substrate vocabulary drops sharply in the second half, consistent with editorial fatigue replacing the fourth dark hunter's cosmic content with polemical application.*

There are four hunters who were sent from the light to fulfill the will of the greatness.

The first hunter is the First Man who was sent from the greatness. He threw himself down to the five storehouses of Darkness, he caught and siezed the enmity [... ...] his net also [... ...] out over all the children of Darkness [... ...]. His ship is his four sons who are swathed over his body. The sea is the land of Darkness [... ...]; his net is [...] and his powers.

The second hunter is the Third Ambassador. This one, for by his light image, which he revealed to the depths below, he hunted after the entire light that is in all things; as it is established in them. His net is his light image, [...] the whole universe and took it prisoner, to this likeness [... His] ship is his light ship. [The sea] is the universe [...] which were hunted after by his net [...] his [glorious] image.

The third hunter is Jesus the Splendour, who came from the greatness, who hunts after the light and life; and he [...] it to the heights. His net is his wisdom, [the] light wisdom with which he hunts the souls, catching them in the net. The sea is the error of the universe, the souls that are drowning in it [...]. He catches them in his net. They are the souls [... th]em by his light wisdom.

The fourth hunter is the great counsel that [...] that lives in the circuit [...] ⟨p.29⟩ entire universe in it today. Yet, at the end, in the dissolution of the universe, this very counsel of life will gather itself in and sculpt its soul in the Last Statue. Its net is its Living Spirit, because with its Spirit it can hunt after the light and the life that is in all things; and build it upon its body. Its ship, in which it is established, is this light cloud whereby it itself travels in the five elements [...], the great fire that will burn all the buildings of [... ...] in its net is the light and the life. It can rescue and free it from all bonds and fetters.

The first hunter is the King of they who belong to the Darkness, who hunted after the living soul with his net at the beginning of the worlds. His net is his fire and his lust that he has put upon the living soul, with which he has entangled it [...], through all his powers.

[The] second hunter is the evil counsel that lives in [...] that hunts after the light [...] and [...] up from [...] the earths to heaven. It binds them with its powers, which bring them to the heavenly worlds above. Its net is [...] whereby they are drawn up from the abyss [to the heig]hts.

The third hunter is lust [...] walks in every power of the flesh that walks [...] in the [...] the living souls [... ...] them in its bodies, which [... ...].

The fourth [hunter is the spirit] of Darkness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Standard frame formula wrapping a substantive cosmological statement that defines the four-fold teaching structure. The statement itself is substrate: it declares the enumeration and its cosmic origin.
- ¶2: Pure cosmic-to-cosmic mapping. Net, ship, sea all map to cosmic entities/domains: net = powers, ship = four sons (cosmic emanations), sea = land of Darkness. 'Five storehouses of Darkness' is signature substrate vocabulary. Both sides of every correspondence stay within the cosmic system. Heavily lacunose but structure intact.
- ¶3: Pure substrate. The Third Ambassador is a cosmic emanation. Net = light image (cosmic manifestation), ship = light ship (cosmic vehicle), sea = universe (cosmic domain). All mappings cosmos→cosmos. Despite lacunae, the correspondential structure is fully preserved.
- ¶4: Jesus the Splendour (Isho Ziwa) is a cosmic entity in the emanation hierarchy, not the historical Jesus. The substrate third hunter is preserved with net = wisdom (cosmic attribute) and sea = error of the universe (cosmic condition). The editorial hand inserted 'his holy church' for the ship and 'the law of sin' as a gloss, both mapping one side outside the cosmic system. Gardner's synopsis confirms: 'Jesus, whose net is the wisdom cast from the church' — the church connection is editorial.
- ¶5: Richest substrate paragraph. Net = Living Spirit (cosmic entity), ship = light cloud traveling in the five elements (cosmic vehicle in cosmic domain), the dissolution and Last Statue = cosmic eschatology. 'Today' describes the cosmic phase (the counsel currently lives in the circuit), not the editor's present — both sides stay within cosmic temporality. 'Five elements,' 'Last Statue,' 'bonds and fetters,' 'dissolution of the universe' — all systematic cosmic process vocabulary. The 'great counsel' / 'counsel of life' is a cosmic entity, mirrored by the 'evil counsel' in P9.
- ¶6: Beatitude closure appended to the light-hunter teaching sequence. 'Blessed is every one who...' is the signature exhortation formula. The seam flag correctly identifies this as a devotional wrapper. The reference to 'the great fire' picks up cosmic vocabulary from P5 but pivots to moral application and audience address.
- ¶7: Editorial transition paragraph. 'Once again he speaks' is dialogue frame formula. 'As I have revealed to you... I will also teach you' is self-referential editorial voice — the compiler narrating his own teaching act. The cosmological terms ('four living hunters of light,' 'four evil hunters who came from the darkness') summarize/preview but are entirely embedded in first-person editorial self-reference. The teaching structure does not require this transition; the enumeration in P8 introduces the dark hunters directly.
- ¶8: Pure substrate. The King of Darkness mapped as hunter: net = fire and lust (cosmic attributes of the dark realm). 'At the beginning of the worlds' is cosmic temporal frame. 'The living soul' is the cosmic entity being hunted. All mappings cosmos→cosmos. Mirrors the structure of the light hunters exactly.
- ¶9: Substrate despite heavy lacunae. The 'evil counsel' mirrors the 'great counsel' (P5) — systematic cosmic parallelism. Cosmic geography: earths, heaven, abyss, heights. The 'evil counsel' that draws light upward from the abyss to the heavenly worlds is a cosmic counter-agent. All mappings stay within the cosmic system. The binding and drawing-up describe cosmic processes, not contemporary institutions.
- ¶10: Very fragmentary but the preserved vocabulary is all cosmic: lust as a cosmic force (not moral exhortation), 'every power of the flesh' (cosmic powers embodied in matter), 'the living souls' (cosmic entities), 'its bodies' (the material forms that imprison souls). Despite lacunae, the cosmos→cosmos pattern is discernible.
- ¶11: The substrate REQUIRES a fourth dark hunter (the enumeration demands it), and 'the spirit of Darkness' is the cosmic entity filling that position. But the substrate description — which should have included cosmic correspondences for net, ship, and sea parallel to the other hunters — has been almost entirely replaced by editorial bridge material. The editorial_bridge score (12.66) is the highest in the chapter. The editor uses the fourth hunter position to polemicize against competing religious movements ('every sect,' 'erroneous teaching'). This is the clearest example in the chapter of one side of the mapping reaching into the contemporary world.
- ¶12: Beatitude closure for the dark-hunter sequence, parallel to P6. 'Blessed is every one who...' formula. 'Knowledge' (gnosis) as the means of escape and 'bond and fetter for ever and ever' as devotional closure. The seam flag correctly identifies this as editorial appendage to the teaching sequence.

## Chapter 6
### Concerning the Five Storehouses that have poured forth from the Land of Darkness since the [Beginning]; the Five Rulers, the Five Spirits, the Five Bodies, the Five Tastes.

*The oldest teaching layer is dominant in the first two-thirds of this chapter: a systematic five-fold cosmological description of the Kingdom of Darkness, mapping storehouses→elements→trees→genera→worlds, then describing the King of Darkness through zoomorphic forms (lion/eagle/dragon/fish), five properties (darkness/putridity/ugliness/bitterness/burning), three faculties (hardness/magic arts/polyglot knowledge), and further properties (movement/terrifying voice/perceptual limitation). The five sub-king descriptions (¶12-16) each contain pristine substrate (zoomorphic face + metal correspondence + taste correspondence) followed by editorial bridge material applying the cosmic spirit to contemporary institutions ('reigns today in the principalities,' 'sects that worship fire,' 'sites of idols,' 'baptism of the waters,' 'soothsayers'). The editorial fatigue pattern is textbook: +10.29 editorial_bridge drift in the second half. Gardner notes textual development and redactorial confusion about the King of Darkness vs. King of Smoke hierarchy. The substrate's five-fold degree structure (Smoke/Fire/Wind/Water/Darkness → gold/copper/iron/silver/lead → salty/sour/sharp/sweet/bitter → lion/eagle/fish/dragon) is a quintessential correspondential map with both sides internal to the cosmic system.*

Five storehouses have arisen since the beginning in the land of darkness! The five elements poured out of them. Also, from the five elements were fashioned the five trees! Again, from the five trees were fashioned the five genera of creatures in each world, male and female. And the five worlds themselves have five kings therein, and five spirits, five bodies, five tastes; in each world, they not resembling one another!

The King of the world of Smoke [ ... ] who came up from the depth of darkness; this is he who is the head of all wickedness, and all malignity. The beginning of the spread of the war occured through him; all the battles, fights, quarrelling, dangers, destructions, fights, wrestling-contests! That is the one who first made arise danger and war, with his worlds and his powers. Afterwards, also, he waged war with the light. He pitched a battle with the exalted kingdom.

Now, regarding the King of Darkness, there are five shapes on him! His head is lion-faced; his hands and feet are demon- and devil-faced; his shoulders are eagle-faced; while his belly is dragon-faced; his tail is fish-faced. These five shapes, the marks of his five worlds, exist on the King of the realms of Darkness!

Now, there are five other properties in him. The first is his darkness. The second is his putridity. The third is his ugliness. The fourth is his bitterness, his own soul. The fifth is his burning, which burns like an iron () as if poured out from fire.

[There are] also three others in him! The first, that his body is hard and very tough, even as she has formed him [ ... ] cruel-hearted; namely Matter, who is the thought of death, [the o]ne who sculpted him from the nature of the land of darkness. This is the manner of the body of the ruler of Smoke. He is harder than every iron, copper and steel and lead; as there is no cleaver at all, nor any iron implement, can [ ... ] him and cut him. For Matter, his fashioner, has formed him [ ... ] strong and hard.

The second, that he wounds and kills by the word of his magic arts. His recitation and hearing, all his foolish instruction, make magic and invocations for him. When it pleases him, he can make an invocation over himself, and by his magic arts be hidden from his companions. Again, when it pleases him, he can be manifested over his powers and appear to them

The third, that the King of the realms of Darkness knows the converse and language of his five worlds. He understands every thing he hears from their mouths, as they address one another; each one of them in his language. [Every] design they will consider against him, every snare they debate with one another to bring upon him, he knows them! He can also understand the gesturing they signal between one another. Yet, his powers and his rulers, who are subject to him, can not understand his wordy converse. While all these things are unveiled to him; still, their heart is not manifested to him. He knows not their mind nor their thought; he can not ponder their beginning and their end. Rather, he only knows and apprehends what is before his eyes.

Also, another different thing is found in the King of the realms of Darkness! For when it pleases him to move, he spreads all his limbs out and walks. When it comes to mind, he with[d]raws his limbs and takes them in, and is rolled (?) to his companions; and he falls to the ground like a grape and a great iron ball! He terrifies by his cry, he is frightful. He frightens his powers with his sound; because when he speaks, being like thunder in the clouds, he resembles the [ ... ] of the rocks [ ... ] When he cries out and [ ... ] and calls [ ... ] over his powers, they shall tremble and to[tter a]nd fall under foot; even as some birds would be [ ... ] the bird [ ... / ... ] and they fall down to the earth. Still, this thing only: he knows not what is far from him, he sees not who is at a distance, nor does he hear him. Rather, whoever is before his face he sees, hearing him and knowing him.

These signs and these evil marks are found in the chief of the demons and fiends, the King of all the mountains of Darkness [ ... the one] to [whom] the land of darkness has given birth, begotten in its cruelty, in its wickedness and its wrath [ ... ]

... more than all his fellow rulers, who are in all his worlds.

Gold is the body of the King of the realms of Darkness. The body of all the powers who belong to the world of Smoke is gold. And also, the taste of its fruits is salty.

In contrast, the King of the worlds of Fire is lion-faced, the foremost of all the beasts. Copper is his body. Again, the body of all the rulers who belong to Fire is copper. Their taste is the sour taste that is in every form.

Again, the King of the worlds of Wind is eagle-face. His body is iron. Also, the body of all they who belong to the Wind is iron. Their taste is the sharp taste that is in every form.

For his part, the King of the world of Water is fish-face. His body is silver. All the other rulers who belong to Water, silver is their body. Also, the taste of their fruit is the sweetness of water, the sweet taste that is in every (form).

Again, [the Kin]g of the world of Darkness is a dragon. His [body is lead] and tin. All the other rulers [who belong to the world of] Darkness, their body is lead and tin. And also, the taste of their fruits is bitterness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic attribution: 'Once again the enlightener speaks to his disciples.' No cosmological content. Standard dialogue frame opener.
- ¶2: Classic emanation hierarchy: storehouses→elements→trees→genera→worlds. Purely systematic, both sides of every mapping internal to the cosmic system. The five-fold pentadic structure is the skeleton of the entire chapter. High substrate score (56.34) confirms.
- ¶3: Describes the King of Smoke's cosmic role as originator of the primordial war. Both sides remain within the cosmic system — 'the exalted kingdom' is the Light realm, not a contemporary institution. Gardner notes redactorial confusion about the King of Smoke vs. supreme King of Darkness hierarchy, but the description itself is internal cosmological narrative.
- ¶4: Quintessential zoomorphic-realm mapping: body parts→animal faces→five worlds. This is the correspondential substrate at its purest. The five shapes ARE the marks of five worlds — domain mapped onto domain. The institutional score (5.36) is a false positive from 'demon- and devil-faced' vocabulary.
- ¶5: Five-fold enumeration of the King's properties. Systematic, impersonal, enumerated teaching. Enumeration markers ('The first is...', 'The second is...') are structural bones of the substrate. Both sides internal to the cosmic system.
- ¶6: First of three faculties. Introduces Matter as personified cosmic fashioner — internal to the cosmic system. Metal comparisons (iron, copper, steel, lead) function as hardness analogies within the correspondential register. Gardner notes the unclear relationship of the supreme King to personified Matter; this passage positions Matter as prior to and fashioner of the King.
- ¶7: Clear mid-paragraph layer boundary. The substrate describes the King's second faculty (magic arts, self-concealment, self-manifestation) — both sides internal. Then 'nowadays' explicitly crosses into the editorial present, followed by first-person exhortation. The editorial_bridge score (8.9) and exhortation score (4.19) correctly flag the later material.
- ¶8: Third faculty: polyglot knowledge but limited perception. Entirely internal to the cosmic system — describes what the King CAN and CANNOT know. The dialectic of power and limitation (knows languages but not hearts, sees what is before his eyes only) is systematic cosmological-correspondential description. The institutional score (2.24) appears to be noise.
- ¶9: Additional properties: movement (extension/contraction of limbs), terrifying voice (thunder analogy), and perceptual limitation (repeated from ¶8 — sees only what is before his face). Heavy lacunae but the substrate voice is unmistakable. Similes (thunder, birds) are cosmological analogies, not contemporary applications. Both sides remain internal.
- ¶10: Summary statement recapitulating the King's attributes. 'The land of darkness has given birth' to the King — cosmic genesis, internal to the system. Heavy lacunae at end.
- ¶11: Fragment continuing from ¶10 across a lacuna. Positions the King above his fellow rulers — hierarchical cosmic statement, internal to the system.
- ¶12: First of the five sub-king descriptions. The substrate maps realm→metal→taste (Smoke→gold→salty). The editorial bridge applies 'the spirit of the King' to contemporary principalities. The boundary is sharp: the mapping cosmos→cosmos stops at 'salty,' then 'The spirit of the King... reigns today' crosses into the editorial present. The editorial_bridge score (12.2) correctly flags the later material.
- ¶13: Second sub-king: Fire→lion-faced→copper→sour. The substrate portion is pristine correspondential mapping. 'In contrast' connects to the preceding systematic enumeration. The editorial extension follows the same formula as ¶12 ('the spirit of the King... is this one who reigns') and adds identification of fire-worshipping sects — unmistakably editorial.
- ¶14: Third sub-king: Wind→eagle-face→iron→sharp. This paragraph is the clearest illustration of the substrate/editorial boundary: 'His body is iron' maps cosmos→cosmos; 'His spirit is the one of idolatry... in every temple' maps cosmos→contemporary religious landscape. Same syntax, different temporal layer. Editorial_bridge score (36.99) is the highest in the chapter.
- ¶15: Fourth sub-king: Water→fish-face→silver→sweet. 'For his part' is a structural transitional marker connecting the systematic enumeration. The editorial extension identifies baptismal sects — likely Mandaeans or Elchasaites, contemporary to the Manichaean editors. 'Reigns today' is the signature editorial bridge formula.
- ¶16: Fifth sub-king: Darkness→dragon→lead and tin→bitter. Completes the five-fold correspondence system. The editorial extension identifies contemporary soothsayers and oracle-givers. Note: the sub-world of Darkness within the broader Land of Darkness — Gardner notes the redactor's confusion about this hierarchical distinction.
- ¶17: Pure devotional closure: 'Concerning this I tell you, my brethren and my limbs' — direct audience address; 'the perfect faithful, the holy elect' — institutional community titles; 'Hold your heart close to you, and you stay away from' — imperative moral instruction; 'break loose their chain and their chastening for ever' — eschatological promise. Every marker of the exhortation layer is present. The 'five enslavements of the five dark spirits' and 'service of their five bodies' reference the substrate's five-fold structure but apply it pastorally — the correspondence is being USED, not BEING. High exhortation score (42.62) confirms.

## Chapter 7
### The Seventh, concerning the Five Fathers

*A well-preserved emanation hierarchy teaching: five fathers, each summoning three powers. The substrate is dominant in ¶2-12, forming a clean systematic sequence. Editorial fatigue is clearly visible from ¶13 onward, where the editors increasingly overlay institutional vocabulary (churches, elect, catechumens) onto the emanation scheme. The substrate identifications survive even in the second half, but the functional descriptions shift from cosmos→cosmos to cosmos→institution. The emanation pattern language ('after the pattern of') is structural substrate. A gospel citation (John 8:38) intrudes at ¶15. The chapter closes with a standard beatitude formula.*

Five fathers exist; they were summoned forth one of one. [Also], one did come out of another!

The first Father is the Father of Greatness, the blessed one of glory, the [on]e who has no measure to his greatness; who also is the first only-begotten, the first eternal; who exists with fiv[e fa]thers for ever; the one who exists before every thing that has existed, and that will exist.

Now he, the glorious Father, summoned from him three emanations. The [fi]rst is the Gre[at] Spirit, the first Mother, who came out of the Father. She appeared first. ⟨p.35⟩

The seco[nd i]s the Belo[ved of] the Lights, the great glorious Beloved, [the one w]ho is honour[ed; w]ho came out of the Father. [He ma]nifested ou[t of h]im.

The third father is the Thir[d Ambassador], the eldest of [a]]] the counsell[ors; who came out of] the first Father. He appeared.

This is the first Father, the first power, the one from whom the three great powers came out. This is the first Father, the first eternal, the root of all the lights; from whom the three emanations came out. They have humiliated the Darkness. They have brought its heart's desire to naught. They have given themselves the victory. They have also given the victory to their aeons.

The second father, who came out of the first Father, is the Third Ambassador, the model of the King of lights. And again, he too summoned and sent out of him three powers.

One is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man; the one who bears up under all things; the great Pillar of blessing; the great porter, who is greater than all the porters.

The second is glorious Jesus the Splendour; [the] one through whom shall be given life eternal.

The third is the Virgin of Light, the glorious wisd[o]m; the one who takes away the heart of the [rul]ers and the powers by her image, as she fulfils the pleas[u]re of the greatness.

The third father, who came out of the second father: he is glorious Jesus the Splendour. And, again, he too summoned three evocations after the pattern of the second father.

The first power whom he summoned is the Light Mind.

The second power whom Jesus summo[n]ed is the gre[at Jud]ge, who gives judgement on all the souls of mankind, [his] dwelling being established in the atmosphere under [ ... / ... ] wheel [ ... ] stars.

The third power is [the Y]outh, the gre[at ... ] light in his two persons, in [ ... ] that which has been established in the summons [and] the obedience.

The fourth father is the Light Mind. And, again, he too summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus.

The first power is the Apostle of Light.

The second is the counterpart, who shall come to the apostle and appear to him, becoming companion to him, sticking close to him everywhere; and providing help to him all the time, from all afflictions and dangers.

The third is the Light Form.

And also the fifth father is this Light Form; the one who shall appear to everyone who will go out from his body, corresponding to the pattern of the image to the apostle; and the thr[ee] great glorious angels who are come with her.

One (angel) ho[ld]s the prize in his hand. The second bears the light garment. The third is the one who possesses the diadem and the wreath and the crown of light. These are the three angels of light, the ones who shall come with this Light Form.

These are the five fathers who have come out of one another. They have appeared and man[ifes]ted through one another!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic frame: 'Once again the enlightener, our father [the] apostle of truth, is sitting in the midst of his disciples.' No cosmological content — purely hagiographic scene-setting.
- ¶2: Thesis statement of the teaching. Establishes the emanation principle: five fathers, each coming forth from the previous. This is the structural kernel the entire chapter unpacks.
- ¶3: First Father identified. Cosmological description: 'first eternal,' 'exists with five fathers for ever,' 'before every thing.' The doxological register ('blessed one of glory') is typical of substrate descriptions of the highest cosmic being. All attributes are cosmic.
- ¶4: First Father's three emanations begin. First emanation = Great Spirit / first Mother. Classic evocation/emanation language. 'Summoned from him' is the core verb of the emanation process.
- ¶5: Second emanation from first Father. Note the heavy manuscript damage but the enumeration structure is preserved. 'Manifested out of him' parallels 'appeared first' — consistent emanation vocabulary.
- ¶6: Third emanation from first Father. 'Eldest of all the counsellors' is a cosmic title. Manuscript damage but identification clear.
- ¶7: Summary and cosmic-processual statement. Recapitulates the first Father, then describes the cosmic victory of the three emanations over Darkness. Both sides stay within the cosmic system: emanations vs. Darkness, victory given to aeons. 'Root of all the lights' is systematic cosmic vocabulary.
- ¶8: Second father identified as Third Ambassador. 'Model of the King of lights' is cosmic correspondence. Pattern language: 'again, he too summoned' establishes the recursive emanation structure.
- ¶9: First emanation of second father. Pillar of Glory as cosmic support structure — 'bears up under all things,' 'great porter.' These describe cosmic function, both sides within the system.
- ¶10: Second emanation of second father. Jesus the Splendour is a cosmic figure (not the historical Jesus). 'Life eternal' describes his cosmic function — what this being provides within the system.
- ¶11: Third emanation of second father. Virgin of Light's cosmic function: seduction of the archons ('rulers and powers') through her image. Both sides cosmic — archons are cosmic beings, the image is a cosmic mechanism, the 'pleasure of the greatness' is cosmic will.
- ¶12: Third father identified. 'After the pattern of the second father' — the recursive pattern language is structural substrate. 'Evocations' used instead of 'emanations' or 'powers' — note vocabulary variation within the same teaching structure.
- ¶13: First clear editorial seam in the chapter. The substrate identification ('the Light Mind') is clean, but the editor immediately bridges to institutional application. 'Our pattern in the holy church' is first-person institutional voice. Institutional score 13.89 confirms.
- ¶14: Second emanation of third father. Great Judge's function (judgment of souls) and cosmic location (atmosphere, wheel, stars) are both cosmic — souls of mankind are the mixed light, the atmosphere is cosmic geography. Despite lacunae, the cosmic-locational description is preserved.
- ¶15: Heavy manuscript damage obscures the original structure. The substrate identification (Youth, light in two persons, summons and obedience) is preserved. 'The summons and the obedience' (Call and Answer) is standard cosmic-processual vocabulary. The gospel citation is clearly late intrusion — 'I am speaking about' breaks from third-person to first-person, marking the editorial voice.
- ¶16: The emanation structure statement ('summoned three powers after the pattern of Jesus') preserves the substrate's recursive pattern language. The institutional clause is inserted between the identification and the emanation statement, a clear editorial interpolation.
- ¶17: Only the identification survives as substrate. The functional description, unlike earlier emanations (Pillar of Glory 'bears up under all things,' Virgin 'takes away the heart of rulers'), describes the being's role in human institutional terms rather than cosmic-processual terms. Editorial fatigue is evident — compare with the clean cosmic functions in ¶9-11.
- ¶18: The counterpart/twin (syzygos) is a standard cosmic being in the emanation system. Its function — accompanying and protecting the apostle — describes a cosmic relationship between two cosmic roles. 'The apostle' here is a cosmic role, not a historical person. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. Compare with Pillar of Glory 'bears up under all things' — same functional-descriptive register. Zero institutional vocabulary score confirms.
- ¶19: Only the cosmic identification survives. The functional description has been entirely replaced by institutional application. Compare with the Virgin of Light (¶11) whose function is described in cosmic terms — the Light Form's function is described entirely in institutional terms.
- ¶20: Fifth father identified as the Light Form. Cosmic function: appearing at death ('go out from his body' = the soul's departure). 'Corresponding to the pattern of the image to the apostle' uses the substrate's recursive pattern language. The three angels are cosmic beings. Compare with ¶14 where the Great Judge 'gives judgement on all the souls of mankind' — same structure of cosmic being with function regarding human souls. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶21: The three-fold angelic description (prize, light garment, diadem/wreath/crown) is systematic correspondential enumeration — each angel carries a specific cosmic item. The summary 'these are the three angels of light' closes the substrate unit. The institutional application is appended as a final clause.
- ¶22: Closing summary of the entire teaching. Returns to the thesis of ¶2 ('summoned forth one of one'). The seam flag ('These are the') is a false positive — this is a natural substrate summary, not an editorial bridge. No institutional vocabulary. Content is purely cosmological.
- ¶23: Classic devotional closure. 'Blessed is he who' beatitude formula, 'life eternal,' 'light garments given to the righteous' — standard exhortation vocabulary. Seam flag correctly identifies this as editorial extension of the teaching. Exhortation score 35.9 is the highest in the chapter.

## Chapter 8
### Concerning the Fourteen Vehicles that Jesus has boarded

*Clear two-part structure: paragraphs 2-8 contain a systematic ten-vehicle cosmic descent teaching (the substrate core), while paragraphs 9-10 are later editorial additions extending the pattern to institutional content and closing with a beatitude. The ten vehicles map a coherent cosmological descent sequence: from the light ship and Ambassador, through the ship of the First Man and Pillar of Glory, through the Call and Answer (summons/obedience), and finally through the five light elements (air, wind, light, water, fire). Gardner notes this reverses the ascent order familiar from Ch. 71, confirming its systematic character. The editorial seam at paragraph 9 is textbook: 'After he had assumed these ten, he came and manifested in the flesh. He chose the holy church in four vehicles.' — the editor mimics the enumeration pattern but applies it to Manichaean community categories. Editorial fatigue data strongly supports this division.*

When Jesus the Son of Greatness came to these worlds, at the time that he unveiled the greatness, he boarded ten vehicles. He journeyed in the universe by them.

The first vehicle is the light ship, since he received instructions from the Ambassador there.

⟨p.37⟩ The second carriage is the ship of the First Man, since his dwelling is established there.

The third is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, since he shone forth there. He came below, he appeared in the world.

The fourth is the summons that he clothed upon his body. The fifth is the obedience that he swathed upon the summons.

The sixth is the living air, and since he assumed it too, he received anointment there. The seventh is the living wind that blows. The eighth is the light that illuminates. The ninth is the living water. The tenth is the living fire that is above them all.

He was journeying in these ten carriages among all the orders and all the [...], those above and those below.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening: 'Once again, the Light-Man speaks to the congregation that is sitting in front of him.' No substantive cosmological question — just attribution formula. 'Light-Man' is a variant of 'enlightener'; 'congregation sitting in front of him' is session-setting.
- ¶2: Sets up the systematic ten-vehicle enumeration. 'Jesus the Son of Greatness' functions as a cosmic entity name within the system — the descending redeemer figure. Crucially says TEN vehicles, not fourteen — the fourteen-count is the editor's extension. Both sides stay in the cosmic system: cosmic figure → cosmic descent through cosmic vehicles. No institutional or contemporary reference.
- ¶3: Enumeration unit #1. 'Light ship' and 'the Ambassador' (the Third Messenger) are cosmic entities/locations. The cosmic figure receives instructions at his first station. Substrate score 26.67 confirms cosmological vocabulary density.
- ¶4: Enumeration unit #2. 'Ship of the First Man' — cosmic vessel of a cosmic entity. The descent continues through cosmic stations. Manuscript page marker preserved.
- ¶5: Enumeration unit #3. 'Pillar of Glory' and 'Perfect Man' are cosmic entities. 'He came below, he appeared in the world' describes cosmic descent into the lower cosmos, not historical incarnation — it stays within the cosmological system. High substrate score (34.78).
- ¶6: Enumeration units #4-5. 'Summons' (the Call) and 'obedience' (the Answer) are cosmic elements in the Manichaean cosmological system — the Call that awakens and the Response. 'Clothed upon' and 'swathed upon' are the assumption/vestment formulas characteristic of the substrate. Both sides stay in the cosmic system.
- ¶7: Enumeration units #6-10: the five light elements (air, wind, light, water, fire). This is the core of the substrate — systematic five-element nomenclature mapped as cosmic vehicles. Gardner notes this reverses the ascent order from Ch. 71. Highest substrate score in the chapter (25.0). 'Living fire that is above them all' establishes hierarchical ordering within the elements.
- ¶8: Summary of the ten-vehicle cosmic descent. 'All the orders... those above and those below' maps the cosmic hierarchy traversed. Lacuna [...] preserved. This closes the substrate teaching — the ten cosmic stations are complete.
- ¶9: EDITORIAL SEAM. The ten-vehicle cosmic teaching is complete at paragraph 8. The editor now extends the enumeration pattern to institutional content: 'He chose the holy church in four vehicles' — holy brothers, pure sisters, male catechumens, female catechumens. Classic pattern mimicry: the editor imitates the enumeration syntax ('One is... The second is... The third is... The fourth is...') but applies it to Manichaean community categories. 'Manifested in the flesh' is the bridge phrase. Institutional vocabulary: 'holy church,' 'catechumens,' 'sons of the faith,' 'daughters of the light and truth.' One side of the mapping now reaches outside the cosmic system into community structure.
- ¶10: Devotional closure merging both layers: 'ten carriages in the zone, and the other four that are in the flesh, they complete and make fourteen.' This summary was written by the same editorial hand that added paragraph 9 — it treats the combined count as unified teaching. Soteriological summary ('made alive, redeemed, given victory') and classic beatitude formula ('Blessed is he who will know these mysteries') confirm exhortation layer. Exhortation score 10.1 in the data. This wraps the chapter with devotional closure.
