## Chapter 9
### The Explanation of the Peace, what it is; the Right Hand; the Kiss; the Salutation.

*This chapter presents the five ritual signs (peace, right hand, kiss, salutation, laying on of hands) as cosmic archetypes established by the First Man's descent to battle and ascent from victory. The oldest teaching layer is a two-series cosmological narrative: Series 1 (descent, ¶3–9) is nearly pure substrate; Series 2 (ascent, ¶10–14) preserves substrate cosmic narrative overlaid with editorial bridge formulas ('appropriate to the mystery of X, this Y has come about among mankind'). Significant editorial fatigue confirmed: substrate drops from 6.68→2.02 while institutional rises 2.18→9.16 in the second half. The chapter transitions from pure cosmic teaching through mixed cosmic+bridge to fully institutional application (¶16–19) and exhortation (¶21–24). A Light Form/Daēnā passage (¶20) preserves genuinely ancient Persian imagery of the soul's cosmic encounter. The Psalm 110:1 citation in ¶14 is a clear editorial insertion within cosmic narrative. The five-fold enumeration structure (peace, right hand, kiss, salutation, laying on of hands) is the substrate's organizational spine.*

This peace and this greeting that occur in the world, [from w]hom [did they come fo]rth? Or likewise, the right hand that occurs in [the world, it being h]onoured by mankind, of whom is it? Or the mystery of the kiss that they embrace one another with, [ ... ]? Or who is the one that reveals this salutation with which they make obeisance to one another? Or this laying on of hands, which an immensely great one bequeaths upon his inferior, giving him an honour and making him great; of whom is this laying on of hands?

these five signs are the mystery of the First Man. He came forth with them from the aeon of light. Also, when he finished his contest, he went up with these good signs. They received him into the aeons of light.

⟨p.38⟩ The first peace is the one that the gods and the angels in the land of light gave to the First Man; when he comes out against the enemy. The gods and the angels were walking with him; escorting him, giving to him their peace and power, and their blessing and fortification. This is the first peace that the gods and the angels gave to the First Man; as he comes forth from the aeon of light.

The first right hand is the one that the Mother of Life gave to the First Man; when he comes out to the contest.

The first kiss is this one with which the Mother of Life embraced the First Man; as he separates from her, coming down to the contest. Even all the gods and the angels who are in the aeons of light were making to embrace him! [ ... ] all of them also, and the church tutelaries (?), and the kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people. They were making to embrace him as [he] separates from them, accompanying him out, embracing him with the kiss of affection and love.

⟨p.39⟩ The first salutation is the salutation with which the First Man made obeisance; when he comes out [to the depths] below. He was bending his knees, making obeisance to the God of truth, and all the aeons [of light] who belong to the household of his people; entreating them for a power to escort him, as he comes forth.

The first laying on of hands is the one that the Mother of Life bequeathed upon the head of the First Man. She armed him and made him mighty. She laid hands on him, and sent him to the battle. He descended. He completed his struggle with the great powers and the enmity.

By the [se] five signs and these five myst[e]ri[es] the First Man came forth from the aeons of light against the enemy. He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.

Also, when he ascended from the war, he came in to the kingdom of the household of his people by these five mysteries!

The peace with which [he ascen]ded is the peace that the summons gave to him, [w]hen it was sent forth from the [Fa]ther of Life. It gave him the peace in the world of darkness. Also, that peace with which he descended is th[e sam]e as the one with which he ascended from the struggle!

this second right hand is the one that the Living Spirit gave the First Man, when he [brou]ght him upward out from the struggle.

this second kiss is the one with which the [Father o]f Life and the Mother of the living embraced the First Man, when he ascended from the struggle.

⟨p.40⟩ [The] second salutation with which the First Man made obeisance [ ... this] time to the God of truth and [ ... ] the blessed aeons and al[l] the aeons [ ... the] whole [land] of light, whe[n] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living brought the Man upward from the abyss of the struggle.

The second laying on of hands is this: When the Living Spirit brought the First Man upward from the war, he saved him from all the surging waves. He brought him upward, he gave him ease in the great aeons of light, which belong to the household of his people. He set [him] firmly before the Father, the Lord of the totality. He received this great laying on of hands that he may become leader of his brethren in the new aeon.

These five mysteries and these five signs came about first in the divine. these mysteries were not at the beginning amongst the powers of Darkness.

At the time of their coming forth, the Light Form shall come forth before them; and she redeems them from the darkness to the light [ ... ] This Light Form calms the person, with the kiss and her quiet, from fear of the demons w[ho de]stroy his body. By her asp[ec]t and her image [the] heart of the elect one, who is come forth from his bo[d]y, shall be calm for [him]. Afterwards, the angel who h[o]lds the victory prize extends to him the right hand. And it draws him out of the abyss of his body, and accepts him in with the [k]iss and love. That soul shall make obeisance to its red[eem]er, who is this Light Form.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The substantive questions define the five-fold teaching structure. They reference worldly practices ('occur in the world') but as the teaching PROBLEM, not as editorial application. The questions belong to the Persian pedagogical tradition — they establish what the teaching will map. - ¶2: The core teaching identifies the five signs with the First Man and establishes the two-part structure (departure and return). 'They are displayed in the world as miniatures' is an interesting cosmological principle about correspondence but is embedded in the editorial introduction and first-person claims. The core cosmological content maps the five signs to the First Man's journey — both sides within the cosmic system.
- ¶3: Pure substrate. Systematic cosmological teaching: maps the first peace to a cosmic event (gods and angels blessing the First Man at departure). Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system: cosmic beings → cosmic action. The enumeration marker 'The first peace' opens the five-fold descent series. No editorial application, no bridge to the world.
- ¶4: Pure substrate. Second element of the five-fold descent enumeration. Extremely concise — maps 'first right hand' to Mother of Life → First Man. High substrate density (20.83) confirms. The brevity itself may be a mark of antiquity — the teaching IS the mapping, with no elaboration.
- ¶5: Substrate: third element of the descent enumeration. The 'church tutelaries (?)' — the translator's question mark indicates uncertainty about the Coptic term. In context these are cosmic beings in the aeons of light, likely a cosmic category rendered with institutional vocabulary by the Coptic translators. 'Kinsfolk who belong to the household of his people' is cosmic household language. The institutional score (2.2) is triggered by this uncertain term; the content is entirely cosmic narrative.
- ¶6: Pure substrate. Fourth element of the descent enumeration. Maps the first salutation to the First Man's obeisance before departure. 'The God of truth' and 'the household of his people' are cosmic references. Both sides of the mapping remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶7: Substrate. Fifth and final element of the descent enumeration. The institutional score (9.43) is triggered by 'laying on of hands' but here this is the COSMIC ARCHETYPE — the Mother of Life performing the act on the First Man. This is precisely the kind of passage where institutional vocabulary appears in substrate because the substrate IS the origin-narrative of the institutional practice. 'He descended. He completed his struggle' has the terse, process-oriented quality of the oldest layer.
- ¶8: Substrate summary closing the descent series. High substrate density (15.62). Terse, impersonal, process-oriented: 'He humbled him. He was victorious against him by them.' This is the summarizing voice of the oldest teaching layer.
- ¶9: Substrate. Transitional summary bridging descent to ascent series — the First Man returns with the same five signs. The substrate score is 0.0 (the vocabulary counter missed it) but the content is unambiguously cosmic narrative: First Man ascending to the kingdom of his people. No editorial application, no contemporary reference.
- ¶10: The ascent series begins. The cosmic content introduces 'the summons' — a specific cosmic entity from the emanation hierarchy — giving peace to the First Man in darkness. This cosmological detail (different agent from the descent series) suggests genuine substrate. The bridge formula 'appropriate to the mystery... here and now' is the editor's consistent method of connecting each ascent element to worldly practice.
- ¶11: The cosmic content is precise: the Living Spirit (not the Mother of Life from the descent series) gives the right hand. Different cosmic agents for descent vs. ascent is a mark of genuine cosmological knowledge. The bridge formula follows the same pattern as ¶10.
- ¶12: The cosmic content again involves precise agents: 'the Father of Life and the Mother of the living' — both receiving the First Man. This specificity (both divine parents embrace him on return) is characteristic of the substrate's systematic cosmic knowledge. The editorial bridge is more expansive here, adding worldly social detail.
- ¶13: Extensive lacunae in the cosmic portion, but the surviving text describes the First Man's obeisance to the God of truth and all the aeons of light upon being brought upward. 'The abyss of the struggle' is cosmic geography. The bridge formula contains 'today' — the most explicit temporal marker of editorial present in this series.
- ¶14: The most complex mixed paragraph. The cosmic narrative is detailed: Living Spirit saving the First Man from 'surging waves,' setting him before 'the Father, the Lord of the totality,' and the new aeon. The Psalm 110 citation ('sit at my right hand... footstool under his feet') is the clearest marker of editorial insertion — a scriptural text applied to the cosmic narrative. This enters via the NT/OT, not from the substrate tradition. The 'new aeon' terminology IS substrate; 'leader of his brethren in the new aeon' describes the First Man's cosmic role.
- ¶15: Transitional paragraph. The two retained statements are genuine cosmological assertions: (1) the five signs originated in the divine realm, (2) they did not exist among the powers of Darkness. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. The removed material bridges to 'an apostle' and 'mankind' — the editorial present. This paragraph marks the seam between the substrate teaching (¶3–14) and the institutional application (¶16–19).
- ¶16: Fully institutional. 'The Light Mind... shall choose his church' applies the cosmic entity to church formation. 'With these five lessons he shall choose his church' is institutional — one side of the mapping reaches into the organized Manichaean community. High institutional score (20.83) confirms. This begins the church-application section that runs through ¶19.
- ¶17: Fully institutional. Describes a step-by-step process of church admission using the five signs: receive peace → become child of peace → elected to the faith → receive right hand → drawn to church. This is catechetical procedure, not cosmic mapping. 'He is elected to the faith' and 'cause him to approach the church' are institutional process language.
- ¶18: Fully institutional. 'Becomes a child of the church,' 'he makes obeisance to the holy church,' 'the hope of the faith, good works' — all institutional community language. Continues the catechetical sequence from ¶17. High institutional score (21.15).
- ¶19: Institutional. Describes completion of the five-sign reception process: 'conformed and built up in the truth,' 'become fulfilled people,' 'make obeisance and give glory to the God of truth.' The five signs are applied to community formation, not cosmic process. 'They shall come in to the Light Mind with these good signs' maps the institutional process to a cosmic entity but the primary domain is institutional.
- ¶20: The Light Form (Daēnā) encounter is genuinely ancient Persian cosmological imagery — the heavenly counterpart meeting the departing soul. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system: Light Form (cosmic being) → soul (cosmic light particle). The angel with the victory prize, the right hand, the kiss, the obeisance — all map the five signs to a cosmic process (soul's ascent). The ending shifts to institutional language ('apostles and chosen') and devotional closure ('crown... glory... for ever'). The substrate score of 0.0 reflects the vocabulary counter's limitations — the cosmic content uses soul/Light Form vocabulary rather than the typical substrate markers.
- ¶21: Classic exhortation/devotional closure. 'Concerning this I tell you, my brethren and my limbs' — standard first-person address formula. 'Let you find these five signs in your hands' — injunction to practice. 'I have brought this to you from the household of the living, out from the Father who sent me' — apostolic authority claim. High exhortation score (20.97) confirms.
- ¶22: Institutional. Audience-directed recapitulation: 'you have understood about the first peace and the first right hand...' — directs the community's comprehension. 'These five signs that I have brought' — first-person apostolic claim. 'You may part from the world' — eschatological promise tied to community membership. The paragraph uses the substrate's enumeration ('first peace... first right hand... first kiss') as content but the voice is institutional — directing the community.
- ¶23: Flagged as PROBABLE EDITORIAL EXTENSION by the seam detector. Fully institutional: 'the teachers and the deacons and the presbyters' — church offices; 'I have laid hands on' — first-person institutional authority; 'whoever will reject and disdain it... commits sin subject to God' — enforcement of institutional hierarchy. The highest institutional score in the chapter (33.66). This extends the laying on of hands teaching to enforce clerical authority — a classic editorial seam where cosmic teaching is appropriated for institutional legitimation.
- ¶24: Exhortation/devotional closure. Heavily damaged text. 'You, my beloved ones' — classic address formula. 'Your heart,' 'listen to him,' 'every deed that you do' — moral injunction fragments. 'He turns to you... from you the afflictions' — eschatological promise. This is the standard Kephalaia closure pattern: moral application appended to the completed teaching sequence.

## Chapter 10
### Concerning the Interpretation of the Fourteen [Great Aeons] of Light, about which Sethel has spoken in [his Prayer]

*Short hermeneutical chapter structured as interpretation of Seth's prayer. The oldest teaching layer is a fourteen-fold enumeration of cosmic entities and their cosmological function — a systematic degree-mapping. The editorial overlay is the identification of these entities with 'praises' in Seth's prayer and the dialogue framing. The chapter's substrate content is concentrated in ¶3-5, with the enumeration (5 elements + call/hearing + 5 sons of Living Spirit + 2 fathers = 14) forming the structural backbone. The final paragraph, though heavily lacunose, contains significant cosmological description of the Ambassador's role. The negative editorial fatigue score reflects the frame-heavy opening giving way to denser teaching in the second half.*

what are the [f]ourteen grea[t a]eons of light?

the [ ... ] below abide in fourteen [great aeons ...]: the five elements, the sons of the First Man, together with the call and the hearing. They anoint them, living in them. These shall make seven, five plus two.

[ ... the] f[ive s]ons of the Living Spirit who [support] all [the wei]ght of the universe; together with the [Li]vi[ng] Spirit [and the Fir]st Man, their fathers. [...] by which the universe is established.

[There are fourt]een aeons of light [ ... ] [...the la]nd that is not revealed. This land [ ... ... is estab]lished. They bore up under the enemy [ ... ... equa]ls them. The hidden plenitude that [ ... ] the Ambassador who came from the Father, the [ ... ] He came and stood firm in the midst of these [ ... ]: he is called the good father of [all] the ae[ons and gods] who are holy and conjoined. He has become leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father, the lord of all these cou[nse]ls. This universe is held together by their will [ ... ] to the end of all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Heavy dialogue frame vocabulary (24.59). The citation of Seth's prayer is an editorial hermeneutic act — it places the teaching within a textual tradition. The substantive question about the fourteen aeons reveals the teaching structure.
- ¶2: Pure dialogue attribution with first-person authority claim ('I am the one who will interpret for you'). No cosmological teaching content — only the speaker asserting interpretive authority. The reference to 'the fourteen great aeons of light about which he has spoken' merely echoes the question without adding teaching.
- ¶3: Systematic enumeration — five elements + call + hearing = seven. This is the first half of a fourteen-fold degree structure. 'They anoint them, living in them' describes a cosmic process (indwelling/anointing relationship between these entities). The enumeration arithmetic ('These shall make seven, five plus two') is characteristic of substrate list-teaching. Substrate score 13.04 confirms.
- ¶4: Completes the fourteen-fold enumeration: 5 sons of Living Spirit + Living Spirit + First Man (the two fathers) = 7, totaling 14 with ¶3. The supporting function ('support all the weight of the universe') and establishing function are cosmological process descriptions — both sides stay within the system. The Seth prayer identification is the editorial overlay binding substrate teaching to the hermeneutical frame.
- ¶5: Dense cosmological content despite heavy lacunae. Describes: (1) cosmic geography — 'the land that is not revealed'; (2) cosmic conflict — 'bore up under the enemy'; (3) emanation — 'the Ambassador who came from the Father'; (4) cosmic kingship — 'leader and a great king after the likeness of the first Father'; (5) cosmic function — 'This universe is held together by their will.' All mappings stay within the cosmic system. The Ambassador's role as king 'after the likeness of the first Father' is a degree-mapping (image of the eternal Father). The 'hidden plenitude' is cosmic geography terminology. Institutional score (1.48) is noise from 'father/lord' terminology that is cosmological here.

## Chapter 15
### [Concerning the ... ...] Five [Parts ...] Worlds of [...]

*Heavily lacunose chapter preserving a substrate teaching about the progressive differentiation of the dark pentad: five elements → five worlds → five trees/fruits → five creations of flesh → ten parts (male/female) → twelve parts (adding fire and lust) mapped to the twelve zodiac signs. The enumeration structure ('first part'... 'fourth part'... 'fifth time') is the skeleton of the teaching, though gaps make reconstruction difficult. Editorial fatigue is textbook: substrate dominates ¶2-7, then ¶8-9 apply the twelve-part mapping to 'the holy church' and 'the twelve sects,' and ¶10 closes with devotional formula. The substrate teaching is the oldest layer and is dominant in this chapter despite the lacunae.*

the two essences [...] Did Death arise and stand firm [...] up? Or else did it turn itself from [...] its armour [...] in its [...] but it finished [...] the two [...].

⟨p.48⟩ [...] it moved it and [...] they that are in the [...] of the earth of Darkness [...] first part.

Yet, after a long time, as there was not [...], it gushed and streamed up [...] the five elements of Death that gushed [...] which are smoke, fire, wind, water and darkness [...] the second time [...] they were sculpted [...] those in which Death was established [...].

The fourth part is the evil fruits [...] in the five trees that are in the five worlds of the land of Darkness.

The fifth time it spread itself out to its creatures that exist in the five worlds; the ones that were formed and generated from the five fruits of Death. They are the five worlds of flesh, the five creations of Darkness in which Death has been established. It spread itself out to ten parts, five male parts and five female [...].

Now, like the land of Darkness, in that its spirit and its [...] they nourished the five elements [...]; this is also [how] the ten parts that are in male and female, the fire and lust of the flesh [...] them, as they yoke them with one another [...] they were established against the First Man. This is [...] of the Darkness, it was condemned for ever.

Alongside the mystery of these twelve parts that came about in the Darkness [...] against the First Man. The twelve [...] the twelve spirits of error that came about from the twelve signs of the zodiac [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The question is substantive: it asks about the two essences (dualistic cosmological categories) and how Death arose. This defines the teaching topic. Frame formula is standard Kephalaia dialogue_frame vocabulary ('Once again, the enlightener is sitting'). The honorific 'great and secret matters of God' is frame language, but 'the two essences' names the cosmological topic and is preserved.
- ¶2: Extremely fragmentary but contains substrate markers: 'earth of Darkness' (cosmic geography) and 'first part' (beginning of an enumeration sequence). No editorial or institutional vocabulary. The enumeration marker places this as the beginning of a systematic degree-teaching.
- ¶3: Classic substrate: the five elements of Death enumerated (smoke, fire, wind, water, darkness) — the standard dark pentad. Process vocabulary ('gushed and streamed up', 'sculpted', 'established') describes cosmic generation. High substrate score (25.0). 'The second time' continues the enumeration sequence. Death functions as a personified cosmic agent throughout.
- ¶4: Enumeration marker ('The fourth part') continuing the degree-teaching. Maps fruits → five trees → five worlds → land of Darkness. All correspondences stay within the cosmic system. High substrate score (26.09). The five trees mapped to five worlds is a classic correspondential structure.
- ¶5: Continues the enumeration ('The fifth time'). The key cosmological move: the five creations of flesh divide into male and female, yielding ten parts. This doubling is the structural core of the chapter. All mappings cosmos→cosmos: five worlds → five fruits → five creations → ten parts (5 male + 5 female). High substrate score (21.31).
- ¶6: Summarizes the cosmic process: ten parts (male/female) energized by fire and lust, yoked together, established against the First Man. All within the cosmic system — no contemporary institutions referenced. 'Now, like' is comparative syntax, not an editorial bridge connective here since it introduces a cosmic analogy (land of Darkness nourishing elements → ten parts yoked). 'Condemned for ever' describes the cosmic fate of Darkness within the dualistic system. The First Man is a cosmic figure, not a contemporary reference.
- ¶7: Maps twelve parts → twelve zodiac signs → twelve spirits. Despite 'spirits of error' firing editorial_bridge vocabulary (13.51), the actual mapping stays cosmos→cosmos: the spirits COME FROM the zodiac signs, which is a cosmological-correspondential relationship. The editorial application of these spirits to contemporary institutions only begins in ¶8. - ¶8: Clear editorial pivot: 'the second living man, who dwells in the holy church' applies the First Man pattern to the Manichaean community. 'Holy church' is institutional vocabulary (13.89 institutional score). The comparison ('the way that they pursued [...] at the beginning of the First Man') explicitly bridges the cosmic substrate to contemporary community experience. One side of the mapping now reaches outside the cosmic system into the editor's ecclesial present. This is exactly where the editorial fatigue pattern predicts later-layer material.
- ¶9: Continues the editorial application: 'the light that dwells in the church' and 'the twelve sects' map the cosmic twelve-part structure onto the editor's contemporary religious landscape. Both editorial_bridge (20.0) and institutional (20.0) scores fire strongly. 'The twelve sects' is a specific identification of contemporary religious groups — the mapping has crossed from BEING to POINTING AT. Very fragmentary but clearly editorial.

Positioned at chapter end, consistent with editorial fatigue pattern. Too fragmentary to determine more, but the formula is characteristic of the devotional closure layer.

## Chapter 16
### [Concerning the Five] Greatnesses who [went forth] against the Darkness.

*The oldest teaching layer is overwhelmingly dominant. The chapter consists of two linked but potentially independent teachings: (1) a five-fold enumeration of the greatnesses (¶2-9) with systematic emanation hierarchy, and (2) five advent similes with cosmic interpretation (¶10-29). Gardner notes the second section is 'in effect a separate and unrelated kephalaion.' The similes use natural correspondences (noble son, judge, king inspecting craftsmen, fig-garden, butter in milk) to map cosmic processes — classic correspondential teaching method where both sides stay within the system. Only the opening dialogue formula (¶1) and closing beatitude (¶30) are clearly later layers. The 'Now, just as' formulas in ¶15 and ¶21 are simile-completion structures integral to the teaching, not editorial seams — they map analogy→cosmic entity with no institutional content. Gardner's note that Jesus the Splendour as fifth greatness 'may be a later development' is worth noting but at the textual level ¶7 follows the same enumeration pattern as ¶2-6.*

[...] the Darkness, it was defeated by five greatnesses.

The first greatness is the Father of Greatness who exists in calm and hiddenness; as he is established in his light-earth, in his own essence.

Four greatnesses were manifested and came forth of him. They were appointed to ten parts, and established in total twelve.

The second greatness poured out and was manifested. It was revealed from the first greatness. It is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, together with the First Man and his five sons; these are the second greatness.

⟨p.50⟩
The third greatness is the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder, the Living Spirit, and the five sons of the Living Spirit. These are the third greatness.

The fourth greatness is the Third Ambassador, and the Pillar of Glory, and all the light powers who were revealed from him. These are the fourth greatness.

The fifth greatness who came from the Father is Jesus the Splendour, and all his powers whom he summoned, his emanations who were [...] They poured out and were manifested from him.

This is, so that they were spread out and [...] fives: these five [sons] of the First Man [...] the entire building; another five are the five sons of the Living Spirit who are spread out to five places, supporting [...]

[...] total in twelve parts that [... There are] another two added to them who are the summons and the obedience. They are the father and the mother whom Jesus the Splendour by his advent has appointed as successors to his place [...] the great earth and the [...] in them.

[The advent ...] of the First Man who came [...] belonging to [...] existing in a kingdom [...] esteem before they who belong to his household [...] his enemies [...] and suffering by their wickedness; and they take from him [...] and a richness; and they imprison [him in] captivity and take from him their companies (?). Yet, he [...] by his humility; and he takes heart and restrains his enemy [...] in silence.

Just as with this man, this noble son whose enemies surround him, this also is how the advent of the First Man happened, [...] rulers, the enemies of the light.

In contrast, the advent of the Living Spirit and the commandment that he received, whereby he came upon all the rulers, it differs from that of the First Man. His advent [...] the advent of the First Man.

⟨p.51⟩
The advent of the Living Spirit is like this, when he was sent from the greatness that he might bring up the First Man. He resembles a judge the king would send to overturn the violence to that man, the noble son against whom his enemies sinned. And he, this judge, [...] hard and cruel [...] and he gives the means [...] by his righteousness and his strength; and he also judges all who are condemned according to their rebellious heart, and repays each according to his deserts. They who are worthy of blows he strikes with hard leather whips. Again, these who are worthy of cutting off their limbs, or indeed their [...] and he repays them accordingly. [...] and he binds and fetters them, and hangs by [...] and thus he does to them with a righteous judgement. And he kills them to flay their skin [...] so that they will be killed by a death [...] until they die. Again, [they for] whom it is fitting [...] to banish from their dwellings he may remove according to a righteous judgement; just as they had faulted and sinned against the noble son.

Also, the associates in the mystery and the assistants of this judge, these who belong to him in great strength; in an instant and haste they shall do the will of their master.

Now, just as this genuine judge, who was sent from the king to overturn the violence to this man, the noble son: this is also the case with the Living Spirit. He also was sent from the King of the lights to overturn the vengeance and violence to the First Man, against whom the King of the realms of Darkness had sinned. He faulted and sinned against him, and also his rebellious powers. Therefore, when (the Living Spirit) came, he bore up the First Man.

Also, all the rulers, the powers of sin who had faulted and sinned against the sons of the First Man, he has judged them according to a righteous judgement. He has bound them in heaven and earth. He put each one to the place fitting for him; he weighed each of them according to his cruelty and oppression. While some of them he enclosed in the prison, others he hung head down. Some [...] are crucified, others are sat down all the time. Some among them are fixed beneath their associates, restricted by a strong chain; others he made the holders of authority over their companions beneath, so that they may do their pleasure amongst these below them.

when the Father of Life established the judgements [...] He was revealed to all the powers of Darkness. He judged them as a true giver of judgement [...] This is the first judgement that the Living Spirit gave the rulers [in respect of the] First Man. Indeed, he gave victory to the First Man corresponding to his victory; but he condemned the King of the realms of Darkness corresponding to that by which he had erred.

[...] this chain, of this sort, became [...] the earth till the end time when the universe will be dissolved; and it perishes in the great fire and is separated. While the light goes to its country, the Darkness remains in the chain and fetter for ever.

The advent also of the Third Ambassador, when he came to inspect they who are imprisoned in the zone: he is like a great king who comes and inspects craftsmen [...] and [...] and [...]

Also, they who are good, of the craftsmen and architects and artisans whom he has there in each place: he shall see that they have worked well, and have fallen to his works in their fashion. He shall rejoice with great joy [...] He shall thank the artisans who have constructed his works [and completed] his orders.

Now, just as this great king who comes and inspects his works and fortifications, so also is the advent of the Third Ambassador. For, when he too came, he saw [his works] established and prepared and [...] series of the rulers prepared and established there, above and below, outside and within, in the great pattern that his brothers had made. They had come before him to the contest, they who had fixed and created the universe before he came. He saw the gods and the angels established and controlling the fortifications [...] guarding the entire ruling-power [...] which his first powers had done. They are the First Man and the Living Spirit and the Great Spirit, the Mother of Life.

Therefore, when he saw them, that they had created everything with great wisdom, [...] as they looked he plundered (?) [...] in his advent, by which he came, he was unveiled to them. He says to the Living Spirit: "The judgement that you have [...] good, what you uttered [...] you gave to them correspondingly; so that they were moved and raised up to that of which they were not worthy".

⟨p.53⟩
The advent also of Jesus the Splendour, the time when he came to all [...] being just like a man if he is sent to pull out a [...] burn a difficult fig-garden with fire. Therefore, again, when he has first cut the bad trees with his axe and has pulled out [...] and their body with his fire, so that they should not return to sprout from this time, nor bear fruit bad to eat [...]

Afterwards, he also planted good plants: the tree of life that will make good fruit.

Again, this is like this simile of the advent of Jesus the Splendour. It happened like this; because he too set out [...] his power and his wisdom, in his [...]-ness [...] a king and a saviour [...] in his glorious wisdom he destroyed them. He bound and crushed them like the fire that shall burn and destroy trees. This is also how he performed his will in the zone, among the many powers, until he reached the form of the flesh of Adam and Eve, the first humans. [He perfected] his will in Eve, he [...] He also gave hope to Adam, and the good news [...] glorious [...] He went up to the heights [...] of light.

⟨p.54⟩
Then the summons and the obedience, the great counsel that came to the elements, which are set in conjunction: it mixed with them, it was established in silence. It bears up [...] until the end time when it can arise and stand firm in the great fire. It will gather to it its own soul, and sculpt it in the Last Statue. You will also find it sweeps out and casts from it the pollution that is foreign to it. However, the life and the light that are in all things it gathers in to it, and builds upon its body.

The time when this Last Statue will be perfect in all its limbs, then it can become free and ascend from that great struggle through the Living Spirit its father, the one who comes and brings a [...] limb. He brings it up from within this gathering (?), the melting down and destruction of all things.

Now, this counsel, that summons and obedience, resembles thus cooled butter. If a person will take and set it in hot milk, it melts down and soaks in with this hot milk. It melts in another time in silence and quiet to the midst [...] yet also, of the power and the taste of this milk [...] is drawn to (the butter), and (the milk) sets and becomes cold [...] in [...] and the [...] this milk it shall clarify and discard. However, the oil and the grease and the 'beauty' of the milk it shall gather in to it.

⟨p.55⟩
Again, this too is what the advent of that thought is like, the counsel of life, which is the summons and obedience that came to the elements. This is the one that at the end can arise and stand firm, and will gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything. It can build them upon its body; it will gather them in to it [...] in its image for ever and ever [...] from this time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Classic mixed paragraph: dialogue frame formula wrapping a substantive cosmological topic statement. The teaching content introduces the five-fold structure that the chapter will develop.
- ¶2: First enumeration unit. Maps the first greatness to its cosmic attributes: calm, hiddenness, light-earth, own essence. Both sides of every mapping are within the cosmic system. The substrate vocabulary score (12.0) confirms cosmological register.
- ¶3: Structural-mathematical accounting of the emanation system. Gardner calls the twelve-part calculation 'undoubtedly forced,' but the content remains within cosmic architecture — counting emanated entities. No vocabulary leaves the system.
- ¶4: Second enumeration unit. Emanation language ('poured out and was manifested,' 'revealed from') is systematic process vocabulary. Maps the second greatness to its cosmic constituents.
- ¶5: Third enumeration unit. High substrate score (58.62) — dense with cosmic entity names. Systematic listing of the creation-phase emanation hierarchy.
- ¶6: Fourth enumeration unit. Same syntactic pattern as ¶4-5. Maps the fourth greatness to the ascent-phase emanation hierarchy.
- ¶7: Fifth enumeration unit. Gardner notes Jesus the Splendour as fifth greatness 'may be a later development of Manichaean doctrine.' However, the text follows the identical enumeration pattern and emanation vocabulary as ¶2-6. At the textual level, this is structurally integral to the five-fold teaching. Classified as substrate with this uncertainty flagged.
- ¶8: Pentadic distribution — mapping how the cosmic sons are spread to their five cosmic places. Systematic spatial cosmology. Heavy lacunae but the visible text is entirely within the cosmic system.
- ¶9: Completes the dodecadic accounting. The summons and obedience are cosmic entities. 'Appointed as successors to his place' describes cosmic role assignment, not institutional succession. Both sides within the cosmic system despite heavy damage.
- ¶10: The 'noble son' simile for the advent of the First Man. This is correspondential teaching through analogy — a natural situation mapping a cosmic process. Both sides (the noble son's situation and the First Man's descent) stay within the cosmic narrative. The simile is integral to the teaching structure: simile (¶10) → cosmic mapping (¶11).
- ¶11: Interpretive key connecting the noble son simile to the cosmic event. 'The rulers, the enemies of the light' maps the analogy squarely onto cosmic entities.
- ¶12: Structural transition introducing the second advent. The teaching compares the Living Spirit's advent to the First Man's — a cosmic-to-cosmic contrast. This is systematic exposition.
- ¶13: Extended judge simile for the Living Spirit. The graduated punishments (blows, cutting limbs, binding, flaying, banishment) describe what the cosmic judge DOES — this is correspondential teaching mapping judicial action onto cosmic process. No side of the mapping leaves the cosmic system.
- ¶14: Continues the judge simile — the judge's assistants correspond to the Living Spirit's cosmic helpers. Still within the simile framework.
- ¶15: SEAM FLAG IS FALSE POSITIVE. The 'Now, just as' is a simile-completion formula integral to the teaching pattern (simile → 'just as X, so also Y' → cosmic mapping). Both sides map within the cosmic system: judge→Living Spirit, king→King of the lights, noble son→First Man, enemies→King of the realms of Darkness. No institutional vocabulary present despite the flag. The institutional score (3.53) appears to be noise.
- ¶16: Cosmic judgement of the rulers — describes how the Living Spirit distributed the defeated cosmic powers into their cosmic positions (bound in heaven and earth, enclosed, hung head down, crucified, chained). This is systematic cosmic process: each punishment corresponds to a cosmic position. Classic substrate mapping of judgement onto cosmic architecture.
- ¶17: Mixed: dialogue address formula ('Happen you know this') wraps substrate teaching about the first cosmic judgement. The core content describes the Living Spirit's judgement in cosmic-correspondential terms: 'corresponding to his victory,' 'corresponding to that by which he had erred' — these are precise correspondential formulas mapping action to consequence within the cosmic system.
- ¶18: Cosmic eschatology — describes the final separation of light and Darkness. Both sides within the cosmic system: light→its country, Darkness→chain and fetter. The chain imagery corresponds to the cosmic binding described in ¶16.
- ¶19: Introduces the Third Ambassador's advent with the king-inspecting-craftsmen simile. 'The zone' is cosmic geography. The simile maps cosmic inspection onto a natural analogy.
- ¶20: Continues the king/craftsmen simile. The craftsmen and artisans correspond to the cosmic builders (the gods who constructed the cosmos). The simile describes cosmic satisfaction with the created order.
- ¶21: SEAM FLAG IS FALSE POSITIVE. Same simile-completion formula as ¶15. 'Now, just as X, so also Y' maps king→Third Ambassador, fortifications→cosmic architecture, craftsmen→First Man/Living Spirit/Mother of Life. Every entity is cosmic. - ¶22: The Third Ambassador addresses the Living Spirit — cosmic entity speaking to cosmic entity. 'You gave to them correspondingly' uses explicit correspondential language. The speech is within the cosmic drama, not editorial frame.

- ¶24: Completes the tree simile — destruction of bad trees followed by planting of the tree of life. 'The tree of life' is a cosmic entity, not a citation. Both sides within the system.
- ¶25: Maps the tree simile onto the cosmic narrative of Jesus the Splendour's work: destroying powers in the zone, reaching Adam and Eve, ascending to the heights. All cosmic events. 'The zone' is cosmic geography. Adam and Eve are cosmological figures (the first humans in the cosmic drama), not historical persons.
- ¶26: Describes the cosmic function of the summons and obedience — mixing with elements, gathering soul into the Last Statue, separating pollution from light. Pure cosmic process. 'You will also find' is a pedagogical convention within the teaching voice, not an editorial address. The Last Statue doctrine is systematic cosmic eschatology: body-building as cosmic gathering.
- ¶27: Continues the Last Statue cosmic eschatology. The Statue ascending through the Living Spirit, the melting down and destruction of all things — pure cosmic process vocabulary. 'Perfect in all its limbs' is body-cosmos correspondence.
- ¶28: The butter-in-milk simile — a remarkable natural correspondence for the cosmic process of the summons and obedience. Butter melting into hot milk, absorbing its essence, then separating = the summons mixing with elements, gathering light, separating from pollution. This is classic correspondential teaching: natural process mapping cosmic process. Both sides within the system.
- ¶29: Maps the butter simile back to the cosmic entity (summons and obedience). 'Gather in to it all the lives, and the light that has remained behind in everything' — cosmic eschatological gathering process. 'Build them upon its body' — body-cosmos correspondence. Minor exhortation score (3.53) likely from 'for ever and ever' — a cosmic duration marker, not devotional closure.
- ¶30: Classic beatitude closure formula: 'Blessed is every one who will believe in these five advents and be confirmed in the knowledge.' This is the devotional wrapper appended to the substrate teaching sequence. The seam flag correctly identifies this as editorial extension. The formula addresses the community ('every one who will believe'), pivoting from cosmic exposition to audience application. Exhortation score (15.62) confirms.

## Chapter 17
### The Chapter of the Three Seasons

*This chapter is dominated by the oldest teaching layer. The substrate presents a systematic temporal-cosmological framework: three periods of cosmic history defined by the actions of cosmic agents (First Man, Living Spirit, Mother of Life, Ambassador, Sin). Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system — no contemporary application, no institutional content, no exhortation. The only editorial material is dialogue frame attribution in paragraphs 1, 7, 8, 9, 10. The teaching about relative durations (first period longest, second shortest, third intermediate) is a distinctive substrate feature — systematic quantitative comparison of cosmic epochs. Jesus here is Jesus the Splendour, a cosmic emanation figure, not a historical reference. The chapter shows no editorial fatigue; it maintains substrate register throughout.*

From the time when the light came forth and was crucified in the darkness, to the season when the Statue will ascend and the entire light, which was conjoined with the darkness, will be purified and cleansed. From that time on, from that time to the season of the end totals three seasons.

Three seasons: from the time when the First Man went down to the abyss, and waged the war and the struggle against the King of the realms of Darkness and his other powers. He was victorious, while his sons remain behind with them in the worlds [... ...] in the [...] until he shall catch all of them [...] he bound them all up, so that not one of them escaped his hand. He won a great victory [... ...] The First Man spent human lifetimes and generations in the land of darkness, until he prevailed [...] ⟨p.56⟩ he fulfilled the will of the greatness that was commanded of him.

Then they sent the Living Spirit, the Father of Life. He brought the First Man up from the struggle, he brought him up to the land of light. This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent.

The second season is from the time when the First Man ascended. The Living Spirit came, and the Mother of Life. They set in order the things of the universe, they set up [...] the Father of Life and the Mother of the living set in order [...] the universe, up to the moment when they go out [...] it was reckoned to the second season.

[...] the universe [...] the Third Ambassador spread [...] that is in the [...] living; he [...] he separated beauty from all the powers and scooped it up to the heights, he swept the waste to the abyss, and he [... ...] Now, from the moment when the Ambassador revealed his image he purified the light. Sin spurted up towards him. He hid his image. Sin came [...] from the darkness. It sculpted the trees and the abortions fell down. Afterwards they sculpted Adam and Eve in the flesh. Until the moment when Jesus was sent and he [...] as he performed there his will. He gave the hope to Adam, and ascended to the heights.

Now, from the time when the Ambassador displayed his image until the season when the Statue will ascend at the end, shall be reckoned to the third season.

[...] lasts long in the time that the First Man spent in the world of Darkness. It is a very great count of time, more than the (other) two seasons!

The first season, which the First Man spent in the world of Darkness, is how much longer than the time that this universe has been? While it is established?

If you should say that [... ...] it is small, if you should [... ...] to him [...] and you consult [...] you will tell him: Twenty times! It is greater again in this other one that you will consult: Thirty times! Less also is this other one [... ...] people will receive the {} of the trees [... ...] the years and the generations [...] earth to the heights. This is [...]

This is the third period, from the time when the Ambassador revealed his image until the Statue will ascend from the lump. It again, that time, is greater than the intermediate; but it does not come equal to the time of the First Man. For this intermediate season, in which the Father of Life and the Mother of Life constructed the universe with its orders, is shorter than that of the First Man.

Great is the period of the First Man! It lasts longer than the season in which the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the world and the Statue ascends. From the time too when the Ambassador displayed his image, until the dissolution of the universe [... ...] when the Father of Life and the Mother of Life shaped the universe.

These are the [...] three periods of time that occur. ⟨p.58⟩

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula wraps a substantive cosmological definition of the total timespan. The teaching content defines the three-seasons structure — the foundational framework for the entire chapter. 'Crucified' as a term for light's entrapment in darkness is distinctive Manichaean cosmological vocabulary.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological narrative of the first period. The enumeration marker 'Three seasons:' launches the systematic teaching. All agents are cosmic (First Man, King of the realms of Darkness). 'Human lifetimes and generations' measures cosmic duration, not human history. The institutional score (2.73) likely triggered by 'generations' but this is cosmological time-measurement, not community reference.
- ¶3: Pure substrate. The summary formula 'This is the first period, from the descent of the First Man to his ascent' is the teaching voice defining the temporal structure. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic: Living Spirit → rescue → land of light.
- ¶4: Pure substrate defining the second period. Enumeration marker 'The second season is' structures the teaching. Cosmic agents (Living Spirit, Mother of Life, Father of Life) perform cosmic construction. Lacunae damage but no editorial content visible in what survives.
- ¶5: Cosmological narrative of the third period. The Ambassador's image-revelation, Sin's reaction (personified cosmic agent), the sculpting of trees/abortions, and the creation of Adam and Eve — all cosmic process within the system. 'Jesus' here is Jesus the Splendour, a cosmic emanation figure who awakens Adam — not a historical reference. Both sides of every mapping remain within the cosmic architecture. The verbs 'separated', 'scooped up', 'swept', 'sculpted' describe cosmic manufacturing processes.
- ¶6: Summary definition of the third period, parallel to paragraph 3's summary of the first period. The 'Now' here is not an editorial bridge connective — it introduces a definitional statement that closes the three-period enumeration. Pure cosmic temporal mapping.
- ¶7: Heavily damaged. The surviving clear text is frame formula ('Then the disciples asked the apostle'). The fragments 'with each other in the light earth' contain the cosmological term 'light earth' but are too damaged to yield intelligible teaching. The question likely asked about the relative durations of the three periods, setting up the teaching in paragraphs 8-12.
- ¶8: Frame wraps a substantive teaching about the first period's duration exceeding the other two. The comparative temporal analysis is core substrate — systematic quantification of cosmic epochs.
- ¶9: Substantive cosmological question within the Persian pedagogical tradition. The question defines the next teaching unit: the ratio of the first period to the universe's duration. This reveals the teaching structure — it IS the substrate. The question stays entirely within the cosmic system.
- ¶10: Heavily damaged but the surviving fragments show quantitative comparison of cosmic durations ('Twenty times!', 'Thirty times!'). The second-person address ('if you should say') is pedagogical voice within the teaching tradition, not editorial application — both sides of the inquiry remain within the cosmic system. The numbers suggest a systematic ratio teaching that was the core point of this passage.
- ¶11: Pure substrate. Systematic comparison of the three periods' durations: first > third > second. This is the core teaching point — a quantitative ordering of cosmic epochs. All agents and domains are cosmic. 'The Statue will ascend from the lump' uses a distinctive cosmological image for the final gathering of light.
- ¶12: Recapitulation of the duration hierarchy. The exclamatory opening 'Great is the period of the First Man!' is teaching voice — emphatic statement of the systematic finding, not devotional exhortation. All content remains within the cosmic temporal system. 'Dissolution of the world/universe' is cosmological eschatology, not contemporary application.
- ¶13: Closing summary of the three-periods teaching. The seam flag for 'These are the' is a false positive — this is not a bridge to institutional content but a natural closure to the enumeration. No institutional, exhortatory, or editorial content follows. The formula summarizes the cosmic teaching and closes the chapter.

## Chapter 18
### [Concerning the Five] War[s that the] Sons of [Li]ght waged with the Sons] of Darkness.

*The oldest teaching layer is strongly dominant. This chapter preserves a systematic five-fold enumeration mapping five cosmic wars to five principal divinities (First Man, Living Spirit, Third Ambassador, Jesus the Splendour, Counsel of Life). The structure is complete and self-contained. Teaching purity is very high (0.842) with minimal editorial fatigue (0.09). The only significant editorial intrusions are: (1) the dialogue frame formula in ¶1, (2) a Gospel citation appended to the first war in ¶2 ('As the saviour has said...'), and (3) a beatitude/exhortation closure in ¶¶8-9. The five-fold enumeration with its systematic cosmic agents, cosmic actions, and cosmic outcomes is classic substrate. Heavy lacunae throughout (especially ¶¶4-5, 7) but the preserved skeleton is clearly correspondential-cosmological.*

The sons of light waged five wars against the sons of darkness. The sons of light humiliated the sons of darkness in them all.

The first war is that of the First living Man, which he waged against the King of the realms of Darkness, and all the rulers that had come forth from the five worlds of [...]. He hunted them with his net, which is the living soul. He [...] in the snare and [...] the face of the [...] his virgin, the living fire [...] they did not find how to escape his net [...] he caught them like fish [...] the five counsels of the five elements [...]. He cut and dug out the root of the five trees, [...] root of the evil tree, like an axe.

The second war is that of the Living Spirit [...]. He has constructed the things and the worlds of light; he spread out the rulers and chained them in the middle [...] the stars in [...] the earths [...] them in the three vessels. For the rulers rebelled and were set against the Living Spirit. They wished to [...] since they knew and realised that he might bind and fetter them with a strong chain. Yet he, the Living Spirit, (appeared to them) with many aspects, with numerous [...], with many hands and great cruelties. He was revealed to them, he imprisoned them with much skill. Not one of them was able to save (himself), and he {...} [...]. Now, since he [...] he trapped them [...] ⟨p.59⟩ [...].

The third war is that of the Third Ambassador who has displayed his image [...] what is below [...] scooped [...] all light and the property [...] the ones that are consumed [...] the entire ruling-power [...] pride of the light [...] extending it [...]. A person comes, who is powerful [...]; he takes his riches [...], silver [...], his pride and wantonness.

This is what the ruling-power is like [...], all riches [...] as they travel [...] light [...]. He cleansed and purified it.

The fourth war is this that Jesus the glorious one waged against all the rebels who had rebelled above and below. When the light was purified from them by the image of the Third Ambassador, their chains loosened [...]. The glorious one in the fourth war; he came and brought [...] a rebellion of the entire ruling-power. He girded himself [...] all of it another time. He constructed the whole structure and he made [...] a great contest; he humiliated therein. [...] he fulfilled the will of the greatness, [he gave the] hope to Adam [...] to his light ship.

The fifth war is this that the counsel of life [...] which is the summons and the obedience [...] great [...] and it placed [...] upon the [...] and the [...] ⟨p.60⟩ [...] of death, which [...] with it. And it was victorious over [...] for ever, and it gave the kingdom [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The thesis statement — five wars, sons of light vs. sons of darkness — defines the entire teaching structure. This is a substantive cosmological proposition, not a frame formula. The dialogue attribution wrapping it is clearly editorial.
- ¶2: The substrate core is rich: First Man, King of the realms of Darkness, five worlds, living soul, living fire, five counsels, five elements, five trees — all systematic cosmic mappings. The fishing/net/snare imagery is correspondential (cosmos→cosmos). The 'root of the evil tree' image was apparently present in the substrate, and the editor attached the Gospel citation to it. The seam is sharp: the substrate says 'like an axe' and then the editor glosses 'As the saviour has said...'
- ¶3: Pure substrate. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: Living Spirit → cosmic construction → chaining rulers → three vessels → stars and earths. The narrative of the rulers' futile rebellion is systematic cosmological process — describing what happened in the cosmic drama, not pointing to contemporary reality. 'Three vessels' is technical cosmic vocabulary.
- ¶4: Substrate. The Third Ambassador displaying his image to seduce the archons and draw out light — a core cosmological process. The parable element ('A person comes, who is powerful...') is not an external citation but a correspondential illustration embedded within the cosmic teaching itself — both sides stay in the system. Silver as cosmic correspondence recalls the metal-realm mappings of Ch.6.
- ¶5: Substrate continuation of the third war. Highest substrate density in the chapter (18.18). Despite heavy lacunae, the preserved text maps cosmic process: ruling-power, light, cleansing/purification — all internal to the cosmic system.
- ¶6: Substrate, though 'Jesus the glorious one' raises a compositional question. In Manichaean cosmology, Jesus the Splendour (Iesous Ziwa) occupies a structural slot in the emanation hierarchy as a cosmic being. Here he fills the fourth position in a five-fold war enumeration — his role is cosmic (subduing rebellion above and below, constructing structure, giving hope to Adam, light ship). Both sides of all mappings are internal to the cosmic system. Whether 'Jesus' was in the original Persian substrate or is Mani's substitution for an earlier cosmic agent is uncertain, but the teaching structure — the systematic role — is clearly substrate.
- ¶7: Substrate. The fifth and final war completing the enumeration. 'The counsel of life' and 'the summons and the obedience' are technical cosmological entities — the Call and Answer pair that effects final redemption. Despite extreme lacunae, the preserved structure (eschatological victory over death, giving the kingdom) maps cosmic process. Highest substrate score (18.87) confirms.
- ¶8: Exhortation/devotional closure. The shift from specific cosmic agents (counsel of life, summons, obedience) to generic categories ('the good... the wicked') marks a layer boundary. 'For ever and ever' is a devotional formula. Exhortation score 23.08 — the only paragraph in the chapter with any exhortation vocabulary. Position is classic: devotional summary appended after the five-fold teaching is complete.
- ¶9: Classic exhortation closure. 'Blessed is every elect one who will...' is the beatitude formula diagnostic of the exhortation layer. 'Elect one' is institutional vocabulary. 'After the likeness of these five' explicitly connects the preceding cosmic teaching to community application. The five-war teaching is complete and self-contained without this paragraph.

'All the apostles' in this context refers to the cosmic chain of light-emissaries whose prophetic work is sealed by the final eschatological event — it stays within the cosmic system as part of the salvation narrative rather than pointing to institutional succession. The closing formula 'they came from him in the beginning, as they will return again to the light' is a pure cosmic-cycle teaching. NOTE: 'all the apostles' is ambiguous — it could reflect institutional vocabulary. Flagging rather than removing because the eschatological context keeps the reference within the cosmic narrative.
- ¶13: 'Blessed is every person who can understand these mysteries' — standard beatitude closure formula. Matches the exhortation layer pattern identified across the corpus: appended to the end of a teaching sequence, direct address to audience, devotional wrapper.

## Chapter 20
### The Chapter of the Name of the Fathers

*Short, highly substrate chapter presenting a three-fold emanation hierarchy mapping divine beings to their cosmic dwelling-places. The teaching structure is: being → realm of greatness → established in it. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system (no contemporary application, no institutional content, no exhortation). Dialogue frame is confined to opening paragraphs. The seam flag on ¶4 is a false positive — institutional vocabulary field is empty. Negative editorial fatigue (-6.00) confirms substrate increases toward chapter end. Compare chs. 7 and 25 for the same emanation hierarchy pattern.*

The Father of Greatness, as he is named; due to what reason is he given the name 'the Father of Greatness'?

The Father, who is the first established thing, shall be called 'the Father of Greatness'. His greatness is the great earth where he lives, he being established in it [...] the essence of the light that swathes all the rich gods and the angels and the dwellings [...] is set over it.

Furthermore, they shall call the Third Ambassador 'father'. His greatness is the light ship of living fire wherein he lives, he being established in it.

Once again, Jesus the Splendour shall himself also be called 'father'. His greatness is the ship of living waters where he lives, he being established in it.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Substantive cosmological question defining the teaching topic: the correspondence between the name 'Father of Greatness' and the cosmic reality it designates. The question itself structures what the teaching will map. Persian pedagogical tradition.
- ¶2: Heavy formulaic disciple response (dialogue_frame score 37.93). 'We beseech you, our master, that you may enlighten us' is pure honorific frame. The question 'who is it that is named the Father of Greatness?' echoes ¶1 without adding new cosmological content.
- ¶3: Core substrate teaching: maps the Father to his cosmic dwelling (the great earth / land of light). The formula 'his greatness is X where he lives, he being established in it' is the structural template that ¶4 and ¶5 iterate. Lacunae suggest manuscript damage. 'The first established thing' is cosmogonic priority language — the Father as ontological ground.
- ¶4: Second iteration of the being→realm mapping. The seam flag is a FALSE POSITIVE: institutional vocabulary field is empty. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic: Third Ambassador → light ship of living fire (= sun). 'Furthermore' is a list continuation marker within the enumeration, not an editorial bridge connective. The mapping structure is identical to ¶3 and ¶5.
- ¶5: Third and final iteration of the being→realm mapping: Jesus the Splendour → ship of living waters (= moon). 'Once again' here functions as list continuation (parallel to 'Furthermore' in ¶4), NOT as dialogue frame formula. The three-fold structure (Father/great earth, Ambassador/ship of fire, Jesus/ship of waters) maps the emanation hierarchy to cosmic dwelling-places. Complete teaching sequence.

## Chapter 21
### [C]oncerning the Father of Gr[eat]ness: [ho]w he is established and determined.

*This chapter is almost entirely oldest-layer substrate. It presents a systematic five-fold emanation teaching: the Father of Greatness established in his five light limbs (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration = the storehouses), his twelve wisdoms, and the five great springs that gush forth from him (light, perfume, living voice, [lacuna], glory). Both sides of every mapping remain within the cosmic system. There is zero editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation vocabulary. The only non-substrate material is the formulaic dialogue opening (¶1) and a minor audience reference in ¶2. Heavy lacunae throughout, especially in ¶¶7-8, but the five-fold enumeration structure is clear. Teaching purity 1.000 confirms the dominance of the oldest layer.*

Very great is [land of] light [...] due to the first Father of Greatness. How he ex[ists and is] established in his five light limbs, which are [ ... the store]houses that have neither limit nor measure to the[m ... ] mind, thought, insight, counsel and [consideration ... ] his twelve light limbs that are his twelve wisdoms.

Now, there are five great light lim[bs] in each one [ ... ] five grea[t] springs of praise gush for[th ... ] five great [ ... ] lig[ht ... ]

The first is his light that enlig[htens ... ] it gushed forth from him and pour[ed ... ] a[l]] aeons.

The second is the perfume of the [ ... ] and pervades all the aeons of greatness.

The third is his living voice of [ ... ... ] all his aeons [ ... ]

The fourth is his [ ... ]-ness [ ... ] gushes forth from him [ ... o]ut [ ... ]

⟨p.65⟩ The fif[th] is his great glorio[us ... ] that [ ... , w]hich [has been ma]nifested all the tim[e], whi[ch contin]ues [ ... ] all of them. It was sculpted in them [ ... ] sealed in the [m ... ] sealed [ ... ] as they send [ ... ] in [ ... ... ] they too receive nourishment [ ... ] immediately [ ... his] living voi[ce ... ] joy [ ... ] set up anew. It shall gush forth from it [ ... ] they shall [ ... ] and become rich in the [ ... ] too is beautiful [ ... ] portray[al ... ] adornment [ ... ] and they live [ ... ... ] for ev[e]r.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic dialogue frame: 'Once again the apostle speaks to his disciples' plus 'Hear this lesson that I will recount to you.' No cosmological content whatsoever. Standard session-opening formula.
- ¶2: Core content is systematic cosmological mapping: Father of Greatness → five light limbs → storehouses (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration) → twelve wisdoms. This is the classic five-faculty/twelve-wisdom degree structure. The audience reference ('they who will hear and know it') is a minor pedagogical insertion. High substrate score (19.12) confirms.
- ¶3: Introduces the five springs/qualities that gush forth from the five limbs. This is the structural pivot that sets up the five-fold enumeration to follow. 'Springs of praise gush forth' — emanation vocabulary mapping limbs to springs. Both sides stay within the cosmic system.
- ¶4: First enumeration unit: light → enlightens → gushes forth → pours through all aeons. Classic emanation language. Enumeration marker 'The first is' preserved as structural element of the teaching.
- ¶5: Second enumeration unit: perfume → pervades all aeons. Sensory correspondence (olfactory) mapped to cosmic pervasion. Substrate score 17.65 from 'aeons of greatness.' The five senses often correspond to the five limbs/springs in this tradition.
- ¶6: Third enumeration unit: living voice → aeons. Auditory correspondence. Despite heavy lacunae, the enumeration structure is clear. 'Living voice' recurs in ¶8, confirming it as a technical cosmological term in this teaching.
- ¶7: Fourth enumeration unit: heavily damaged but preserves the enumeration marker and the 'gushes forth' emanation formula consistent with the other four units. The lost quality name would complete the five-fold sensory-cosmic mapping.
- ¶8: Fifth and final enumeration unit: glory/splendor. This is the longest unit, elaborating the fifth spring with process vocabulary: sculpted, sealed, nourishment, gushing forth, beauty, portrayal, adornment, living forever. All describe cosmic processes within the aeons — both sides remain in the cosmic system. 'For ever' describes the eternal nature of the aeons, not an eschatological promise to an audience. Despite heavy lacunae, the substrate character is unmistakable.

- ¶32: Very fragmentary. The cosmological claim — that the chaining in the lump is eternal — is substrate. The institutional framing (apostle, teacher) follows the same pattern as ¶17-18, ¶30. Highest institutional score in the chapter but the surviving cosmological content is minimal.

## Chapter 25
### Concerning the Advent of Five Fathers from the Five Limbs of the Father

*Very short, remarkably clean chapter. The oldest teaching layer dominates — a compact five-fold emanation-to-faculty mapping (P2) plus a process summary (P3), wrapped in a thin dialogue frame (P1). No editorial bridge, no institutional content, no exhortation. The substrate shift score (+17.19 in second half) reflects the teaching content concentrated in P2-P3. The five emanation figures (Ambassador, Beloved of the Lights, Mother of Life, Jesus the Beloved, Virgin of Light) are mapped to the Father's five limbs (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration) — a classic Persian-tradition correspondential enumeration.*

The [Ambas]sador [came] from his mind; the Beloved of the Lights [from his thought]; the Mother of Life from his insight; [Jesus] the Beloved from his counsel; the Virgin of Lig[ht f]rom his consideration.

So in this way were summone[d these five] fathers. These five advents came from the five limbs [of the Father]. One came from another and was manifested in its season.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Standard formulaic opening: 'Once again the apostle speaks concerning... [He] speaks thus:' The topic label ('the advent of five fathers from the Father, one after another') is embedded within the frame attribution and is fully restated in P2-P3, contributing no unique substrate content.
- ¶2: Pure five-fold emanation-faculty mapping. Both sides internal to the cosmic system: cosmic emanation → faculty/limb of the Father. The systematic structure (five beings mapped to five intellectual faculties) is characteristic of the oldest correspondential teaching. 'Jesus the Beloved' here is a cosmic emanation figure within the Manichaean system, not the historical Jesus — the name may represent a later overlay on an older cosmic figure, but the mapping structure itself is clearly substrate.
- ¶3: Emanation process description — summarizes the sequential structure of the five evocations. 'One came from another and was manifested in its season' describes the temporal ordering of emanations within the cosmic system. Both sides remain internal. The summarizing formula 'So in this way' is mild but the content is entirely cosmological process.

## Chapter 26
### Concerning the First Man and the Ambassador and the Ship; what they resemble.

*A collection of five cosmic parables/similes mapping redemption processes within the cosmic system. Gardner notes the original contexts are lost and these were assembled during transmission. Teaching purity is 1.000 with zero editorial fatigue—the substrate dominates almost completely. Each parable maps a cosmic agent (First Man, Great Spirit, Living Spirit, Ambassador) to a simile figure, with both sides staying within the cosmic system. The only non-substrate material is the final paragraph (¶6), which pivots to exhortation/application directed at the audience. The 'Once again' markers at the head of each parable are minimal editorial collection markers, easily stripped to reveal the teaching underneath. The systematic structure (parable → cosmic referent) is Persian pedagogical tradition: teaching through analogy within the cosmic architecture.*

The First [Man] is like a great distinguished man whose treasure has been taken away from him. He came to pursue his treasure. This is how the [First] Man too came after his sons from the heights, so that he might redeem them and retrieve his treasure, which had been scattered amongst his enemies.

[He is like a man] whose two sons were taken away from him by [ ... He] came out toward them to redeem them. This is how the First Man [ ... ] shines in the heights because of his two sons, who were separated from him. They are his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four.

The ship [of the day] is like a great spear [ ... ... The Great Spirit too, which is in it, is like a wise [crafts]man who might beautify and set aright [all] the armour for war.

The Living Spirit is like a combatant who might go towards a king's son who has been taken prisoner of war, and he redeems him from the hands of his enemies.

[The Amba]ssador too is like a great man [ ... ] because of the treasure [ ... ] hand of his prisoners [ ... ] these light powers [ ... ... the] sons and their soul, so that they would [ ... ... (the soul) might] be brought forth in the land wherein it was imprisoned.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The parable maps cosmic entity (First Man) to simile figure (distinguished man pursuing treasure), then explains the cosmic referent (sons = living soul, enemies = powers of Darkness). Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. The 'this is how' connective is the teaching's own explanatory mechanism linking parable to referent. Bridge connective detected by NLP but 'Once again he speaks' is simply the dialogue frame opening for the chapter.
- ¶2: Highly systematic cosmological content: 'his living soul that exist in two powers, which makes four' is precise degree-structure teaching. The two-becoming-four formula is characteristic of the oldest substrate arithmetic. The 'Again, this is how' connective within the parable links simile to cosmic referent—kept as teaching structure but normalized 'Again' to start fresh sentence. Substrate score 6.35 confirms cosmological vocabulary density.
- ¶3: Maps two cosmic entities to simile figures: the ship of the day → great spear, the Great Spirit → wise craftsman. Both sides remain within the cosmic system. The Great Spirit's role of beautifying and setting aright maps to its systematic cosmic function (cf. Gardner: 79.29-30, 102.27). Heavy lacunae obscure some content. Substrate score 7.69 is the highest in the chapter.
- ¶4: Maps the Living Spirit's cosmic function (redeeming the First Man) to the simile of a combatant rescuing a king's son. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'King's son' = First Man, 'enemies' = powers of Darkness. Compact and systematic—no editorial extension. Substrate score 6.06.
- ¶5: No dialogue frame marker—begins directly with the cosmic entity. Maps the Ambassador (Third Ambassador) to a simile figure, with the cosmic referent being the redemption of light powers / living soul from imprisonment. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. Heavily lacunose but the substrate structure is clear. Despite zero substrate vocabulary score (due to lacunae destroying diagnostic terms), the structure and content are fully substratic.
- ¶6: Clear pivot from impersonal cosmic teaching to direct audience address: 'Again he turned, and he says to his disciples' is dialogue frame; 'You yourselves must be purifiers and redeemers of your soul' is imperative exhortation; 'so that you may be counted to the company of the fathers of light... in the new aeon, in the place of joy' is eschatological promise. The cosmic redemption theme from the five parables is now APPLIED to the community's own situation—the mapping crosses from cosmos to contemporary audience. This is the devotional closure appended to the substrate teaching sequence.
