## Chapter 27
### Concerning the Five Forms that exist in the Rulers of Darkness

*This chapter is dominated by the oldest teaching substrate. It systematically maps the five forms of the ruler of Darkness onto the five worlds (fire, wind, smoke, darkness, water) using zoomorphic correspondences (lion-faced, eagle-faced, dragon-faced, fish-form, daemon-form), then enumerates two sets of three qualities/capacities. Gardner notes comparison with Chapter 6, confirming this is the same cosmological-correspondential tradition. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system throughout ¶1-11. Only ¶12-13 shift to exhortation/devotional closure addressed to 'my beloved ones.' Minimal editorial fatigue. The teaching structure is clean: five forms (¶2-3), three qualities (¶4-7), three more qualities (¶8-11), then editorial closure.*

Five forms exist in his body, corresponding to (the archetype) of the five creations that exist in the five worlds of darkness.

Indeed, his head is lion-faced, which came about from the world of fire. His wings and his shoulders are eagle-faced, after the likeness of the children of the wind. His hands and his feet are daemons, after the likeness of the children of the world of smoke. His belly is dragon-faced, after the likeness of the world of darkness. His tail is the form of the fish, which belongs to the world of the children of water. These five shapes are in him, and they came from the five creations of the five worlds of darkness.

Should he wish, he shall walk on his two feet [ ... ] world of smoke. Also, when he wants, he shall [ ... ] four [ ... ] on his hands and feet [ ... ] like the children of fire. Should he wish, he shall rise up on his wings, like the children of the wind. Also, should he wish, he shall plunge down to the waters like the children of water. Again, should he wish, he shall crawl on his belly like the children of darkness. These five shapes are in him.

Also, there are three other (qualities) in him.

The first: [ ... ] his powers [ ... ]

The second: Shall a [ ... ] his magic arts. When he wishes, he shall make an invocation over himself, and hide from his powers. When he wants, he shall show himself to them and strike a death-blow by his magic arts. Any word he might utter at that moment, he shall [wreak magic] by it.

The third: His body is strong, a[1]] [ ... ] nails and claws of his powers are not able to penetrate it. No iron nor copper body will equal him [ ... ]; they will not be able to destroy him, because he was formed and fashioned from the hard-hearted counsel of Matter, the mother of demons and fiends.

Again, there are three others in him.

When he wants, his fire shall {blaze), and his entire body be made like the [ ... of] fire.

Also, when he wishes, he shall send out frost, and his entire body freezes like a [ ... of] snow.

The third: Whenever his powers are stood before him, he shall look at them and understand; for [whatever ex]ists in their heart he shall understand, what is in their heart and their face so long as they are stood before him. When they retreat before him and distance themselves from him, he shall not understand what is in their heart. There is no life in him whatsoever, but his 'life' is the bile of anger that is apparent on his face and in his fear. Shall a [ ... ] destruction that is establ[ished be]fore him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opening formula 'Once again, the apostle is sitting among the congregation' is standard dialogue frame. 'Consider the ruler' is an imperative address form. But the substantive cosmological statement about five forms corresponding to five creations in five worlds is the teaching thesis — it defines the entire chapter's structure.
- ¶2: Quintessential substrate: zoomorphic-realm correspondences. Lion-faced → fire, eagle-faced → wind, daemons → smoke, dragon-faced → darkness, fish → water. Body-part mapping (head, wings/shoulders, hands/feet, belly, tail) onto five worlds. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. The diagnostic markers lion-faced, eagle-faced, dragon-faced, fish are all present. Compare Chapter 6.
- ¶3: Continues the five-form mapping but now describes the ruler's modes of locomotion corresponding to each form. Each capacity maps to a cosmic realm. Lacunae damage the first two entries but the structure is clear. Systematic, impersonal, process-oriented.
- ¶4: Transitional enumeration statement introducing a new teaching structure — three qualities after the five forms. This is the substrate's organizational voice.
- ¶5: Heavily damaged by lacuna. Enumeration marker 'The first:' preserved. Only fragments survive but the teaching structure is visible.
- ¶6: The editorial_bridge score (14.29) is triggered by 'magic arts' vocabulary, but in context this describes what the ruler of Darkness IS and CAN DO within the cosmic system — his second faculty. Compare the explicit substrate identification in the instructions: 'The King of Darkness wounds and kills by the word of his magic arts — describes what his second faculty IS within the degree structure.' Both sides of the mapping stay cosmic. No contemporary application.
- ¶7: Third quality: invulnerability. Metal-body reference (iron, copper) stays within the cosmic register. 'Matter, the mother of demons and fiends' is a cosmological designation — a personified cosmic entity functioning as origin-agent. Both sides remain within the system.
- ¶8: Second transitional enumeration statement introducing another set of three qualities. Parallel structure to ¶4. Substrate organizational voice.
- ¶9: First of the second set of three: fire capacity. The ruler's body corresponds to the element of fire. Cosmic mapping, both sides internal.
- ¶10: Second of the second set: frost/cold capacity. Cosmic elemental correspondence. Parallel syntactic structure to ¶9.
- ¶11: Third of the second set: limited perception — understands his powers only when they face him, not when they retreat. Then a crucial cosmological statement: 'There is no life in him whatsoever, but his life is the bile of anger.' This describes what the ruler IS at the deepest level — an absence of life, an appearance only. Both sides of the mapping remain within the cosmic system. This is the substrate's characteristic impersonal voice describing the nature of Darkness.
- ¶12: Clear shift to devotional closure. 'my beloved ones' is a diagnostic exhortation marker. Direct address to community, imperative voice ('do not lay up/on yourselves'), moral application of the preceding teaching. The teaching about the ruler's forms is now being APPLIED to audience behavior — classic devotional wrapper.
- ¶13: Continues exhortation with eschatological promise ('inherit life for ever and ever'). 'Mind of truth that has been manifested to you' addresses the audience. 'become zealous and perfect' is imperative moral instruction. Standard devotional closure appended to the substrate teaching sequence.

## Chapter 28
### [Concerning the T]welve Judges [of] the Father

*This chapter is dominated by a clean twelve-fold enumeration of cosmic judges — a systematic substrate teaching with strong structural integrity. Each judge is mapped to a cosmic being and a judicial function; both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. The enumeration is bookended by an introduction (¶2) and a recapitulation (¶15) that form an inclusio around the Father of Greatness. Only ¶1 (dialogue frame) and ¶16 (exhortation) are clearly later layers, plus one institutional insertion ('holy church') in ¶13. The editorial fatigue data confirms the exhortation shift in the final paragraph. The ¶15 seam flag is a false positive — it is a recapitulation with 0.0 institutional vocabulary, not an institutional extension. The teaching purity (0.729) reflects the dominant substrate layer with minimal overlay.*

Twelve judges exist; the great mighty givers of judgement! They were sent and came from the exalted greatness to this place. They were assigned to different works according to the command that the Judge, who belongs to the greatness, entrusted to all these judges. Now he, the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges, who exists and is established in the aeons of greatness; he is the Father of Greatness.

The first judge is the First Man, he who humiliated the ruler of Darkness in the beginning. He judged him according to a judgement of righteousness; for he had exalted himself against a kingdom that was not his own.

The second judge is the Living Spirit, he who came and bore up the Man. He too made a judgement on all the powers of Darkness; which had wounded the garments of the First Man.

The third judge is the Mother of Life, the Great Spirit, she who beautified and set up the heavenly part of the universe, according to her pleasure; which [...] her. She judged the rulers above; she fettered and set them firmly in the place that is fitting for them.

The fourth judge is the Great Builder, he who built the new aeon of joy. He judged, according to a judgement of righteousness, the storehouses of the enemy; that death may not well up from this time on. He has constructed a prison for the enemy. Also, upon the crown of the building he has constructed a throne for the First Man and all the fathers of light; they who engaged the struggle with the evil one and were victorious over him.

⟨p.80⟩
The fifth judge is the great King of Honour, he who dwells and is established in the seventh firmament. He is the judge of all the firmaments who gives a true judgement, according to a judgement of righteousness, upon all the powers and all the kingdoms of the firmaments.

The sixth judge is [the Th]ird Ambassador, he who came and displayed his image. He purified the light [...] which his own [...] also of the enemy, he has [...]

The seventh judge is ... ... the worlds [... as he gives the victory to] they who are steadfast; but he condemns the ones who are convicted ... ... he shall become for them the steps of the way (?) [...]; but the convicted he sweeps and casts out to the depths.

The eighth judge is Jesus the Splendour, the father of all the apostles; because after the Ambassador had displayed his image and the light was taken [...] a mass of rulers rose up from above and below. Then they sent Jesus the Splendour. He came ... ... he descended and made a judgement in the firmaments. He separated the steadfast from the convicted.

The ninth judge is the Virgin of Light, [she who took the heart] of the powers by her image, gathering her own ones into her. She makes a judgement on the ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry.

The tenth judge is the Judge who dwells in the atmosphere, judging all mankind. He makes a separation between they who are good and they who are evil, setting apart the righteous from the sinners.

The eleventh judge is the Light Mind, he who shall come and appear in the world. And he unveils [...] and separates light from darkness, and sets the truth apart from lawlessness.

The twelfth judge is the great counsel, which is the summons and the obedience, which is appointed in the elements. It is the one that makes a separation between the good and the evil ones. At the end also it can gather itself together and sculpt its own self in the Last Statue. And it separates light from darkness.

These are the twelve great and mighty judges who were sent. They came from the great Judge, he who transcends all the judges. He is the Father that is hidden and immeasurable, the one who exists; who is established in the aeons of greatness upon the great throne of honour, which is the King of Honour [...] in his kingdom.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic dialogue frame: 'Once again he speaks to his disciples.' No cosmological content. Bridge connective flag correctly identifies this as frame.
- ¶2: Establishes the cosmological framework: twelve judges emanated from the Father of Greatness as transcendent Judge. Systematic, impersonal, structural. Both sides of the mapping (judges ← greatness) stay within the cosmic system. This paragraph sets up the enumeration structure that ¶3-14 will fill.
- ¶3: Enumeration unit #1. First Man → cosmic judicial function against the ruler of Darkness. Both sides within the cosmic system. 'In the beginning' anchors to cosmic time, not historical time.
- ¶4: Enumeration unit #2. Living Spirit → rescue of First Man and judgement on powers of Darkness. 'Garments of the First Man' is cosmic-correspondential vocabulary (garments = the five light elements). Pure substrate.
- ¶5: Enumeration unit #3. Mother of Life → cosmic architectural function and judgement of the rulers. Lacuna present. Both sides within cosmic system. 'Fettered and set them firmly' describes cosmic binding.
- ¶6: Enumeration unit #4. Great Builder → cosmic construction (new aeon, prison, throne). All cosmic architecture. 'Storehouses of the enemy' is systematic cosmic vocabulary. Longest enumeration unit — the Builder's dual function (prison for enemy, throne for light) is elaborated.
- ¶7: Enumeration unit #5. King of Honour → seventh firmament → judge of all firmaments. Cosmic geography (seventh firmament, kingdoms of the firmaments). Both sides within cosmic system. The King of Honour will recur in the recapitulation (¶15).
- ¶8: Enumeration unit #6. Third Ambassador → image display and light purification. Heavily damaged by lacunae. What remains is cosmic — 'displayed his image' and 'purified the light' are cosmic process descriptions.
- ¶9: Enumeration unit #7. Very heavily damaged. The name of the seventh judge is lost. What survives describes cosmic judicial separation (victory to steadfast, casting convicted to depths). 'The depths' is cosmic geography.
- ¶10: Enumeration unit #8. Jesus the Splendour is a cosmic entity in the Manichaean emanation hierarchy, not the historical Jesus. 'The father of all the apostles' is borderline — it could be an editorial epithet linking to apostolic succession, or it could describe Jesus the Splendour's cosmic function as source of the apostolic manifestation (he sends the Light Mind). I retain it as substrate because the phrase describes a cosmic role, but flag the ambiguity. The rest is clearly cosmic process: rulers rising, descent, judgement in firmaments.
- ¶11: Enumeration unit #9. Virgin of Light → image-seduction of powers → judgement on cosmic rulers. 'The ruler of the moist, and the ruler of the dry' are cosmic correspondences mapping elemental domains to archontic rulers. Pure substrate.
- ¶12: Enumeration unit #10. The cosmic Judge in the atmosphere — cosmic geography. His function is cosmic-judicial separation. 'All mankind' and 'righteous from sinners' could edge toward application, but they describe the cosmic function of this judge within the system, not a specific contemporary situation. The 'atmosphere' as cosmic zone keeps both sides within the system.
- ¶13: Enumeration unit #11. The Light Mind's cosmic functions (appearing in the world, separating light from darkness, setting truth apart from lawlessness) are substrate. 'He chooses the holy church' is an editorial insertion that maps the Light Mind's function onto the institutional community — one side of the mapping leaves the cosmic system.
- ¶14: Enumeration unit #12. 'The great counsel' = cosmic entity. 'Summons and obedience' = the Call and the Answer, a fundamental cosmic pair. 'Appointed in the elements' = cosmic placement. 'Last Statue' = the cosmic eschatological concept of the final gathering of all light. All within the cosmic system. This closes the twelve-fold enumeration.
- ¶15: The seam flag here is a false positive. This is a recapitulation that forms an inclusio with ¶2, returning to the Father of Greatness as transcendent Judge. Institutional vocabulary score is 0.0. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system (aeons of greatness, throne of honour, King of Honour, kingdom). 'These are the twelve' is a natural closing formula for enumeration teaching, not an editorial bridge connective. The substrate score (18.33) is the highest in the chapter.
- ¶16: Clear exhortation/devotional closure. Every diagnostic marker fires: first-person address ('I am telling you about this, my beloved ones'), moral imperative ('walk in true heart on this path of righteousness'), community instruction ('May a brother speak with his brother in truth'), eschatological promise ('receive the victory from these twelve great judges, and go to rest in this place of rest for ever'). The exhortation score (25.0) is the highest in the chapter. Editorial fatigue confirmed: this is appended after the complete substrate teaching (¶2-15).

## Chapter 29
### Concerning the Eighteen Great Thrones of all the Fathers

*This chapter is dominated by the oldest teaching layer — a systematic enumeration of 18 cosmic thrones (9 in the eternal realm, 9 in the material world). The substrate is remarkably intact with minimal editorial overlay. The only significant editorial intervention is in ¶17, where the ninth worldly throne is identified with Mani's own apostolic seat ('the apostle who has come to you at this time'). The devotional closure 'for ever and ever, amen' in ¶11 is the only exhortation. The editorial fatigue pattern is confirmed: institutional vocabulary only appears in the final third (¶10, ¶17). The teaching structure — numbered hierarchy mapping cosmic beings to cosmic positions and functions — is classic cosmological-correspondential substrate.*

Eighteen [great thrones for all the fathers] exist and are appointed, which are established in eighteen places! While there are nine among them [appointed in] the outer aeons, they exist [i]n all the worlds of the damage.

The first throne is the throne of the Father, the God of [trut]h, the King of the aeons of greatness, the one who exists; who is established [...] he alone, in his own essen[c]e, [...] apart. This is the first throne [...] the one that surpasses all the thrones.

⟨p.82⟩ The second throne is that of the [gl]orious Mother of Life. She is the origin of all the emanations that have come to this world.

The third throne is constructed for the father, the First Man. He is the one who humiliated the entire first enmity, which existed in the worlds of Darkness.

The fourth throne is that of the Beloved of the Lights, the great honoured Beloved of the aeons of greatness.

The fifth throne is that of the glorious Great Builder; the great architect who built the new aeon for a newness, for [a ...] for [the] fathers of light; also for a place of binding and a prison for the enemy and his powers.

[The sixth thro]n[e] is that of the Living Spirit, the [glorious] mighty one, he who bore up the First Man out from the [land of Darkness]; who also set in order and constructed the world [s ...] both for a cleansing of the light, [and] for a binding [of the] powers of the enmity.

The seventh th[rone is] that of the Third Ambassador, the king of the zone, also the master of all advices.

The eighth throne is that of Jesus the Splendour, who [is] the releaser and redeemer [of] all souls.

The ninth throne is the throne of the great M[ind, he to whom] all the churches shall gather; and all the [life] that will be purified from the world returns to him.

These are the nine thrones of ho[no]u[r] appointed in the outer aeo[ns], <which are established> for the fathers of the glorious realm; [in the kingdom of the King of] Honour.

In contrast, these are the other nine thrones that are appointed in this world of the zone.

Three thrones in the ship of [the d]ay: one is that of the Ambassador, the second i[s that of] the Great Spirit, the third is that of the Living Spirit.

⟨p.83⟩ Once again, there are another three in the ship that [belongs to the night]: the first is the throne of Jesus the Splendour, [the second is that of] the First Man, the third is tha[t of the Virgin of] Light. These six thrones are established in the two ships.

Also, the seventh throne is established in the seventh firmament. The great King of Honour sits upon it.

The eighth throne is established in the atmosphere. The Judge of truth sits upon it, he who judges all mankind. Three paths shall be distinguished before him: one to death, one to life, on[e] to the mingling.

The ninth throne appointed and established is for the [a]postle.

Th[ese] are the [nin]e great [th]ro[nes ...] that are established in this world of [...] of the glorious realm, the one that the [... ...] honour them [... ...] and the [...].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula wraps a substantive cosmological statement that defines the entire teaching structure (18 thrones, 9+9 distribution). The structural overview is substrate; the attribution is editorial frame.
- ¶2: First enumeration unit. Maps first throne → highest cosmic entity (the Father). Both sides stay within the cosmic system. Lacunae suggest damaged manuscript but the teaching structure is clear.
- ¶3: Second enumeration unit. Throne → cosmic entity (Mother of Life) → cosmic function (origin of emanations). Pure cosmological mapping. High substrate score (26.92) confirms.
- ¶4: Third enumeration unit. Throne → First Man → cosmic function (defeating the primordial Enmity in the worlds of Darkness). 'The entire first enmity' is a cosmic-process term. Capitalized 'Darkness' as it functions as a cosmic domain.
- ¶5: Fourth enumeration unit. Highest substrate score in the chapter (35.0). Pure cosmic entity identification with no functional description — just the being and its cosmic position.
- ¶6: Fifth enumeration unit. Throne → Great Builder → cosmic function (constructing the new aeon, creating a binding-place for the enemy). Both sides stay within the cosmic system. 'Place of binding and a prison for the enemy' is cosmic architecture, not metaphor for earthly institutions.
- ¶7: Sixth enumeration unit. Throne → Living Spirit → dual cosmic function (rescue of First Man from the land of Darkness; world-construction for light-cleansing and enmity-binding). This is systematic cosmic-process description.
- ¶8: Seventh enumeration unit. Throne → Third Ambassador → cosmic titles (king of the zone, master of all advices). 'Zone' is cosmic geography; 'advices' is a cosmic-faculty term.
- ¶9: Eighth enumeration unit. 'Jesus the Splendour' is the cosmic Manichaean entity (not the historical Jesus), functioning as the releaser of souls — a cosmic process. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system.
- ¶10: Ninth enumeration unit completing the first set. 'Churches' (ekklesia) here functions cosmically as 'assemblies of light' — the Great Mind's function is gathering purified life, confirmed by the parallel clause 'all the life that will be purified from the world returns to him.' Both sides stay within the cosmic system. Institutional score (6.25) is a minor flag; the word 'churches' triggers it but the mapping is cosmic. Some uncertainty remains.
- ¶11: Structural summary closing the first set of nine thrones. The summary mirrors the transitional paragraph 12 that opens the second set — both are structural markers within the enumeration. Only the liturgical 'for ever and ever, amen' is late.
- ¶12: Structural transition introducing the second set of nine thrones. 'This world of the zone' is cosmic geography. This mirrors the opening statement in ¶1 about the 9+9 structure and is part of the teaching's organizational skeleton.
- ¶13: First triad of the worldly thrones. 'Ship of the day' is cosmic vessel/geography. Maps three cosmic entities to their thrones in the solar ship. High substrate score (16.67).
- ¶14: 'Once again' here is a continuation marker within the teaching structure (not the dialogue frame formula 'Once again the enlightener speaks'). Second triad: three entities in the lunar ship. The summary 'These six thrones are established in the two ships' is a structural sub-total within the enumeration.
- ¶15: Seventh worldly throne. Maps cosmic position (seventh firmament) → cosmic entity (King of Honour). High substrate score (22.22). Pure cosmological mapping.
- ¶16: Eighth worldly throne. Maps cosmic position (atmosphere) → cosmic entity (Judge of truth) → cosmic function (tripartite judgment). The 'three paths' (death, life, mingling) are cosmic destinies within the system, not contemporary application. 'Judges all mankind' is cosmic function, not institutional content.
- ¶17: The enumeration REQUIRES a ninth worldly throne; the substrate core identifies this position for 'the apostle' (a cosmic function completing the hierarchy). Everything from 'in the holy church' onward is the institutional layer mapping this cosmic position to Mani's own authority. Gardner synopsis confirms: 'Mani illustrates to his audience that he is now occupying it.' The institutional score (12.82) is the highest in the chapter, confirming the layer shift.
- ¶18: Closing summary of the second set of nine thrones, structurally parallel to ¶11's summary of the first set. Heavily damaged by lacunae but the structural function is clear. No devotional closure ('amen') survives — either it was absent or lost to damage.

## Chapter 30
### Concerning the Three Garments

*Very short chapter (2 paragraphs) with heavy lacunae throughout. The oldest teaching layer is dominant: it describes the Living Spirit's three garments (wind, fire, water) as elemental coverings that he brings, establishes across the universe, and then divests/stretches over the cosmic zone from bottom edge to topmost point. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system (cosmic agent → elemental garments → cosmic geography/zone). The only non-substrate material is the dialogue frame formula and address language in paragraph 1. Negative editorial fatigue score is consistent with frame vocabulary concentrated in the opening. Despite low teaching purity score (0.125), this is driven by the small sample size and heavy frame formula in paragraph 1; the actual teaching content is almost entirely substrate.*

when the Living Spirit came he brought the three garments; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He assumed them from [ ... ] he has [ ... ] in them in those three garments [ ... ] he established [them] for all the things of [this univers]e, both above and below. He arranged them in the [ ... ] he was displayed. Afterwards [ ... ] his works [ ... ] the [ ... h]e stripped them off before [the ri]ghteous ones.

## Chapter 31
### Concerning the Summons, in which Limb of the Soul it descended to the First Man

*The oldest teaching layer is dominant. This chapter is remarkably clean substrate: body-cosmos correspondences (head=First Man, limbs=sons/soul), the Pillar of Glory as cosmic body, the five intellectual faculties (mind, thought, insight, counsel, consideration) as emanations, and cosmic process narrative of gathering/restoration. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. Editorial material is confined to the dialogue frame in ¶1-2 (formulaic attribution, citation formula, self-reference). No editorial bridge, no institutional content, no exhortation. Negative editorial fatigue confirms the editorial vocabulary is front-loaded in the framing paragraphs. The body-head analogy in ¶3-5 is genuinely correspondential (mapping physical body structure onto cosmic hierarchy), not mere illustration.*

In which limb of [the liv]i[ng] sou[l] did [the] summons descend to the Man, who exists in the [a]eon?

Indeed, the First Man dispersed himself and he [...] limb of the living soul [...] of the living soul; he has [...] he did not know [... ...] but the blessed, glorious Man came [in] secret; in his image; in his shape; in his love; in his holy virgin, she who is the virgin of light, the soul of the father.

After the fashion of this fleshly body: as the root of all the limbs hang upon the head, so that should one of the person's limbs be cut off, while the head exists he has hope for [...] but if his head should be cut off, the entire body will die and he is lost.

This is also the case for the Firs[t] Ma[n]. He is the head, while his sons attach to it the limbs [of] his soul. Or, conversely, like the living air, on which all flesh entirely lives as it breathes and [...] therein. His head is placed on the body of the Pillar of Glory in the heights of the universe.

[This i]s also the case [for] the father, the blessed Man; as he is like the head that is upon the body and the air that upon the Pillar is set i[n ...] being made strong by his light virgin, [she] who is his soul that he clothes [...]

[ ... ] t[h]ose [...]: the mind, thought, insight, counsel and c[on]sideration that he produced and sent forth from hi[m to] do his will. He sprang and travelled behind them. [...] of his living soul, which is entwined among the rebels; as they are like the limbs of its body, and [...] universe.

And when they were sent, at [that ti]m[e] he was found with the Virgin of [Lig]ht and [he] stood up, asking and entreating for a p[o]wer. [ ... he] gave him peace and a ki[ss ...] he [gave] him goo[d] tidings [...] Man. The Man him[s]elf gave [his ...] his limbs and gathered his soul i[n ...] he built it in its place like this tower [...]

[...] shaped it and beautified it skillfully [...] for[m ...] the voices that he sent [...] he might sink in and quench [... ...] so[u]l that was crushed by the enemy. They were gathered in. They came, set firm once more, in the image of their father.

**Temporal observations:**

- ¶2: High substrate score (12.86) confirms the cosmological density. The 'virgin of light, the soul of the father' is a cosmic entity identification. Heavy lacunae obscure much of the first half.
- ¶3: Pure body-cosmos correspondence: the physical body's head-limb relationship is mapped onto the cosmic hierarchy (First Man as head, soul-limbs as body). This is correspondential teaching where the physical body is the natural expression of the cosmic structure. All scores at 0.0 — no editorial vocabulary whatsoever. The analogy serves the systematic mapping in ¶4-5.
- ¶4: High substrate score (11.67). Two parallel correspondences: (1) First Man = head, sons = limbs — body-cosmos mapping; (2) First Man = living air on which all flesh lives — element-cosmos mapping. Then the Pillar of Glory as the cosmic body with the First Man as its head. All mappings stay within the cosmic system. The Pillar of Glory is a core cosmic-architectural entity.

- ¶7: Cosmic process narrative: the sending of the faculties, the First Man with the Virgin of Light, receiving power, gathering the soul, building like a tower. All cosmic events within the system. 'Built it in its place like this tower' — architectural cosmic metaphor for the Pillar of Glory / Last Statue. All scores at 0.0.
- ¶8: Concludes the cosmic process narrative: shaping/beautifying (cosmic craftsmanship), voices sent (the summons), soul crushed by the enemy (Darkness) being gathered and restored 'in the image of their father' (the First Man). [The second: He spread about the one who had rebelled, he crucified [h]im in every body.

The third: He [tr]ampled, piled up, and pierced the essences of death.

[The f]ourth: He constructed the ships of light.

The fi[f]th: He evoked his five sons, and distributed them about. They [took] possession of the zone, they bore up under all the universe's weight. Also, he evoked three living words. He set [them] upon the th[re]e vessels, another one upon the giant. [Again, he evoked the] summons, that it might be mixed with the five shining men.

And at the end [of] all the [wor]ks he to[ok s]ome of them into his storehouses, and he appointed others at the watch-posts.

The [sev]enth: A[t the] time when the Ambass[ador dis]pl[aye]d his glorious imag[e], the L[iving] Spirit [established] many [go]ds and angels. They [ ... ] Th[ey to]ok hold of it, that the entire building [ ... ] not [ ... / ... ] one another against [the] light, which he had [ ... ]

Also another gr[ea]t and glorious work he will enact at the e[nd] is [the] Last [Statue], which he will bring up to the aeons of [light]; and he [ ... ] in, and [he ta]kes hold, and he smites [ ... ], and [he] builds, and he [ ... ], and he [ ... t]hem.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The frame formula is late editorial attribution. The substantive statement defines the teaching structure (seven works) and belongs to the oldest layer.
- ¶2: First enumeration unit. The pearl-from-sea simile is correspondential — pearl maps to First Man, sea maps to the contest/abyss. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. The pearl image is deeply archaic in the correspondential tradition.
- ¶3: Second enumeration unit. 'The one who had rebelled' is the cosmic rebel (Darkness/archons). 'Crucified him in every body' describes the binding of dark substance into material forms — cosmic process, not Christological reference. Both sides within cosmic architecture.
- ¶4: Third enumeration unit. 'Essences of death' — cosmic dark substance. Three verbs (trampled, piled up, pierced) describe the cosmic act of subjugating dark matter for universe-construction.
- ¶5: Fourth enumeration unit. 'Ships of light' = sun and moon as cosmic vessels. Terse, systematic, impersonal.
- ¶6: Fifth enumeration unit — the longest and most detailed. Dense with cosmic entities: five sons, zone, three living words, three vessels, the giant, the summons, five shining men. 'Evoked' (emanation verb) appears three times. The five-fold structure and emanation hierarchy are hallmarks of the oldest teaching layer. Substrate score 29.31 confirms heavy cosmological vocabulary.
- ¶7: Sixth enumeration unit. Zone construction, circuit-binding, storehouses, watch-posts — all cosmic architectural terms. The process is systematic: construct → send powers → bound circuit → finish → distribute to storehouses and watch-posts. Both sides of every mapping stay within cosmic architecture.
- ¶8: Seventh enumeration unit, heavily damaged by lacunae. The Ambassador, his image-display, the Living Spirit establishing gods and angels, 'the entire building' — all cosmic architecture. Despite the damage, the teaching structure (enumeration marker + cosmic process) is clearly preserved.
- ¶9: Supplementary work beyond the numbered seven. 'Also another' marks it as addendum, but the content is entirely cosmological: the Last Statue, the aeons of light. The Last Statue is the eschatological gathering of all light — cosmic process, not institutional application. No editorial bridge vocabulary. Heavily damaged but structurally intact as cosmic teaching.
- ¶10: Classic beatitude closure: 'Blessed is he who knows him in the new aeon.' The 'Blessed is he who...' formula is a standard devotional wrapper appended to substrate teaching. Shifts from impersonal cosmic description to direct address about eschatological reward ('glory and joy'). Positioned at chapter end — classic exhortation placement.

His e[yes (?) ...] His feet are his [...]

This is how each of the worlds h[ave been ha]rmonised (?). Five gods are fastened in him. [...] they are his soul and his life [...] man is a sinner [...] the Living Spirit and the Ambassador [...] the five sleepless guardians.

He has [...] the Keeper of Splendour, the mind that is [...] in the wo[r]ld of the mind that is above. He has [...] who]le [...] of the powers of hea[ven that] are in [...] of the great King of Honour, [who is the thought that exists i]n the seventh firmament. He has humbled [...] also of the Adamant of Lig[h]t, who is the insight [... ] from {} he has given [...] because of lust [... He has] also appointed the King of Glory, who is the coun[sel ...] patience over the three images; that of wind, that of fire, and that of water. He has handed over to him the [...] do evilness. [...] the Porter, who is the consideration [...] the wi]sdom in the [...] which is below. He has made him [...] the foundation [below ...]

⟨p.92⟩
Furthermore: The Ambassador refined from them five intellectuals of life. Also, the summons and the obedience were situated there. Now, they made six sons of the Living Spirit, together with the six sons of the First Man!

Further: The Ambassador placed in them the great Mind; who is the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man.

Furthermore: Jesus the Youth was set there; who is the image of the living word, of the utterance and the obedience.

He has made strong these camps, those above and those below [...], (so that) each one of them will be secure in the circuit of his watch-district, so that neither uprising nor treachery are made in his watch-district.

And look, see! The Keeper of Splendour is set firm in the great mind, in the camp above the prison of the bound ones, for he brings to nothing all the gloom of death. An[d a] treachery came about, and an uprising! The Sin abor[te]d, [it t]angled i]n with the soul. It became mixed with this light that it expelled toward the image of the Ambassador. It went [...] in the third firmament that is above the watch-t[ower ...] the Keeper of Splendour. From that place also it tangled in with the light. It was detached and came down to that which is dry and that which is moist. It [fashio]ned the trees [up]on the dry (land); but in the sea it immediat[ely] took form and made a great uprising in the sea.

Again, look, see! The great King of Honour, who is the thought, he is in the thi[r]d firmament. He is made [...] with wrath. And an uprising came about! A treachery and a[n] anger happened in his camp. The watchers of heaven, who came down to the earth in his watch-district, they did all the deeds of treachery. They have revealed crafts in the world and have unveiled to people the mysteries of heaven. An uprising came about, and a destruction, on the earth [... ] to it.

The Adamant [...] the fulfillment. He is set firm upo[n ...] and a treachery came about in his camp; the occasion when the abortions fell to the earth. They formed Adam and Eve. They begat them so as to reign through them in the world. They fashioned every object of lust upon the earth. The entire world was filled by their lust.

Once again: In the watch of the great King of Glory, wh[o] is the great counsel, he who exercises authority over the thre[e] wheels. A [dis]turbance came about, and an affliction, for [they were ... ] pained and oppressed in the three earths. After the Ambassador displayed his image, the paths closed to their [...] their ascent was impeded by them [...] the w[ind], the water, and the fire ascend on them.

[O]nce again: In the watch of the Porter, he who humiliates [...] upr]ising of the abysses below [...] bent, the fastenings underneath were loosened [...] in the foundation below.

On account of the earthquake that happened in the watch of the Keeper of Splen[dour], the Pillar of Glory came forth; as it [...] helper of the Keeper of Splendour. It bore up under all [burden]s.

Conversely, because of the treachery and the uprising that happened in the watch of the great King of Honour, which is the watchers who came down to earth from the heavens; four angels were called upon about them. They bound the watche[r]s with an eternal chain, in the prison of the blackened ones (?). [Th]ey obliterated their children from upon the earth.

[Th]en, again, the abortions descended in the watch of the Adamant and begat Adam and Eve; because of that great treachery wh[ich] happened, and the mystery of wickedness, he sent Jesus [...] the prayer of the five sons. He assumed them [...] the abortions. He fastened them beneath the [...] the mind of Adam.

⟨p.94⟩
Also, because of the earthquake that happened in these three earths, and in that the paths were hindered and the springs of wind and water and fire were impeded, Jesus cast himself down. He assumed Eve; and he straightened the tracks of the [win]d, the water, and the fire. He opened the springs for them, and he set [in ord]er the path of their ascent.

Once [again]: Since the earth beneath the Porter was loose[ned from] the fastenings that [...] because of this too Jesus went down, assuming [Ev]e until he reached tha[t] place. [H]e set in [or]der and stre[ng]th[ened] the fastenings beneath. He returned, [he came] up to his [...] rest.

Look at all these watches of the zon[e], the ones in which these great gods are master, w[atch]ing over them [...]. Uprisings have happened, and treachery, in them. The [...] in them from time to time; with a great hum[iliati]on, until they humbled the powers of the enmity.

So also is this body! A mighty power lives here, even if it is small in its stature. Nevertheless, Sin dwells within, and the old man who is lodged in it. Certainly he is cruel, with great cunning; until the Lig/ht Mind finds how to humble this body, and drive it [according to] his pleasure.

Just so, in the w[atch-]districts of his great outer brothers, they who are masters in the zone: in them, in the great body, the earthquake and treachery happened from time to time. So, also, is the watch-district of the Mind, which is the body of the flesh. Sin raises [up], from time to time, its agitations in the body.

the powers of light are good. The beginning and the end are unveiled to them. All that they do is being done with correct judgement. Indeed, because of this they may permit the enmity to initiate error, and do its pleasure for a moment. Then they seize [...] they have acted first with a righteous judgement.

These gods, in that [...] are great and mighty ones. And each one of them is enclosed and hard pressed in this place wherein he is set; like trees holding to their taproot. So also this is how each one of them has held to his taproot in the world, according to the k[ind] of place in which he is set, bearing up till [...]

the world is set firm, being ordered [...] the five sons of the Living Spirit in all its members [...]. Sin took this body out from [the] la[nd (?) ... co]nstructed it in its members. It took its [bod]y from the five b]odies of Darkness. (Sin) constructed the body. Yet, its [sou]l i[t] took from the five shining gods.

(Sin) bou]nd (the soul) in the five members of the body. It bound the mind in bone; the thought in sinew; the insight in vein; the counsel in flesh; the consideration in ski[n].

(Sin) set f[ast] its five powers: its mind upon the mind of the soul; its thought upon the thought of the soul; its insight upon the insight of the soul; its counsel upon the counsel of the soul; its consider[atio]n upon the consideration of the soul. It placed its five [an]gels and authorities upon the five members of the soul, which it had brought in and bound in the flesh.

(The soul) assumed error and forgetfulness. It for[go]t its essence and its race and its kindred; kn[ow]ing not the door of the place to pray to him, nor call up to him. It became hostile to its father [...] itself, being wicked [...] its own light [...]

The Light Mind comes and finds the soul [...] it assuming it in the [...] its wisdom [...] he shall become for it [...] the bonds [...] members in the body. He shall loosen the m[ind of the soul and relea]se it from the bone. He shall release the th[ought of the soul] from the sinew; and s[o] bind the thought [of the Sin in] the sinew. He shall release the insight of the soul from the vei[n]; and so bind the insight of the Sin in the vein. [He sh]all loosen the counsel of the soul, and release it from the flesh; and so bind the counsel of the Sin in the flesh. He shall release the consideration of the soul from the skin; and bin[d] the consideration of the Sin in the skin.

⟨p.97⟩
This is how he shall release the members of the soul, and make them free from the five members of Sin. Conversely, these five members of Sin, which were loose, he shall bind them.

He shall set right the members of the soul; form and purify them, and construct a new man of them, a child of righteousness. [And] when [he] fashions and const[r]ucts and purifies the new man; then he shall bring forth five great living members out from the five great memb[ers]. And he places them in the members of the new man. He shall place his m[ind], which is love, in the mind of the new man. Also, the th[oug]ht, which is faith, he shall pl[ace in] the thought of the new man; whom he shall purify. His insight, which [is perfection, he shall place] in the insight of the new man. His counsel, which [is] patience, he shall place in his counsel. Also, [w]isdom, which is his consideration, in the consideration of the new m[an]. He shall make the image of the word pure from the word of Sin; and he adds to it his word, so that his [...] becomes nourisher and strengthener [...]

Now, when he shall perf[ect ... the tw]elve members. This [is how ...] and his wisdom. His [...] becomes righteous, as he perf[ects ...] While formerly he was running [...] but instead of this, now he runs [...] his road and his path and his [...] also [he mounts up] to the heights, to the great aeons [...] thus the old man is bound in the [...] and his lust, his [...] his foolishness in these five members of the bo[dy ...] the dark spirit is imprisoned with them in a bond and in severe misery.

Also, the new man reigns by his love, by hi[s] faith, by his perfection, by his patience, and by his wisdom. Yet, his king [...] the Light Mind, he w[ho is] king over the totality. He re[igns] o[ver it according to] his pleasure. While the members [...], thus Sin is imprisoned. Still, the Light Mind is king, and an affliction may arise in the body from t[im]e to time.

There are occasions when Sin shall mount up with its foolishness; and it disturbs the consideration, and it clouds the wisdom and the understanding of the person. It causes truth to be split into doubt in him, and he utters some foo[li]sh words and some [...]

Sin mount up again, another time, from the consideration to the counsel. It takes his patience f[rom him], and bestows upon him faint-heartedness and hurt. [...] A battle and a war shall arise (between) the Light Mind and the Sin in the counsel [...]

Sin shall rise [...] and clothe him with lust and vanity and pride.

Sin shall mount up, the thought of death, to his thought. He shall [...] a vanity; and causes his f[ai]th and his truth to le[a]ve him.

(Sin) shall rise up and assume his mind; and disturb his mind, which formerly was calm. It shall disrupt his love

The Mind, who was in him, sh[a]ll disperse from him [...] He shall be filled by evil spirits; and they deal with [him], dragging [hi]m hither and thither. [...] [He] will change and become as if a b[i]rd, were its feathers plucked out. He becomes an earthly man.

So al[so] these watch-keepers, for they are set [...] fi[rm in] the zone, (but) they are not visible. Again, this too is the way of the Light Mind, [for he is] not visible in the body.

Also, [corr]esponding to th[ese] outer [watch-keepers]: in that while they are great, [y]et have they contorted themselves. They have [...], they have become small, to the measure of the task that is apportioned to each o[n]e [of t]hem. Again, this too is like the Mind, himse[lf] a great one, and exalted; but he is bent over and [has] become small, to this little worthless [bo]dy.

[Even so] the gods in the outer [z]one: they are transcendent and purified, they are set [f]a[st in] the mingling of the totality, (but) are not defiled. [This too is like the] Light [Mind]. While he is placed in hi[s ...]

⟨p.100⟩
Again, behold the mightiness and the activity of the Light Mind: how vast he is over all the watch-districts of the body! He stays fast at his camp. He shuts off all deliberations of the body from the beguilements of Sin. He limits them and distributes them out. He sets them down at his pleasure.

Also, he does another work, surpassing and ex/ceeding (these). He bestows a great spirit upon the ele/ct one. Indeed, now may you find him, as he stands on the earth, [r]ising up in his heart and ascending to the Father, the G[o]d of truth. He who exists and is established above all the things of loss. [Onc]e again, he may push dow[n] in his insight and his consideration, and descend to the land of Darkness, from where the Darkness has poured forth. His heart shall run and touch everything, as it [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The disciple recaps previous substrate teaching about the five counsels in the five limbs — this IS the soul-body binding formula being referenced. Frame wraps substantive cosmological content.
- ¶2: Substantive cosmological question in the Persian dialogue tradition. Defines the teaching problem: how do rebellions arise despite the binding? This structures the entire watch-district teaching that follows.
- ¶3: Substantive cosmological question about the contraction of a great being into a small space — answered later by the taproot analogy (¶53).
- ¶4: Substantive question about purity/defilement paradox. Much damaged by lacunae. Answered by ¶54.
- ¶5: Enumerated substantive question. 'Fourthly' is an enumeration marker integral to the teaching structure. Answered by ¶52.
- ¶6: Fifth question about Mani's apostolate — this is biographical/institutional content, not a cosmological question. Sets up the institutional section (¶59-66).
- ¶7: Pure frame formula — disciple's entreaty. High dialogue_frame score (26.67) confirms.
- ¶8: Mani's response referencing 'my church' — institutional language in dialogue frame. No cosmological content.
- ¶9: Disciple restates desire to know about the Light Mind. Formulaic exchange before teaching begins.
- ¶10: Rhetorical preparation: 'I will make the living fountain overflow for the thirsty.' Figurative language but functioning as pedagogical frame, not systematic teaching.
- ¶11: The body-cosmos correspondence begins. The universe is constructed as a human body: head=garments (heavens), ribs=firmaments, navel=zodiac, loins=three earths, feet=zone. Classic correspondential substrate — both sides stay within the cosmic system.
- ¶12: Pure body-cosmos correspondence continuing from ¶11. Organ→cosmic domain: heart=mankind, liver=quadrupeds, lung=birds, spleen=fish, kidneys=reptiles, skin=surrounding wall, veins=springs and wells. Every mapping stays internal to the cosmic system. This is the remnant of the body-cosmos teaching tradition.
- ¶13: Summary: five gods as soul/life of the cosmic body, with the Living Spirit, Ambassador, and five sleepless guardians. Fragmentary but clearly substrate — cosmological hierarchy.
- ¶14: Five-fold faculty mapping: Keeper of Splendour=mind (above), King of Honour=thought (seventh firmament), Adamant of Light=insight, King of Glory=counsel/patience (over three images of wind/fire/water), Porter=consideration/wisdom (below). This is the quintessential substrate: cosmic being → intellectual faculty → cosmic station. All three sides stay within the system.
- ¶15: Emanation hierarchy: Ambassador refines five intellectuals, summons and obedience make the sixth. High substrate score (34.21). The enumeration of six sons of the Living Spirit with six sons of the First Man is systematic cosmological structure.
- ¶16: Highest substrate score in chapter (44.44). Identification: great Mind = Pillar of Glory = Perfect Man. Triple cosmic identification.
- ¶17: Bridge connective flagged but this continues the emanation sequence from ¶15-16. Jesus the Youth is a cosmic figure (not the historical Jesus), identified as the image of the living word / utterance and obedience. Completes the cosmic architecture. 'Furthermore' is a sequence marker within the enumeration.
- ¶18: Establishes the watch-district system. Both sides cosmic: camps above and below → watch-districts → security against uprising. This sets up the five watch-district narratives that follow.
- ¶19: First watch-district narrative: Keeper of Splendour (mind). Sin's treachery — aborting, tangling with light, mixing, descending to form trees on land and uprising in sea. Entirely within the cosmic system.
- ¶20: Second watch-district: King of Honour (thought) in the third/seventh firmament. The watchers narrative is the Enochic tradition integrated into the cosmic architecture — cosmic history, not contemporary application.
- ¶21: Third watch-district: Adamant of Light (insight). Core narrates the abortions forming Adam and Eve — cosmic history. The final sentence extends the narrative to persecution of churches and apostles, crossing from cosmic to contemporary. The seam is at 'Also, {} they persecute...'
- ¶22: Fourth watch-district: King of Glory (counsel) over the three wheels. Disturbance in three earths, impeded ascent of wind/water/fire. Both sides cosmic: cosmic ruler → cosmic domain → cosmic process.
- ¶23: Fifth watch-district: Porter (consideration/wisdom) at the foundation below. Uprising of abysses, fastenings loosened. Very fragmentary but clearly substrate — cosmic structure.
- ¶24: Response to first watch treachery: Pillar of Glory aids the Keeper of Splendour. High substrate score (22.22). Cosmic beings responding to cosmic crisis — both sides internal.
- ¶25: Response to second watch treachery: four angels bind the watchers. Enochic tradition fully integrated into the cosmic architecture. Both sides stay within the system.
- ¶26: Response to third watch treachery: cosmic Jesus sent to awaken Adam. 'Jesus' here is the cosmic figure (Jesus the Splendour), not the historical Jesus. 'The mind of Adam' — the faculty system applied to the primal human. Both sides cosmic.
- ¶27: Response to fourth watch treachery: cosmic Jesus opens the paths of wind, water, and fire. Cosmic process vocabulary: springs, tracks, paths, ascent. Both sides internal.
- ¶28: Response to fifth watch treachery: Jesus strengthens the foundation beneath the Porter. Completes the five-fold watch-response sequence. Cosmic narrative.
- ¶29: Summary of the five watch-district teaching. Transitions from the macrocosm to the microcosm. The summary recapitulates the substrate teaching.
- ¶30: The pivot from macrocosm to microcosm. The body is the watch-district of the Light Mind, with Sin and the old man as the internal adversary. Both sides remain within the cosmic system — this IS the macrocosm-microcosm correspondence.
- ¶31: Explicit macrocosm-microcosm parallel: watch-districts in the zone → watch-district of the Mind in the body. Both sides cosmic. The mapping is the teaching.
- ¶32: Teaching about the strategic nature of light powers — they permit evil temporarily because they see the whole cosmic process. Both sides stay within the system: light powers → enmity → judgement. The 'Now you, understand thus' is editorial address.
- ¶33: Answers the second question (how can a great God appear in a small body). The taproot analogy: each god is enclosed in its place like a tree holding to its taproot. This is substrate — it describes the NATURE of cosmic beings' self-contraction. The frame reference to the earlier question is editorial.
- ¶34: Key transition: from macrocosm to body. The world ordered by five sons of Living Spirit → Sin constructs body from five bodies of Darkness, takes soul from five shining gods. This sets up the binding formula in ¶35. High substrate score (18.46).
- ¶35: THE QUINTESSENTIAL SUBSTRATE. The soul-body binding formula: mind=bone, thought=sinew, insight=vein, counsel=flesh, consideration=skin. Highest substrate score in the chapter (60.61). This is pure correspondential mapping — cosmic faculty → body member. Both sides stay entirely within the system. This is the ancient binding formula preserved in the Coptic.
- ¶36: The substrate continues the five-fold system: Sin overlays its five powers upon the soul's five faculties. Then the editorial bridge crosses into the contemporary world: 'worship of idols,' 'idols of wood and gold and silver,' 'worshipping beasts.' The seam is after 'bound in the flesh.' The editorial_bridge score (2.05) confirms.
- ¶37: Describes the soul's fall into error and forgetfulness after the binding. 'Forgot its essence and its race and its kindred' — the soul's amnesia of cosmic origin. Both sides cosmic: bound soul → forgetfulness of divine nature.
- ¶38: THE REVERSAL FORMULA. The Light Mind systematically reverses the binding of ¶35: mind released from bone / Sin's mind bound in bone; thought released from sinew / Sin's thought bound in sinew; and so on through all five. This is pure substrate — the correspondential reversal. Each faculty is released and its dark counterpart bound in the same member. The structural perfection of this five-fold reversal is the hallmark of the oldest teaching layer.
- ¶39: Summary of the binding/loosing formula: soul's five members freed, Sin's five members bound. The reversal principle stated explicitly.
- ¶40: Five-fold virtue mapping: mind=love, thought=faith, insight=perfection, counsel=patience, consideration=wisdom. These five virtues replace the five bound faculties in the 'new man.' Both sides stay within the cosmic system — faculty → virtue. The 'new man' is a cosmic process term (the transformed person), not an institutional one.
- ¶41: Completion of transformation: twelve members perfected, old man bound, dark spirit imprisoned in the five members. Very fragmentary but the substrate structure is clear — it narrates the cosmic process to completion.
- ¶42: The new man reigns through the five virtues (love, faith, perfection, patience, wisdom — the same sequence as ¶40). The Light Mind as king. Sin imprisoned but able to generate affliction — this sets up the progressive-attack sequence. Both sides cosmic.
- ¶43: First stage of Sin's progressive attack: Sin disturbs the CONSIDERATION (outermost faculty = skin). Clouds wisdom. This follows the five-fold system from outer to inner. Substrate — the attack is described within the body-cosmos framework.
- ¶44: Seam flag correctly identifies institutional extension. 'The church,' 'the teachers,' 'the presbyters' — specific institutional vocabulary. Describes the community's response to a member's foolishness. The faculty vocabulary (consideration, wisdom) is used within an institutional framework. Classified as institutional rather than mixed because the paragraph's PURPOSE is to describe the church's role.
- ¶45: Second stage: Sin rises from consideration to COUNSEL (flesh). Takes patience — patience is the virtue of counsel (¶40). The substrate progressive attack continues through the faculty system. Institutional overlay describes community correction.
- ¶46: Brief substrate fragment: Sin rises and clothes with lust/vanity/pride. The rest is institutional description of behavioral signs within the community. The substrate portion is minimal but preserves the progressive-attack structure.
- ¶47: Third stage: Sin rises to THOUGHT. 'The thought of death' replaces thought; faith and truth depart — faith is the virtue of thought (¶40). The progressive attack continues inward. Institutional overlay describes church elders' response.
- ¶48: Brief exhortatory/pastoral statement. Exhortation score (17.65) is the only score firing. 'It is possible he could live and be victorious over the sin' — hopeful application, not systematic teaching.
- ¶49: Fourth and final stage: Sin rises to MIND (innermost faculty = bone). Disrupts love — love is the virtue of mind (¶40). The progressive attack reaches the deepest level. Institutional overlay describes the social consequences within the community.
- ¶50: The cosmic process conclusion: Mind departs, evil spirits fill the void. The bird-without-feathers correspondence is striking — a vivid image for the soul stripped of its light faculties. 'He becomes an earthly man' is the cosmic reversal of the transformation. The institutional overlay references the church and the apostle.
- ¶51: Summary frame: 'Then, have I taught you...' Mani restates the macrocosm-microcosm correspondence but as meta-teaching reference, not new teaching. Formulaic.
- ¶52: Answers the fourth question (¶5): why is the Light Mind not visible? Mapping: watch-keepers not visible in the zone → Light Mind not visible in the body. Both sides cosmic.
- ¶53: Answers the second question (¶3): how can a great God appear in a small body? Mapping: great gods contort and become small for their tasks → Mind becomes small for the body. The word 'corresponding' (ⲉⲕⲁⲧⲁ) explicitly marks the correspondential method.
- ¶54: Answers the third question (¶4): how can a pure Mind dwell in a defiled body? Mapping: gods in the zone not defiled by mixture → Mind not defiled in the body. Fragmentary but the correspondence is clear.
- ¶55: Describes the Light Mind's governance of the body's watch-districts. Process vocabulary: shuts off, limits, distributes. The body is the cosmic domain; the Light Mind is the cosmic governor. Both sides stay within the system.
- ¶56: The Light Mind's supreme work: enabling cosmic traversal through the faculties. Ascending through the heart to the Father; descending through insight and consideration to the land of Darkness. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. 'Elect one' is Manichaean institutional vocabulary but may be a Coptic translation choice for the substrate concept of 'the transformed one.' The cosmological content is overwhelmingly dominant. Flagged for uncertainty on 'elect one.'
- ¶57: Frame: 'Then he says to that disciple: I have taught you the deeds of the Light Mind!' The perception teaching ('whoever has an open eye') is generic wisdom, not systematic correspondence. Transitional frame to the apostolate section.
- ¶58: Transition to the apostolate section. 'My work... my leadership.' Mani's self-reference. Pure frame.
- ¶59: Mani's apostolate claims: 'I, a single Manichaios, I have come to the world alone.' References races, kindreds, gold, silver, brass — worldly powers submitting to him. Apostolic narrative, not cosmic teaching.
- ¶60: Mani's comparison of his mission to worldly conquest. 'I have conquered without breastplate and without armour distant cities by the word of God.' Apostolic narrative — reaching outside the cosmic system into historical claims.
- ¶61: Kings, nobles, officials striving against Mani. Historical-biographical narrative about Mani's persecution and triumph. Institutional/apostolic layer.
- ¶62: Mani's third claim: giving freedom to his spiritual children. Institutional — describes his community work.
- ¶63: Mani's fourth claim: the 'breastplate of wisdom' for his children. Contains some exhortatory overtone ('so it is also like this for my children') but the primary function is apostolic boasting. Exhortation score (9.23) captures the devotional edge.
- ¶64: Mani's fifth claim: establishing the church. 'I have chosen... this great election,' 'I have made my church strong,' 'Apostles and ambassadors I have sent to all countries.' Highest institutional score in the chapter (11.24). Pure church-building narrative.
- ¶65: Apostolic succession claim: Mani surpasses all former apostles except Jesus. 'This hard generation' anchors to the editor's/Mani's present. Institutional.
- ¶66: Cosmic vocabulary ('gods and the angels and mankind, and all the spirits and the living souls') but used within Mani's self-referential claim: 'opened by me.' The cosmic language serves institutional purpose — Mani's mission extends to all cosmic beings. Still institutional despite substrate vocabulary.
- ¶67: Closing devotional frame: disciples promise to 'make ourselves strong in your faith; and persevere in your commandments.' Formulaic closure typical of Kephalaia chapters. Exhortation and dialogue frame interwoven.

## Chapter 39
### Concerning the Three Days and the Two Deaths

*This chapter is overwhelmingly substrate teaching. It presents a systematic cosmological classification of three great days (cosmic narrative of First Man, reign in new aeon, Father's eternal unveiling) and two deaths (mixing of light with darkness, eternal separation of sinful souls). Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system throughout — no contemporary institutions, no application to the editor's world, no editorial bridge. The only editorial material is dialogue framing in ¶1-3 and ¶13. The seam flags on ¶5 and ¶12 are false positives (empty institutional vocabulary). No editorial fatigue pattern. A remarkably clean teaching chapter with a Persian-style enumeration structure.*

There exist three great mighty days in all; and also two great and bitter deaths.

What are these three great days? Or again, what are these two deaths that are bitter?

The first day is our father, the Man. From the time when the First Man went down to the abyss of darkness and drowned in it; and the Living Spirit was sent, who went and bore him up. After the Man had ascended, the Father of Life and the Great Spirit set things in order, and the worlds above and below.

Furthermore, the glorious Ambassador came and dwelt in the ship of the day. He displayed his glorious image, and he purified the light out of all things. He walked in the heights of the heavens. Till the time when the world will be dissolved, all things destroyed, the great fire released, and the Last Statue ascends. These three deeds were begun on this first day, when they sent the First Man with his five sons.

⟨p.103⟩ In contrast, [the first death] is the darkness that mixed with the five sons of the First Man.

The second day is the time when the fathers of light, who were victorious in the struggle, will sit on their thrones in the new aeon and dwell on the new earth. And they reign in the new aeon till the Father unveils his image above them.

The {length of the sojourn} in the new aeon equals the measure of the first day spent in affliction. They will reign like this and rejoice in the new aeon.

The great third day [is the time] when the Father will unveil over them his image ... ... and he raises them up [ ... ] and he receives them into his hidden treasury ... and he gives [ ... ] his soul. He can pour out upon them ... his ambrosia and his sweet perfume, which takes away all affliction that they have seen with their eyes. [ ... ] fullness and a joy eternal! When his grace would sate them, he gives to them his [ ... ] and they are made anew in them.

Also, he can summon them once more in peace and silence, and perfect their stature in same measure as his first aeons. And he sets each power in its aeon, in its dwelling-place. They will dwell in the new aeon in their aeons!

Now, when they dwell in their aeons, they will become rich beyond measure, for ever, beyond time. From then on they will not count that season amongst them; nor the number of the days, nor the hours, generation after generation, for ever and ever. The Father will not be hidden from them, from this time on. Rather, he has accomplished (his) unveiling to them [ ... ] he being revealed to them for ever.

These are the three great days, [which the Father of Greatness has given number to in the reckoning].

In contrast, the two deaths are these.

⟨p.104⟩ The first death is from the time when the light fell to the darkness, and was mixed in with the rulers of Darkness; until the time when the light will become pure, and be separated from the darkness in that great fire. The remainder left behind there can build and add to that Last Statue.

Now, the second death is the death in which the souls of sinful men shall die; when [they will] be stripped of the shining light that illuminates the world. And also they are separated from the living air, from which they receive [living] breath; and they are deprived of this living soul, which [ ... ] in the world; and they are separated and swept away from this Last Statue [ ... ... ] and bound on [ ... ... ] the female will become [ ... ] their torture and their affliction; for their deeds [ ... ] those three places; because they have blasphemed and despised the Holy Spirit since [ev]ery generation of the world.

These are the two deaths. The first death is temporal; [but] the second death is eternal. It is the sec[ond deat]h!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Standard dialogue frame formula wrapping a substantive cosmological proposition. The three-days/two-deaths structure is the teaching's skeleton — it defines the enumeration that follows.
- ¶2: The question defines the teaching structure — it IS the substrate. The Persian pedagogical tradition structures teaching through such questions. The frame wrapping ('Tell us our father') is editorial.
- ¶3: Pure dialogue attribution with no cosmological content.
- ¶4: First enumeration unit. Maps the first day to the cosmic descent-rescue narrative. All entities (First Man, Living Spirit, Father of Life, Great Spirit) are cosmic agents. 'Our father, the Man' — standard epithet for the First Man within the cosmic system, not editorial devotional address.
- ¶5: Seam flag is a false positive — the institutional vocabulary field is empty. 'Furthermore' connects cosmic content to cosmic content (Ambassador, ship of the day, purification, great fire, Last Statue, First Man with five sons). All entities and processes are within the cosmic system. This continues and summarizes the first-day teaching.
- ¶6: Highest substrate density in the chapter (36.84). Maps the first death to a cosmic event — Darkness mixing with the five sons. Both sides pure cosmic domain. 'In contrast' marks the structural alternation between days and deaths.
- ¶7: Second enumeration unit. Maps the second day to cosmic eschatology — fathers of light, thrones, new aeon, new earth, Father's unveiling. All within the cosmic system. The 'new aeon' and 'new earth' are cosmic domains, not contemporary institutions.
- ¶8: Cosmological-temporal correspondence: duration of second day equals duration of first day. Maps time-domain to time-domain — a structural correspondence. The {length of the sojourn} preserves the manuscript's gap marker.
- ¶9: Third enumeration unit — heavily lacunose. The surviving text maps the third day to cosmic restoration: Father's unveiling, hidden treasury, ambrosia, sweet perfume. These are correspondential registers — sensory qualities (perfume, taste) mapped to cosmic processes (restoration, renewal). Despite heavy damage, the substrate structure is clear.
- ¶10: Continues third-day teaching. 'Perfect their stature in same measure as his first aeons' — cosmic restoration to original measure. 'Sets each power in its aeon, in its dwelling-place' — systematic cosmic ordering, place-assignment. Pure process description.
- ¶11: Describes the eternal state of the third day — what it IS, not what the audience should do. 'The Father will not be hidden from them' is a cosmic-relational statement. No audience address, no exhortation. The slight exhortation vocabulary score (4.05) comes from devotional-sounding phrases ('for ever and ever') but these describe cosmic eternity, not moral application. The 'Now' opener is a transitional particle, not an editorial bridge.
- ¶12: Recapitulation formula closing the three-days enumeration. Seam flag is false positive — empty institutional vocabulary. 'These are the three great days' is a standard enumeration closure integral to the teaching structure. The restored text attributes the three-fold numbering to the Father of Greatness — cosmic attribution, not editorial.
- ¶13: Structural transition from the three-days to the two-deaths enumeration. 'In contrast' parallels the same phrase in ¶6, marking the alternation. The first-person 'I have proclaimed to you' is dialogue frame inserted into the transition.
- ¶14: Expands on the first death introduced in ¶6. Maps it to the full cosmic process: light falling, mixing with rulers of Darkness, purification in great fire, Last Statue. Both sides entirely within the cosmic system. 'Rulers of Darkness' capitalized as personified cosmic agents.
- ¶15: Maps the second death to systematic cosmic separation: stripped of shining light, separated from living air, deprived of living soul, swept from Last Statue. This is a correspondential stripping sequence — the cosmic elements (light, air, soul) are removed one by one. 'Sinful men' is a cosmic category, not contemporary moral address. 'Blasphemed the Holy Spirit' describes rejection of a cosmic entity. 'Since every generation of the world' is cosmic temporal framing. The moral language stays within the system — it describes WHAT happens and WHY, not an exhortation to avoid it. Heavy lacunae in the second half.
- ¶16: Recapitulation formula closing the two-deaths enumeration, paralleling ¶12's closure of the three-days. The temporal/eternal distinction is a cosmological classification. 'It is the second death!' — emphatic closing. The phrase 'second death' occurs in Revelation 20:14, but here it arises naturally from the teaching's own two-deaths structure without citation markers. The substrate builds this term from scratch through enumeration.

## Chapter 40
### Concerning the Three Things that were established by the Light

*A remarkably pure chapter. After the formulaic dialogue frame opener (P1), the entire teaching body (P2-6) is oldest-layer eschatological cosmology. The three-fold enumeration maps cosmic necessity: (1) the new luminous earth built over this earth, (2) the coffin/prison of the dark fire, (3) condemned souls crucified on the enemy. The final paragraph describes the sealing of the final lump and the permanent separation/sterilization of Darkness. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system throughout. No citations, no institutional references, no contemporary application, no exhortation. The zero substrate vocabulary score reflects the narrow vocabulary list (zoomorphic/metal/taste markers), not the actual teaching character. Significant lacunae in P3-4 obscure details of the first two cosmic necessities.*

Three things would be fashioned by the Light through necessity. For there can be no other way nor other result, to allow them to stay without coming about. For the Darkness, the enemy, must be bound by them; he who stood up against the Light from the beginning. Due to this, these things have no (other) result, to allow them to stay without coming about.

Now, the first thing is the new luminous earth, which was set and built on top of this earth [ ... ] it, namely that alien body [ ... ] built upon it.

The second thing is this: They will [ ... ] and they [ ... ] the new earth, the coffin [ ... ] ⟨p.105⟩ of the dark fire, the companion of the enemy.

The third thing is the souls of all the sinners who have been condemned by their deeds. They were [ ... ] over them woe; they have been condemned according to the wicked deeds they had committed, and due to this they will be crucified on the enemy, he who had raised himself up against the greatness.

They will seal up this final lump when all the likenesses and images of every shape will be nailed in it. Also, those will be bound by this last fetter for eternity, and be placed as a footstool and a mat and a rag for this coffin [ ... ] which is the prison of the enemy. It will be placed, being a boundary-marker and a great void between the two, between the Darkness and the enemy, so that they cannot reach one another, nor can they hear their voices; rather they will become sterile as they are separated from each other for ever and ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic attribution: 'Once again he speaks to his disciples.' The additional qualifier 'these who are not associates in the mystery of his knowledge' is unusual but still part of the frame — it characterizes the audience, not the teaching content. Bridge connective correctly detected.
- ¶2: Impersonal cosmological teaching: describes the systematic necessity within the Light-Darkness opposition. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic entities (Light, Darkness). The repeated emphasis on necessity ('no other way', 'no other result') is characteristic of process-oriented substrate teaching describing what MUST happen given the cosmic architecture. The repetition may be deliberate rhetorical structure in the teaching tradition.
- ¶3: First enumeration unit of the three-fold teaching. Describes cosmic geography: the new luminous earth built upon this earth. 'Alien body' is a cosmological term for the constructed entity. Significant lacunae obscure detail. Enumeration marker preserved as per protocol.
- ¶4: Second enumeration unit. Despite heavy lacunae, the surviving terms are all cosmic: new earth, coffin (as cosmic container/prison), dark fire, the enemy. The 'coffin' functions as a correspondential image for the imprisonment of darkness — both sides stay within the cosmic system. Page marker ⟨p.105⟩ preserved.
- ¶5: Third enumeration unit. 'Souls of sinners' functions as a cosmic eschatological category — the final lump of unredeemed light-substance — not a moral address to a contemporary audience. 'Crucified on the enemy' is cosmic process: the damned souls become part of the enemy's prison. No voice shift to exhortation; no 'therefore you should' pivot. Both sides stay within the cosmic system.
- ¶6: The culmination of the teaching: the sealing of the final lump and the permanent separation of Darkness. 'Footstool and a mat and a rag' are correspondential descriptions of the lump's cosmic function — not contemporary metaphors. 'Boundary-marker and a great void between the two' is cosmic geography. The sterilization of Darkness through separation of its powers is systematic cosmological teaching about the final state. The low exhortation score (2.83) is likely triggered by 'for ever and ever' as a closing formula, but here it describes cosmic duration, not devotional closure. No voice shift to audience address anywhere.
