## Chapter 52
### Concerning the [ ... ] of the Light

*This chapter is predominantly later editorial material — a homiletic structure using cosmological narrative (light's unity vs. darkness's disunity) as a springboard for worldly analogy and community exhortation. Teaching purity is 0.000; no systematic correspondential mapping (zoomorphic, metal, taste, five-element) is present. Paragraphs 1-3 contain cosmological narrative fragments where both sides stay within the cosmic system (light entities, dark powers, abyss, disunity), but these are simple cosmological principles rather than the systematic domain-mapping characteristic of the strongest substrate chapters. Paragraphs 4-8 are entirely editorial bridge and exhortation. The possible Gospel echo ('kingdom divided within itself', cf. Mark 3:24) in paragraph 3 adds uncertainty. Heavy lacunae in paragraphs 1-2 obscure the original structure. Overall, this chapter preserves at best fragments of old cosmological teaching, heavily wrapped in editorial application.*

[Now as to what affects the] light, behold, at the beginning the Darkness was strong against it; [ ... ] no disunity [ ... ] exists in the light.

[ ... ] how far [ ... the lig]ht that came forth from [ ... ] in that place in a [ ... ] alone [ ... ] ⟨p.128⟩ the greatness of the light with one another for ever, and nor did one of them transgress the command and order that his brother had entrusted to him forever.

However, the Darkness, the enemy, was doomed and overcome; because of the arising of disharmony it was divided within itself. Even the kingdom was divided within itself; [and] ev[en] the [powers, who had] left their abyss behind and come up fo[llow-ing] it, were doomed because of the disunity that was present in all of them. The Darkness was destroyed, and its other powers were humiliated

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula is standard dialogue layer. The cosmological assertion — Darkness strong against light at the beginning, no disunity in the light — states a cosmic principle with both sides within the cosmic system. However, this is a simple theological principle rather than systematic correspondential mapping, so substrate classification carries uncertainty.
- ¶2: Heavily lacunose but surviving text describes the internal order of light entities — their mutual harmony, their hierarchical commands, their eternal cooperation. Both sides of the description stay within the cosmic system (light entities, their relationships, their commands). The language 'command and order that his brother had entrusted' describes hierarchical cosmic governance. Uncertainty is high due to lacunae — we cannot see the opening and cannot rule out frame material in the lost sections.
- ¶3: The cosmic narrative (darkness overcome by disunity, powers leaving abyss, darkness destroyed) describes events within the cosmic system — both sides stay internal. The final clause ('functions in the same way as the kingdom of this world') is the editorial seam that bridges to the worldly analogy. Note possible Gospel echo in 'kingdom divided within itself' (cf. Mark 3:24), which could indicate editorial shaping of the cosmic narrative, but the concept of a divided dark realm is independently attested in Manichaean cosmology. Uncertainty is genuine.
- ¶4: Entirely worldly kingdom analogy — describes how a kingdom prospers when its associates agree and surpasses rival kingdoms. One side of the mapping (kingdoms, associates, glory, marvel) is outside the cosmic system. This is the editor's illustrative analogy connecting the cosmic principle (light's unity) to worldly experience, preparing for the community exhortation in paragraph 6.
- ¶5: Continuation of the worldly kingdom analogy — the united kingdom condemns and humiliates the divided one. Still entirely outside the cosmic system, describing political dynamics. The vocabulary ('associates in the mystery', 'condemn', 'come under the hand') mirrors paragraph 4 and completes the bridge analogy before the pivot to exhortation.
- ¶6: 'In exactly the same way, you yourselves' — bridge connective flagged by the pipeline, correctly. This is the explicit pivot to community application. Direct address ('you yourselves'), reference to 'this living truth that is unveiled to you', and injunction to 'conquer the sin that is set up against you' are all hallmarks of the exhortation layer. The cosmic principle (light's unity defeats darkness) is now APPLIED to the audience's spiritual struggle.
- ¶7: Continued exhortation with escalating emotional register — 'may it not happen!' is a devotional interjection. Direct address, warning about consequences of division ('condemned', 'humiliated under the hand of sin'). The exhortation vocabulary score (6.9) confirms. References to 'this truth that is unveiled to you' mark the community's present experience.
- ¶8: Devotional closure with imperative voice: 'Neither be divided... you will conquer those above and those below; and bring under your feet every thing that is and that will be!' Classic exhortation closure — moral injunction + eschatological promise. The language of triumph ('conquer', 'bring under your feet', 'every thing that is and that will be') is devotional-eschatological, not cosmological-correspondential.

---

## Chapter 53
### Concerning the First Man

*This chapter is almost entirely oldest teaching substrate. The only editorial element is the dialogue frame formula in ¶1. The teaching describes the First Man's descent, the construction of the universe from captured dark powers, and the devastation of the dark storehouses — using two correspondential analogies (marsh/net and body/head-limbs). The body-cosmos mapping in ¶5-6 is a recognized substrate marker. No institutional, exhortation, or editorial bridge vocabulary appears anywhere in the chapter. Despite heavy lacunae damage throughout, the cosmological process narrative is coherent and systematic.*

At the time when the First Man was sent to the war in the first garment of [living fire], which he gave to the first nature that exists [...] in that single garment. [...] he hunted Matter, the thought of death, [...] and all its other powers.

⟨p.130⟩ When the First Man came up from the abyss, [...] the Living Spirit brought the rulers whom he had hunted [...] element, that [...] of [the land of] darkness, that he might construct from them this universe and fashion everything appropriate for the greatness. From that time on, the place of the storehouses of the elements [...] he destroyed and laid waste the [...] all that were in it.

Just like a marsh, of which the fish and birds are snared in a net, and only the waters are left behind and the reeds and the plants that grow in it, this is also the case with the storehouses of death. The [First] Man snared their counsels and [their k]in that were [in them], and the storehouses were left behind in their [dwelli]ng place.

So then, the case of the land of darkness is like this, as is that of the storehouses that were in it.

Just as the body of man has many limbs in it, [the land of darkness] has [storehouses from] its beginning [...] all [the limbs] that hang from the great head of the [body]. A single root is the root of them all, because they all hang from the great head that is on top of the body. Should a limb be cut from the tip of [the]se small [h]eads, the great head that is on top of them [al]l stays in its place the way that it is [...] you find this person alive. Al[l] these sm[all h]eads are firmly set in it [...]

Wh[en] they will cut from them [...] all of them, these little [heads] shall [...] the corpses, because they might see [...] be firmly set [...] in the body, which they joined wi[th it ...]

In exactly this way, it is also the case that the storehouses of darkness are the same, the things from which the [darkness gushed] forth.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula is standard Kephalaia compilation marker. The teaching content — First Man's descent in the garment of living fire, hunting Matter — is cosmological process narrative. 'Matter, the thought of death' is a substrate epithet describing the personified cosmic antagonist.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological process: First Man's ascent, Living Spirit's demiurgic construction from captured rulers, devastation of storehouses. 'Construct from them this universe' and 'fashion everything appropriate for the greatness' describe the cosmic architecture's formation. Heavy lacunae but the process structure is clear.
- ¶3: Correspondential analogy mapping natural domain (marsh, fish, birds, net, reeds) onto cosmic domain (storehouses of death, counsels, kin). Both sides of the mapping remain within the cosmic system. The marsh IS the correspondence for the depleted dark storehouses. Classic substrate technique — expounding through natural correspondence.
- ¶4: Brief transitional summary linking the land of Darkness to its storehouses. Functions as a structural connector between the marsh analogy and the body-head analogy that follows.
- ¶5: Body-cosmos correspondence: the body's head-limb hierarchy mapped onto the land of Darkness and its storehouses. This is a recognized substrate marker (body-cosmos mapping, cf. Ch. 70). 'A single root is the root of them all' — systematic hierarchical description. Despite lacunae, the structural logic (great head vs. small heads, cutting limbs vs. cutting the head) is the teaching itself.
- ¶6: Continuation of the body-head analogy. Very fragmentary — heavy lacunae obscure the exact argument, but the vocabulary (corpses, little heads, body, firmly set) remains within the body-cosmos correspondence domain. No editorial markers despite the damage.
- ¶7: Closing summary applying the body-head analogy back to the storehouses of Darkness. 'The things from which the [darkness gushed] forth' — cosmological process language describing the storehouses' original function. The chapter closes cleanly within the cosmic system with no editorial extension, exhortation, or institutional application.

---

## Chapter 54
### [Concerning] the Quality of the Garments

*This chapter is almost entirely oldest teaching layer. Teaching purity is 1.000 with zero editorial fatigue. The chapter describes the Living Spirit's construction of the universe from the mixture of light and darkness, using craft analogies (architect, carpenter) that belong to the ancient demiurge-as-craftsman tradition. No institutional, exhortation, or editorial_bridge vocabulary appears anywhere. The only later material is the brief dialogue frame formula in P1. The craft metaphors in P3-5, while not pure cosmos→cosmos correspondential mapping, serve the cosmological teaching and bear no marks of editorial extension — they describe HOW the cosmic builder works using timeless craft analogies rooted in ancient Near Eastern cosmological pedagogy. P4's seam flag for 'Furthermore' is overridden: content continues the same analogical teaching with no register shift to institutional material.*

At the time when the F[ir]st [Man ...] he gave the leadership to his five [sons ... the] Living Spirit and the Mother of Life [...] as the entire ruling-power is entwined and crucified [...] they were poured out and mixed with each other, the light with the Darkness and the Darkness with the light.

At that time the Living Spirit called one of his sons, one rich with power. He gave to him the leadership of the garments. Now, he himself [...] of [...] he hung them and set them up [...] of the Father of Life [...] he set up all things [...] he opened [...] ten firmaments [...] the wheel of the stars [...] them and the [...].

⟨p.132⟩ The Living Spirit is like this: Just as [...] and an architect who might build a dwelling for the king, and divide it up into separate houses and [separate ...] everything that is required for beauty [...] and he cleans them. And also the stones [...] them for beauty and {loveliness}, and he arranges them and makes them so that should the king come and inhabit that palace, he might glorify him.

Furthermore, the Father of Life resembles a wise and skilled carpenter [...] before it was worked [...] before it was established and made beautiful and [...] carpenter [...] works in it, but [...] in him in his [...] in his understanding, and he brings the saw and the adze, and cuts it into separate pieces [...] it and he pulls the [...] it with the {plane} and he smooths it. He shall make many [...] wooden implements, arranging them shape by shape; he cuts and produces wooden implements from it, wooden implements of different kinds, (such as) dishes, bowls. This wood, that [...] since previously it had not been worked before it was sealed by [the ski]ll and wisdom of this dextrous carpenter. He cut it up into different sorts of implements useful to his master.

So is it also with the Father of Life. In his wisdom he has [...] in limbs [...] palace [...] in it or in a [...] useful [...] the ships of light [...] all the other works [...] in the [...] so that when the great king comes, he who is the Third Ambassador, who [...] he might dwell in this universe [...]

⟨p.133⟩ ... live. The light, however, that is in it [...] wise, and he takes it up to the land; but the waste and the Darkness will be gathered and thrown down to the abyss.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Frame formula wraps substrate cosmological teaching about the primordial event: First Man's sacrifice of five sons, the resulting mixture of light and darkness. High substrate score (21.67). The teaching describes the foundational cosmological event — the mixing — in impersonal process language. 'Entwined and crucified' and 'poured out and mixed' are systematic cosmic process vocabulary.
- ¶2: Highest substrate score in chapter (24.24). Pure cosmic construction narrative: Living Spirit delegates to his son, who constructs the cosmic architecture — firmaments, wheel of stars. 'Ten firmaments' and 'the wheel of the stars' are classic substrate cosmological vocabulary. Despite heavy lacunae, the structure describes emanation hierarchy and cosmic construction.
- ¶3: Architect-as-cosmic-builder analogy. While this maps cosmos→human craft rather than cosmos→cosmos, the demiurge-as-craftsman is one of the oldest cosmological figures in Near Eastern tradition. The analogy serves the cosmological teaching: it describes HOW the Living Spirit constructs the cosmic dwelling for the 'king' (the Ambassador). Low substrate vocabulary score (2.74) reflects analogical rather than technical cosmological language, but zero scores in all editorial categories. No marks of later editorial extension.
- ¶4: Seam flag fires for 'Furthermore' bridge connective, but OVERRIDDEN: content continues the same craft-analogy teaching sequence with no shift to institutional vocabulary. The carpenter metaphor parallels the architect metaphor in P3 — both describe how the cosmic builder shapes raw material (the mixture) into useful cosmic structures. The progression from architect (macro structure) to carpenter (working raw material into implements) mirrors the cosmic process: the universe is built, then the mixed substance is worked into functional cosmic apparatus. Zero scores in all editorial categories. The 'Furthermore' is intra-teaching progression, not editorial extension.
- ¶5: Application of the craft analogies back to cosmic entities: Father of Life, ships of light, Third Ambassador dwelling in the universe. 'So is it also with' closes the analogical sequence by mapping back to the cosmic system. This confirms P3-4 as pedagogical analogies within the cosmic teaching, not editorial extensions. 'Ships of light' is substrate cosmological vocabulary. The Third Ambassador as the 'great king' who inhabits the cosmic palace completes the architect/carpenter teaching.
- ¶6: Cosmic eschatological summary: light ascends to the land of light, darkness/waste descends to the abyss. This is pure cosmic process teaching — the final separation. Despite zero substrate vocabulary score (the paragraph is very short at 35 words with heavy lacunae), the content is systematic cosmological process: the dual destiny of light and darkness. 'Gathered and thrown down to the abyss' describes cosmic-structural eschatology, not moral exhortation.

---

## Chapter 55
### Concerning the Fashioning of Adam

*This chapter is overwhelmingly substrate teaching with minimal editorial overlay. The oldest layer dominates: a systematic cosmological teaching about the Ambassador's image display, the rulers' unauthorized fashioning of Adam and Eve as an imperfect copy, and a three-fold enumeration of figures fashioned outside intercourse (sea giant, sea monster/Adamant, vegetation). A correspondential parable (the noble woman) is embedded as a teaching device — both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. The only clearly non-substrate material is the dialogue frame attribution in ¶2, the pedagogical address formulas in ¶1/3/9, and the transitional marker in ¶8. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation layers are detectable. The substrate vocabulary actually increases in the second half, consistent with the chapter-level shift data (+0.44). The term 'crucified in the totality' in ¶7 is distinctively Manichaean but describes cosmic process rather than contemporary application.*

There are some amo]ng us telling us that we [know (?) ... ] that the (Ambassador) consents about [tha]t f[igure] of flesh; he who shall be fashi[oned ... ] at another time. And next, he says: [The Ambassa]dor displayed his image in the un[iverse]. The rulers and the powers of the universe saw h[is image], and they formed their shapes after his likeness, who are Adam and Eve. Behold, you see that god con[sen]ts to its happening; because of this, he has displayed his image to the rulers; and they constructed [ ... ... ] If he was not consenting to humankind, he would not have uncovered his image, nor they fashioned mankind after his likeness!

Regarding the Ambassador, the occasion when he came displaying his image, he [d]id not come so th[a]t he might reveal his image to the ru[l]ers in the universe. Rather, he came and displayed to the universe be[cause of h]is soul and his son, who is [ ... ] every [ ... ], that he might enable him to live [ ... ] and make for him a release; and to free (the soul) from every bond and all the fetters, in which it is entangled and bound.

The rulers and the authorities, in that they had no form in [ ... ] him, they lusted for his image. They reflected upon themselves, that [they] had no [one] like him in their creation. They sealed hi[s im]a[g]e in their heart, within their soul. Afterward[s, they] built it according to his likeness; they formed Adam and [Ev]e. They sealed the image of the exalted one in their [soul]. So, they [co]pied with an imitation; but they have not [copied i]n truth! Rather, just as [ ... ... ] it is made as these, for [i]t came [ ... ] but it is not l[ik]e him in truth. [Again, so too] is this figure of ma[nkind. They copi]ed it against the image of the exalted one [ ... ]

Similarly the [t]ree, for while it is like the [ ... it is] not [copi]ed in truth.

This matt[er is comparable to] this: As a great, fre[e] woman [ ... ] within her [ ... ... ] rich in wealth [ ... ] po[ss]essions; she is also beautiful in her beauty, and is virtuous in her modesty. She walks in a free and reputable position. The fame of her beauty has spread in every c[i]ty. She sur[passes ... ] Behold h[er] chamber [ ... ] one who is mad, because [of] the nobles and the great ones. Al[so], they lust for her beautiful face. They did not [ ... ] her, because she is hidden in her palace [ ... ] within in a [ ... ] meet that woman [ ... ] his so[n]; or rather the head [ ... t]hat garment. This free woman shall [ ... ] leave the cha[mber] behind her and come to the street [ ... ] her head [ ... ] and she reveals her [face and her beauty ... ] because of her beloved broth[er]. This wom[an], on whom no man ever looked, nor did they ever see her fa[ce], as she neither desires nor rejoices [ ... ... ] leave her chamber behind her, and come in the midst of mankind [ ... ] and everyone view her. The hones[t] men and the nobles, even the servants too, and the [ ... ] look at her.

⟨p.135⟩ In accord with this simile: Again, [this] too [is] how [ ... ] the Ambassador becomes [ ... ... ] as this free woman [ ... not] according to wantonness, in pride, nor [ ... she co]nsented to the outer world, she [ ... ... ], she revealed her face and her [beau]ty [ ... ] but only due to the constraint of grief [about] her [bel]ov[ed one].

Now, this too is the way of the Third Ambassador, when he came and display[ed his] image with a show towards all the powers. [He did] not [co]me so that all the rulers and the author[it]ies would sculpt a form [after h]is likeness. R[ather], he came because of his son being crucified in the totality; [fo]r he would release him, free him, and rescue him from oppres-sion. Yet, when the rulers looked o[n] him, [th]ey lusted for his image. They fashioned after his [like]ness Adam and Eve, without the consent of the gre[at]ness.

For, [a]s with Adam and Eve, whom the rulers fashioned and [se]aled after the image of the exalted one, so too this [i]s how a manifold divinity and many powers and angels [ ... received] the stamp of that image, which was displayed to them. For, in the majesty of the l[ig]ht and (the image's) love, they glowed with their desire and they were undone. Th[ey] were eager for their lust; like a m[an who looks] to the majesty of a lusted after face, and [ ... ] and he is eager for the {} of the desire.

⟨p.136⟩ This is [als]o the case with those powers, when they looked on that majestic and beautiful [imag]e, they () [ ... ] a first image. They received the stamp of th[at image], which was displayed to them. They copied [ ... wh]ich th[ey co]pied out in a likeness, it being made [ ... ... ] the ones that they had put forth, which had received a [stamp of this im]age; but inward even to the rocks [ ... ... ] which occur from place to place. The {} of that image, which was displayed in the universe [ ... ] they received a stamp, it being revealed there, and an impress.

Now, this new birth that they begot in a new [ ... ] in the world, is Adam and Eve. [There is not a] form in their world since the beginning like it; because that [im]age [occ]urs not in the worlds b[el]ow.

Not all the figures that have been fashioned were fashioned by birth from displayed intercourse. Rather, there are other figures fashioned [ ... ]

One of them is the sea [gi]ant, the 'sweeping' from the sphere, which the [Father] of Life discharged into the sea. Its own fire and thought fashioned it [in] a figure.

The second figure is the one [that] fell to the earth. It made the sea its womb. Its own l[us]t became its sculptor. It alone fashioned it to a nature, which is the root of death. Yet, when it came up from the sea, it [ ... ] that it might destroy the works [ ... ] of Life. They immediately sent against it the Adamant of Light, the great 'instruction of vigour'. He humbled it in the districts of the north. He has trampled underfoot and set h[im]self upon it, until the end of the world.

⟨p.137⟩ [The] third figure is the nature that fell on wh[at] is dr[y. It] fashioned the tree and set itself there. [ ... the] new figure that was fashioned [ ... d]isplays.

So, in accord with the essence they [ ... the dark]ness; but yet th[ey] did not come with the King of Dark[ness to the wo]rld. Rather, their fashioning [came] about [ ... ] they are composite and have been displayed in the world.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The question is substantive — it presents the cosmological argument that the Ambassador's image display implies divine consent to the human form. The cosmological content (Ambassador displayed image → rulers formed shapes → Adam and Eve) defines the teaching topic. 'There are some among us' references the community but is part of the question's substance in the Persian dialogue tradition.
- ¶2: The teaching content is pure substrate: the Ambassador's purpose was to free the soul from bonds and fetters, not to reveal his image to rulers. 'Soul-body binding formula' (bonds, fetters, entangled, bound) is a classic substrate marker. The negation-correction structure ('did not come so that... Rather...') is a teaching device clarifying cosmic process.
- ¶3: Core teaching about the mechanism of unauthorized fashioning: rulers lusted → sealed image in their soul → built according to likeness → copied with imitation, not in truth. The distinction between 'imitation' and 'truth' is a fundamental substrate category — the copy is not the original. The process described (seal → build → form) is systematic cosmological process.
- ¶4: Brief substrate continuation extending the imitation/truth distinction to the tree. The tree analogy maps within the cosmic system — vegetation as another domain of imperfect copying. Despite heavy lacunae, the substrate logic is clear.
- ¶5: The parable is a teaching simile: the noble woman hidden in her palace who reveals herself to save her brother corresponds to the Ambassador revealing his image to rescue the entrapped soul. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system — this is correspondence being used as a teaching method, not pointing to contemporary institutions. The 'comparable to' formula is a standard teaching introduction in the Persian pedagogical tradition. Significant lacunae but the correspondential structure is intact.
- ¶6: Completes the correspondential mapping: the Ambassador = the free woman. The revelation was not from wantonness but from grief over the beloved one (the entrapped soul/son). Both sides of the mapping stay in the cosmic system. 'In accord with this simile' is a teaching structural marker. Heavy lacunae but the teaching logic is clear.
- ¶7: Restates the substrate teaching with specificity: the Third Ambassador's purpose was release/rescue, not consent to fashioning. 'Crucified in the totality' is distinctively Manichaean christological language (Jesus patibilis concept), but here it describes a cosmic state — the son/soul distributed and entrapped in matter — not a contemporary application. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'Without the consent of the greatness' explicitly answers the question posed in ¶1.
- ¶8: Pure dialogue frame transitional marker. First-person address ('I have enlightened you'), reference to the question asked, instruction to memorize. No cosmological content whatsoever. Functions as an editorial seam between the first teaching section (Ambassador/Adam) and the second (stamp of the image on powers).
- ¶9: Extends the substrate teaching: not only Adam and Eve but also many powers and angels received the stamp of the displayed image. The teaching maps cosmic process: image display → desire → stamp reception. The simile ('like a man who looks to the majesty of a lusted after face') is a teaching device within the substrate, mapping human experience back to cosmic process.
- ¶10: Continues substrate teaching about the stamp of the image permeating the cosmos — from powers down to rocks. This is a systematic degree-mapping: the image's stamp extends through levels of being (powers → their productions → rocks). Both sides stay within the cosmic system. The term 'impress' alongside 'stamp' suggests a technical vocabulary of image-transmission in the substrate.
- ¶11: Substrate teaching identifying Adam and Eve as unprecedented — a 'new birth' with no prior form in the lower worlds. The image occurs not in the worlds below, establishing that the human form derives from above, not from the dark realms. Maps cosmic geography (worlds below) to the origin of form.
- ¶12: Introduces the three-fold enumeration of figures fashioned outside intercourse. This is a structural transition within the substrate — it sets up the systematic list that follows. The distinction between intercourse-fashioned and otherwise-fashioned figures is a cosmological category.
- ¶13: First enumeration unit. The sea giant was fashioned by its own fire and thought — an internal fashioning process, not sexual generation. 'Sweeping from the sphere' and 'Father of Life discharged into the sea' are cosmic process descriptions. The enumeration marker 'One of them is' is integral to the teaching structure.
- ¶14: Second enumeration unit. Rich substrate content: the figure's own lust as sculptor, the sea as womb, 'root of death' as cosmic category. The Adamant of Light subduing it 'in the districts of the north' is cosmic geography. 'Until the end of the world' is cosmological time-frame, not editorial eschatology. The substrate vocabulary score (4.12) is the highest in the chapter, confirming the cosmological density.
- ¶15: Third enumeration unit, heavily lacunose. The nature fell on dry land and fashioned the tree. This completes the three-fold pattern: sea giant (sea), sea monster (sea→earth), vegetation (dry land). The spatial mapping (sea/earth/dry) is systematic cosmic geography. Connects back to the tree mentioned briefly in ¶4.
- ¶16: Concluding substrate summary for the three-fold enumeration. These figures are from Darkness in essence but did not arrive with the King of Darkness — they are 'composite,' fashioned during the cosmic history from the mixture. This is a fundamental cosmological category: composite beings that belong neither purely to light nor to darkness. The teaching completes the chapter's argument: the human form and these other figures are products of the cosmic process, not of original divine or dark design.

## Chapter 56
### [Concerning Saklas and his Powers]

*This chapter contains a strong, dominant substrate layer organized around two major teaching units: (1) the transmission of the Ambassador's image through Sin/Matter to the rulers who shaped Adam and Eve (¶1-4), and (2) the faculty-storehouse system of the body (¶6-24, 27-28), which is one of the most systematic body-cosmos correspondences in the Kephalaia. The faculty-storehouse teaching maps five senses to five faculties, each with internal storehouses and repositories, governed by the heart-faculty. This is followed by a gates/watchmen correspondence mapping body orifices to a fortified camp. The Light Mind's entry and transformation of the body's systems completes the cosmological architecture. Editorial additions are light: dialogue frame attributions (easily stripped), one pedagogical summary closure (¶5), one editorial bridge applying the transformation teaching to institutional Manichaean worship practices (¶26), and a final exhortation (¶29). The chapter shows mild editorial fatigue in ¶25-26 where worldly examples and institutional vocabulary enter the application of the faculty transformation. Gardner notes the unusual shift from 'Light Mind' to 'Holy Spirit' terminology in ¶28.*

All [these a]bortions, amongst them are Saklas and his consort and the ones whom they have served [ ... ], they are the ones that shaped Adam and Eve. How did they find this beautiful image laid over their shape, even though when it was displayed to their fathers they were not in existence? They never saw the image of the Ambassador! And how did they take the seal of the image of the Ambassador? They added it to [the] shape of Adam and Eve.

The Sin that spurted out from the rulers, which is Matter, shot up [tow]ards the image of the Ambassador. It was cut off from [tha]t place and came down to the earth, for they did not accept [it wi]thin the firmaments. When it came do[wn to] the earth, it formed the tree. It was established within the wood and formed the fruits. And when [the] abortions fell [to] the ground, to the earth, at the [second] time [ ... / ... ]

⟨p.138⟩
[... in the] fruits, it assumed the[m] and spoke in the ruler, their leader. He says to his companions: Come! Give me your light, and I will construct for you an image aft[er] the likeness of the exalted one. What he said, they did: they gave it to him, and he constructed [ ... ].

The Sin that s[purted out ... / ... ] which is the Matter that saw the im[age of the Ambassa]dor and that formed the tre[e and was established i]n [i]t; afterwards it came up in the [fruits ... ]. It went in to the rulers [ ... ] they formed Adam and Eve after the [likeness of the] exalt[ed one]. Through the energy of the Sin that had s[een the image of the] Ambassador, it went into the (rulers) [through the fruits. It] assumed them and they [t]oo sculp[ted ... they] begat each of them according to his destiny [ ... ]; one he resembles, one he does not resemble.

The moulder placed in the form of Adam and Eve limbs, outside and within, for perception and activity. He was apportioned house by house. For everything that his perceptions and elements will receive externally there are internal storehouses and repositories and cavities; and what is received in to them is stored in them. Whenever they will be questioned about what is deposited in their internal storehouses, they bring out what they have received within and give it to the questioner who requested it of them.

In this way his faculty [ ... ] outer [limbs] to look at [ ... ] every type with [in ... ]

⟨p.139⟩
Also [the fa]culty of the eyes [has] houses and cavities and repositories and stores within, so that every image it might see, whether good or evil, whether loveable or detestable or lustful, it can receive into its storehouses and repositories. Also, when the faculty of the eyes is pleased to send out the image that it saw and took in, it can go in to its storehouses [at the] time and think and seek [ ... ] and it brings it out [and] gives it to the questioner who requested it and the one who wan[t]ed it—whether it be something from lu[st ... or a]n image of love [or ... / ... something] hateful. And thus shall that faculty (of the eyes) produce and do what it does in each category.

The faculty of the ears has its own storehouses also. Every sound it might receive, whether good or evil, shall be taken in and placed in its houses and inner repositories, and it is guarded in its [ ... ] for a thousand days. After a thousand days, if someone comes and asks that faculty about the sound that it heard [at] this time and took into its storehouses, immediately it [sh]all g[o] into its repositories and seek and review and search after this word, and send it out from where it was first put, the place in which it was kept.

[In] like order, the faculty of scent [s]hall function just as that of the eyes and that of the auditory organs. Every [o]dour it shall smell it shall take in [to it] and [d]eposit in its inner storehouses. Every time it will be [a]sked by a questioner, it shall go in [ ... ] and [ ... ] storehouse and remember [ ... ] only these things.

⟨p.140⟩
However, even the mouth and the tongue within it, and the taste organ, have a faculty dwelling in them. Again, that faculty too, of taste, has thus cavities and repositories set apart for it. It too receives these tastes and gathers them in. A[nd] at any moment when someone will ask of a taste, if [ ... ] it shall send it out and remember tha[t] taste. It shall snare and give even the mar[k of] th[a]t taste; give its memory to the qu[est]ioner who asks for it.

Again, the fac[ul]ty of touch by the hands is also s[o]: When it might touch, touch shall [receive i]ts memory. And it [takes] it in to its inner repository [until] som[eo]ne will ask this faculty for the memory. [Immediately], it shall go in again and bring out the memory of this touch that it made, and give it to whoever asks for it.

And the faculty of the heart that rules over them all is much the most like this. Every thing that these five faculties will receive and put in store for the faculty of the heart it shall receive and guard. Any time that they will ask for their deposit it shall send out and give every thing that they gave to it.

For the counsel of the heart is like the king. All the ad[v]isers of his city and the leaders of his a[r]my are placed with him. Any time if his genera[ls] will entreat him for whatever they wish, he shall give them the ad[v]ice and opinion by which they move.

Furthermore, it is like a cellarman: [Eve]ry thing that will be entrusted to it by them, and given to it, it shall receive and put in separate vessels and places. Any time [ ... / ... ]

⟨p.141⟩
... in the place that it put it.

This also is the way of the faculty of the body. The vision that the eyes will see and give to it, and it receives it in, it shall bring out again from the storehouses wherein it had placed it; and give it to whoever asks for it.

Again, [the] sound that the ears will hear and give to it, and it receives it from them. At any time when someone will ask it for that sound, it shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked it for that sound. It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked.

Again, [the] faculty of smell in this same pattern; and that of taste is of the same [ty]pe. The touch by the han[ds ... / ... ] they shall bring it out in to [ ... / ... ] And whenever they will as[k it] for it, it shall bring it out and gi[ve it].

Indeed, watchmen are a[t] these doors guarding them, and bolts are fastened on the doors at the hands of the guards that guard them!

Just like watchmen guarding a camp with towers and watch-posts. As the holding rooms come out from there and the bolts are upon the gates, the guards control the bolts of the gates while they sit at the entrance [of] the watchposts. So, someone who belongs to their city as a fellow [cit]izen of them, belonging to their country, for him they shall open [the ga]te and receive him in by their will. However, the foreigner who comes they shall prevent, and leave him standing at the gate. They shall not yield to let him enter. Again, another one whom they will receive in, with great trouble before [they] take him in, you shall find many holding rooms [ ... / ... ] that [ ... ] there are a mass [ ... / ... ] them. The number of d[oor] bolts [ ... ] are [ ... ] Whenever someone will wish [ ... ] the bolts at his hand, opens the gate and brings out whoever is hidden within.

This body too is like the mighty camp. And the gates of the camp with their guards are like the orifices and organs of the body. Now, the orifices of the body are of sight, hearing and smell; and they that send out words. There are many sentinels and a mass of guards placed over the limbs of the body, guarding their orifices. And the faculty in it is the directive of the body, the queen of the entire camp; so that whenever it wishes it can open, or when it wishes it can shut.

However, if and wh[en] the Light Mind comes, it shall tu[rn i]n to the orifices of the body. The rulers, who [a]re the door gua[rds], shall h[in]der and restrain it from en[ter]ing. They shall bind the circu[it] of the body so that the foreign[er] can not enter it.

Nevertheless, the Light Mind by its wisdom and awe and diligence shall hum[ble] the guards who are set at the body's orifices. When it finishes humbling them it shall take fr[o]m them bolts to all the orifices of the body. Now, the orifices that had been opened before to the parades of lust, to receive in the sights of pleasure, to transfer the [wor]ds of the practice of li[f]e's concerns, to receive in the t[astes] of the different foods and unclean meals together with the sin therein; whoever stops receiving th[is] in, his heart and mind shall follow after.

By the power of the Light Mind that has suffused him and lived in the body he has humbled Lust, the goddess of the body.

[S]imply, all orifices in similar fashion were opening at one time to wickedness, but now have opened to the good. Sin was ruling in the body this time, doing anything it liked, having none in command.

Yet, [n]ow, authority has been taken from the Sin in the org[ans] of the body. The Living Spirit has become lord over all its limbs. It has bound them with a chain of peace, and sealed them with the seal of truth. It has opened the orifices of the body to the good, and because of this the good enters them through the ears and the eyes. It dwells in the heart. The H[o]ly Spirit reigns. It does all that it pleases. It annuls [the] will of [S]in, the begin-ning and the end. [In contrast], the will of the [Ho]ly Spir[it] comes about, and the purpose of the Light Mind.

- ¶5: 'Behold, I have taught you this lesson' is a pedagogical closure formula bridging the first teaching section (image-transmission) to the second (faculty-storehouses). The summary content restates what was already taught — no new cosmic information. Functions as a session-transition marker.

The heart receives from all five, guards their deposits, and returns them on demand. High substrate density (12.9) — the most concentrated substrate paragraph in the chapter.
- ¶14: First simile illustrating the heart-faculty: heart as king. King → advisers → generals is a standard correspondential hierarchy mapping governance. - ¶15: Second simile: heart as cellarman/storekeeper. - ¶16: Fragment continuing from the preceding lacuna. Too damaged for detailed analysis but structurally part of the cellarman simile or the heart-faculty teaching.
- ¶17: Recapitulation: the body-faculty's interaction with the eye-faculty through the storehouse system. 'This also is the way' signals a systematic recapitulation pass through the senses. The teaching structure cycles: first iteration (¶8-12), governing principle (¶13-16), second pass (¶17-19).
- ¶18: Recapitulation for the ear-faculty. Note the repetition at the end ('It shall bring out and give the sound of the ears to whoever has asked') — possibly scribal dittography, but preserved as-is per manuscript.
- ¶19: Abbreviated recapitulation for scent, taste, and touch — 'in this same pattern,' 'of the same type.' The teaching has established its structure sufficiently that the remaining senses can be covered by reference. This is characteristic of systematic substrate teaching.
- ¶20: Transitional statement opening the third teaching unit: the watchmen/gates correspondence. Maps body orifices → doors, spiritual entities → watchmen/guards, authority → bolts. Both sides within the cosmic system.
- ¶21: Extended camp/gates correspondence. Body → camp, orifices → gates, spiritual entities → guards/watchmen, impressions → citizens/foreigners. The classification of entrants (citizen vs. foreigner) maps what the body admits vs. rejects. 'Unclean meals together with the sin therein' is cosmic teaching about the mixture in matter, not institutional dietary rules. The whole paragraph describes the cosmic process of authority-transfer at the body's gates.
- ¶25: This paragraph marks the transition from substrate cosmic process to editorial application. But the surrounding material applies the transformation to the 'righteous/chosen person' with worldly examples (garments, gold, pearls, silken women). The editorial voice is clear: it describes what the audience should experience.
- ¶26: Editorial application of the transformation teaching to the ear-faculty. The structure mirrors ¶25 (before/after under Light Mind) but reaches into Manichaean institutional life: 'the sounds of the lessons of righteousness, the words of the psalms and of the prayers, the praise of the hymns and the lessons of truth, and the knowledge of charity.' These are specific worship practices of the community. Institutional vocabulary score (4.46) confirms. 'Sin was ruling in the body... doing anything it liked, having none in command' — personified Sin as cosmic ruler of the body with no higher authority. The 'but now' marks cosmic sequence (before/after Light Mind), not editorial temporal reference. Both sides stay within the body-cosmos system.
- ¶28: Climactic cosmic description: authority transfer from Sin to the Living Spirit/Holy Spirit/Light Mind. Maps cosmic agents → body governance: bound with chain of peace, sealed with seal of truth, orifices opened to good, good enters through ears and eyes, dwells in heart. Gardner notes the unusual terminological shift from 'Light Mind' to 'Holy Spirit' — this could reflect an older layer where the governing entity was named differently, or editorial harmonization with Christian pneumatology. The content remains purely cosmic: entity → body → authority → transformation. Sin is personified ('the will of Sin, the beginning and the end').
- ¶29: Clear exhortation closure. 'About this I tell you my brothers, my loved ones and my limbs' — first-person address to the community with affective vocatives. Imperative commands: 'make yourselves secure,' 'be watchful people,' 'be you prepared.' Eschatological promise: 'your rest will come about.' The content repurposes the body-orifice teaching for moral application: guard your senses against Sin. ## Chapter 57
### Concerning the Generation of Adam

*The oldest teaching layer is the five-fold powers succession (year, month, day, hour, moment) in ¶3-6 — a systematic enumerated teaching mapping cosmic temporal authorities to generational lifespan, classic substrate. The problem statement (¶2) with its zodiac observation is also clearly old. The second half introduces a complementary light-withdrawal explanation (¶7) and a birth-mechanism teaching (¶10) whose substrate status is less certain — the editorial fatigue score (0.74) and substrate vocabulary dropping to 0.00 in the second half suggest editorial extension. Gardner confirms the two-section structure: powers explanation (144.15-146.8) followed by a 'more immediate lesson' (146.8-147.17). The chapter's low teaching purity score (0.133) reflects the absence of canonical substrate markers (zoomorphic forms, metals) rather than absence of old teaching — the five-fold powers nomenclature is differently structured.*

about Adam, the first man. When he was fashioned, how did they sculpt him? Or, how did they beget him? Rather, is his begetting like the begetting that is brought forth today, amongst humankind; or not? Does a distinction exist between his birth and the one that they bring forth today?

Now, no[te] that Adam is mighty in his stature, and great is his size. Even his years are many! He spent many years living in the world. And he is not the only one we have found to be mighty; but all the others who were begotten in his generation, many are their years too. Still, these people who are born today, their stature has been diminished in power; even their years are lessened. Also, why is the birth today, of [the]y who are born, altered compared with that of these first [ ... ] place to place? See, even the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions. Why now have the age and the years of these last ones diminished, and he has also become smaller, compared to these ancient ones who belong to these first generations?

They increased, have turned, and diminished; because there are five types of authorities and leaders appointed in the sphere of zodiacal signs, and the heavens above it. They have names they are ca[lled] by: the first name is the year; the sec[on]d is the month; the third is the day; the fou[rth] is the hour; the fifth is the moment. Now, these five positions, and these five houses, exist in the sphere and the heavens. These places have five powers, and they are made masters over them. The master of the year exists; the master of the month exists; the master of the day exists; the master of the hour exists; the master of the moment exists. In turn one is above his companion; and in turn the one above is made the master over the one below him.

So, mankind is begotten, and [the ani]mals. They are born in these powers. And, thus, these powers have received authority since the beginning of creation till the end of the world. Now, in the beginning of the generations the powers who were masters over the years reigned. Therefore, because of this, the offspring born in their generations and their lifetime: their years were found to be greater. They attain a long life span in the generation of Adam, and [tha]t of Sethel his son, and that of the ones who came after him.

After they ceased to reign, namely the powers who are masters over the years; the powers who are masters over the months themselves reigned. Like the months, for they are less than the years; again this [is] also the case with the offspring w[ho were] born in them. Their life span is less [than the life span of the ones born] in the generation of the powers who master the years!

When the authority of the powers who master the months was completed; the powers of the days received authority after them. Therefore, like the days, for they are less than the months, this also is the case with the offspring who were born in them. Their life span is less than the life span of the ones born in the powers who are masters over the months. Again, even so the offspring who were born in the hours, and of the moments. Their {conduit} is less than that of the months and the days.

The life span of the people has drawn in to nothing. Their days have diminished. Their years have become less; because the life and the ligh[t that] was in the world in these first generations was more than that of today. Indeed, because of this have they diminished in their stature. Their life span has become less for them.

By what sort of birth was the entire offspring of mankind made from one man and one woman, Adam and Eve?

For the manner of these first generations is that they are richer in their light and purer in stature. Even their years are greater. Thus, again, even their offspring is much more than the offspring of this tim[e]. They were spending a longer time [ ... ] in [the w]omb of their mother. Afterwards, they [ ... ] a number in a single womb. There are times five were brought forth from a single womb, and times six were brought forth from [a] single womb! There are some lesser than this; again, some greater than this. Indeed, because of this the offspring of Eve multiplied, and of her children. The earth was filled by reason of them alone.

For, they whom they beget today, in these last generations, they stand diminished and maimed. And, indeed, one by one do they bri[ng t]hem [forth] from a single womb. Hardly two, or [l]ess or more. They are even made ugly in their aspect, being diminished in their size, and feeble in their limbs.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The question defines the teaching's problem structure: why does Adam's generation differ from the present? 'Today' here functions as a cosmic-temporal reference (era of moment-powers) rather than editorial present anchor. The question about sculpting/begetting Adam is substantive — it sets up the cosmological mechanism the five-fold teaching will explain.
- ¶2: This is the extended problem statement — cosmological observation that the zodiac (cosmic structure) hasn't changed, yet generations have diminished. The observation 'the stars and signs of the zodiac continue in their positions' is diagnostic substrate: it establishes a cosmic paradox that requires the five-fold powers teaching to resolve. The 'today' references are within the cosmic-temporal comparison structure, not editorial application. No dialogue frame markers present.
- ¶3: This is the heart of the chapter's substrate: a five-fold enumeration of cosmic temporal authorities. The systematic structure — five types, five names, five positions, five houses, five masters, hierarchical ordering — is classic oldest-layer enumerated teaching. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: sphere → positions → powers → mastery hierarchy. The enumeration integrity is pristine.
- ¶4: Continues the five-fold teaching — applies the hierarchy to generation. The year-powers reign first, producing the longest-lived generations. Adam and Sethel are cosmic-historical figures within the system (not contemporary references). The span 'from the beginning of creation till the end of the world' maps the entire cosmic cycle. Pure process description — impersonal, systematic.
- ¶5: Second iteration of the succession teaching: year-powers → month-powers. The correspondence is systematic: as months are to years, so are month-generation lifespans to year-generation lifespans. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic temporal system. Contains lacunae restoration in brackets.
- ¶6: Completes the five-fold succession: months → days, then compresses hours and moments into a summary. The {conduit} term is interesting — could be a scribal substitution or a cosmological technical term for the channel of life/light through which generations receive their measure. The five-fold teaching is now structurally complete: year > month > day > hour > moment, each producing proportionally diminished generations.
- ¶7: This introduces a second causal explanation: light withdrawal from the world. The concept is within the cosmic system (progressive extraction of light), but it's presented in application voice after the complete five-fold teaching. The editorial fatigue data shows substrate dropping to 0.00 in the second half. The light-withdrawal explanation may be complementary substrate (describing the same process from a different angle) or editorial extension applying the teaching. Genuine uncertainty here — flagged.
- ¶8: Pure dialogue frame — the catechumen recaps what he was told. No new teaching content. 'Once again speaks this Babylonian catechumen to our enlightener: I entreat you, my master!' is formulaic. The rest summarizes ¶3-7. High dialogue_frame score (12.5) confirms. This is editorial machinery creating a transition to the next question.
- ¶9: Substantive question about the mechanism of human propagation from Adam and Eve. This defines the second teaching topic — how one pair populated the earth. The question reveals the teaching structure and belongs to the oldest layer per the Persian pedagogical tradition principle.
- ¶10: The specific numerical detail (5-6 per womb) feels like old teaching — editors don't typically invent such precise cosmological mechanism. The correspondence: greater light → longer gestation → more offspring per womb → rapid population from one pair. Both sides map within the cosmic system (light quantity → biological mechanism). However, this falls in the chapter's second half where editorial fatigue is strong. Genuine uncertainty about whether this is pre-compilation substrate or Mani's own teaching elaboration. Lacunae present.
- ¶11: The birth-count contrast (one per womb vs. 5-6) completes the teaching structure from ¶10. The physical diminishment (smaller, uglier, feebler) maps the cosmic process of light withdrawal onto bodily form — still within the cosmic system. But 'Their teachings and considerations are full of wickedness' crosses from physical description to moral commentary on contemporary humanity — the mapping leaves the cosmic system. 'Today' is embedded in the substrate sentence and cannot be cleanly removed without paraphrase.
- ¶12: Pure closing formula — catechumen thanks the enlightener. 'When that catechumen had listened... he paid homage and gave glory' is standard session-closing frame. 'I thank you, my master, for you have satisfied my heart' is devotional closure. Institutional vocabulary (catechumen, 6.82) confirms editorial layer. No teaching content.

## Chapter 58
### The Four Powers that grieve

*A short chapter with a clear two-layer structure. The substrate teaching (¶2-3) describes two cosmic powers—Mother of Life and First Man—grieving for their children/sons who are bound in Darkness and assaulted by enemies. This is pure cosmic-to-cosmic mapping: cosmic parent → cosmic offspring → cosmic bondage/conflict. The editorial layer (¶4-5) extends the enumeration pattern to Mani himself as 'the third one who grieves,' applying the cosmic grief template to his church community and unredeemed souls. The editorial seam at ¶4 is clean: the substrate enumeration ('The first...', 'The second...') is complete at two cosmic powers, and 'The third one who grieves am I' is Mani's self-insertion that extends the pattern to institutional content. Gardner notes the title promises four powers but only three appear, suggesting possible textual loss or compositional irregularity. The substrate portion is intact and coherent as a teaching about cosmic parental grief.*

The first who grieves is the first Mother of Life. She is sad because of her children who are set in affliction, for they were conjoined with the Darkness and the [poi]son. They have been bound with the entire ruling-power. For what was she grieving, [wa]tching over them taking these afflictions? Whenever she might [s]ee them and how suffering is brought upon them, she shall grieve and be unhappy on account of them.

[The s]econd one who is troubled of heart is the First Man, whose [great]ness and kingdom is established in the li[ght] ship [...]. And he too is troubled of heart because of his five sons who are set in the midst of danger. For any time he may see that they are given trouble, always he might hear the sound of their weeping as they cry out because of the blows and wounds of the enemy who assaults them daily; and as he sees them like this constantly, he shall g[ri]eve and mourn on account of his [s]o[n]s.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening ('Once again the enlightener speaks') followed by Mani's first-person self-identification as one of three cosmic grieving powers. While 'three powers that are heart-broken, who have come from the Father' gestures at cosmological content, it is entirely embedded within Mani's apostolic self-insertion ('I am one of they that grieve') and cannot be cleanly extracted without the first-person claim. This paragraph is the editorial hinge that allows extending a two-power substrate teaching to include Mani as a third cosmic griever.
- ¶2: Pure substrate. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: Mother of Life (cosmic parent) → her children (light elements) → conjoined with Darkness and poison (cosmic bondage) → bound with the ruling-power (cosmic hierarchy). Enumeration marker 'The first who grieves' is structural. High substrate score (13.51). The rhetorical question 'For what was she grieving...' is a teaching device within the substrate pedagogical tradition, not an editorial addition.
- ¶3: Pure substrate. First Man (cosmic being) in the light ship (cosmic geography) grieving for his five sons (five light elements) attacked by the enemy (forces of Darkness). Every mapping stays cosmic-to-cosmic. Enumeration marker 'The second one who is troubled' continues the teaching structure. The 'five sons' is a key substrate signature—the five-element system. The phrase 'the enemy who assaults them daily' describes ongoing cosmic conflict, not contemporary persecution. Low substrate vocabulary score (2.02) despite clearly substrate content—likely because the passage uses narrative/emotional language rather than technical terminology.
- ¶4: Editorial seam. 'The third one who grieves am I' mimics the substrate enumeration pattern ('The first...', 'The second...') but applies it to Mani and his institutional church. 'The holy church that I chose,' 'slavery of the authoritarians,' 'tribulation and persecution'—all reach outside the cosmic system into Mani's contemporary world. This is the classic pattern-extension seam: the substrate two-fold enumeration (Mother of Life, First Man) was complete, and the editor extended it to include Mani as a third griever for institutional purposes. The entire paragraph is one editorial act—not mixed.
- ¶5: Institutional extension. Mani's first-person pastoral concern for 'souls who have refused hope.' References to gehenna, repentance, and 'the right hand of peace and mercy that I brought from the Father' are all apostolic-missionary language. Institutional vocabulary score is highest in the chapter (9.33). This is the 'fourth grief' that the title may reference—but it is entirely within Mani's apostolic voice, not cosmic-to-cosmic mapping.

## Chapter 59
### The Chapter of the Elements that wept

*The oldest teaching layer is dominant. Paragraphs 2-7 form a complete, self-contained four-fold cosmological teaching about the weeping of the light elements (garments/sons of the First Man) at four stages of the cosmic drama. The enumeration structure (first, second, third, fourth) is systematic and internally complete. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: cosmic beings weep at cosmic events. The teaching cycle closes naturally at P7 with the explanation of why the fourth weeping is worst. P8 then bridges to apostolic mission with 'Therefore, due to' — a classic editorial seam extending the completed teaching to justify apostolic suffering. P9 is a brief closing exhortation. No editorial bridge or institutional vocabulary intrudes into the core teaching sequence. No NT citations detected. Negative editorial fatigue score (-0.52) is consistent with the editorial material being brief and confined to the end.*

The garments, the sons of the First Man, wept bitterly three times. Then they were silent. There is a fourth time of weeping, for they will weep at the end. And then their crying can stop, and they will never again weep from this time on.

Now, the first time of their weeping is when they had seen the darkness, the enemy being about to burst out against them. They understood everything that would happen to them. They were aware of the one that would rise and come upon them, but by necessity they hastened to him and fought with him. They mastered him, they detained him. They bound him with their body and their limbs, they overcame his death and his fire and his darkness.

[The] second time that they wept is [when the]ir father, the First Man, [ascend]ed from the [abyss] of darkness and left them behind; that is the time when he stripped [them off] and left them below. Indeed, when th[ey] s[aw] how the First Man had risen out of the abyss and [l]eft them behind, and that they were abandoned in the enmit[y, th]ey wept and were sore of heart. Still, what could they do, wishing [ ... / ... ] their heart to every affliction. They [remained b]ehind and grew [s]ick under the pressure of the weighty burden of all the works, until the end time.

[The] third time that they wept is when the Living Spirit stripped off the three garments of activity, placing them beneath all things. He gave them a sign to lift their heads and rise to the heights from that place; and to pass and journey through all the earths until they should reach the land of tranquilli[t]y and peace, the place of rest prepared for them. So, when they had seen him, that he had separated himself from them, while they themselves remained behind after him and had not ascended; at that moment when they stayed behind these three garments wept. After their crying, they turned their [hear]t to all the heights that were concealed from them, and were of one mind. For [they would] soar up and come to the heights; and pass over all things until the time that was ordained for them all [ ... ] at the end of the worlds.

[The f]ourth time when they will weep is when the [S]tatue will be taken up on the last day, and they will weep [for] the souls of the liars and blasphemers; for they may give [ ... ] because their limbs have been severed [ ... ] of the darkness. And also [th]ose souls, when the Statue will go up and th]ey are left alo[n]e, they will weep in that they will rema[in] behind in affliction for ever. For they wi[ll be cut off] and separated from the Last Statue. An[d it is] a necessity to take these souls who are ready for loss as retribution for the deeds that they have done. They go i[n to] this [darkne]ss and are bound with the Darkness; just as they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it. At that very moment, when the Last Statue rises up, the[y wi]ll weep. And then they will scream out loud because they will be severed from the company of this great Statue. And they remain behind for ever. This great weeping [ ... ] is terrible, it occurs in front of the souls and [ ... ] who are ready for loss in accordance with their deeds. And they [will] weep with that crying, they will never be silent; because they will not attain rest from this time on.

So, they too are the garments of these elements. This fourth time of weeping is heart-rending for them. It is much worse for them than these other three times of weeping before. This fourth time the weeping is not for themselves. Rather they weep and are heartsore for these soul[s] who will be cut off from peace, because rest will not come about for them, to allow them to rest from torment for ever.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic dialogue frame. 'Once again he speaks to his disciples' — no cosmological content, no substantive question. Bridge connective flag fires but this is simply the standard frame formula.
- ¶2: Overview of the four-fold teaching structure. Introduces the systematic enumeration: three past weepings plus a fourth eschatological one. 'The garments, the sons of the First Man' — cosmological entities identified by their cosmic role. The entire paragraph sets up the degree structure. Impersonal, systematic voice.
- ¶3: First enumeration unit. Describes the cosmic event: the elements' first encounter with Darkness. 'They bound him with their body and their limbs' — soul-body binding formula applied to the cosmic combat. Both sides internal: light elements vs. Darkness. 'his death and his fire and his darkness' maps qualities of the enemy realm. Process-oriented voice describing what happened within the cosmic system.
- ¶4: Second enumeration unit. Cosmic geography: the First Man ascending from the abyss, the elements remaining below in mixture. 'the weighty burden of all the works' describes the cosmic condition of entrapment. Lacunae present from manuscript damage. All entities and events are cosmic-internal.
- ¶5: Third enumeration unit. The Living Spirit, the three garments of activity (wind, water, fire per ch. 30) — cosmic entities acting within the cosmic system. 'all the earths' and 'the land of tranquillity and peace' are cosmic geography. The entire passage describes the process of ascent through cosmic levels — a systematic emanation/return teaching. 'the time that was ordained for them' — cosmic teleology. The longest substrate paragraph reflects the complexity of this stage.
- ¶6: Fourth enumeration unit — eschatological. The Last Statue is a cosmic entity; souls 'bound with Darkness' uses the binding formula. 'liars and blasphemers' and 'in accordance with their deeds' are moral categories but function as cosmological designations for souls that chose Darkness — the cosmic mechanism of attachment ('they desired it and loved it, and placed their treasure with it'). Both sides remain within the cosmic system: the Statue ascends, lost souls are bound into Darkness. No contemporary institutions or practices are identified. The 'treasure' language describes cosmic attachment, not contemporary behavior.
- ¶7: Explanatory conclusion to the four-fold cycle. Explains why the fourth is worst — the elements weep not for themselves but for the permanently lost. This closes the teaching structure: overview (P2), first (P3), second (P4), third (P5), fourth (P6), explanation of the fourth (P7). The teaching cycle is complete and self-contained at this point. 'cut off from peace' and 'rest from torment' are cosmic-eschatological conditions, not contemporary applications.
- ¶8: Clear editorial seam. 'Therefore, due to' — bridge connective flagged by NLP pipeline. The teaching about the fourth weeping (cosmic drama) is now APPLIED to justify apostolic mission: 'all the apostles and the fathers, the revealers of the good and the true prophets.' These are institutional/historical categories — one side of the mapping has left the cosmic system and entered the editors' world of apostolic succession. 'the second death' retains cosmic vocabulary but serves the editorial argument. 'Not a single one of all the apostles wished to receive his reward on earth' — hagiographic characterization of apostolic behavior. 'being crucified in their body' echoes Christological/apostolic narrative. This paragraph would be removed without loss to the cosmic teaching, which closed at P7.
- ¶9: Closing admonition addressed to the audience. 'the wise person must constantly be attentive to this lesson' — direct injunction to behavior. 'the gods and the apostles' mixes cosmic ('gods') with institutional ('apostles'). Fragmentary (lacuna at start). Classic devotional closure appended after editorial bridge. Position at chapter end is typical for exhortation layer.

## Chapter 60
### Concerning the Four Fathers; what they are like.

*A remarkably pure body-cosmos correspondence teaching with minimal editorial overlay. The four-fold mapping (directive→Father, eyes→light ships, body→living soul, tongue→Jesus the Splendour) is systematic and complete. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system throughout. The only editorial material is the dialogue frame opening (¶1 attribution) and closing (¶9). The negative editorial fatigue score confirms substrate dominance increasing toward the chapter's end. 'Jesus the Splendour' functions as a cosmic emanation entity (the revealer principle) rather than a historical figure; the name may overlay an older cosmic revealer figure from the Persian tradition. The teaching structure itself—mapping four cosmic powers to four body faculties—is characteristic of the oldest correspondential substrate.*

Just as the corporeal body has limbs that are hidden, so it also has others that are visible. They can be seen upon it, they can be grasped and receive blows and wounds, whilst others can neither be grasped nor receive blows.

So it is also with this entire greatness, as we resemble it. Some of it is hidden, whilst some of it is visible; some of it is far away from the blows and suffering, whilst some of it receives blows and wounds.

Now, thus the directive of the body that lives within, hidden in the storehouses inside the body and unseen. So it is also how the Father, the God of truth, exists; as he too is hidden in his kingdom, not displayed before this outer desolation.

Thus, too, the body has two eyes that are visible and seen, and are even the body's source of illumination; so there are also these two light ships that shine and are seen in the entire universe and the whole of creation. The light has never been hidden, just like the two eyes of the body.

The living soul too is like this; it is established in apparent silence. In its apparent silence it is grasped and receives blows from these five fleshes, which destroy (the soul) and strike it. It can be likened to the mystery of the corporeal body, as it can be grasped and mastered, can receive blows and wounds.

⟨p.152⟩ Also, thus this tongue that speaks, proclaims, reprimands and reveals about everything happening in this body, inside and outside, also the considerations and bodily knowledges. The tongue, which is outside, makes it manifest. It also proclaims about the honour and shame of the body. And it speaks, unveiling the pain of the body that is sick from its sores.

This is also what Jesus the Splendour is like, who shall be sent from the greatness. He manifests and reveals about everything, both the external and the internal, both what is above and what is below. Be it about the outer aeons that are hidden and not displayed, or about the ships of light that are seen: he is the one who unveils about them. He is also the one who reveals about their glory, and the greatness that dwells in them.

And regarding this beating and wounding of the living soul, Jesus is the one who reveals it. He also preaches about (the soul) and its peace. He also reveals about its cleansing and healing.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The attribution formula 'Once again the apostle speaks to his disciples' is standard dialogue frame. The teaching that follows sets up the foundational body-analogy (hidden/visible, graspable/non-graspable) that structures the entire four-fold correspondence. The body analogy itself is substrate—it defines the two axes (visibility and vulnerability) that will map the four cosmic fathers.
- ¶2: Pivots from body to cosmic system ('this entire greatness'). 'As we resemble it' is a classical microcosm-macrocosm statement—the body IS the cosmic system in miniature. This is the correspondential thesis: the body's structure maps onto the cosmic structure. Both sides remain within the system. No editorial vocabulary detected.
- ¶3: First correspondence: directive faculty → Father. 'Storehouses inside the body' uses the correspondential 'storehouses' vocabulary found elsewhere in the substrate. 'Outer desolation' is a cosmic-geographical term for the realm of mixture. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic. The 'Now, thus' opening is a teaching connector, not an editorial bridge—it continues the systematic enumeration.
- ¶4: Second correspondence: two eyes → two light ships (sun and moon). 'Light ships' is systematic cosmic vocabulary. The mapping is precise: eyes illuminate the body / light ships illuminate the universe. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. The substrate vocabulary score (7.14) correctly flags 'light ships' as substrate terminology.
- ¶5: Third correspondence: body → living soul. 'Five fleshes' is a key substrate term for the five material kinds. 'Apparent silence' describes the soul's cosmic condition—present but passive, vulnerable. The substrate score (10.53) is the highest in the chapter, correctly reflecting 'living soul' and 'five fleshes' as diagnostic substrate vocabulary.
- ¶6: Body-side of the fourth correspondence: the tongue's function. This paragraph describes WHAT the tongue is within the body—the faculty that reveals hidden and manifest conditions. The cosmic mapping (tongue → Jesus the Splendour) follows in ¶7. The two-paragraph structure (body-side, then cosmic-side) is characteristic of systematic correspondential exposition. Manuscript page marker ⟨p.152⟩ preserved.
- ¶7: Cosmic-side of the fourth correspondence: tongue → Jesus the Splendour. 'Who shall be sent from the greatness' is emanation language—how a cosmic entity proceeds from a higher degree. 'Outer aeons,' 'ships of light,' 'the greatness' are all cosmic-system vocabulary. Both sides of the mapping stay within the system. 'Jesus the Splendour' functions here as a cosmic emanation entity (the revealer principle), not a historical figure; the name may overlay an older cosmic revealer from the Persian tradition. The substrate score (2.44) is low because the vocabulary is distributed rather than concentrated in diagnostic terms, but the content is entirely cosmic-correspondential.
- ¶8: Completes the fourth correspondence by linking the tongue/Jesus mapping back to the soul/body mapping: as the tongue reveals the body's sickness, so Jesus reveals the soul's suffering and healing. 'Living soul' is substrate vocabulary. 'Cleansing and healing' are cosmic-process terms describing the soul's restoration within the system. Both sides remain within the cosmic architecture.
- ¶9: 'Behold, I have shown to you' is a first-person speaker-to-audience closure formula. The content that follows ('the power of revelation of these four fathers, and how each one of them has his appointed work, whether in secret or visible') is a back-reference summary of the teaching rather than new correspondential content. The four-fold teaching is already complete in ¶3-8. This paragraph wraps up the pedagogical session—a dialogue frame closure. The bridge connective flag correctly identifies this as non-substrate.
