## Chapter 61
### Concerning the Garment of the Waters: how great is its Measure.

*This chapter is dominated by a biographical narrative frame (Mani visiting King Shapur, standing on the Tigris quay during flood). The oldest teaching layer — about the First Man assuming elemental garments, the cosmic rulers consuming vast waters, the binding of the mighty giants, and the eschatological dissolution of the material universe — is embedded within this narrative but extractable. The substrate teaching about the measure of the garment of waters is genuine cosmological-correspondential material about cosmic scale and elemental combat. The Tigris flood serves as a pedagogical occasion that the compilers used to frame this teaching. Gardner's note that the chapter's value lies in 'historical reference' confirms that the narrative frame is the editors' contribution. Paragraph 9 is the purest substrate passage. The chapter lacks the systematic zoomorphic/metal/taste mappings seen in Ch. 6 but preserves cosmic process vocabulary (elements, garments, rulers, binding, dissolution).*

How great is the power of the garment of the waters? How far shall [it extend]? The source from which all these waters burst forth, how great will it be?

[At] the time when the First Man assumed the elements, and stood [fi]rm against the first enmity that had come about in the Darkness, he defied these great and powerful rulers. They are immeasurable, greater than the extent or size of thousands and ten thousands of parasangs. At the start, when he spread the fight against them, he gave them fire and wind; and afterwards he gave the garment of the waters to the rulers of death. So they, the rulers, drank in and consumed those waters. Many [waters] poured forth from the garment of waters [...] the rivers flow. The mouth of the rulers [...] those [...], the warriors of destruction [...] laboured and brought them forth.

There were seven (rivers) flowing in to the mouth of each one of them, as they shall not [...] to a single stomach, to their fill; because in turn the descent to its stomach shall burn them with its fire and heat. Those rulers were like the [...] of [...]; they were not emptied; rain and [...] their existent days.

The measure of that river was expanded seven times, as they flow [...] of the body of the [...] waters [...] now.

[...] is how great a struggle, since it has [...] these creatures of destruction, the mighty giants. Behold, they are entwined, bound, and [...] in every place. They are hidden, not revealed before the eyes of every flesh that exists on the land of death [...]. These rulers [...] revealed. The entire universe of flesh will be destroyed and dissolved [...]; all pass away [...]. They will be destroyed with their angry voice, which is mightier than the thunder.

this small stream is reckoned [...] before the vastness of these waters. For only one river from thousands of rivers of water, from the secrets that are not revealed, is the river Tigris upon the earth.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure narrative frame: references King Shapur (historical proper noun), sets the biographical scene. dialogue_frame score 16.13 confirms formulaic framing. No cosmological content whatsoever.
- ¶2: Extended narrative scene-setting: the Tigris flooding, the month of Pharmouthi, the city being submerged. All register scores are zero — this is purely biographical description establishing the occasion for the teaching. Heavily lacunose but clearly narrative throughout. The specific temporal marker (month of Pharmouthi) and geographical detail (gates of the city, market place) anchor this firmly in the editorial biographical layer.
- ¶3: Narrative scene-setting identifying the participants. institutional=10.2 fires on 'leaders of his church' — institutional vocabulary embedded in the dialogue frame. The phrase identifies Mani's disciples in terms of the organized Manichaean community structure. Primary function remains frame (setting up the dialogue participants), with institutional coloring.
- ¶4: The substantive cosmological question defines the teaching topic: the power and measure of the garment of the waters, and the nature of the source. 'The garment of the waters' is substrate elemental terminology. The question structure reveals the original teaching outline. The Tigris-specific observations are the editorial frame wrapping the question.
- ¶5: Transitional dialogue attribution: 'Then the apostle says to him' + rhetorical setup ('Why are you astonished... listen to what I am about to tell you'). dialogue_frame=16.0. The phrase 'th[e r]iv[er] that came into being during the first time' is a thin cosmological teaser but functions as transition, not teaching. The paragraph exists entirely to connect the disciple's question to Mani's response.
- ¶6: This is the main teaching paragraph. substrate=7.03 confirms cosmological vocabulary. The substrate content describes: (1) the First Man assuming elemental garments, (2) the first enmity in the Darkness, (3) the immeasurable scale of the rulers, (4) the sequential deployment of elements (fire, wind, then water), (5) the rulers consuming the waters. This is cosmic process description — both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'Darkness' capitalized as cosmic agent. The citation to 'the Great Gospel' (institutional=3.12) is the only editorial intrusion — a self-referential link typical of the Manichaean editorial layer.
- ¶7: Substrate teaching about the cosmic rulers consuming the seven rivers: the body-process description (mouth, stomach, fire/heat in the descent) is correspondential — mapping cosmic consumption through bodily metaphor. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. The Tigris comparison ('like the great river Tigris [that you] see') bridges to the narrative frame and was likely added when this teaching was embedded in the biographical episode. Heavily lacunose in the final clauses.
- ¶8: Very heavily damaged paragraph. dialogue_frame=5.97, institutional=2.99. The dialogue attribution and 'Marvel at' exhortation are clearly later framing. The teaching fragment about the sevenfold measure of the river is substrate (cosmic scale measurement). 'That elect one' is institutional vocabulary (Manichaean ecclesiastical term). The heavy lacunae make confident extraction difficult — I have extracted only the clearly identifiable teaching fragment.
- ¶9: The purest substrate passage in the chapter. substrate=5.06, all other scores zero. Describes cosmic processes impersonally: (1) the binding of the creatures of destruction / mighty giants, (2) their concealment from material beings, (3) the eschatological dissolution of the material universe, (4) destruction by a voice mightier than thunder. Every mapping stays within the cosmic system: 'land of death' is cosmic designation for the material world, 'every flesh' is a cosmic category, 'their angry voice mightier than thunder' is correspondential description. 'Behold' is a pedagogical presentation marker found in ancient teaching voice. No editorial intrusions detected.
- ¶10: This paragraph teaches about cosmic scale through comparison: the Tigris is one river from thousands of cosmic rivers 'from the secrets that are not revealed.' The scale teaching (cosmic waters vastly exceed observable waters) is substrate — it's the answer to the chapter's question about the measure of the garment of waters. 'The secrets that are not revealed' is substrate epistemological vocabulary. The Tigris reference, while anchored to the narrative frame, functions here as the teaching's pedagogical illustration of the correspondence between observable and cosmic waters.
- ¶11: Exhortatory closure: 'See [...] and be amazed', 'Marvel at', 'understand how great' — imperative voice directing the audience to wonder. References substrate concepts (measure of the elements of waters, stature of the Man who bore them, the First Man assuming the garment) but does not teach them — instead points back to the preceding teaching with devotional emphasis. The cosmological references serve the exhortation rather than constituting independent teaching. substrate=3.39 fires on the references to First Man and elements but the paragraph's function is exhortation.
- ¶12: Formulaic devotional closure by the disciples: 'We revere you, our master, and we give thanks.' dialogue_frame=15.38. Standard session-closing formula. 'All the greatnesses that are hidden from every person' echoes ¶9's 'hidden, not revealed' and ¶10's 'secrets that are not revealed' — the compilers are tying the frame closure back to the substrate vocabulary, but the function is purely frame/devotional.

## Chapter 62
### Concerning the Three Rocks

*A compact three-fold cosmological teaching about modes of absolute stability ('rocks') established in the cosmic structure. The oldest teaching layer is dominant: a thesis paragraph, three enumerated items, and a summary recapitulation. The only significant editorial intrusion is in ¶5, where the third rock's cosmic placement has been replaced with 'the holy church' — an institutional substitution visible by comparison with ¶3's cosmic location ('this zone') and ¶6's summary ('three great places of these selfsame worlds'). The chapter otherwise preserves clean substrate with systematic emanation and placement patterns.*

Three great rocks, which exist, were appointed by the greatness. They were set in these outer worlds.

So, the first rock is the [Pillar] of Glory, the Perfect Man, which was summoned [by the] glorious Ambassador. He set it in this zone; [ ... he] brought forth its base (?) from the earth to heaven. It bore up under the [en]tire uni[verse], and became first of all the weight-carriers in [it]s powers. In its firmness it has been set upright, and has made fast every [t]hing above and below.

[The] second great and glorious rock is the new earth of light, which was set upon the earth of Darkness. It has squashed the fastenings of the five storehouses of death. It has established firmness, with great firmness, over and beyond the earth of Darkness. It has flattened, crushed and fixed the root of death.

[The] third rock is the great thought, which was summoned from Jesus, the glorious one. It has stood firm, with a true fixity, for ever.

[These] are the three great rocks, they that have come forth in great confidence, in three great and honoured places. They were appointed and distributed in three great places of these [self]same worlds. They were made fast according to the will of the greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic dialogue frame: 'Once again he speaks to his disciples.' No cosmological content whatsoever.
- ¶2: Thesis statement introducing the teaching. 'The greatness' is cosmic agency; 'these outer worlds' is cosmic geography. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. Sets up a three-fold enumeration.
- ¶3: First enumeration item. Pillar of Glory / Perfect Man — cosmic entity. Summoned by the Ambassador — cosmic emanation. 'This zone' — cosmic location. Function described entirely within the cosmic architecture (bearing up the universe, weight-carriers, firmness above and below). Classic substrate pattern: entity → summoner → location → function. Lacunae preserved.
- ¶4: Second enumeration item. 'New earth of light' set upon 'earth of Darkness' — cosmic geography. 'Five storehouses of death' — systematic five-fold cosmological nomenclature (the five dark realms). 'Root of death' — cosmic entity. All mappings cosmos→cosmos. The substrate vocabulary score (16.36) is the highest in the chapter.
- ¶5: 'Summoned from Jesus, the glorious one' parallels 'summoned by the glorious Ambassador' in ¶3 — this is the cosmic emanation pattern (Jesus the Splendor as cosmic figure, not historical Jesus). The institutional vocabulary score (16.28) fires only here. 'It has stood firm, with a true fixity, for ever' describes the rock's quality (paralleling firmness language in ¶3 and ¶4) and is retained as substrate. The institutional insertion ('holy church') breaks the cosmic-location pattern established by the first two rocks.
- ¶6: Summary recapitulation closing the enumeration. 'Three great places of these selfsame worlds' — cosmic geography that implicitly confirms all three rocks belong to the cosmic architecture. 'The will of the greatness' — cosmic agency. Both sides within the system. This summary actually provides evidence that the original third rock had a cosmic location, not an institutional one.

## Chapter 63
### Concerning Love

*Short binary teaching (Love/Hate) with a clear editorial insertion at ¶3. The substrate teaching maps Love → Father of Greatness and Hate → first death/land of Darkness. The editor extends the Love mapping to the holy church and apostle, mimicking the 'single body' pattern from ¶2 and adding an NT citation (John 15:13). The Hate sequence (¶4-5) remains substantially intact as cosmological substrate, with ¶5's 'today' language describing the ongoing cosmic middle-time rather than pointing at specific contemporary institutions. The seam flag on ¶4 is a false positive (zero institutional vocabulary, purely cosmological content). The derivation chain at the end of ¶5 ('the hated from the hated; the envy from the envy') is a distinctive substrate formula mapping ontological origin.*

What is love? [...][...] each [...], calling it 'love'.

Love is the Father of Greatness, who dwells in his glorious land. The entirety of the divine has been revealed [there]in! These two are a single living body, the Father and his love; because he has given himself for every thing, as he exists in his aeons. Indeed, due to this, they called the Father, the Lord of the totality, 'love'. For he gave the victory to his aeons and his limbs.

What, conversely, is hate? Hate is the first death that welled up. It revealed the land of Darkness. This one called 'hate', because it has ruined all its perdition's offspring; it sinned even against a power foreign to it, and ruined it. Due to this they shall call it 'hated'; for it has caused destruction, both wounding in flesh, and what is not its own.

Today again it causes wounding and destruction in all flesh. It kills them all with diverse deaths; these that are displayed today in every thing, in every type. Even the Cross of Light, the living soul, likewise too it ruins and kills them; as it did at the start. Indeed, due to this have they called it 'hated', this one from the hated; the envy from the envy; the devil from the devil; the bad that came about from the bad.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue frame formula wraps a substantive cosmological question ('What is love?') that defines the teaching topic. The question belongs to the teaching structure. Heavy lacunae make most content unrecoverable.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological-correspondential mapping: Love → Father of Greatness → his land → his aeons. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'These two are a single living body' is a body-unity formula mapping divine quality to divine being. 'They called' uses the impersonal naming formula characteristic of the substrate teaching voice. No contemporary references or institutional vocabulary.
- ¶3: Clear editorial extension of the ¶2 substrate pattern to the Manichaean church. The editor mimics the 'single body' formula ('the Mind and the church, a single body') and applies it to the apostle and church. Contains NT citation (John 15:13: 'There is no love greater than this...'). The phrase 'the church here' and 'the apostle too shall give his self for his church' locate the teaching in institutional present. Institutional score of 16.0 confirms. This is precisely the pattern-mimicry editorial seam: the cosmic teaching about Love/Father was complete at ¶2, and the editor added one more iteration applying it to the church.
- ¶4: The seam flag is a false positive — institutional vocabulary is 0.0 and the content is purely cosmological. The teaching maps Hate → first death → land of Darkness. 'A power foreign to it' refers to the Light attacked by Darkness — core cosmological narrative. 'They shall call it' uses the impersonal naming formula. The binary structure (Love ¶2 / Hate ¶4) is the teaching architecture. 'Wounding in flesh, and what is not its own' describes the cosmic aggression of Darkness against the foreign Light substance.
- ¶5: The 'today' language describes the cosmic middle-time (the epoch of mixture), NOT specific contemporary institutions. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system: Hate → wounding/destruction → 'all flesh', 'every thing', 'every type' — these are cosmic generalities, not institutional identifications. Compare with editorial bridge markers ('reigns today in the principalities') which point to specific institutions. 'As it did at the start' explicitly links present cosmic process to the primordial attack — this is the first-time/middle-time temporal structure of the cosmological teaching. 'Cross of Light' and 'living soul' are core substrate terms. The derivation chain at the end ('the hated from the hated; the envy from the envy; the devil from the devil; the bad from the bad') is a distinctive substrate formula mapping ontological origin through correspondential derivation. Substrate score 6.17 is the highest in the chapter.

## Chapter 64
### [Concerning Adam]

*This chapter is overwhelmingly oldest-layer substrate. The teaching is highly systematic: a three-fold enumeration of Adam's cosmic preeminence (image, soul-structure, cosmic seal) followed by a seven-fold enumeration of the rulers' purposes in creating Adam. Every mapping stays within the cosmic system — no paragraph reaches into contemporary institutions, social structures, or religious practices. The only non-substrate material is the dialogue frame opening (¶1) and a brief editorial bridge phrase in ¶6. The body-cosmos correspondence (zodiac sealed in Adam), the elemental distribution teaching, and the seal-ring metaphor are all classic substrate features. Negative editorial fatigue score confirms no drift toward later layers. This is one of the purest teaching chapters in the corpus.*

Three great things that are distinct were revealed in Adam the first human. Indeed, due to this, he was found to be better and more outstanding than the conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth.

The first: The image of the exalted one was placed upon him. The creators and moulders of his body sealed him after the shining form of the image that had been displayed to them above. Indeed, due to this, the image of Adam was found to be surpassingly beautiful beyond any conjoined power above and below.

The second thing: Adam was shaped in his structure through the light of the first-born of heaven and [earth ... ] the five sons. Indeed, due to this, his structure was found to differ from the structure of even the other creations; [sin]ce the form of all creations is different. As for Adam, the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements. Therefore, he has intelligence surpassing that of the other creations and beasts.

The third are the teachings and counsels and the seal of all the powers above and below. The creators, who set him in order, gathered them and sealed them in him. Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years. For the seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam. Indeed, due to this, heaven and earth moved because of him; trouble and disorder arose on his account between they that are good and they that are evil. So, the good induce him to life because of their image and shape placed upon him; while the [wi]cked were drawing him to death, so that they would have power through him and they would conceive the kingdom, and through him humiliate the entire universe.

The rulers determined and formed Adam so that they would be masters through him and conceive the kingdom because of seven things.

⟨p.158⟩
The first: He became the enlightener in their creation; instead of the light of the image of the Ambassador who had displayed himself to them above. For they all desired the likeness of the image of the Ambassador. Their heart was drawn after it.

The second: So that they would scoop up the light through him and restrain and obstruct it from its ascent to the heights.

The third: So that because of the image of the Ambassador that is placed upon him the entirety of the divine that is above and below would be humiliated; on account of the likeness of the splendour, of the beauty of his superiority. For he is superior in his appearance to all creatures.

The fourth: He is a seal-ring and a new impression, so that all births who will be begotten might be brought forth after his seal.

The fifth: That might be displayed through him all the skil[ls] of the powers, they above and below, secret and manifest; and that they might determine through him the totality, and every thing be revealed in him.

The sixth: That there would come to be a great protection for them through the image of the exalted one that was placed upon him; for as the heavenly would [ ... ] even rulers; and no evil thing would be done to them on account of Adam.

The seventh: That he might become chief and king and master over all creatures; and they might conceive the kingdom through him.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic attribution: 'Once again he speaks to his disciples, who listen to his words.' No cosmological content. High dialogue_frame score (41.67). Standard compilation-layer framing.
- ¶2: Introduces the three-fold enumeration structure. 'Conjoined powers that are in heaven and on earth' is cosmic vocabulary — mixed/bound beings in the cosmic architecture. Both sides of the description stay within the system. The 'Indeed, due to this' formula recurs through ¶3-5 as a structural causal connector within the teaching.
- ¶3: First enumeration item. Maps the Ambassador's cosmic image → Adam's form. 'Creators and moulders' are the archontic powers — cosmic agents. 'Shining form of the image displayed above' refers to the Ambassador's manifestation in the cosmic narrative. Entirely within the system.
- ¶4: Second enumeration item. Critical substrate feature: 'the formation of his soul fits over the correct distribution of the elements' — this is the body-cosmos correspondence mapping soul-structure to elemental distribution. 'The five sons' references the five-element system. Contains lacunae [earth ... ] and [sin]ce. Despite substrate score of 0.0, the content is unmistakably oldest-layer cosmological teaching.
- ¶5: Third enumeration item. Contains the zodiac-body correspondence: 'Adam and his consort Eve became a dwelling and home for the signs of the zodiac and the stars; and the months, the days and the years.' This is the body-cosmos mapping identified in Ch.69-70 context. 'The seal of the entire universe is stamped upon Adam' — Adam as microcosm. The cosmic struggle between good and evil forces is entirely within the system. Contains lacuna [wi]cked.
- ¶6: Bridge connective flagged by seam detection. However, the content that follows (¶7-13) is entirely cosmological — no institutional vocabulary or contemporary application. The seam flag fires on the connective but the extension stays within the cosmic system. Only the transitional phrase is editorial; the seven-fold enumeration it introduces is substrate.
- ¶7: First of the seven purposes. Maps Adam's role to the Ambassador's cosmic image — the rulers substitute Adam for the divine light-image. 'Enlightener in their creation' means Adam functioned as their light-source, replacing the Ambassador. Both sides cosmic. Manuscript marker ⟨p.158⟩ preserved.
- ¶8: Second purpose. Describes cosmic light-capture mechanism: Adam as vessel through which the rulers restrain light from ascending. 'Scoop up the light' and 'ascent to the heights' are systematic cosmic process vocabulary. Entirely within the system.
- ¶9: Third purpose. The divine is humiliated through Adam bearing the Ambassador's image — cosmic image-theology within the system. 'The entirety of the divine that is above and below' is cosmic geography.
- ¶10: Fourth purpose. Seal-ring/impression metaphor — Adam as template for all subsequent generation. This is the cosmic process by which the form is transmitted through births. Systematic, impersonal.
- ¶11: Fifth purpose. Adam as the microcosm in which all cosmic powers — above and below, secret and manifest — are displayed. 'Determine through him the totality' — Adam as the instrument of cosmic control. Contains lacuna skil[ls].
- ¶12: Sixth purpose. The divine image on Adam protects the dark rulers from heavenly retribution — cosmic shield-function. Contains lacuna [ ... ] disrupting the causal clause. Both sides cosmic.
- ¶13: Seventh and final purpose. Adam as ruler over all creatures — the dark powers' instrument for seizing cosmic sovereignty. 'Conceive the kingdom' is the rulers' cosmic ambition expressed through Adam. Closes the seven-fold enumeration. Both sides internal to the cosmic system.

## Chapter 65
### Concerning the Sun

*Substantial substrate teaching organized in three systematic sections: (1) seven goods of the sun contrasted with seven evils of the night (¶5-11, ¶14-20), (2) five archetypes of the sun (¶23-27), (3) three qualities corresponding to the mystery of the Father (¶30-32). The oldest teaching layer is dominant. Editorial bridges are interspersed at section transitions (¶3, ¶12, ¶21, ¶33) polemicizing against 'the sects' for not recognizing these cosmic mysteries. The chapter closes with a Gospel of Thomas citation (¶36) and classic exhortation/beatitude closure (¶37-38), confirming the editorial fatigue pattern flagged by the computational analysis. Low substrate vocabulary scores throughout reflect the absence of this chapter's key terms (sun, night, light, darkness) from the substrate marker list (which targets zoomorphic/metal/taste correspondences), NOT the absence of substrate content. The 7+7+5+3 enumeration structure is the teaching's skeleton. Gardner/McBride's suggestion of heliolatry native to Egyptian Manichaeism may be true at the compilation level, but the correspondential structure (sun as sign of the light-realm, night as sign of Darkness) belongs to the older substrate layer.*

the greatness of the sun and its divinity: how was it fashioned after the likeness of the first greatness?

it is the gate of life and the vessel of peace, to this great aeon of light [...]. it is the gate of the souls' departure

Every day, would this great light-giver shine its light, it shall do seven good things for them.

The first good thing that it does for them is its light, which it shines upon them. It opens the eyes of all mankind. They see underfoot as they walk by it. It takes away the blindness of the night, which made them blind with its darkness.

The second: When (the sun) is come to the world, with its coming it shall bring calm and peace to the entire world. With its coming it shall also take away from them all the fear and trembling, which their heart is filled with the whole night; for it shall take away the darkness present by its light, and sweep it out. Also, this is how it extinguishes fear by its calm.

The third is its vigil; because when (the sun) comes to the world all sleeping mankind shall arise and awake. And the watchmen too shall cease from watching. The guards who sit at the gates of camps, they break vigil at the watchposts, because of its light that has shone upon them in the world.

⟨p.160⟩ The fourth: It shall nourish, and give strength, taste and scent, to the trees and fruits and vegetables, and all the herbs, and the flowers and grasses, which are upon the entire earth.

The fifth: When (the sun) shines in all the world, the evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts that are filled of wickedness, they shall flee to hiding in their dens.

The sixth: When it shines its light, the wound of any person is eased from pain. Its light shall melt away the evil arts of every doer of wickedness.

The seventh: (The sun) shall display and manifest to the world the sign of the splendour of the aeons of light, from which it has come out; it here in this world being their sign. It brings light to the whole of creation.

it has all wickednesses therein. Every occasion, would the night fall, it shall do seven evils in the world.

The first wickedness that the night shall do in the world is the darkness, with which it shall fill the entire world; because when the sun sinks from the world and draws to it its rays, at that instant the shadow of the night shall spread out over the whole world. The eyes of the people fill with blackness. They neither see nor wonder with their eyes, because of the darkness of the night which shall fill the whole world.

The second evil thing that the night shall do is its fear; because when its shadow looms forth, its fear and its trembling shall possess all of mankind. The entire world shall fill of the night.

The third: When the night falls, all creatures take up revolt. They rebel in their heart, to do wickedness and perdition.

The fourth: The heaviness of sleep shall lay upon them, and they fall asleep; they slumber and doze, and they are like these corpses in the night.

The fifth is its ugliness; again, when the night would fall, the form of the people shall be hidden in the darkness and the ugliness of the night. The beauty and the form of men and women shall not be displayed before one another, because of its darkness and its ugliness, which it shall spread out over all their forms.

The sixth occasion is when the night would fall on the world. At that instant evil people shall come out to do wickedness; the adulterers, and the robbers and poisoners. The evil beasts shall come out, and all the reptiles that are filled with wickedness, from their dens. And they stalk in the night. Also, the demons and fiends find power to do wickedness in the night. Again, the blows and buffets and wounds and discharges shall torment people in the night; because in it the whole of evil shall find strength.

The seventh: The night shall display the sign of the Darkness, its father. It has arisen out of its essence; because this night that came out of this first Darkness has been displayed in the world. Note this night, the shadow of this first Darkness, the one that is entwined and bound in all things above and below.

There are five archetypes occur in the sun, it making them [...] as it finds the world.

⟨p.162⟩ The first is its light; for it illumines by its light the world, and all the creatures who exist in it.

The second is its beauty; because when it shines, it shall flood beauty and loveliness upon all creatures and everything.

The third is its peace; for, again, when the sun would shine on the world, all people shall receive affection through it, and give peace to one another.

The fourth is the life of the living soul, which it shall release from all the bonds and fetters of heaven and earth.

The fifth: It gives a strength to the elements; and also it gives a scent and a taste to the entire Cross of the Light.

its light illumines more than all lights that occur in the world. Also, its beauty is more beautiful than all the beauties of mankind. Again, its peace is more surpassing than all the powers and guards who exist in the world. Its releasing action, by which it releases the living soul, is a full day ahead of all releasing actions! The strength that it gives to its limbs is a great strength, being mightier than all strengths!

Once again, there are another three archetypes made apparent by the sun, in respect of the mystery of the first greatness.

The first is the filling up of the disk of its ship; because its ship has filled up every season, and shall not wane at all, the way that the ship of the moon wanes. This continuous filling, by which it is filled, displays the mystery of the Father, the great greatness; from whom all the powers and the gods have come forth. He shall never wane, nor shall lack ever exist in him!

The second: (The sun's) light surpasses the light of all the stars, and all the lights that occur in the universe; corresponding to the mystery of the light of the Father; for his light surpasses and is greater than the light of his aeons.

⟨p.163⟩ The third: This sun is very high above all else. It surpasses the height of all the mountains and hills that exist upon the entire earth, corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness; for he is high above all the household of his light-earth. Also, because the Father himself is high above all the heights and the mountains of his light-earth. He is 'filled up' in his image [all] the time and every hour from eternity to eternity!

The mystery of the first great light is the sun, which is this second light-giver, the one that comes daily to the world. It displays all the marks of its graces, displaying and signifying on its own behalf that it is good, out of the essence of the good. It came from the good Father and appeared in this world.

this night that is made of fear exists after the mystery of the Darkness. Behold too the mystery of its essence! It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses. It displays this mystery daily in the world.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Dialogue frame formula wraps a substantive cosmological question. The question 'how was it fashioned after the likeness of the first greatness?' defines the teaching structure — the sun as a likeness/correspondence of the First Greatness. This is the Persian pedagogical tradition: the question IS the substrate.
- ¶2: Two substrate teachings extracted: (1) sun = gate of life and vessel of peace, connected to the aeon of light; (2) sun = gate of souls' departure. Both map cosmos→cosmos. The anti-OT polemic identifies the OT God as 'Satan' — a Manichaean editorial layer that uses these substrate facts as ammunition. Note the OT citation in the footnotes (Deut. 1) confirming the editorial bridge to biblical material.
- ¶3: Pure editorial lamentation about human blindness. 'Now, people are blind about everything!' opens with editorial voice. 'even today' anchors to the editorial present. 'Behold: How many are the charities that it does for mankind!' — rhetorical editorial exclamation. No substrate teaching content; the paragraph is the editor's commentary on human ignorance rather than the teaching itself.
- ¶4: This is the structural announcement of the seven-fold enumeration. 'Seven good things' defines the teaching skeleton. The rhetorical question is the editor's voice echoing ¶3's lamentation about blindness.
- ¶5: First enumeration in the seven goods of the sun. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system: sun's light → removes night's darkness, opens eyes. The enumeration marker 'The first' is substrate structure.
- ¶6: Second enumeration. Sun → peace/calm, removes night's fear/trembling. Cosmos→cosmos throughout. The teaching describes what the sun IS and DOES as a cosmic force.
- ¶7: Third enumeration. Sun → awakening/vigil. 'The guards who sit at the gates of camps' describes social effects but as part of the systematic teaching about what the sun DOES. These are cosmic effects, not contemporary institutional references.
- ¶8: Fourth enumeration. Sun → nourishment of the vegetable kingdom. Note the substrate vocabulary: 'taste and scent' — these are the same sensory-quality registers found in the five-worlds teaching (Ch.6). Sun gives these qualities to vegetation — cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶9: Fifth enumeration. Sun → driving creatures of Darkness into hiding. The 'evil reptile and the sharp-toothed beasts' belong to the dark-world fauna — cosmic entities, not social categories.
- ¶10: Sixth enumeration. Sun → healing and dissolution of evil arts. 'Melt away the evil arts' uses the same process-vocabulary found in substrate descriptions of light's action on darkness. Cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶11: Seventh and culminating enumeration. Sun = sign of the aeons of light. This is the correspondential principle stated explicitly: the sun in THIS world is the SIGN of the light-realm from which it emanated. Substrate score 6.98 confirms — 'aeons of light' triggers the vocabulary marker. Both sides within the cosmic system.
- ¶12: Editorial polemic against 'the sects of error.' 'Behold: How great is the foolishness that has befallen the sects of error!' — one side of the mapping reaches into the editorial present ('the sects'). Editorial bridge vocabulary score 8.33 confirms. Structurally, this is an editorial bracket between the seven goods of the sun and the seven evils of the night.
- ¶13: Bridge connective detected. The first-person transition voice ('I have shown you... hear what I teach you') is editorial framing that connects the two seven-fold lists. The substrate content preserved announces the night's seven evils, parallel to ¶4's announcement of the sun's seven goods.
- ¶14: First enumeration of night's seven evils, directly parallel to ¶5 (sun's first good = light). Night → darkness, blindness. The process description ('when the sun sinks... draws its rays... shadow spreads out') is systematic substrate process-vocabulary.
- ¶15: Second evil, parallel to ¶6 (sun's second good = peace/calm). Night → fear/trembling. Cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶16: Third evil, parallel to ¶7 (sun's third good = vigil/awakening). Night → rebellion/revolt of creatures. Cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶17: Fourth evil. Night → sleep as death-likeness ('like corpses'). This is correspondential language: sleep in the night corresponds to death/unconsciousness in the cosmic system.
- ¶18: Fifth evil. Night → hiding of beauty/form. The language of 'form' (morphe) being hidden by darkness is correspondential: beauty/form belongs to the light, darkness hides it.
- ¶19: Sixth evil — the longest enumeration. Night enables a four-level hierarchy of wickedness: (1) evil human behavior, (2) beasts/reptiles, (3) demons/fiends, (4) physical afflictions. While 'adulterers, robbers, poisoners' names social types, these function as part of a systematic cosmic description of what night enables across all levels of being — not as identification of contemporary targets. The hierarchy itself (human → animal → demonic → physical) is substrate structure.
- ¶20: Seventh and culminating evil, directly parallel to ¶11 (sun = sign of light-realm). Night = sign of the primordial Darkness, its 'father.' Substrate score 6.9 confirms. This is the correspondential principle for the dark side: the night in this world is the shadow/sign of the first Darkness. 'Entwined and bound in all things above and below' is cosmic process-vocabulary. Darkness capitalized as personified cosmic entity (functioning as father/origin).
- ¶21: Editorial seam detected. 'Behold, I have taught you about these seven wickednesses' — first-person editorial summary. 'The sects have not seen this black night: what it is; nor from whence is its essence' — polemic against the sects, one side reaching into the contemporary religious landscape. Structurally parallel to ¶12 (editorial bracket after the seven goods). Classic editorial bridge: summarizes substrate teaching, then polemicizes.
- ¶22: Dialogue frame wraps the structural announcement of the five archetypes. The teaching content — five archetypes in the sun — defines the next teaching sequence. The lacuna [...] preserves manuscript damage.
- ¶23: First of five archetypes. Sun → light → illumination of all creatures. Cosmos→cosmos. Clean enumeration structure.
- ¶24: Second archetype. Sun → beauty/loveliness. Cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶25: Third archetype. Sun → peace/affection. Cosmos→cosmos. The social effect (people giving peace to one another) describes what the sun's cosmic force produces, not a contemporary institutional reference.

The content announces three further archetypes corresponding to the 'mystery of the first greatness' — this defines the correspondence target for the next teaching sequence. - ¶30: First of three archetypes mapping sun → Father. Sun's permanent fullness (vs. moon's waning) corresponds to the Father's inexhaustible emanation. Both sides cosmic: (1) sun-ship's permanent disk, (2) Father's eternal plenitude. Classic cosmos→cosmos correspondence.
- ¶31: Second archetype. Sun's surpassing light → Father's surpassing light above his aeons. 'Corresponding to the mystery of' is the explicit correspondence formula. Cosmos→cosmos. The word 'corresponding' is the substrate voice naming the mapping operation.
- ¶32: Third archetype. Sun's height → Father of Greatness's supremacy above his light-earth. 'Corresponding to the mystery of the Father of Greatness' — explicit correspondence formula again. 'Light-earth' is cosmic geography. The scare-quoted 'filled up' suggests a technical term recognized by the translator, linking back to the first archetype (¶30). Substrate score 3.7 triggers on this cosmic geography.
- ¶33: Editorial summary and polemic. '[Beho]ld the two mysteries I have taught you, O my beloved ones!' — first-person address with exhortation vocabulary ('O my beloved ones'). 'The sects have not seen them in their error!' — polemic against sects. The cosmological content ('the mystery of the light and the mystery of the darkness are displayed daily in the created order') restates what ¶11 and ¶20 already taught — it is editorial recapitulation, not new substrate teaching. Exhortation score 8.77 confirms devotional register.
- ¶34: Concluding substrate teaching on the sun. Sun = mystery of the first great light = second light-giver. 'Out of the essence of the good' — ontological origin. 'It came from the good Father' — emanation from the Father of Greatness. Both sides within the cosmic system: the sun's presence in the world corresponds to its origin in the light-realm. This completes the sun-side of the dual mystery.
- ¶35: Parallel to ¶34 (sun/light), this teaches the night-side of the correspondence. Night = mystery of the Darkness. 'It displays it in the world in all these wickednesses' — the night reveals the Darkness's nature through its effects. Darkness capitalized as personified cosmic entity. The polemic tail against 'the sects' is editorial bridge, removed.
- ¶36: Gospel of Thomas citation. 'The saviour cast an allusion to his disciples' — citation formula. The saying ('Understand what is before your face, and then what is hidden from you will be unveiled to you') is from Gos. Thom. logion 5. However ancient the saying itself may be, it ENTERED THIS TEXT via a 3rd-century Manichaean citation act. 'Hidden from the sects' anchors to the editorial polemical frame. - ¶37: Editorial seam detected. 'Once again the apostle speaks:' — dialogue frame formula reintroducing Mani's voice for the closing section. 'You are the children of the day and the children of the light... the children of the night and the children of the darkness' — applies the substrate day/night teaching to the audience. One side of the mapping (light/darkness) is cosmic; the other ('You') is the audience. The correspondence is being USED to categorize the community. This is application, not teaching.
- ¶38: Classic beatitude/devotional closure. 'Blessed are you, my beloved ones!' — beatitude formula. 'Blessed is he who can know this mystery... inherit life eternal!' — eschatological promise. ## Chapter 66
### Concerning the Ambassador

*Clean chapter structure: dialogue frame (¶1) → substrate teaching (¶2-6) → exhortation (¶7) → dialogue frame closure (¶8). The substrate core is a tightly structured cosmological correspondence teaching mapping two daily solar phenomena (sunrise/sunset) onto two terminal cosmic events (First Man's descent into Darkness / Last Statue's final ascent). Both sides of every mapping remain within the cosmic system. The teaching uses the natural solar cycle as a correspondential key to the entire cosmic drama — the 'second time' of mixture is the daylight between two eternal nights. The exhortation in ¶7 is a clear later addition applying the teaching to pastoral urgency. No editorial bridge material detected. The substrate dominates this chapter.*

Two great mysteries are apparent in this shining sun, at its rising and setting. Now, at the moment when it rises and is visible in the universe, all creatures of flesh shall lift up their heads fr[om their sleep] towards the splendour of the sun, and open their doors. While at evening time, when the sun sinks from the universe, all people from every place and even all beasts shall go in to their dark hiding places. The population of the entire universe shuts their doors and dark night overcomes them. They go inside and stay within the dark night in their hiding places.

Very great is this mystery revealed by the sun! For when the sun rises and [be]come[s] visible at the beginning of the day all people open their doors, and all of humanity and every creature emerges onto the ear[th] to destroy this Light Cross that is established i[n] silence.

This pertains to the mystery of the First Man, because when he came out from the grea[t]ness he shone forth over all the children of Darkness. At that mome[nt] all the children of Darkness came forth from their storehouses, its powers and its armies, abandoning their caves and their black abyss. They destroyed the [outer] body of his five sons, who were swathed over hi[s b]o[dy, in t]hat first struggle. ⟨p.165⟩

Again, when the sun sinks from the universe and sets, and all people go in to their hiding places and houses and conceal themselves; this also pertains to the mystery of the end, as it presages the consummation of the universe. For, when all the light will be purified and redeemed i[n] the universe at the last, the collector of all things, the Last Statue, will gather in and sculpt it/self. It is the last hour of the day, the time when the Last Statue will go up to the aeo[n of] light. The enemy too, death, will go in to bondage; to the prison of the souls of the de[ni]iers and blasphemers who loved the darkness. They will go in with it to bondage and the dark night overcomes th[em]; and thenceforth this name 'light' will not shine upon them.

Behold the two mysteries made visible daily in the sun, which is this splendrous light-giver.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure dialogue frame formula: 'Once again he speaks to his disciples' plus a formulaic attention-calling instruction ('Pay attention, look and understand in the way that I show you'). No substantive cosmological content — the instruction to attend is pedagogical framing, not teaching. High dialogue_frame score (26.32) confirms.
- ¶2: Establishes the correspondential framework: two solar mysteries (rising/setting) mapped to the behavior of all creatures. Both sides are cosmic-natural — sun's cycle maps to universal creature response. Entirely impersonal, systematic, observational. The teaching structure IS the two-mystery framework that the subsequent paragraphs will develop. No temporal markers pointing outside the system.
- ¶3: Develops the first mystery (sunrise). The natural phenomenon (creatures emerging at dawn) is mapped to a cosmic reality: the destruction of the Light Cross established in silence. Both sides remain within the cosmic system — 'Light Cross' is a cosmic entity (the suffering light bound in matter), not a contemporary institution. The mapping is cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶4: Maps sunrise-mystery to the primordial cosmic event: First Man's descent and the attack by the children of Darkness. 'This pertains to the mystery of...' is the substrate's own correspondential voice — it connects domain A (solar) to domain B (cosmogonic). Both sides remain cosmic: First Man, five sons, children of Darkness, storehouses, caves, black abyss — all cosmic geography and agents. The five sons and the first struggle are core cosmological architecture.
- ¶5: Maps sunset-mystery to the eschatological cosmic event: the Last Statue's ascent and the final imprisonment of Death. Highest substrate score in the chapter (7.09). Both sides remain cosmic: Last Statue, aeon of light, enemy/death, bondage, light/darkness. 'Deniers and blasphemers who loved the darkness' are eschatological soul-categories within the cosmic framework, not identifications of contemporary groups — they describe a cosmic fate, not a social institution. The entire paragraph maps sunset → consummation within the system.
- ¶6: Summary closure of the two-mystery teaching. 'Behold' here is demonstrative (pointing to what was just taught) rather than exhortative. Content remains entirely within the cosmic system: two mysteries, the sun, daily visibility. This restates and closes the framework established in ¶2.
- ¶7: Clear exhortation layer. 'I say to you, my brothers and loved ones' is direct community address. 'Take trouble and train yourselves, and preach to the souls' is pastoral mission instruction. 'Seize the moment, while alive, for their repentance' maps the teaching (daylight = time of mixture) into pastoral urgency — one side of the mapping now reaches outside the cosmic system into the audience's present situation. 'Before the door is shut' echoes the sunset teaching but applies it to the community's mission. Institutional vocabulary (4.23) confirms. This is a compiler's devotional application appended to the substrate core.
- ¶8: Formulaic disciple-response closure: 'When his disciples heard this, they were frightened and alarmed and disturbed.' Standard dialogue frame reaction pattern. Heavy lacunae in the remainder. The response formula and desire for salvation ('saved from death... imperishable aeon for ever') are conventional session-ending language.

## Chapter 68
### Concerning Fire

*A very clean, compact substrate chapter. Five-fold systematic classification of fire's works — consistent with the oldest teaching layer's approach of mapping natural/cosmic processes through numbered enumerations. Paragraph 3's four-fold separation of wood (fire, light, ash, smoke) is distinctly correspondential, mapping fire's action as element-separator. No editorial bridge, no institutional content, no exhortation. The only non-substrate material is the brief dialogue frame attribution in ¶1. NLP scores all read 0.0, likely because the chapter uses basic natural vocabulary (fire, wood, light, ash) that doesn't trigger the curated diagnostic term lists — but the substrate quality is structural (five-fold enumeration, systematic classification) rather than lexical.*

This fire, which burns and is apparent today in the world, it does five works.

The first work that it does is this: It incinerates and burns wood, and everything that shall be placed upon it.

The second: It separates four aspects of the wood, which are these: fire and light, ash and smoke.

The third: It gives a taste and an {aroma} to whatever will be cooked on it. It also gives heat and cheer to whoever will be close to it.

The fourth work that [it] does: All craftsmen shall work the utensils that they will fabricate in it.

The fifth: It shall display light, and the people see by [it].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: 'Today in the world' here is cosmological — distinguishing manifest/physical fire from the cosmic fire element — not the editorial 'today' that anchors to contemporary institutions. The five-fold structure introduction is substrate.
- ¶2: First enumeration unit. Describes fire's primary natural action — combustion. Both sides within the natural/cosmic system.
- ¶3: The most correspondentially dense paragraph. Fire as separator yielding four products — fire, light, ash, smoke — maps onto element categories. This is the kind of systematic natural-process analysis characteristic of the oldest teaching layer.
- ¶4: Maps fire to sensory qualities (taste, aroma) and experiential qualities (heat, cheer). Taste-mapping is a characteristic substrate register (cf. Ch. 6 taste-world correspondences). The {aroma} gap marker preserved.
- ¶5: Fire's transformative function — enabling material craft. 'Craftsmen' describes a natural function of fire (material transformation), not a contemporary institution. Both sides within the system. Lacuna [it] preserved.
- ¶6: Fire's illumination function — light enabling vision. Maps fire → light → sight. Lacuna [it] preserved. Closes the five-fold enumeration cleanly.
