## Chapter 69
### Concerning the Twelve Signs of the Zodiac and the Five Stars

*Extremely high substrate purity. The chapter contains systematic cosmological-correspondential teaching mapping zodiac signs and planets to the five worlds of darkness, then mapping zodiac triplicities to four cosmic domains of affliction. Both mappings stay entirely within the cosmic system — no contemporary application, no institutional content, no exhortation. The editorial layer is limited to dialogue frame formulas (opening/attribution) and first-person recap framing in ¶10-13. The seam flags on ¶3 and ¶10 are false positives — both paragraphs contain pure cosmos→cosmos mappings with zero institutional content. Gardner notes the structural tension of fitting twelve into pentads and reconciling the seven rulers with Mani's system; these tensions are visible in the teaching itself (uneven 2-2-3-3-2 distribution) and represent genuine substratic difficulty, not editorial confusion. The negative editorial fatigue score confirms the substrate runs strong throughout.*

the twelve signs of the zodiac that are set in the sphere. How are they determined? Or to what place do they belong, according to their essence? Or else again, these five stars that circle in them, from whence are they? Or why has the demiurge appointed them as the authorities and leaders? He is the great constructor who has appointed the entire creation; and he has bound and fixed them on the sphere.

Whether these twelve signs of the zodiac that are fixed on the sphere, or these five stars that circle upon them; those and these are rulers, all of them, according to their essence. They are all enemies and rivals to each other ⟨one oppresses another, they threaten each other⟩. They plunder one another by the action of the great craftsman who has formed all the worlds. He has gathered [them] and bound them on the sphere above, which circles constantly. Now, while it circles and tumbles them, so they shall do everything that they desire, above and below, according to their pleasure. Nevertheless, a guardian is over them, a debt-collector above them; compelling, extorting, and taking what is theirs away from them!

Now, this is how it can rightly be understood. They were [s]eized from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere. Two zodiacal signs were taken per world. Gemini and Sagittarius belong to the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aries and Leo belong to the world of fire. Taurus, Aquarius and Libra belong to the world of wind. Cancer and Virgo and [P]isces belong to the world of water. Capricorn and Scorpio belong to the world of Darkness. These are the twelve rulers of depravity, the ones that wicked[ness] shall not [ ... ] For they cause all the evil and [ ... in the wo]rld, whether in tree or in flesh.

⟨p.168⟩ And understand also about these five stars, the leaders: from where were they formed? The star of Zeus (Jupiter) was generated from the world of smoke, which is the mind. In contrast, Aphrodite (Venus) came about from the world of fire. And then Ares (Mars) belongs to the world of wind. Hermes (Mercury) belongs to the world of water. Cronos (Saturn) belongs to the world [of] Darkness.

And also, the two ascendants are counted to fire and desire, the dry and the moist. They are the father and the mother of all these.

And also, the sun and the moon, which are counted in with them as a rule ⟨[counte]d to their kind, but why did they count them in with them?⟩ so that they would reign over them. You will discover they subjugate them at every moment.

These seven we have named, the five stars and the two ascendants: They are the doers of wickedness that do every wicked and bad act in every land, the whole of it, above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh.

[They] are distributed, appointed on four sides, three per angle in these four places. And they are fixed to this revolving sphere. Aries, Leo and Sagittarius, they three belong to a single side. In contrast, Taurus, Capricorn and Virgo, these other three belong to another side. And then, Gemini, Libra and Aquarius belong to another side. Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer belong to another corner. Now, they are placed like this, appointed to these four parts, and distributed on the sphere.

So, when the side of Aries, Leo and Sagittarius will be plundered by the guardian who is over it, who extorts from it and the leaders who move upon it; at that instant shall affliction strike all the four-footed creatures below. However, when [the side] of Taurus, Virgo and Capricorn will be plundered; [affliction] shall ⟨p.169⟩ at once befall the herbs, together with the vegetables and all the fruits of the trees. Yet again, when the side of Scorpio, Pisces and Cancer will be plundered; scarcity shall befall the waters upon the earth, and drought be from place to place. Conversely, should the side of Gemini, Libra and Aquarius be plundered; deformity and stuntedness shall befall the form of mankind from place to place.

these twelve signs of the zodiac: They were fashioned from the five worlds of Darkness, and bound on the sphere.

the other five, the stars: They too came about from the five worlds of the land of Darkness.

the [t]wo ascendants: They are set to the mystery of fire and desire. They are the dry and the moist, the father and the mother.

the sun and the moon: They are strangers to them. Still, because of the constraint of the arrangement, in that the thing receives light, and as they belong and plunder them; because of this they were counted in with them in relation to the calculation of the number. For, the sun and the moon are from out of the greatness, not belonging to the stars and the signs of the zodiac.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Substantive cosmological questions define the teaching structure: zodiac distribution, planetary origin, demiurge's role. These are the bones of the Persian pedagogical tradition. The 'demiurge' / 'great constructor' terminology is cosmological, not editorial.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological description. The 'guardian' and 'debt-collector' are cosmic agents (the demiurge/Living Spirit), not contemporary officials. The sphere as binding mechanism, plundering among bound rulers, and the overseer extracting from them — all cosmos→cosmos. The ⟨marginal gloss⟩ may be a scribal clarification.
- ¶3: Core correspondential mapping: zodiac signs → five worlds of Darkness. The seam flag is a false positive — 'Now, this is how it can rightly be understood' introduces the systematic mapping, not institutional content. Distribution is uneven (2-2-3-3-2 = 12), reflecting the genuine difficulty of fitting twelve into pentads noted by Gardner. 'World of smoke, which is the mind' — the mind-smoke correspondence is a hallmark of the five-worlds substrate. 'In tree or in flesh' = vegetable and animal kingdoms, cosmic scope.
- ¶4: Second systematic mapping: planets → five worlds of Darkness. One planet per world, clean pentadic structure. The Greek god-names (Zeus, Aphrodite, Ares, Hermes, Cronos) are the Coptic translators' rendering — the substrate likely used Babylonian/Persian planetary names. Jupiter→smoke/mind, Venus→fire, Mars→wind, Mercury→water, Saturn→darkness.
- ¶5: Teaching about the two lunar nodes (ascending/descending). Maps them to fire and desire, dry and moist, father and mother. These are cosmic polarities. The 'father and mother of all these' designates their generative function in the cosmic system — the nodes as progenitors of the dark powers.
- ¶6: Explains the sun and moon's relationship to the dark powers. The ⟨marginal gloss⟩ preserves what appears to be a scribal question about the tradition. The teaching itself describes a cosmic hierarchy: sun and moon are counted with the dark powers not because they belong to darkness but because they rule over them. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶7: Summary identifying the seven (5 planets + 2 nodes) and their cosmic scope. 'Above and below, in every creature, in the dry and the moist, in tree and flesh' is cosmic domain language, not contemporary application. The 'dry and moist, tree and flesh' pairs are correspondential categories for mineral-vegetable and animal kingdoms.
- ¶8: Second systematic distribution: zodiac signs mapped to four sides/angles of the sphere, three per quadrant. This is the standard astrological triplicities: fire signs (Aries/Leo/Sagittarius), earth signs (Taurus/Capricorn/Virgo), air signs (Gemini/Libra/Aquarius), water signs (Scorpio/Pisces/Cancer). Pure cosmos→cosmos mapping. Note that this four-fold distribution operates on a different axis than the five-fold mapping in ¶3.
- ¶9: Magnificent correspondential teaching: each zodiac triplicity maps to a domain of affliction. Fire signs → four-footed creatures (animal kingdom). Earth signs → herbs/vegetables/trees (plant kingdom). Water signs → waters/drought (elemental). Air signs → human form. Both sides stay within the cosmic system — the 'plundering' is a cosmic process, the effects are natural-cosmic categories. The fourfold structure (animal-vegetable-water-human) is a classic correspondential degree system.
- ¶10: Recapitulation of ¶3's teaching. The frame 'Behold now, I have explained to you about' is editorial/dialogue layer recap voice. The cosmological content restates the substrate mapping.
- ¶11: Recapitulation of ¶4's teaching. Brief substrate restatement: five stars from five worlds of darkness.
- ¶12: Recapitulation of ¶5's teaching. Restates the two ascendants' correspondences: fire/desire, dry/moist, father/mother. The 'mystery' language may be a Coptic translation choice for what was originally a more direct cosmological term.
- ¶13: Extends ¶6's teaching with fuller explanation. Sun and moon are 'from out of the greatness' (light realm) — 'strangers' to the dark powers. They are counted with them only 'because of the constraint of the arrangement' and 'the calculation of the number' (i.e., to make seven and twelve). This is the substrate's own attempt to reconcile the tradition of seven planetary rulers with the doctrine that sun/moon belong to light. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.

---

## Chapter 70
### Concerning the Body: It was constructed after the Pattern of the Universe

*Overwhelmingly substrate chapter — the oldest body-cosmos correspondence teaching dominates. Teaching purity 0.967 with no editorial bridge, no exhortation, and negligible editorial fatigue. Gardner notes redaction from multiple sources/lessons, visible in the distinct teaching blocks: (1) body→cosmic structures (¶2-3), (2) five watches and their conflicts (¶5-16), (3) five body regions→cosmic rulers with ethical framing (¶17-24), (4) four body worlds with seven rulers each (¶25-29), (5) zodiac-body correspondences in two schemas (¶31-36), (6) numerical powers in the body (¶38-39). The ethical framing in ¶18-22 ('Whoever will master X resembles cosmic ruler Y') is the main editorial overlay — it wraps substrate body-cosmos mappings in application to the elect. The three dialogue frame paragraphs are minimal. This chapter preserves some of the most systematic body-cosmos correspondence teaching in the Kephalaia.*

This whole universe, above and below, reflects the pattern of the human body; as the formation of this body of flesh accords to the pattern of the universe.

Its head is like the first-fruits of the five garments. And, from its neck down to the site of its heart, it resembles the pattern of the ten firmaments. And again, the heart accords to the wheel of the rotating sphere. And, from its heart down to its intestines (?), is like this atmosphere that extends from the sphere down to the earth. The [male] part of the body corresponds to this great earth. And also, from its intestines (?) down to its loins is like the three earths. And also, its shinbones to the pattern of the space in which the Porter stands. Its footsoles to the great earth upon which the Porter is stood; and the four fastenings that are under his feet. Its liver to the vessel of fire. Its flesh to the vessel of darkness. Its blood to the vessel of water.

the small body corresponds to the macro-cosmos in its firmaments, in its orderings, in its mountains, its walls and its vessels

There exist five great camps in this great outer zone, of which the five sons of the Living Spirit are masters.

So, in the first watch-station above, of which the Keeper of Splendour is master, on the battlement of all the works, is found his authority over the three heavens. Then the ones below him [ ... ... ] the great King of Honour.

The second watch, of which the great King of Honour is master, his own authority (extends) over the seven firmaments beneath him.

The third watch, of which the Adamant [is master, his authority] reigns from the firmament down below to the [earth]. [And he] invigorates, according to his authority, the sphere and the worlds of the atmosphere; and the other four worlds that are appointed on this earth.

The fourth watch, over which the King of Glory has power, is the three wheels. And his authority lies over the three earths upon the head of the Porter.

The fifth watch is the watch over which the Porter is authoritative. Again, he is master, according to his authority, over this great earth he stands upon, and the four fastenings under his feet.

Affliction has come about in these five watches from time to time.

So, in the watch of the Keeper of Splendour, Sin desired to spurt up towards the image of the Ambassador. Yet, they barred it from that place, and it fell back down in shame.

Again, an earthquake and treachery occurred in the watch of the great King of Honour, which the watchers who had arisen at the time and had [ ... ] they who were sent came down, until they were humbled.

Also, in the watch of the Adamant, the abortions came to the earth and fashioned [the] figure of flesh.

Again, in the watch of the King of Glory, a commotion happened in these three earths above the Porter. The pathway was obstructed for the ascent of these three wheels; that of wind, that of water, and that of fire.

Also, in the watch of the Porter, the fastenings beneath were laid bare and strained their chains. A great earthquake befell that place.

[Like] these five watch-stations, which exist in this great [ ... these five] camps: this is also the case with this body. There are another five camps there, and the Light Mind is watching over them, and the new man who is with him.

The configuration of his face, which is above his body: the mystery of the Keeper of Splendour, who rules over the watch above the zone.

His heart: the sign of the great King of Honour, who subdues the seven heavens.

His genitals: the mystery of the Adamant of Light, the one who subdues Matter.

The stomach and the fire that is in him: the likeness of the King of Glory, the one who turns the wheels, sending the life above.

The ruling-power that is beneath in his feet: the Porter, the one who subdues with his footsoles the abysses below.

Also, the teaching of wisdom, which circulates in that body: to the likeness of the Virgin of Light, who goes up and comes down in this zone, above and below.

love and joy, faith and truth: they are to the likeness of these two light ships. For, the living soul should go up in them and become free through them; and it ascends from the abysses below and arrives at the heights above.

There exist four worlds in this body of the flesh, and there exist countless times seven rulers in the body of the four worlds!

So, the first world is from its neck up; and also the seven rulers who are in this upper world are these: the two organs of sight, and the two of hearing, and the two of smell. The other one is the mouth, which is the organ of taste.

The second world is from its neck down to its entire upper torso. And also, the seven rulers who are in this second world are: its two arms that accord to the pattern of its two ears, which occur in its first world above; and its two breasts that are to the pattern of its organs of smell; and the two eyes of its heart; and the gullet of the stomach between the two breasts, which in the chest is the pattern of the mouth.

Once more, there exist seven rulers in the third world of the body, which are these: the fat, the lung, the spleen, the liver, the gall, and its two kidneys.

Also, the fourth world below. There are another seven there of this same kind: its two buttocks (?), and its two testicles, and its two loins; and the member out of which the seed comes, that begets all the forms.

Aries and Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Scorpio, Sagittarius, Capricorn, Aquarius and Pisces; these are the twelve signs of the zodiac that occur in the worlds above, on the sphere. They are interlocked and bound on this turning wheel, the one in which they are [ ... ], fixed, and planted. And, regarding all these twelve signs of the zodiac, this is how they were appointed and made leaders [ ... ] on the sphere beneath the heaven that is the wheel of the stars. Now, they are appointed like this, they are thus, so that you can find the head of [ ... ] and you find the tail too [ ... ... ]

Again, they occur like this, these that we have recited, one after another in this body. They are counted by order and number from the head to the feet. Its head is Aries. Its neck and its shoulders are Taurus. Its two arms Gemini. Its upper torso is Cancer. Its stomach is Leo. Its belly is Virgo. The vertical spine and its intestines (?) are Libra. Its genitals Scorpio. Its loins are Sagittarius. Its knees are Capricorn. Its shinbones are Aquarius. The soles of its feet are Pisces.

Behold, these are also distributed one after another. They exist in this body, as if turned to the side and bent to the pattern of the twelve signs of the zodiac. They too are thus, arranged one against another, head to tail, as they occur on the wheel [ ... ] up.

Also, they are counted by order and number of the body and all its members. So, we have proclaimed that these are turned to the side, bent, and spread out; because from its head down to its hip shall count six to its left, and another six to its right.

The six that are down its right are these: the right temple is Aries; its right shoulder is Taurus; its right arm is Gemini; its right rib-cage is Cancer; its stomach is Leo; the right side of its reproductive organs are Virgo.

the other six which are to its left, in contrast they come from its hip to its head: its left [ ... ] is Libra; its left rib-cage is Scorpio; its left breast and left kidney is Sagittarius; its [left] elbow is Capricorn; its left shoulder is Aquarius; its left temple is Pisces. This is how the creator of the body himself has appointed them. He has [set] them in order and arranged them, one against its companion, head to tail.

There exist many powers in this body. They are the house-dwellers who are made the leaders in it. There are eight hundred and forty times ten thousand rulers made chiefs in the human body! They are distributed and set firm, quartered according to house. The count and number of their houses is two hundred and ten times ten thousand.

When all these rulers come creeping and moving within the body, they will meet one another; and they shall beset and destroy one another, and [ ... ] in them there. When [ ... t/il]] they shall erupt from the body of the person who [will] die; and make putrid boils and sores and burning wounds in the body. Either they might have made him sick within; or else they might come forth upon his outer side. Those wounds shall first be squeezed and discharge their liquid, and empty out their pus, until they are squeezed [ ... ... ] and the suppuration forced out from the wound. Afterwards, their scars shall close over; and they are healed and obtain relief from the places that came out upon them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic scene-setting: 'the apostle is sitting down among the church in the midst of the congregation.' Institutional vocabulary ('church', 'congregation') scores 19.23. No cosmological content.
- ¶2: The thesis statement of the chapter's body-cosmos correspondence teaching. Microcosm = macrocosm stated as structural principle. Both sides of the mapping (universe, body) stay within the cosmic system. No frame formula, no attribution.
- ¶3: Systematic body-cosmos mapping traversing from head to feet, then internal organs. Every mapping: body part → cosmic structure. Head→garments, neck-heart→firmaments, heart→sphere, torso→atmosphere, loins→three earths, shinbones→Porter's space, footsoles→Porter's earth+fastenings, liver→fire vessel, flesh→darkness vessel, blood→water vessel. Pure substrate — both sides remain within cosmic architecture. Substrate score 18.49.
- ¶4: Summary statement closing the first body-cosmos mapping section. The cosmological summary vocabulary (firmaments, orderings, mountains, walls, vessels) echoes the preceding teaching. The first-person attribution marks editorial voice.
- ¶5: Introduces the five-watch teaching. The substantive content (five camps, five sons of the Living Spirit) defines the teaching structure — this is a cosmological question-answer unit. The frame formula is stripped but the teaching thesis preserved.
- ¶6: First in the five-fold watch enumeration. Maps first watch → Keeper of Splendour → three heavens. Lacuna present. Both sides remain within cosmic system. Substrate score 18.6.
- ¶7: Second watch enumeration. King of Honour → seven firmaments. Highest substrate score in chapter (50.0). Pure systematic mapping.
- ¶8: Third watch enumeration. Adamant → firmament to earth, sphere, atmosphere, four worlds. Restorations in brackets. Describes cosmic process ('invigorates').
- ¶9: Fourth watch enumeration. King of Glory → three wheels → three earths above Porter. Substrate score 40.0. Pure cosmic geography.
- ¶10: Fifth and final watch enumeration. Porter → great earth + four fastenings. Completes the five-fold structure. Despite 0.0 substrate vocabulary score, content is pure cosmic mapping.
- ¶11: Transitional thesis statement introducing the five cosmic conflicts. Describes cosmic-temporal process. Brief but structurally essential — opens the second teaching block within the five-watch framework.
- ¶12: First cosmic conflict. Sin (capitalized as cosmic agent) acts against the Ambassador — both cosmic entities. Process description: Sin rises, is barred, falls. Both sides within the cosmic system.
- ¶13: Second cosmic conflict. 'Watchers' (egrēgoroi) are cosmic beings in the tradition. Lacuna present. Describes cosmic upheaval and resolution. All entities remain within the system.
- ¶14: Third cosmic conflict. 'Abortions' are cosmic entities (archontic offspring) in Manichaean cosmology. They fashion the body of flesh — cosmic process of embodiment. Both sides within system.
- ¶15: Fourth cosmic conflict. Three earths, three wheels (wind, water, fire) — systematic cosmic geography. Describes obstruction of cosmic process. Substrate score 30.0.
- ¶16: Fifth cosmic conflict completing the enumeration. Fastenings and chains — cosmic binding structures. Pure process description.
- ¶17: Bridge from macrocosmic five-watch teaching to body-microcosm. The correspondence (five cosmic watches → five camps in body) is substrate. 'Light Mind' is a cosmic entity (Nous) in the emanation hierarchy. 'The new man' may be Manichaean/Pauline overlay — uncertain. Institutional score 3.7 from 'elect' reference.
- ¶18: First in the five-fold body-ruler correspondence series (¶18-22). The substrate mapping (face ↔ Keeper of Splendour) is the only place this correspondence appears in the chapter. The 'whoever will master' framing converts impersonal correspondence into ethical application. Institutional score 4.88.
- ¶19: Second body-ruler correspondence. Heart ↔ King of Honour. Same ethical application pattern as ¶18.
- ¶20: Third body-ruler correspondence. Genitals ↔ Adamant of Light ↔ Matter. The substrate maps a body region to its cosmic ruler and the cosmic ruler's function (subduing Matter).
- ¶21: Fourth body-ruler correspondence. Stomach/fire ↔ King of Glory (wheel-turner, life-sender). The substrate describes the cosmic function: turning wheels, sending life upward — cosmic process.
- ¶22: Fifth and final body-ruler correspondence completing the enumeration. Feet/lower power ↔ Porter subduing abysses. Parallel structure to ¶10 (macrocosmic Porter description).
- ¶23: Maps inner wisdom circulation to Virgin of Light's movement in the cosmic zone. Both sides within the system — wisdom as cosmic faculty, Virgin of Light as cosmic entity. Body-cosmos correspondence in process mode (circulation ↔ ascent/descent).
- ¶24: Maps inner virtues (love, joy, faith, truth) to the two light ships. Soul ascent described as cosmic process. The 'should' in 'should go up' may reflect Coptic subjunctive describing the process rather than ethical command — retained as process description. The brief prescriptive 'in which the person should live' is the only clearly editorial element.
- ¶25: Thesis statement opening the four-worlds-in-the-body teaching block. The numerical system (four worlds × seven rulers) is pure substrate teaching structure. Introduces ¶26-29.
- ¶26: First world enumeration: head region → seven rulers (sense organs: 2 sight + 2 hearing + 2 smell + 1 taste = 7). Pure systematic body mapping. Despite 0.0 substrate vocabulary score, content is clearly oldest layer — systematic enumeration of body-cosmos structure.
- ¶27: Second world enumeration: torso → seven rulers. Remarkably, each torso organ is mapped to its corresponding sense organ in the first world (arms↔ears, breasts↔nostrils, heart-eyes↔eyes, gullet↔mouth). This is body-cosmos correspondence within the body itself — degree mapping domain onto domain. Pure substrate.
- ¶28: Third world enumeration: abdominal organs. Seven rulers: fat, lung, spleen, liver, gall, 2 kidneys = 7. 'Once more' is a mild teaching connective, part of the enumeration structure. Pure substrate.
- ¶29: Fourth and final world enumeration completing the four-fold structure. Lower body → seven rulers: 2 buttocks + 2 testicles + 2 loins + 1 generative member = 7. 'That begets all the forms' — substrate description of cosmic-generative function.
- ¶30: Pure formulaic transition: 'Once more the enlightener speaks to his hearers.' Marks the seam between source blocks (four-worlds teaching → zodiac-body teaching). Gardner notes the chapter is redacted from multiple sources — this frame marks one such join.
- ¶31: Lists all twelve zodiac signs and describes their cosmic placement on the sphere. Multiple lacunae. 'Head to tail' describes the zodiacal arrangement — astronomical substrate. 'You can find' is generic pedagogical address within the teaching tradition, not editorial application. Substrate score 48.28.
- ¶32: Linear zodiac-body mapping, head to feet: Aries→head, Taurus→neck/shoulders, Gemini→arms, Cancer→torso, Leo→stomach, Virgo→belly, Libra→spine, Scorpio→genitals, Sagittarius→loins, Capricorn→knees, Aquarius→shinbones, Pisces→feet. Standard zodiacal melothesia — ancient body-cosmos correspondence. 'These that we have recited' is minor pedagogical self-reference within the teaching tradition. Substrate score 40.91.
- ¶33: Introduces the second zodiac-body schema (circular/lateral arrangement). 'Head to tail' astronomical terminology. 'As they occur on the wheel' — body arrangement mirrors zodiacal arrangement on the sphere. Lacuna present.
- ¶34: Explains the lateral distribution: six zodiac signs on the right side, six on the left. 'We have proclaimed' is minor pedagogical self-reference, part of the teaching voice. The structural principle (6 right + 6 left) is substrate.
- ¶35: Right-side zodiac-body mapping: Aries→right temple, Taurus→right shoulder, Gemini→right arm, Cancer→right rib-cage, Leo→stomach, Virgo→right reproductive organs. Pure zodiacal melothesia in lateral schema. Substrate score 38.3.
- ¶36: Left-side zodiac-body mapping completing the circular schema: Libra→left [...], Scorpio→left rib-cage, Sagittarius→left breast/kidney, Capricorn→left elbow, Aquarius→left shoulder, Pisces→left temple. 'The creator of the body' — refers to the archontic craftsman who structured the body; cosmic agent, not editorial reference. 'Head to tail' is astronomical-structural terminology. Lacuna and restoration present.
- ¶37: Minimal formulaic transition: 'Once more he speaks.' Marks another source seam — transition to the rulers/illness teaching block.
- ¶38: Numerical body-cosmos teaching: 8,400,000 rulers in 2,100,000 houses within the body. 'House-dwellers' and 'quartered according to house' — cosmic-architectural vocabulary describing the body's internal structure. The precise large numbers are characteristic of the substrate's systematic-enumerative style.
- ¶39: Process description: cosmic rulers within the body conflict, causing illness and suppuration. Describes the natural process of wound formation and healing as a function of inner-ruler activity. Pure body-cosmos process — both sides (rulers, bodily manifestation) stay within the system. No ethical application, no contemporary reference. Multiple lacunae. The detailed natural-process observation (pus discharge, scar closure, healing) is characteristic of the substrate's descriptive voice.

## Chapter 71
### Concerning the Gathering in of the [E]lements

*A very short chapter with a remarkably clean substrate core. Paragraph 2 is the heart: a systematic degree-based return sequence mapping the gathering-in of the five light elements upward through cosmic intermediaries to the aeons. This is pure correspondential substrate — an ascending chain where each domain gathers into the next. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. The chapter shows mild editorial fatigue: the brief devotional closure in P3 ('place of rest for ever and ever') is appended after the teaching is complete. The substrate teaching in P2 is notable for its restoration brackets, indicating manuscript damage that has been editorially restored — the Pillar→First Man→Ambassador chain is reconstructed.*

In what way shall the elements be gathered in, from one to another?

The light shall be gathered in to the fire, and the fire itself gathered in to the water, and the water gathered in to the wind, and the wind gathered to the air, and the air gathered to the answer, and the answer gathered to the summons. Also, the summons to the purified Mind, which is the intellectual. The intellectual to the Pillar of Glory, [the Pillar of Glory to the First Man, the Man to the Ambassador, the Ambassador to] the aeons of greatness.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The formulaic opening 'Once more the enlightener speaks' is dialogue frame. But the question itself is substantive and cosmological — it defines the teaching topic (the sequential gathering-in of the elements) and structures the teaching that follows. The question belongs to the Persian pedagogical tradition.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological-correspondential substrate. A systematic ascending chain: five light elements (light→fire→water→wind→air) gather upward, then transition through cosmic intermediary beings (answer→summons→Mind→Pillar of Glory→First Man→Ambassador→aeons). Both sides of every mapping remain entirely within the cosmic system. This is a degree-based return hierarchy — the reverse of an emanation sequence. The bracketed restoration [the Pillar of Glory to the First Man...] fills manuscript lacuna but follows the systematic pattern perfectly. Note the transition from impersonal elements to personified cosmic entities at the answer/summons level — this is the boundary between the elemental and the intellectual/personal degrees.
- ¶3: Devotional closure appended after the substrate teaching is complete. 'Place of rest for ever and ever' is a classic devotional/eschatological formula. The exhortation score (21.43) confirms. 'They are gathered in like this' summarizes back to the teaching, then 'go up above to the place of rest for ever and ever' shifts to eschatological promise — the voice moves from describing cosmic process to assuring outcome. The datum 'on one occasion only' may preserve a genuine teaching point about the once-only eschatological gathering, but it is embedded in devotional closure language and cannot be cleanly separated.

---

## Chapter 72
### Concerning the worn and torn apart Garments, or the tattered Clothes, or the Elements, the Cross, and the rest too

*A remarkably clean teaching chapter. The substrate dominates: a systematic terminological exposition mapping seven names for the devoured light to their cosmic referents. No editorial bridge material, no exhortation, no institutional content. The editorial layer is confined entirely to the dialogue frame (¶1, 4, 5, 6) and minor framing phrases in ¶2, 3, 7. The teaching structure is: (1) enumerate terms as questions, (2) define each term by mapping name→cosmic entity/condition, (3) conclude with restoration theology. Every mapping stays within the cosmic system. The 'we have proclaimed/termed/called' self-referential phrases in ¶8-12 are part of the systematic definitional voice, not editorial insertions.*

what then are these 'great garments'? Or again, this you sometimes call the 'rags', what are those 'rags'? Or the 'worn and torn apart'? Or what are the 'images and the tattered clothes'? Or what is this name: 'light element'? Or conversely, what is the 'Cross of Light'? Or why named otherwise: the 'counsel of life'? Or what is the 'soul that is slain'; being killed, oppressed, and murdered in the enemy? What are all these names?

how have they changed with all these names and become set in altered forms

The garments that are named 'great garment' are the five intellectuals that make perfect the body of the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man. They were purified by the coming of the Ambassador.

In contrast, the ones named 'rag': the rags are the devoured enlightening power. It is compounded and entangled in the body of the universe in the rulers who are above, who exist in all the firmaments. Also, its shine is that which lights up and is displayed in the stars of the chain and the powers of the course. And likewise, these rags that are entangled in these three earths below, above the head of the Porter [...] on the earth that is under the Porter. These are named the 'rags', because [...] set [...]

However, the 'great worn and torn apart garments', which we have proclaimed, is this entangled living power. It is reaped, cut, and devoured. It is overpowered by the five worlds of the flesh, which creep as they move from place to place.

On the other hand, [these] we have termed the 'images and the great tattered clothes' [...] are the three great garments of the wind, the fire, and the water. These the Living Spirit cloaked upon his body. With them he set in order things in the universe. With them also he emptied the vessels. He swept them out and laid them bare in front of the Porter at the foundation of all things.

Conversely, '[the ele]ments', we have called them this name, are this power that remains in all things below. It rises up in the wombs of all the earths, gathering together, and pouring forth upon all things.

And then, the '[Light] Cross' [we have termed] this bound 'light power'. It [...] all [...] upon the earth, in the dry and the moist.

Also, the one named the 'counsel of life' is the summons and the obedience. It moves in all these powers that we have named. It awakens them, gives them ease, and drives them to their movement.

The one named 'the soul that is slain', which is killed, oppressed and murdered; it is this power of fruit, vegetables and seeds. They shall be threshed, plucked and cut down; and they give nourishment to the worlds of the flesh. Further: the wood should it be dry, and the garment should it become ragged, will perish. Again, it is [a pa]rt of this entire soul that is slain and killed; since also even this whole part of it they kill [...]

For all these names [...] are a single [...] since the beginning, it [...] as it enlightens; but they separated into all these parts in this first contest. They became set in all these altered forms, and these many names. Of course, if now all these varieties are laid bare, and stripped of all these appearances, and parted from all these names; they will gather together and make a single form, and a single name, unaltered and unchangeable for ever in the land of their first essence, from which they were sent forth against the enemy.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening: 'Once more the apostle is sitting in the congregation of his disciples.' Standard Kephalaia session marker with no cosmological content.
- ¶2: The enumeration of seven cosmological terms as questions defines the entire teaching structure of this chapter. These are substantive cosmological questions in the Persian pedagogical tradition. The 'written in the scriptures' framing is editorial but the term-list itself is substrate — each term answered systematically in ¶7-14.
- ¶3: This question asks HOW the light came to have different names and forms — a different question from ¶2's WHAT. It is answered in the concluding ¶15. The 'written and sealed... by a multitude of scriptures' is clearly editorial reference to Manichaean canon.
- ¶4: Pure dialogue transition formula: 'His disciples say to him:'
- ¶5: Purely honorific disciple response with no cosmological content. 'Source of all wisdoms,' 'fount of all wisdoms' — hagiographic address. The disciples' deflection back to the teacher is a standard dialogue convention.
- ¶6: Pure formula: 'Then speaks the apostle to them:' with manuscript page marker ⟨p.177⟩.
- ¶7: First definition in the series: 'great garment' = five intellectuals of the Pillar of Glory / Perfect Man. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic entities. The five intellectuals, the Pillar of Glory, the Perfect Man, and the Ambassador are all cosmic-system beings. This is the purest substrate form: name→cosmic referent.
- ¶8: Second definition: 'rags' = devoured enlightening power distributed throughout the cosmic structure. Maps into firmaments, stars of the chain, powers of the course, three earths, the Porter — all cosmic geography. Lacunae at end indicate manuscript damage. The 'stars of the chain' and 'powers of the course' are technical cosmological terms for astral bodies in the cosmic mechanism.
- ¶9: Third definition: 'worn and torn apart' = entangled living power consumed by the five worlds of flesh. The 'five worlds of the flesh' are the five archontic species — a core cosmological category. 'which we have proclaimed' is the systematic teaching voice referring back to the enumeration, not editorial insertion.
- ¶10: Fourth definition: 'images and tattered clothes' = the three elemental garments (wind, fire, water) that the Living Spirit used to structure the cosmos. Maps name→cosmic elements→cosmic agent's action. All within the cosmic system: Living Spirit, vessels, Porter, foundation — all cosmic architecture.
- ¶11: Fifth definition: 'elements' = the power remaining in things below, rising through the earths. Describes a cosmic process — the light ascending through the cosmic structure. 'wombs of all the earths' is correspondential language mapping reproductive processes onto cosmic geography.
- ¶12: Sixth definition: 'Light Cross' = bound light power in earth, dry and moist. Despite lacunae, the structure is clear: name→cosmic condition. The 'dry and moist' are elemental qualities within the cosmic system, not contemporary references.
- ¶13: Seventh definition: 'counsel of life' = the summons and the obedience. These are cosmic entities (the Call and the Answer) that animate the other light-forms. This maps name→cosmic agent→cosmic function. The summons/obedience pair is a fundamental correspondential structure — the call from above and the response from below.
- ¶14: Eighth definition: 'soul that is slain' = power in vegetation (fruit, vegetables, seeds). The description of threshing, plucking, cutting describes cosmic processes — how the light trapped in vegetation is consumed by the worlds of flesh. The wood/garment analogy extends the teaching within the natural-correspondential register. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶15: Cosmological conclusion answering ¶3's question (how did they change?): all names are one light, separated in the first cosmic contest, to be restored to unity in the land of first essence. 'The first contest,' 'the land of their first essence,' 'sent forth against the enemy' — entirely cosmic-historical. This is the teaching's resolution: multiplicity of names reflects the differentiation of light in mixture; restoration returns to unity. No editorial extension, no exhortation — the chapter ends on the teaching itself.

## Chapter 73
### Concerning the Envy of Matter

*The oldest teaching layer is a compact cosmological narrative about Matter's envy expressed through three cosmic episodes: the First Man, the Ambassador's image, and Adam. This substrate is clearly bounded — paragraphs 2-4 are pure cosmological narrative with both sides of every mapping internal to the cosmic system. The editors extended the three-fold enumeration to a fourth item (Christ and the apostles), then to Mani and his church, then to practical community warnings, then to exhortation. The editorial fatigue pattern is textbook: institutional vocabulary jumps from 0.0 to 8.30 in the second half. Teaching purity is very low (0.109), reflecting that the substrate core is small and the editorial extension substantial.*

[The] entirety of envy is this first nature that occu[red] in the worlds of darkness.

In this envy that [ ... ] in it, the First Man [ ... ] its poiso[n ... ] its evil-doing. His garment[s] were [ ... ] it cut them and drew them; [ ... ]

⟨p.179⟩ It envied the first image of the Ambassador, which he displayed to it in the heights abov[e], i[n] his form that it saw. It established the seal and the image of flesh, and put the impression of t[h]at image on flesh, its very own birth.

[The th]ird time it envied Adam, the fir[st hum]an. It seized a power from him, and bore it forth from a [rib of] of Adam's [flesh].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The thesis statement — envy as the 'first nature' in 'the worlds of darkness' — is a cosmological identification with both sides internal to the cosmic system. The frame formula is standard dialogue layer wrapping.
- ¶2: Despite heavy lacunae, the cosmological content is clear: Matter's envy directed at the First Man, a cosmic entity. 'His garments' are the five light elements in Manichaean cosmology (the Sons of First Man). The cutting and drawing of garments describes the cosmic capture. Both sides of the mapping are internal to the cosmic system.
- ¶3: The second stage of the cosmic enumeration. The Ambassador (Third Messenger) displaying his image and Matter's reactive creation of the fleshly copy — this is standard Manichaean cosmological narrative. 'The seal and the image of flesh' describes Matter's imitation of the cosmic image. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶4: The third stage. Adam here is a cosmic figure in the Manichaean drama, not a historical person. The seizing of power from his rib is the creation of Eve — a cosmic event in the entrapment narrative. Both sides internal to the cosmic system. Note 'the first human' as a cosmic title.
- ¶5: The 'fourth time' mimics the substrate enumeration pattern but the content shifts decisively: 'the blessed Christ who is the father of all the apostles' (institutional title), 'the wood of the cross' (historical event), 'fathers of righteousness' and 'congregations' (institutional terms). One side of the mapping now reaches into the historical/religious world. The three-fold cosmic enumeration (First Man, Ambassador, Adam) was likely the complete substrate teaching; this fourth item is an editorial extension into salvation history.
- ¶6: Editorial seam correctly flagged. 'Furthermore' bridges from the teaching enumeration to Mani's first-person institutional narrative. Dense institutional vocabulary: 'my church', 'my catechumens', 'this last generation', 'great person or a disciple, a man or a woman, a male catechumen or a female catechumen.' The entire paragraph is one editorial act extending the cosmic teaching to the Manichaean community's present.
- ¶7: Describes how sin/envy operates in the contemporary community: 'false reports and lying accusations', 'the teacher and the righteous elect person', 'He who preaches.' The cosmic force (Matter/Sin) is mapped onto specific community dynamics — one side of the mapping reaches into the editor's world. 'Teacher', 'elect person' are institutional roles.
- ¶8: The cosmological terms 'the desire of Matter, the thought of death' and 'the envy of its wickedness' and 'the entire kindred of life' are present but deployed within an editorial summary. 'They fall from the faith' describes community members. 'From the very beginning until my time, from me too to my church' explicitly anchors to Mani's institutional present. The 'This is' formula here functions as editorial summary, not substrate description.
- ¶9: Classic devotional closure: 'Therefore, it is appropriate for the wise person that he be steadfast and strong.' Moral application addressed to the audience. 'Stand firm in God', 'stay with his hope and his faith', 'he overcomes' — all standard exhortation vocabulary. The 'Therefore' signals the editorial pivot from teaching to application.

## Chapter 74
### Concerning the living Fire: It is present in Eight Places.

*Exceptionally clean substrate chapter. A systematic eight-fold enumeration mapping the living fire (one of the five light elements) to eight cosmic locations, with the fire identified as 'consideration' (one of the five intellectual faculties). Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system — land of light, the Father, the First Man's armour, the ship of the day, the light-giver of the night, the Living Spirit's body, the new earth, eschatological revelation. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation material detected. The only non-substrate material is the opening dialogue frame formula. Heavy lacunae in several paragraphs but the teaching structure is intact.*

The [holy] living f[ire] is present i[n] eight places.

The first place wherein it is, is this [light] land [where it is present and] exists. It is established beneath [it ... ut]terly beyond the totality. It is the foundation and ba]sis of that great earth, and the consideratio[n th]at is beneath them all.

The second place wherein it is, is the Father, the God of truth; because [the Fathe]r is established in his great, mighty consideration. So, [firs]t in the Father. The other powers that came from him were revealed through him, and (the fire) too came w[i]th them [and was revea]led by them.

The third [place wherein it is], is the First Man, h[e] wh[o ... ... ] armour. Again, it is this fire [ ... ... ] on the body of the Perfect Man.

[The fourth is this fir]e that was purified from all the [light] powers, [which is i]n the outer wall, in the ship of the day.

[The fifth i]s this fire that is in the light-giver of the night [ ... ] within the water.

[The] sixth is this f[i]re that is purified from the totality. The Living [Spirit] swathed it upon his body, over the summons and the obedience. He has established many things by it. He went down and stripped himself of it before the Por[t]er.

The seventh is this living fire that is of the new earth, and is the consideration. For it is the first foundation in the new earth, it they built anew [ ... ... ] the first course, that is a crown for the entire earth. T[hey] are below the totality, over the entire earth of da[rkne]ss [ ... ] in it, in the [ ... ] whole [ ... ]

[The] eigh[th] is the fire that shall be revealed in this [ ... ... ] person, and it signifies fear and war. Furthermore it [ ... ] it displays and manifests its sign in [its ter]ror. It shall be r[ev]ealed in these two light ships.

This i[s the way] that the holy living fire exists in these [eight places].

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Standard dialogue frame attribution wrapping the thesis statement that defines the eight-fold enumeration. The thesis itself is substrate — it establishes the teaching structure.
- ¶2: First enumeration: fire mapped to the light land. Identified as 'foundation and basis' of the great earth, and as 'consideration' — the faculty correspondence linking fire to the intellectual quality. Both sides of the mapping are cosmic. The term 'consideration' connects to the five intellectual faculties system (Ch.56).
- ¶3: Second enumeration: fire mapped to the Father, linked again to 'consideration.' Describes emanation process — powers coming from the Father, fire revealed with/through them. Classic emanation hierarchy vocabulary. Both sides cosmic.
- ¶4: Third enumeration: fire mapped to the First Man's armour/body. Heavy lacunae but the cosmic mapping is clear — fire swathed on the body of the Perfect Man. Body-cosmos correspondence pattern.
- ¶5: Fourth enumeration: fire mapped to the outer wall / ship of the day (sun). Cosmic geography — 'ship of the day' is a standard cosmic vessel designation. Process vocabulary: 'purified from all the light powers.'
- ¶6: Fifth enumeration: fire mapped to the light-giver of the night (moon) and its association with water. Cosmic geography. Lacuna may have contained further description.
- ¶7: Sixth enumeration: fire mapped to the Living Spirit's body. Describes cosmic process: swathing, the summons and obedience (cosmic call-and-response events), descending, and stripping before the Porter (cosmic being). All entities and processes are within the cosmic system.
- ¶8: Seventh enumeration: fire mapped to the new earth, again identified as 'consideration' (the faculty correspondence). 'First foundation' and 'first course' describe cosmic architecture. 'Crown for the entire earth' is structural-cosmic. Heavy lacunae but substrate throughout. The recurrence of 'consideration' confirms the fire-faculty mapping.
- ¶9: Eighth enumeration: fire mapped to eschatological cosmic events — revelation, fear, war, terror as cosmic signs, and the two light ships (sun and moon). This is cosmic eschatology, not contemporary application. 'Fear and war' and 'terror' are cosmological qualities of fire's eschatological manifestation, not editorial moralizing.
- ¶10: Closing summary recapitulating the thesis. Standard enumeration closure formula. Purely cosmological — no exhortation, no application, no editorial extension.

## Chapter 75
### [Concerning the Letter (?)]

*A compact chapter with a clear two-part structure. ¶2-3 form a complete cosmological teaching pair: the summons (call) as a cosmic letter from Living Spirit to First Man, and the obedience (answer) as a cosmic letter from First Man to Living Spirit. Both cosmic beings and the letter metaphor stay entirely within the cosmic system. ¶4-6 then extend this 'letter' pattern to the institutional church — Light Mind as letter to the holy church, good works as letter to Christ. The editorial seam is sharp: the word 'also/too' in ¶4 explicitly extends the cosmic list, but the target is now the editor's community. Editorial fatigue data strongly confirms: substrate 12.50→0.98, institutional 0.00→16.09. The oldest teaching layer is the primordial call-and-answer narrative, which maps the summons and obedience as cosmic communications containing all foreknowledge.*

the summons and the obedience

Consider the summons, which was sent from the Living Spirit in the beginning. He sent it to the First Man. It is a peace letter and greeting that he wrote and sent to his brother, in which all the tidings are written down, together with everything it will bring about and establish by that summons. That summons, the First Man [... is ordained to happen.

The answer that was sent from the First Man came to the heights, to the Living Spirit. That is also a letter, which was sent from the First Man to the Living Spirit, in which all the tidings are written down; and every war and the struggles that he had waged. It did [...] in the answer that the First Man [... ...] everything that had happened and everything [that would happen], all that had been done and whatever is made ready to be done by him. The Living Spirit knew all the wars and the dangers and the contests that the rulers had waged against the First Man.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The topic identification names two cosmic entities — the summons (call) and the obedience (answer) — which are divinized beings in Manichaean cosmology (sixth sons of Living Spirit and First Man respectively). The naming of the teaching topic is substantive and defines the structure of what follows.
- ¶2: Pure cosmological narrative. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: Living Spirit → First Man, summons → letter, cosmic tidings → foreknowledge. 'His brother' describes the cosmic kinship between Living Spirit and First Man. 'Consider' is pedagogical voice within the teaching tradition, not exhortation. 'In the beginning' anchors to cosmic time. The 'letter' metaphor describes what the summons IS as a cosmic communication — it does not reach outside the system.
- ¶3: Completes the cosmic pair: ¶2 described the summons (call downward, Living Spirit → First Man); ¶3 describes the obedience (answer upward, First Man → Living Spirit). Both sides remain cosmic: First Man, Living Spirit, the heights, the rulers, the wars. 'Everything that had happened and everything that would happen' — the letter contains cosmic foreknowledge, a substrate teaching about the nature of divine communication. The teaching unit is structurally complete after this paragraph.
- ¶4: Classic editorial seam. 'The Light Mind also... is a letter of peace too' — the word 'also/too' explicitly extends the cosmic letter pattern from ¶2-3, but now maps a cosmic entity (Light Mind) to a contemporary institution ('the holy church'). One side of the mapping reaches outside the cosmic system. 'The beloved Christ' uses devotional language. Institutional vocabulary score 14.08 confirms. The cosmic teaching was complete in ¶2-3; this paragraph applies it to the editor's community.
- ¶5: Continues the editorial extension. 'This light product, that shall be generated from the holy church' — maps the cosmic letter pattern to the church's collective good works. 'Fasting and prayers and good works' is fully institutional vocabulary (20.79 institutional score). 'Blessed Christ' is devotional. The paragraph is badly lacunose but the surviving text consistently applies the letter metaphor to church practice. Flagged by the pipeline as probable editorial extension.
- ¶6: Bridge connective 'Indeed, due to this' detected by pipeline. First-person address 'I say to you' pivots to audience. 'Everything that you will ask for will be given you' echoes NT promise language (cf. Matt 7:7, John 16:24). Devotional closure applying the letter teaching to the audience's prayer life. Despite heavy lacunae, the surviving fragments are entirely exhortatory.

## Chapter 82
### The Chapter of Righteous [Judgement]

*This chapter is predominantly later editorial material — ethical/pastoral teaching about community discipline (righteous anger) structured in a Q&A dialogue frame. The only potential substrate is ¶9, which describes the luminaries' dual function (love/beauty and confrontation/fear) in purifying the soul that passes through the totality. Both sides of this mapping stay within the cosmic system, meeting the substrate diagnostic, but the paragraph serves contextually as cosmic justification for the ethical argument and uses the chapter's 'love/fear' framework rather than technical correspondential vocabulary. Teaching purity = 0.000 confirms minimal substrate presence. The chapter is distinctive for its ethical rather than cosmological focus, with the doctor analogy (hot/cool remedies) as a pedagogical device rather than a correspondential mapping.*

For the light-givers that are in the heavens shall be revealed with these two aspects, in the aspect of love and the aspect of fear. They display these two aspects of the powers above that are in the heavens, because of their soul that passes through the totality. For in these two appearances, the aspect of love that is beauty and sublimity, together with the other aspect of confrontation that is in them, they shall purify in them the powers above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Formulaic disciple question with 'I beseech you' petition pattern. The question is about community ethics (righteous anger toward obdurate brothers/sisters), not cosmological architecture. High dialogue_frame score (4.3) confirms. 'Brothers and sisters' language points to institutional community context.
- ¶2: Very short (25 words), heavily lacunose continuation of the disciple's question about using anger for righteous judgement. Still part of the question setup — no cosmological content, no substrate markers.
- ¶3: Continuation of disciple's question with stereotyped honorific: 'I beseech you, my master, that you may instruct me.' The substantive question ('whether there is sin for me in this anger') is ethical, not cosmological. Heavily lacunose in second half.
- ¶4: Opens with dialogue_frame attribution ('Then the apostle speaks to that teacher with words of sweetness') followed by institutional/pastoral teaching about righteous anger as community discipline. 'Censure and upbraid that brother,' 'strip him of the obduracy' — community correction language. Institutional score 5.32, dialogue_frame score 4.26. Classified by dominant content layer (institutional); frame attribution is secondary.
- ¶5: Ethical taxonomy of anger: evil anger distinguished from constructive anger. 'Angry in his pride and boastfulness,' 'curses and wishes to humiliate the meek,' 'retaliation and violence against his ill-treated brothers.' All community-context language. No cosmological content. The classification of anger types is applied ethics, not correspondential mapping.
- ¶6: Continues the distinction between anger and 'the strong reprimand that leads to repentance.' Institutional vocabulary (institutional score 4.29): community discipline, 'hard words, in cruelty and harshness.' The renaming device ('its name is the strong reprimand') is a definitional move typical of pastoral teaching.
- ¶7: Doctor analogy — 'you are LIKE a wise doctor.' Pedagogical simile, not correspondential mapping. Hot/cool remedies are analogical, not systematically mapped to cosmic domains. 'Corresponding to the simile' explicitly marks this as analogy. Embedded in the community teaching sequence; the analogy serves the ethical argument about when to use harsh vs. gentle correction.
- ¶8: Extends doctor analogy into the 'two aspects' framework (love and fear). 'The brother in two aspects [...] love, but the other one with fear' — community application. 'Obduracy in one of his brothers [...] foolishness' — community context. Institutional score 3.77 confirms. The love/fear duality is the ethical framework of the chapter, applied to community leadership.
- ¶9: HEAVY UNCERTAINTY. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system: light-givers → two aspects → purification → soul passing through totality → powers above. This passes the substrate diagnostic. The luminaries' dual function in purifying trapped light-substance (attractive/beauty mode and confrontational mode) corresponds to known cosmological teaching about the vessels of light. However: (1) teaching purity = 0.000 for the chapter; (2) the 'love and fear' terminology matches the chapter's ethical framework and may be editorially shaped; (3) the connective 'For' links this to the preceding ethical argument as cosmic justification; (4) the paragraph lacks the systematic, enumerated character typical of strong substrate (no five-fold mapping, no metal-body-taste correspondences). This could be pre-compilation cosmological teaching about the luminaries' dual operation, or it could be the editor's own cosmological knowledge deployed to validate the ethical argument. Classified as substrate because no late-layer category fits the content — it is impersonal cosmic process description — but confidence is low.
- ¶10: Direct application to the audience: 'Also, you too, whenever you stand firm in these two aspects' bridges from cosmic teaching (¶9) to individual practice. 'Your treasure will also be for you in that place' echoes Matthew 6:21/Luke 12:34 — Gospel allusion marks this as late material. 'The truth and the watchful teaching' is community-values language. Classic exhortation: moral application of preceding teaching to the audience.
- ¶11: Dialogue closure formula: 'When that teacher heard that lesson, he said: This is truly told, my master.' Stereotyped affirmation pattern marking the end of the Q&A session. Institutional score 6.45 reflects 'teacher' and 'master' terminology. Formulaic session-closing device.

## Chapter 86
### The Chapter of the Man who asks: Why [am] I sometimes at peace, and troubled at other times?

*The chapter divides cleanly into an extended dialogue-frame question (¶1-5) and a cosmological teaching answer (¶6-11). The substrate teaching is distinctive: it maps psychic disturbance and peace to two cosmic mechanisms — (1) stellar/archontic influence via roots and birth-signs, and (2) the light/dark ratio in nourishment, including a transmigration doctrine about 'justified souls' whose 'dues have ceased' entering through food. Both mechanisms stay entirely within the cosmic system. Gardner flags the 'dues have ceased' passage as potentially Buddhist-influenced (karmic consequences). The dialogue frame is unusually elaborate — three paragraphs describing the disciple's experiential states. ¶4 introduces Manichaean institutional practices (fasting, psalms, prayer) into the question. The seam flag on ¶11 is a false positive — the paragraph recaps substrate conduit-teaching without institutional application. Negative editorial fatigue score and increasing substrate density in the second half confirm the teaching core is concentrated in ¶6-11.*

⟨p.215⟩ The soul that assumes the body, when the Light Mind will come to it, shall be purified by the power of wisdom and obedience, and it is cleansed and made a new man. There is no trouble in (the soul), nor confusion nor disturbance.

However, when a disturbance will arise for him and he will be troubled, this disturbance shall go into him in [ ... ], first through his birth-signs and his difficult stars that [ ... ] they turn over him and stir him and trouble him with lust and anger and depression and grief, as he wills. Also, as he wills, the powers of heaven shall trouble him through their roots, to which he is attached.

Again, there are times you shall find the powers of heaven are peaceful and tranquil for him, and it comes to [ ... ] a difficult part comes into him by the nourishment that he has eaten, or else indeed in some [ ... ] or in the water that they have drunk. Again, trouble and confusion and anger will increase in him, and lust multiplies upon him together with depression and grief; because of the nourishment of the bread he has eaten and the water he has drunk, which are full of bothersome parts, a vengeful counsel. They shall enter his body, [mixed in] with these foods, and they even become joined in with the wicked parts of the body and the Sin that is in him; transferring the anger and the lust and the depression and the grief, these wicked thoughts of the body.

should you be troubled [ ... ... ] them through the conduits and the roots of they above [ ... ... ] the flood that comes into you.

There are times, however, if you shall find that the nourishment that comes into you is pure [ ... ... ], so it is greater in light and life while the waste is less in it, and the evil-doing is diminished from it. Even the atmosphere above is still, being purified through the [ ... ... ] also the image is calmed from [ ... ... ] increases in you through ⟨p.216⟩ those good parts of the food that come into you. And they find you at ease and tranquil, as you are well governed in your abodes because of the living part, which is greater on account of the light of these justified souls that are in it. The ones that have perfected their deeds. Their dues have ceased, while their soul is carefree [ ... ] they have been allied with these living souls that [are p]resent in you. Due to this you shall find them in you, calm in tranquillity, and they come from you without trouble; and you are found flourishing of body. Even your deeds are determined, being well constructed after their manner, and your doctrines are in order, as your words [ ... ] your soul is carefree within you, ascending like a bird.

when [your bo]dy is troubled, you shall be troubled by the conduits of they below, the difficult parts that come into you by food and the act of eating. Again, when the conduits above are calmed from you, even your food that has come into you is clear and pure. Your heart shall be found ordered in its place, your counsels at peace from disturbance.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening: 'Once again a disciple speaks to our enlightener: I beseech you, my master.' High dialogue_frame score (36.0). No cosmological content whatsoever.
- ¶2: First-person phenomenological description of the peaceful state. Part of the disciple's extended question. Terms like 'Mind,' 'soul,' 'wisdom' appear but in experiential voice, not cosmic mapping. No substrate vocabulary score. The phenomenon described is what the teaching will EXPLAIN, but the description itself is not teaching.
- ¶3: Extended first-person description of the troubled state. Contains a substantive mechanistic question ('Are they revealed to me, or indeed did they enter me from outside?') that the teaching will address, but the framing remains experiential and personal. No cosmological vocabulary. The question about internal vs. external origin of disturbance defines the teaching topic but does not contain cosmic mapping.
- ¶4: Third experiential state (carefree/joyful) described in distinctly Manichaean institutional terms: 'fasting with which I fast,' 'praying,' 'psalms,' 'prayers,' 'preaching.' Institutional score 9.09. This paragraph may be an editorial elaboration of the question — the answer addresses only two states (troubled/peaceful), not three. The institutional practices color the question with the compiler's community context.
- ¶5: Formulaic closing of the question: 'I beseech you, my master.' Dialogue_frame score 11.43. Transitions from question to answer.
- ¶6: Teaching begins. The substrate describes the soul-body relationship and the Light Mind's purifying function as a cosmic process: soul assumes body → Light Mind comes → purification → new man (no trouble). Both sides of the mapping stay cosmic. The 'new man' language could be Pauline influence in translation, but the underlying mechanism (Light Mind purifying the soul in the body) is substrate cosmology.
- ¶7: Pure substrate teaching: maps celestial mechanism → psychic disturbance. Two cosmic channels: (1) birth-signs and stars that cause lust, anger, depression, grief; (2) powers of heaven acting through their 'roots' to which the person is 'attached.' Both sides stay within the cosmic system. 'Roots' (conduits connecting celestial powers to embodied souls) is key substrate vocabulary — cf. Ch.48 on roots and conduits.
- ¶8: Pure substrate: maps nourishment mechanism → psychic disturbance. Even when celestial powers are calm, food/drink containing dark 'parts' enters the body and joins with the body's own dark element ('the Sin that is in him'). The teaching describes cosmic mixture at the level of food: bread and water as carriers of bothersome/vengeful parts that transfer to the body's faculties. 'Sin' capitalized as it functions here as the cosmic dark substance inherent in the material body — a remnant of the original light-dark mixture.
- ¶9: Heavily lacunose transitional paragraph. Substrate vocabulary survives in fragments: 'conduits,' 'roots of they above,' 'the flood.' These are cosmic architecture terms — conduits and roots as channels between celestial powers and embodied souls. The 'flood' entering the person describes the mechanism of cosmic influence. Despite lacunae, the substrate terminology is clear.
- ¶10: The richest substrate paragraph. Maps the positive mechanism: pure nourishment (greater in light, less waste) → peace. Introduces a transmigration doctrine: 'justified souls' whose 'dues have ceased' enter through food and ally with the living souls in the person. Gardner flags 'their dues have ceased' as potentially Buddhist-influenced (karmic consequences). 'Ascending like a bird' is a soul-correspondence. 'Abodes' likely refers to the body's cosmic chambers/faculties. 'The image is calmed' — 'image' as a cosmic term for the body's form. All mappings stay within the cosmic system: nourishment quality → light/dark ratio → transmigrated souls → alliance with inner light → psychic peace → soul ascending.
- ¶11: Summary paragraph with dialogue frame wrapping substrate recap. The teaching content distills the two-mechanism model into a clean formula: (1) conduits below + bad food → trouble; (2) conduits above calmed + pure food → peace. 'Conduits of they below' and 'conduits above' are key substrate architecture terms. The binary conduit system (above/below) summarizes the teaching of ¶7-10. Despite the seam flag, no institutional vocabulary is present — this is a recapitulation of cosmic mechanism.
- ¶12: Narrative closure: the disciple glorifies the apostle. 'You are glorious and blessed!' — formulaic praise response. No cosmological content. Standard Q&A session ending in the Kephalaia compilation format.

This [liv]ing sou[l] that is set in mixture; for it wishes to [as]cend and go to the house of its people, but it kn[ow[s a]nd understands that it has no open door in all the powers of heaven and earth. Indeed, it has no open door except the sun and the moon themselves, the light-givers of the heavens [ ... / ... ] they become the place of rest for it. And they become a door that opens for it [in] the coming forth, and (the soul) comes out through them to the country of the household of i[ts] people.

The light-givers of the heavens have no place of rest amongst all the powers of heaven; except for the lan[d] of light, that has indeed been theirs for ever.

## Chapter 90

*Heavily institutional chapter. ## Chapter 94
### Concerning the Purification of these Four Elements [that have been place]d in the Flesh

*This chapter is dominated by the oldest teaching layer. The core is a systematic four-element body-cosmos correspondence: each of four elements (fire, water, light, wind/tree) disgorges a three-fold pollution chain (bodily emission → emotion → physical condition), yielding twelve emissions total. This is classic correspondential substrate — both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. The NLP pipeline's substrate vocabulary (zoomorphic, metal terms) does not fire because this chapter uses a DIFFERENT substrate register: element→body→emotion correspondences. The only non-substrate material is the dialogue frame (¶1) and the institutional application in ¶9 (elect/sinners). The chapter is compact, structurally complete, and remarkably uncontaminated by editorial extension.*

The four elements [are what is] purified. From the beginning they are holy ones, living ones, and enlighteners. However, when they undertood [ ... ] in that first struggle, the waste was [mixed] in with them, and the darkness of the enemy was mixed with them. And when they were come into the body, stripping off these polluted garments, they set them in the flesh. Their pollutions, which they have stripped off, shall be displayed in a [ ... ] and become a danger to the body.

The discharge of this pollution, that [the fir]e shall disgorge and place in the body, is [blo]od. The discharge of the blood is anger. The discharge of anger is the humours.

Also, the emissions that the waters discharge from them are lust. The discharge of lust is bitterness. The emission of bitterness is f[e]ver.

The emission that light shall disgorge is flesh. The emission of flesh is gloom. [The] emission of gloom is obstinacy.

The emissions that the tree shall disgorge, they are the voices of the winds of shame. The emission of winds of shame [is ... ]

The emission of the [tear] is the [ ... ] of these twelve emissions.

[ ... ] these four elements shall [ ... ] what are gathered in are [f]ound by the management of this soul food [that] enters the body. When they enter the body, they are cleansed and purified and established [i]n their living image, which is the new man. They shall live [ ... ] and receive the Light Mind and be purified in their image; and they come forth, being cleansed and holy. They attain their first rest.

they shall be cleansed and go u[p to] the land of the living ones; but these that come to [ ... ] and pass through them and [ ... / ... ] in sins. Their end will occur in transmigration and spirit.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic frame: 'the apostle is sitting among the congregation. He speaks thus.' No cosmological content whatsoever. High institutional score (17.65) from 'apostle' and 'congregation.'
- ¶2: Establishes the cosmological framework: four elements descended, became mixed with Darkness in the first struggle, entered the body, stripped off pollutions. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system. 'That first struggle' references the primordial cosmological conflict — substrate language. The voice is impersonal and process-oriented.
- ¶3: First element-body correspondence chain: fire → blood → anger → humours. Three-fold systematic mapping. Both sides internal to the cosmic-bodily system. Classic correspondential structure: element mapped to bodily emission, then to emotion, then to physical condition.
- ¶4: Second chain: water → lust → bitterness → fever. Same three-fold structure. 'Also' is a continuation marker within the enumeration, not an editorial bridge. Note the variation between 'discharge' and 'emission' — may reflect Coptic translation choices for different terms in the source.
- ¶5: Third chain: light → flesh → gloom → obstinacy. Same three-fold structure maintained. The correspondence of light-element to flesh is notable — the physical body as the 'garment' of light is a deep correspondential concept.
- ¶6: Fourth chain: wind/tree → winds of shame → [fragmentary]. Gardner notes 'the tree' is unclear but the fourth element must be wind. The text becomes fragmentary here. 'Tree' as a designation for wind is unusual — possibly a translation artifact or textual corruption. The three-fold structure is interrupted by lacuna.
- ¶7: Summary statement: 'twelve emissions' (4 elements × 3 emissions each). This confirms the systematic structure of the teaching. Despite heavy lacunae, the summary number '12' proves the four three-fold chains were the intended structure. '[tear]' may be the missing third term of the fourth chain.
- ¶8: Describes the cosmological purification process: elements gathered via food, enter body, cleansed, established in 'living image,' receive Light Mind, come forth holy, attain rest. Both sides of all mappings stay within the cosmic system. 'Soul food' functions as a cosmological mechanism term (light-in-matter), not an institutional prescription. 'New man' names the reconstituted light form. 'Light Mind' (Nous) is a cosmological entity. Some terms (new man, Light Mind) may be Manichaean-specific vocabulary layered onto an older process teaching, but the PROCESS described (element-gathering → purification → reconstitution → ascent) is substrate. Substrate score 2.56 confirms presence of oldest-layer vocabulary.

But 'the land of the living ones' and 'transmigration' are cosmological substrate — the two fates of the elements (ascent vs. continued cycling). Institutional score 3.7 confirms the institutional vocabulary presence. The substrate teaching embedded here: purified elements ascend; unpurified elements continue in transmigration.
