## Chapter 96
### The Three Earths that Exist, they bear Fruit.

*A short chapter with a clear two-level correspondential teaching (natural earth → cosmic soul) that has been extended by the editor to three levels by adding the institutional church as a third earth, then closed with exhortation. The substrate is a natural-cosmic correspondence: farming the physical earth maps onto the sun and moon 'farming' the living soul through purification. The editor extends this to a third level (holy church / Light Mind) and appends moral application. The three-fold announcement in ¶1 may reflect an original three-level teaching whose third term was replaced with the institutional identification, or the editor may have expanded a two-level teaching. The substrate that survives (¶2-3) is clean correspondential mapping: domain→domain, both sides internal to the cosmic system.*

Three earths exist in the universe, and are being toiled over. At first they [...] the farmer in them. Afterwards they bear [good] fruit to they who toil over them.

One of them is this great earth that people are living upon. They are ploughing in it [...], it with iron hoes, they cultivate, [making] the furrows, turning it over [...] Afterwards they harvest fruit from [it ...

The second field is the living soul, the holy one. It is mixed in the entire zone, above and below. The farmers who toil over it are these two great light-givers, which journey through the heavens. They are toiling and cultivating it, so that they will draw it towards their aspect. And it leaps up and comes to the heights. It goes upwards, out from every place wherein it is, and it adds to them.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The substantive teaching announcement ('Three earths exist in the universe') defines the correspondential structure. The three-fold enumeration may originally have had a different third term than 'holy church'; alternatively the editor expanded a two-fold to three-fold. The teaching framework itself — multiple earths as correspondential domains — is substratic.
- ¶2: The natural degree of the correspondence: physical earth, physical farming, physical fruit. This establishes the base from which the higher correspondences will be drawn. Both sides of the mapping are entirely natural/physical. Heavy lacunae but the structure is clear.
- ¶3: Classic correspondential mapping: cosmic soul = field, sun and moon = farmers, purification/ascent = harvest. Both sides stay within the cosmic system. 'Mixed in the entire zone, above and below' describes the cosmic mixture. 'Two great light-givers' = sun and moon as cosmic agents. 'Draw it towards their aspect' = the purification mechanism. 'Leaps up and comes to the heights' = light ascent. This is the cosmological level of the farming correspondence — entirely impersonal and process-oriented.
- ¶4: Editorial extension mimicking the enumeration pattern ('The third earth is...') but applying it to 'this holy church with its elect and its catechumens' — one side of the mapping reaches directly into the Manichaean institutional community. Light Mind as farmer is a genuine cosmic entity, but the field being cultivated is identified as the institutional church, not a cosmic domain. 'Elect,' 'catechumens,' 'holy church' are institutional vocabulary (institutional score 10.1). The seam flag correctly identifies this as an editorial extension of the preceding two-level teaching. If removed, ¶2-3 form a complete natural-cosmic correspondence pair.
- ¶5: Bridge connective 'Now, just as' pivots from teaching to moral application. Direct address ('You yourselves'), imperatives ('Do the farming of righteousness,' 'Preach and enlighten every soul!'), eschatological promise ('live of them for ever and ever'). Classic devotional closure appended to the teaching sequence. The vocabulary is entirely hortatory: 'be good farmers,' 'open the eyes,' 'reveal to them life and death.' Heavy lacunae in the second half but the exhortatory character is clear throughout.

## Chapter 97

*This chapter is almost entirely oldest teaching layer — a systematic cosmological classification of plant life according to the type of cosmic ruler whose 'thought' produced them. Despite heavy lacunae, the teaching structure is clearly preserved. The only non-substrate material is the single-line dialogue frame opening. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation material is present. The zero substrate register score reflects the pipeline's narrow marker vocabulary (zoomorphic/metal/taste), not the actual content, which is unmistakably substrate teaching about cosmic process → natural taxonomy.*

All these animals that you see, and also the rest of the [crea]tures [...] of the earth, confirm them in three likeness[es ...]: some from [...] abortions. Others, however, form their own selves in their shape [...].

Also, their nourishment is thre[e ...] light in them, if they shall wish [...]. [...] they eat see[ds and] fr[uit ...]; they eat seeds and fruit. The [...] it entirely, and they [...] the three archetyp[es] from their nature and the [...] the time when they are on the ea[rth ...]; they are seized [...] in their [...]. The Ambassador [...] all powers [...].

[N] it [...], first [...]. ⟨p.247⟩ Plant them generally to this name: shoots and grass. It formed the bad, bitter trees, the reeds and the thorns and the [...] and these others that are like them.

For that thought belongs to the flesh-eating rulers. Still, these [that were] planted (belong to) grass and gourds, since they are in their essence. Nevertheless, [this t]ho[ugh]t that came down from the rulers, as they are in fruit and grass first and shall not be planted in flesh and blood, it formed the trees that shall give nourishing fru[it], as their gourds are sweet.

Again, this thou[ght] that came down from the powers that eat m[ea]t and also eat grass and gourds, it has formed the trees that shall not produce any nourishment. Their fruits belong neither to good nor evil.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic attribution with no cosmological content. 'Once again our enlightener speaks to his disciples' — standard dialogue frame marker.
- ¶2: Three-fold cosmological classification of creatures: brought forth, begotten by abortions, self-formed. This is the chapter's core taxonomic structure. Heavily lacunose but the systematic three-fold enumeration is characteristic of the oldest teaching layer. 'You see' is pedagogical address within the teaching tradition, not editorial framing.
- ¶3: Three-fold nourishment classification paralleling the three-fold creature classification. 'Three archetypes from their nature' is systematic cosmological teaching. The Ambassador (cosmic figure) and powers are elements of the cosmic narrative. Extremely fragmentary but the structure is clearly substrate: cosmic agents → natural processes.
- ¶4: Plant taxonomy within the cosmic classification system. 'Bad, bitter trees, reeds, thorns' are natural categories produced by the cosmic process. Both sides of the mapping stay within the system: cosmic formation process → plant types. The impersonal 'it formed' is characteristic substrate voice.
- ¶5: Core cosmological-correspondential mapping: ruler type (flesh-eating) → their 'thought' → the type of plant produced. The 'thought' (ennoia) is the cosmic driving force of reproduction. Both sides internal to the cosmic system. The logic is systematic: the nature of the ruler determines the nature of the plant. 'Sweet gourds' and 'nourishing fruit' are correspondential qualities mapped to the cosmic origin.
- ¶6: Completes the three-fold classification: powers that eat both meat and vegetation → trees with neutral, non-nourishing fruit. The 'neither good nor evil' quality corresponds to the mixed nature of the ruler type. 'Again' marks systematic enumeration continuation, not editorial bridge. Both sides remain within the cosmic taxonomy.

## Chapter 98
### What is Virginal; or, otherwise, what is Continent?

*A highly intact five-level descent teaching mapping 'virginal' and 'continent' across cosmic degrees from the eternal kingdom to the human body. The substrate is dominant — the editorial layer is confined to dialogue frame openings (¶1-3) and closure (¶11). The five-fold structure is systematic and self-contained: each level maps the same paired principle (untouched vs. purified-after-mixture) at a successively lower degree. The 'Furthermore' connectives mark degree-transitions within the teaching, not editorial extensions. Notable that ¶9's seam flag fired on bridge connective alone with no institutional vocabulary detected. Negative editorial fatigue (-1.96) confirms the second half is purer teaching, not more editorial. The one genuinely ambiguous element is 'the elect' in ¶9 — potentially institutional, but functioning as a cosmic category in context.*

What is the virgin that is named 'virginal'? Or the [one that] is called 'continent', what is it? Or by what category is the [vi]rgin called 'virginal'? Or [the co]ntinent, which is given this name, by what category is it named '[c]ontinent'?

What is called 'virginal' is assigned to the mystery of the aeons of greatness. And they called it 'virginal' by the category of all the light that did not [taste] of death, nor come to the enemy's war.

However, what is called 'continent' occurs to the mystery of the light that came and was mixed with the darkness. It was defiled in bodies [ ... ] and went up to the light. [They] called [it 'co]ntinent'.

Furthermore, the holy vir[gin]als [ ... ... ] should they come out against the en[emy ... ... not] taste death, nor [ ... ].

On the other hand, what is called 'continent' [is this li]ght that mixed with the darkness, was purif[ie]d, came [up f]rom all the creatures, and was confirmed in the great[ness]. They called it 'continent' since it tasted [of the l]ust; but afterwards was cleansed from it and [become an] holy one.

Furthermore, what is called ['vi]rginal' is this light that was purified by the ima[ge] of the [A]mbassador. It wen[t up and] attained [the] he[ights. It was] confirmed in the image of the gods.

Again, what is [called] 'continent' is the remainder of this light th[at] remained behind, from what was cleansed. It goes up and comes down in transmigration. Al[so], there is a part [of] it that shall come and attain the form of [hum]an [fl]esh. Again, that one too shall be chosen in the flesh, [accept] the hope, and become an holy one. And they name it 'continent'.

Furthermore, what is named 'virginal' is the Light Mind, should he come and assume the images [of] the ele[ct]. However, on the other hand, what is called 'contin[ent]' is the new man, should it be purified from this old man. And it is cleansed and strips off the Sin that is compounded with it, and it becomes a continent one.

And also, what is called 'virgi[n]al' in the flesh, is a man if he has [ne]ver joined himself to woman, has not been defiled by intercourse. However, [i]n contrast, what is called 'continent', is the man who has a wom[a]n in the world. Afterwards, he cleanses himself from her [and] renounces her. And because of this he [ ... ] and he becomes an holy contin[en]t one.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The substantive question defines the teaching structure — it asks about the CATEGORIES of virginal and continent, which is exactly what the five-level descent teaching answers. The question is the Persian pedagogical tradition setting up a systematic mapping. 'Congregation of his church' is institutional framing.
- ¶2: Pure formulaic disciple response with honorific address ('my master') and flattery ('all good gifts are given to us through you'). No cosmological content whatsoever.
- ¶3: First level of the descent teaching (virginal side). Maps 'virginal' to the light in the aeons of greatness that never entered mixture. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system: virginal = untouched light. 'They called it' uses impersonal attribution — the teaching received this terminology, it was not coined here.
- ¶4: First level (continent side). Maps 'continent' to light that entered Darkness, was defiled, and returned. Classic substrate: cosmic process description with both sides internal. The pairing with ¶3 is systematic — virginal/continent as untouched/purified-after-mixture.
- ¶5: Second level (virginal side). Heavily damaged but the cosmic content is clear: gods who come against the enemy but do not taste death. 'Furthermore' here is a degree-transition marker within the teaching sequence, not an editorial bridge. The content maps cosmos→cosmos.
- ¶6: Second level (continent side). Maps 'continent' to light that mixed, was purified from creatures, and was confirmed in the greatness. The 'tasted of the lust... afterwards was cleansed' formula describes a cosmic process — purification after mixture. Both sides remain within the cosmic system.
- ¶7: Third level (virginal side). Maps 'virginal' to light purified by the Ambassador's image — a specific cosmic event. 'Confirmed in the image of the gods' keeps both sides within the cosmic hierarchy. 'Furthermore' is degree-transition, not editorial extension.
- ¶8: Third level (continent side). Maps 'continent' to light remaining in transmigration. This paragraph begins the descent toward the human level — 'attain the form of human flesh' bridges cosmic and anthropological. The transmigration cycle is a cosmic process. 'Accept the hope' could be editorial overlay but in context describes a stage of the cosmic descent.
- ¶9: Fourth level (both sides). Maps virginal→Light Mind and continent→new man. The editorial seam flag fired on 'Furthermore' alone — the institutional vocabulary field is EMPTY. The Light Mind is a cosmic entity (divine intellect that descends). The new man / old man is the anthropological correspondence of cosmic purification. 'The elect' is ambiguous — could be institutional or cosmic ('the chosen forms'). In context of the descent structure, I read it as cosmic. Capitalized 'Sin' as personified cosmic entity — 'the sin that is compounded with it' describes the cosmic mixture at the human-soul level.
- ¶10: Fifth and final level — the natural/human correspondence. Maps virginal→never joined to woman; continent→joined then renounced. This is the lowest degree of the five-level descent, completing the systematic mapping. Crucially, this paragraph DEFINES ('what is called... IS...'), it does not EXHORT ('you should be...'). The human body is the final register of the cosmic system. 'In the flesh' explicitly marks this as the bodily degree of the same principle. Without this level the five-fold descent is incomplete.
- ¶11: Closure formula. 'Behold, I have taught you' is first-person dialogue frame addressing disciples. The second sentence ('what is virginal, or otherwise what is continent, is in many forms') restates what was shown through the five levels but adds no new teaching content. The chapter's teaching is complete at ¶10.

## Chapter 100
### Concerning the Dragon with Fourteen Heads; what it is and [...]

*Chapter 100 explicitly cites the Persian Magian tradition ('the laws of the Magi') as the source of the fourteen-headed dragon teaching. The core substrate is concentrated in paragraphs 4-6, which systematically map: 14 dragon heads → 7 upper sense organs + 7 lower body parts; 5 dens → tongue, lungs, heart, spleen, blood. The key identification (dragon = 'the thought of the body') appears in paragraph 7 embedded in exhortation. The teaching is framed by editorial dialogue (P1-2) and closed by a beatitude (P9). Despite the chapter's modest length, the body-correspondence substrate is remarkably clean and well-preserved. The explicit Persian provenance is significant — the teaching is presented as a spiritual reinterpretation of a Magian cosmological image, suggesting the substrate preserves an older Persian body-cosmos correspondence tradition.*

there is a dragon with fourteen heads, and it is gathered in and [ ... ] den with five refuges.

Again, this is how this earth [ ... ] to it, as it is accounted a corporeal dragon [ ... ] the dragon is this doctrine [ ... ] of the flesh [ ... ] also the fourteen heads that are distributed over the dragon are these.

The seven sense organs on the head of the body are the two of sight by which one sees; the two of sound by which one hears; the two of scent by which one smells; also the tongue [ ... ] by which one selects, receiving there the different tastes that occur in every form.

These are the seven heads of the dragon, the seven sense organs that are on the upper part of the person; [ ... ] below. The seven of the torso are these: two [ ... ]; the two [ ... ] ruler [ ... ] the seven heads that are above; so with the ones below they total and make fourteen heads of the dragon.

Conversely, the five dens about which they have spoken, where the dragon is congregated and protrudes, are these: the first is the tongue; the second is the lungs; the third is the heart; the fourth is the spleen; the fifth is the blood that dwells in them. Again, if it shall look out from them [and reveal its likeness and its observation above and below, it wages war by] its fourteen heads.

the dragon, which is the thought of the body

the dragon that exists with its fourteen heads and five dens, wherein it is concealed and hidden. It does through them [ ... / its des]ire all the time.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The disciple's question references 'the laws of the Magi' — explicitly sourcing the teaching to the Persian Magian tradition. The substantive cosmological content (14 heads, 5 refuges) defines the teaching structure and belongs to the oldest layer. The meta-question about corporeal vs. spiritual is pedagogical framing.
- ¶2: Entirely Mani's hermeneutical framing — 'Then the apostle says to him:' is dialogue attribution; the content positions Mani vis-à-vis the Magi ('the Magi did not understand'). The claim 'this is a spiritual lesson' is meta-commentary about the nature of correspondence, not a mapping itself. The substrate PRACTICES correspondence; this paragraph EXPLAINS that it is spiritual. That explanatory stance is editorial.
- ¶3: Heavily lacunose but contains core identifications: the earth/body is accounted as a dragon; the dragon = doctrine/thought of the flesh. Also introduces the enumeration of 14 heads. The lacunae make precise extraction difficult but the teaching structure is visible.
- ¶4: Pure body-correspondence mapping: 7 sense organs on the head = 7 upper heads of the dragon. Systematic, impersonal, process-oriented. Note 'the different tastes that occur in every form' — taste-correspondence vocabulary characteristic of the substrate. Both sides of the mapping stay within the body-cosmos system.
- ¶5: Completes the 14-head enumeration: 7 upper + 7 lower = 14 heads of the dragon. The seam flag on 'These are the' is a false positive — this is a teaching identification statement within the correspondence system, not an editorial bridge. Both sides remain within the body-dragon mapping.
- ¶6: Maps 5 dens → 5 internal organs with classic enumeration structure ('the first is... the second is...'). 'About which they have spoken' likely references the teaching tradition generically, not specifically the Magi as editorial target. The dragon's warfare through the 14 heads describes its cosmic-somatic function. Both sides stay within the body-correspondence system. Note the enumeration integrity: first through fifth preserved.
- ¶7: The key identification that completes the teaching structure: dragon = thought of the body. This is embedded in an exhortatory sentence about recognizing, struggling, and overcoming. The institutional vocabulary ('elect, good person') and eschatological reward ('the day of his coming forth') mark the application layer. The dragon-slaying motif may be ancient (Persian tradition), but the specific institutional and eschatological language is Manichaean editorial.
- ¶8: Recapitulates the teaching structure (14 heads, 5 dens) and adds new process information: the dragon acts through the dens by desire. 'It does through them its desire all the time' describes how the dragon functions within the body-cosmos system — both sides stay within the system. The dialogue frame closure 'I have taught you' is stripped.
- ¶9: Classic devotional closure: dialogue frame ('Then that disciple says to him'), satisfaction formula ('My heart has been persuaded, my master'), beatitude ('Blessed is every one who will slay and kill it'), eschatological promise ('He will live for ever'). The beatitude formula 'Blessed is every one who' is a diagnostic marker of the exhortation layer. The dragon-slaying concept may be ancient but the devotional wrapper is editorial.

## Chapter 101
### [Concer]ning why, if the Person shall look down into Water, [ ... ]

*This chapter preserves correspondential teaching that maps physical observations (water reflection, shadows) onto cosmic events and body-cosmos correspondences. The substrate is dominant: inverted reflection maps to the descent of the Summons and ascent of the Obedience, to stellar-zodiacal parentage of earthly beings, to birth inversion, and shadow-earth connection maps to the body's origin from the abyss. No institutional or exhortation content. Editorial material consists entirely of dialogue frame and pedagogical framing phrases ('I reveal to you', 'listen and I may teach you'). Gardner notes 'loosely grouped teachings' that 'may evidence textual development' — consistent with several originally independent correspondential units joined by editorial transitions. The negative editorial fatigue score reflects frame material concentrated at the chapter's opening. The teaching represents what Gardner calls Mani's 'totally integrated science' — natural phenomena explained through cosmic correspondence.*

When the person is above water and looking down [into the wat]ers, if he shall see his face reflected why shall you find his head turned upside down and also his feet upwards? Yet, when he stares down into the waters, his face shall not be visible, nor can he see it there?

[And] this: The face of people and the shape of trees shall appear turned upside down in the water as this is signified in [the] mystery of the summons, when it was sent down to the worlds of Darkness towards the First Man. Sin[ce ...] in this way it cast itself down [...] like a person [who] plunges headlong into water; so it is also with the summons. It cast itself headlong to the worlds below, with strength and diligence.

And this too: Shall the person lift his head up from the water, his face shall not be visible to him, being bent below. This characterises the mystery of the obedience that ascended from below, from with the First Man his father to the Living Spirit.

The face of people and of animals and of trees is visible in the water, hanging upside down. This occurs to the mystery of the stars and the zodiac, which hang upside down and are visible in the great sea. For the face of people and beasts and all trees hang on the root of the stars and the zodiac, being begotten from them. Just as their fathers, who are spread out above, hang upside down; so it is also that the mystery of their fathers is being revealed to them whenever they stare down into the water. This is, that you shall find their shapes inverted.

Not only this, but [the] child who is born; as the ho[ur] approaches for him to be born, he hangs upside down and is born head first. Indeed, due to this, his mystery shall be revealed in these waters people stare down into.

Behold, you will find the shadow of the person who walks along is joined to the earth all the time. If the person is troubled [...] his shadow up upon the earth [...] above. It is impossible for him to do [... ...] on the earth. See, this mystery is [a] great sign, signifying that the entire body came from the earth and had ascended from the abyss. Again, due to this, its shadow is joined on the earth, turned downwards, and hanging from above.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: The question defines the teaching structure: two physical observations (inverted reflection; invisibility when looking straight down) that the subsequent paragraphs will map onto cosmic correspondences. The question itself is substantive — it sets up the entire teaching sequence. Persian pedagogical tradition.
- ¶2: Pure dialogue frame: 'Then the apostle says to him:' followed by a general transitional statement ('this universe is established of mystery'). The claim that the universe is full of mystery is a framing assertion that prepares for the teaching — it does not itself map domain onto domain. Very high dialogue_frame score (52.17).
- ¶3: Maps physical observation (inverted reflection) → cosmic event (descent of the Summons/Call to the First Man in the worlds of Darkness). Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. The Summons plunging headlong corresponds to the inverted image in water. '[And] this:' functions as a teaching-unit marker within the correspondential sequence. Lacunae preserve manuscript damage.
- ¶4: Maps the second physical observation from paragraph 1 (can't see face when looking up) → cosmic event (ascent of the Obedience/Answer from the First Man to the Living Spirit). Completes the paired cosmic mapping: Summons descends (¶3), Obedience ascends (¶4). Both sides cosmic. 'And this too:' is a teaching-unit connector paralleling '[And] this:' in ¶3. The Summons-and-Answer pair (Call and Obedience) is a fundamental substrate cosmological structure.
- ¶5: Maps inverted reflection → stars and zodiac hanging inverted in 'the great sea,' with earthly beings (people, animals, trees) 'begotten from' and 'hanging on the root of' their celestial parents. Classic body-cosmos / zodiac-body correspondence. The concept of stellar 'fathers' whose forms are revealed through water-reflection is deeply correspondential. Both sides of the mapping stay within the cosmic system. The spiritual/corporeal distinction (noted by Gardner as a redactor's organizing principle) is an editorial classification imposed on substrate material.
- ¶6: Maps the physical process of birth (child inverted, born head first) → the inversion mystery visible in water. Body-cosmos correspondence: the birth process itself enacts the cosmic inversion pattern. Both sides within the natural/cosmic system. 'Not only this' is a teaching-unit connector continuing the correspondential sequence. No editorial voice, no institutional content.
- ¶7: Maps shadow permanently joined to earth → body's origin from earth/abyss. Classic body-cosmos correspondence: the shadow's earthward binding reveals the body's material origin. Both sides within the cosmic system. The bridge connective detected by the seam flag marks an editorial TRANSITION between originally separate teaching units (reflection teachings → shadow teaching), but the content itself is substrate. Significant lacunae may obscure additional correspondential detail. Gardner identifies this as the chapter's final teaching unit.

## Chapter 104
### Concerning Food: It shall be allocated to Five Products of the human Body.

*Exceptionally clean substrate chapter. A systematic five-fold degree mapping of food → body productions, moving from the most subtle (rapture/Mind) to the most gross (corporeal offspring). Both sides of every mapping stay within the body-cosmos system. No editorial bridge, no institutional content, no exhortation, no citations. The only non-substrate material is the 7-word opening dialogue frame formula. The five-degree structure follows the characteristic pattern: invisible/immeasurable → progressively material → visible. The closing observation about the visibility gradient (parents notice only the fifth because the others 'are not displayed') is part of the teaching's explanatory structure, not editorial addition. Negative editorial fatigue score confirms no drift toward later layers.*

This sustenance of various kinds that people gather in and eat, and enters the body; it shall be distributed to five productions.

The first product is this that shall issue from the person in rapture and rises up in the Mind, and it comes out from all his limbs, and is immeasurable.

The second is this that shall issue from the person in voice and word.

The third is this that shall spring from them in strength and activity.

The fourth is what is engendered by the pleasure of lu[s]t in men and women.

The fifth is this that shall be formed and sculpted in the flesh, and be engendered and come from them. It is this corporeal creature; only its parents can recognise this creature that they beget. They are attentive to its desire and its thought and its [lo]ve every day, all the time. However, these other four forms they neither notice nor are concerned about; because they are not displayed.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic attribution with no cosmological content. 'Once again he speaks to his disciples' — standard dialogue frame marker.
- ¶2: Sets up the five-fold teaching structure. Impersonal, systematic description of a natural process (food → body products). Both sides of the mapping are within the body-cosmos system. No editorial markers despite zero substrate vocabulary score — the pipeline's substrate vocabulary targets zoomorphic/metal/taste markers absent from this body-process teaching.
- ¶3: First degree of the five-fold enumeration. The most subtle production: rapture rising in Mind, issuing from all limbs, immeasurable. This is the highest degree — invisible, diffuse, connected to Mind (nous). Classic correspondential description of spiritual heat or inner illumination as body-process.
- ¶4: Second degree. Speech/voice as body production from food. Still subtle but more defined than the first (rapture). The degree structure descends toward materiality.
- ¶5: Third degree. Physical strength and activity. Midpoint of the five-fold sequence — bodily exertion, corresponding to sweat/physical output.
- ¶6: Fourth degree. Sexual fluids. More material than strength, approaching the fully corporeal. Lacuna [s] in 'lu[s]t' preserved. The description stays impersonal and systematic.
- ¶7: Fifth and final degree — the most material production: children, corporeal creatures. The closing observation about visibility is part of the teaching structure, not editorial addition. It explains the degree hierarchy: the fifth is visible ('displayed'), the other four are not. This is a teaching about the gradient from subtle to gross — the structural explanation of why people recognize only the grossest production. Both sides of the mapping remain within the body-cosmos system. Lacuna [lo]ve preserved.

## Chapter 106
### There is no Joy that shall remain in the World till the End.

*An exceptionally pure substrate chapter. The entire teaching maps the luminaries' perception of cosmic conditions (the Enemy's rule, the soul's captivity) and the eschatological resolution. Both sides of every mapping stay firmly within the cosmic system. The two-fold enumeration (¶3-4) is classic substrate pedagogy. No editorial bridges, no institutional content, no exhortation to audience. The only non-substrate element is the dialogue frame formula in ¶1. Negative editorial fatigue confirms teaching dominance throughout. The chapter preserves a compact, self-contained cosmological teaching about the incomplete joy of luminaries during mixture.*

There is not [o]ne person [existing in] the world, standing firm [i]n mixture, for whom joy remains to the end.

Not only people, even the sun and the moon, the light-givers of the heavens, [ ... ] nor are they oppressed, they know not [ ... s]et firm in a summons and an [obe]dience. Nevertheless, they too, they do not rejoice completely.

So, one: Their eyes stare at their enemy, they [perceiv]e him and also look at his ugly shapes. They [s]ee him, that he is alive and established and is king above and below, in secret and manifest. He does every thing that he wants according to his wil[l].

[The s]econd: They perceive the living soul, they see [i]t and that it is ensnared and set in a great [fetter] above and below, in the tree and in the flesh, [ ... ] with every oppression. It is being pressed, drawn near to [and] sliced and eaten as it comes up and down; from above below and from below above. It [is] despoiled and moved from body to body.

Now, because of their living soul that is loved by them and honoured of them, when they see it in oppression th[ey can] not [re]joice completely.

So, one about the soul; and one about their enemy whom they see ruling [in] what is not his own. He is proud and satisfied with a wealth that is not his own. Indeed, due to this, they do not rejoice in total; but bear up and swallow their heart until they cleanse the living soul and take it from the enemy's hands. Then they obliterate the death that is their enemy and they gather it in [and fetter it in the lump] for ever and ever.

When they will chain their enemy in the bond at the [las]t, then they will rejoice and no longer grieve from this t[ime]. Also, they become the very same as they were from the [start] for ever!

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Clean division: formulaic dialogue frame attribution followed immediately by a cosmological principle about mixture. The teaching statement itself is impersonal and systematic.
- ¶2: Extends the cosmic principle from persons to luminaries. The sun and moon are described as cosmic agents ('light-givers of the heavens') set in a summons and obedience — their station within the cosmic architecture. Both sides of the mapping are internal to the system. Lacunae suggest lost material that may have further described their cosmic condition.
- ¶3: First enumeration unit ('one:'). The Enemy is described in cosmic-structural terms: 'king above and below, in secret and manifest' — a spatial-ontological description of his dominion within the system. 'Ugly shapes' is zoomorphic-register description of the dark powers. No side of the mapping leaves the cosmic system.
- ¶4: Second enumeration unit. Dense cosmic-process vocabulary: 'ensnared', 'fetter', 'pressed', 'sliced and eaten', 'moved from body to body'. The soul-body binding and transmigration formula ('moved from body to body') is a substrate marker. 'In the tree and in the flesh' describes the soul's entrapment in the vegetable and animal kingdoms — cosmic domains, not contemporary institutions. The parallel 'above and below' structure echoes ¶3.
- ¶5: 'Now' here is a logical connective within the teaching, not an editorial bridge marker. The paragraph draws a causal connection between the luminaries' love for the soul and their incomplete joy. Both sides remain cosmic: luminaries perceiving the soul's condition.
- ¶6: Recapitulation and cosmic-process conclusion. The recapitulation ('one about the soul; and one about their enemy') is structural, not editorial — it closes the enumerated teaching. The process vocabulary is substrate: 'cleanse the living soul', 'obliterate the death', 'fetter it in the lump'. The lump (βῶλος) is a technical cosmological term for the final prison of Darkness. The minor exhortation score (3.45) comes from 'for ever and ever' but this is a cosmological-temporal formula here, not devotional closure.
- ¶7: Eschatological closure within the cosmic system. 'Chain their enemy in the bond' parallels ¶6's 'fetter it in the lump'. 'They become the very same as they were from the start' is the restoration principle — return to the primordial state before mixture. This is a cosmic-temporal statement (from the start / for ever), not devotional exhortation. The chapter closes with the cosmic cycle complete.

## Chapter 107
### Concerning the Form of the Word, that [ ... 30 ... ]

*A short, remarkably pure teaching chapter. The substrate maps word-production through five bodily members onto coin-production through five craftsmen — a classic both-sides-internal correspondence. Five-fold structure is the hallmark of the oldest teaching layer. The only editorial material is the brief dialogue frame attribution in ¶1. All NLP register scores are 0.0 because the vocabulary here (throat, tongue, teeth, lips, craftsmen, coin) falls outside the typical cosmic-entity keyword lists, but the STRUCTURE (five-fold systematic process mapping) is unmistakably substratic. Gardner's synopsis confirms the coin analogy as the chapter's core.*

The production of the word t[hat comes] from the mouth and is heard by the [ears]. [ ... ⟨p.261⟩ ... ] and the throat draws it up and the tongue spreads it out and the teeth cut it and the lips g[at]her it! The word shall come forth through the power of th[ese five] members and be heard outside.

Simila[rly] the coin: One shall pour it out and an [othe]r beat it and another trim it as it is turned, and a[nother] put the stamp on it and another wipe it in the sieve (?). [Behold], these five craftsmen shall shape and beautify [their] coin, and it comes amongst mankind. It becomes a posse[ssion] to be received and given.

This is also the case with the [wor]d, as it is formed and embellished by five [member]s. It comes forth and is heard by the ears [of] others.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with standard dialogue frame formula 'Once again he speaks about...' then 'He says'. The teaching itself is a five-fold body-process mapping: throat → tongue → teeth → lips → (implied fifth from lacuna, possibly the chest/lungs). This is systematic, impersonal, process-oriented — classic substrate voice. The five-member structure is the backbone of the oldest teaching layer.
- ¶2: Pure correspondential analogy: five craftsmen mapped to five stages of coin production, corresponding to the five bodily members that form the word. Both sides of the mapping stay within the system — body-process → craft-process. No editorial extension, no contemporary application, no exhortation. The 'Simila[rly]' is a teaching-internal connective linking the two halves of the correspondence, not an editorial bridge.
- ¶3: Summary closure that completes the correspondence by returning to the word-formation side. Impersonal, systematic voice. 'Five members' restates the structural principle. This is the teaching tying its own bow — not editorial addition.

## Chapter 108
### Concerning the Seed Grain that shall be formed by the Elements, and also be destroyed by them

*Short, heavily damaged chapter preserving a substrate teaching about the dual nature of cosmic processes in the world of mixture: the same elements that form and nourish also destroy. The teaching maps this principle across agricultural (seed/tree), cosmic (five elements/righteousness), and material (Matter's shadow) domains. Only clear editorial intrusion is 'the teachers and the elect' in ¶3. Gardner notes the meaning is unclear, but the substrate structure is a cosmological principle about formation-and-destruction as inseparable in mixture. Chapter title itself encapsulates the substrate teaching. Very little editorial overlay for such a short chapter.*

This seed grain and this barley grain shall be formed and [...] and beautified by the five elements. Now, the warmth and the cold nourish the seed grain and the entire tree. So, just as these things nourish it [...] it is destroyed [...] through [...] hunger.

In this way also righteousness gathers the five to it.

In this way too, a lack and a shortfall shall occur from place to place through them; and they who have chosen it have gathered it in and beautified and attained it, while an affliction shall arise for it through those.

People also shall be caused to stumble on their account, through the energy of Matter that dwells in them. It has poured its shadow over them, just as it shall also pour its shadow over the elements and destroy the fruits of all the trees.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening with institutional attribution ('the builder of the church'). No cosmological content. Standard Kephalaia dialogue frame formula.
- ¶2: Core substrate teaching about the five elements as agents of both formation and destruction. 'Five elements' is fundamental substrate nomenclature. The agricultural correspondence (seed grain, barley, tree) maps cosmic elemental process onto natural growth — both sides within the cosmic system. Heavily damaged with multiple lacunae but structure is clear: formation/nourishment → destruction/hunger.
- ¶3: 'Righteousness gathers the five to it' is substrate: righteousness as cosmic principle, 'the five' as the five lights/elements — both sides within the cosmic system. The editorial extension applies this cosmic process to 'the teachers and the elect' (institutional vocabulary, score 9.09). Clear seam at 'So, it shall be chosen by...' where the mapping crosses from cosmos→institution.
- ¶4: Returns to impersonal process description after ¶3's institutional intrusion. Zero institutional vocabulary (score 0.0). Describes the fundamental cosmic principle: gathering creates lack; formation leads to affliction. 'From place to place' suggests cosmic geography. Both sides of the mapping stay within the system. 'In this way too' is a teaching-structural connective linking analogy levels. Some ambiguity: 'they who have chosen it' could echo the institutional actors from ¶3, but without institutional vocabulary, the referent is likely the cosmic elements themselves. Flagging this uncertainty.
- ¶5: Purely cosmological process description. 'The energy of Matter' is Matter personified as cosmic agent — substrate. 'Poured its shadow' is correspondential language for Matter's action in the mixture. 'People' here are inhabitants of the mixed world (part of the cosmic architecture, not a contemporary audience). The mapping from people → elements → fruits/trees keeps both sides within the cosmic system. Completes the chapter's teaching cycle by returning to the agricultural imagery of ¶2 (trees, fruits) through the agent of destruction (Matter). No institutional, editorial, or exhortatory markers.

## Chapter 109
### Concerning the Fifty Lord's Days; to what Mysteries do they correspond? Or the Second (Fifty); to whose Sign (are they)?

*This chapter is primarily institutional — its purpose is to explain church fasting practices (fifty lord's days for catechumens, fifty for elect) by grounding them in cosmic mysteries. The oldest teaching substrate is concentrated in paragraphs 2-4, which describe the First Man's five garments with five powers each, the summons and obedience, and the cosmic food cycle. This substrate cosmological material (the five-fold multiplication yielding 25, doubled to 50 by the call-and-answer pair) has been appropriated to justify institutional fasting schedules. The second half of the chapter (P5-8) shows strong editorial fatigue — institutional vocabulary dominates as the chapter pivots to Gospel narrative (Christ's forty days fasting, seven at Simon's house, three in the tomb) and Mani's first-person institutional claims. The substrate fragments preserved here are genuine (five-garments teaching, summons/obedience emanation pair, cosmic living food) but are embedded in a thoroughly institutional framework.*

[In eac]h one of these five garments that the [First] Man summoned there are five powers. They shall be counted to the [ ... each] other, these five, and five times [ ... ] their number. However, at the time [ ... ] the summons went down. The answer wished to ascend with it. And the summons with the obedience [constitute] twenty-five characteristics in their twenty-five limbs, [which] are the sons of the Man. The summons and [the obed]ience were raised to the heights through them.

⟨p.263⟩ Just as their fa[ther] the living Man raised himself up through them, by these these other ones have set up all things a[bov]e and below. They fasted from their [foo]d, [w]hich is their own.

So, from that time until [the time] of [the adv]ent of Jesus the Splendour, the glorious one who came [ ... / ... t]heir food which is the [ ... ] of sou[l], which is the summons and the obedience [ ... ] that spiritual food. They rele[ased ... ] all these fifty days, which [ ... ] this living food suffices for all of them. It filled them all with power and richness and life. Light [ ... ] this living food that [ ... ] became rich.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Combines dialogue frame formula ('Once again a disciple stood up... I beseech you, my master') with entirely institutional question content: 'fifty lord's days,' 'catechumens fast,' 'the elect.' The question defines the chapter's institutional purpose. No cosmological content in the question itself — it asks about church practice, not cosmic structure.
- ¶2: Core substrate teaching: the five-garments-of-the-First-Man with five powers each (5×5 structure) is a fundamental cosmological-correspondential pattern found across the Kephalaia. The summons and obedience (call and answer) as a cosmic emanation pair yielding the 25 characteristics mapped to 25 limbs — this is systematic cosmic architecture where both sides stay within the system. The mathematical structure (5×5=25) is characteristic of the oldest layer's degree-mapping method.
- ¶3: The substrate elements describe cosmic process: the living Man's ascent, the setting up of all things above and below, cosmic beings fasting from their own food (abstaining from the food of life in the world of mixture). Both sides of these mappings stay within the cosmic system. The editorial bridge sentence interrupts the cosmic narrative with institutional identification.
- ¶4: Describes the cosmic food cycle: spiritual food identified as the summons and obedience, living food that fills with power/richness/life. Both sides of the mappings stay within the cosmic system (cosmic food → cosmic nourishment). 'Jesus the Splendour' is a cosmological entity in the Manichaean system (the cosmic awakener of the Third Evocation); the NAME may be a Manichaean overlay on an older substrate cosmic figure, but the teaching about living food and spiritual nourishment is substrate vocabulary. Heavy lacunae make certainty difficult. The temporal framing ('from that time until the advent of...') integrates this into cosmic narrative rather than institutional practice.
- ¶5: Transitional paragraph connecting cosmic fifty to institutional hundred days. 'The one hundred holy days,' 'the elect fast,' 'these mysteries of these first fifty' — the paragraph's function is institutional: explaining why there are two sets of fifty fasting days. Substrate vocabulary appears ('the summons and the obedience set them in their splendrous places at the coming of Jesus') but is embedded in and subordinate to the institutional framework. Heavily damaged, making clean extraction impossible. The paragraph bridges from cosmic teaching (P2-4) to the institutional explanation (P6-8), marking the editorial fatigue boundary.
- ¶6: Flagged as probable editorial extension by seam detection. First-person institutional voice: 'I have revealed for the catechumens,' 'I have revealed for the elect.' References to 'the sects' and their pentecost practice. Claims apostolic precedent ('the apostles themselves fasted for these fifty days'). Entirely institutional — Mani establishing his fasting calendar by appeal to apostolic authority and polemical comparison with rival sects. No substrate content.
- ¶7: Gospel narrative applied to institutional fasting: Christ's forty days fasting on the mountain, seven days at Simon the leper's house, three days in the tomb = fifty days. This is a NT citation/calculation entering the text via Manichaean editorial construction. The 'second fifty' framework mapping onto Christ's biography is a Manichaean Christological construction, not substrate cosmological teaching.
- ¶8: First-person institutional claim: 'I, I have bestowed them on the entire church.' Summarizes the chapter's institutional scheme: catechumens' fifty after the First Man, elect's fifty after 'the second man who was revealed in the church.' 'The entire church,' 'catechumens fast,' 'revealed in the church' — saturated institutional vocabulary. The pairing of 'First Man' (cosmic) with 'second man' (Jesus/church) confirms editorial construction connecting substrate cosmology to institutional practice.

## Chapter 110
### Concerning the Nourishment of the Person, for there are Powers in it [ ... / ... ]

*Heavily damaged chapter with extensive lacunae, but the readable portions are almost entirely oldest-layer body-cosmos correspondence teaching. The chapter describes the human body as a microcosmic purification machine: 250,000 seals and 250,000 rulers, cleansing/purification processes, expulsion of dark rulers through transmigration. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system — body structures correspond to cosmic powers, digestive purification corresponds to light-dark separation. The only editorial element is a fragmentary dialogue frame opening. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation material is present. The computational pipeline returns all zeros, likely because the heavy lacunae prevent vocabulary density calculations from registering, but the text is clearly substrate in character.*

[ ... ] exists [ ... ] daily to the body of the person [ ... ] there are two hundred and fifty thousand seals in it [ ... ] there are another two hundred and fifty thousand rulers [ ... ] they are cleansed and purified and enter [ ... ] ⟨p.265⟩ by the voice, by the word, and by the [ ... ... ] quiet with the silence at the time when his heart [ ... ... ] of God the Father.

Also, the twelve [ ... ... ] rulers that belong to the Darkness shall be swept out outside in wandering and in transmigration [ ... ... ] some of them [ ... through] this transmigration, and others among them shall be brought back.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Opens with fragmentary dialogue frame formula '[Once again] speaks [...]' followed by substrate body-cosmos mapping. The teaching describes precise numerical correspondences (250,000 seals, 250,000 rulers) in the human body — classic substrate enumeration of cosmic powers inhabiting the microcosm. The purification process ('cleansed and purified') and the sensory-spiritual channels ('by the voice, by the word') are cosmic-process vocabulary. 'God the Father' likely renders the Father of Greatness — a cosmological entity within the system. Heavy lacunae obscure much of the original structure.
- ¶2: Pure substrate cosmological process description. The twelve rulers of Darkness are cosmic entities; their expulsion through 'wandering and transmigration' describes a systematic cosmic mechanism — the body expelling dark powers as dregs after purification. Both sides of the mapping remain within the cosmic system: dark rulers → transmigration process. The differentiation ('some... others') is characteristic of substrate teaching that discriminates degrees within a process. Capitalized 'Darkness' as it functions as a personified cosmic domain.

## Chapter 111
### Concerning the Four Archetypes that occur in the Eye, and the Fifth that is hidden in them; to whom do they belong?

*Extremely fragmentary chapter preserving a body-cosmos correspondence teaching: four archetypes of the eye mapped to cosmic elements (fire, darkness/abyss, light/living air) and cardinal directions, with a hidden fifth. The substrate is dominant despite heavy lacunae. The teaching maps eye-structure → element → cosmic domain, and connects to the First Man emanation. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation material detected — only dialogue frame formulas wrap the teaching. Gardner notes it is too fragmentary to derive overall sense, but the substrate structure (four + hidden fifth, element correspondences, cardinal points, First Man) is clearly discernible.*

There are four aspects among mankind that perceive, as they perceive [...] without these four that are hidden in them [...] they belong [...]. So, [...].

[...] that is in the [...] belongs to the fire. The darkness belongs to the abyss; but the light that perceives what is hidden in all these things, being clear, is the living air.

How shall the pupil of the eye perceive? While it perceives by day, yet come the night it shall not perceive. [...] The pupil of the eye shall not [...] the mystery of the [...] is the door [...] below [...] the other four cardinal points. For in the [...] above. He has [...] great [...] they came down [...] living [...]. The First Man came forth from it and knew all things.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Despite heavy lacunae, the substantive content establishes the chapter's teaching structure: four perceptive aspects with a hidden component among them. This matches the chapter title's 'four archetypes and the fifth hidden in them.' The teaching defines a body-faculty system.
- ¶2: Classic element-to-domain mapping with both sides within the cosmic system: fire, darkness/abyss, light/living air. The 'light that perceives what is hidden in all these things' identifies the hidden fifth from the chapter title — mapped to 'the living air.' Five-element nomenclature (fire, abyss/water, living air) is diagnostic substrate vocabulary.
- ¶3: The teaching maps eye-pupil → door → four cardinal points, a body-cosmos correspondence. The day/night perception contrast likely maps to cosmological light/darkness cycles. 'The First Man came forth from it and knew all things' is emanation hierarchy language. Despite extreme fragmentation, the structural bones are visible: pupil as door, four cardinal points as spatial frame, First Man as the knowing emanation. The first-person 'I am the one who will explain' is the compiler's voice inserting pedagogical authority.

## Chapter 113
### The Chapter on whether any [Lig]ht comes from the Three Vessels

*Short, predominantly substrate chapter describing the cosmological mechanism by which light is refined from the three vessels surrounding the universe. The teaching describes gates at four compass points opening downward to the vessels, light rays entering through these openings, and a purification/clarification process through the firmaments. The only editorial overlay is the standard dialogue frame attribution in paragraphs 1 and 2. No editorial bridge, institutional, or exhortation material detected. The teaching is systematic cosmic-process description — both sides of every mapping remain within the cosmic architecture (vessels, gates, firmaments, rays, light, living ones). This is a compact, well-preserved substrate teaching unit.*

this bit of life and light that comes from [these three] vessels that surround the universe [ ... ] life comes up [ ... ]

[ ... ] b[u]t should the [ ... ] ascend [ ... ] light of the rays that [ ... ] which are received, which shine and go in through these great open gates, through these doors, firmament by firmament.

For four [gates o]pen in each world, distributed at the four points of the compass, opening downwards to the vessels. So, due to this, the [light of the] rays shall go in through these openings and it shines on [that] place, and pulls through them the light and the little pi[llar (?) that ex]ists there.

From that place [it] shall be poured [out to the] firmaments. It shall also be clarified in these firmaments [ ... ] a part there is cleansed and it is [all] taken up with the living ones. However, its other part [ ... ] shall all [be cast] down.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: High dialogue_frame score (24.32) confirms the formulaic opening. The substantive question identifies the teaching topic: light from the three vessels surrounding the universe. This is a cosmological question that defines the teaching structure.
- ¶2: Despite heavy lacunae, the surviving text is clearly cosmic-process description: rays received, shining through gates and doors, ascending firmament by firmament. The architectural vocabulary (gates, doors, firmaments) is systematic and impersonal. Zero substrate vocabulary score likely reflects lacunae destroying diagnostic terms.
- ¶3: Pure cosmological architecture: four gates at four compass points, opening downward to the vessels, light rays entering through openings, pulling light and the pillar. Systematic spatial description — 'each world', 'four points of the compass' — mapping cosmic geography. Both sides of every description remain within the cosmic system. The 'little pi[llar]' may be a technical term for a light-conduit within the vessel.
- ¶4: Cosmic purification process: light poured to firmaments, clarified, cleansed, separated — one part taken up with the living ones, the other cast down. This is the classic substrate separation/refinement vocabulary. The substrate score (12.24) is the highest in the chapter. The binary separation (taken up / cast down) is a fundamental cosmological-correspondential pattern. Despite lacunae, the teaching structure is clear.

## Chapter 114
### Concerning the Three Images that are in the righteous Person

*This chapter is almost entirely oldest teaching substrate. After a single-line dialogue frame opening (¶1), the remaining nine paragraphs form a cohesive three-degree anthropological teaching: corporeal → psychic → spiritual images, with a process description of how the living soul ascends through these degrees by divesting itself of what does not belong to it. The teaching is systematic, impersonal, and process-oriented. Both sides of every mapping stay within the cosmic system — no editorial bridges, no contemporary application, no institutional content, no exhortation. The light virgin, the new man, the Light Mind, and the three images are all cosmic entities/structures. The negative editorial fatigue score (-2.99) confirms that the later-layer vocabulary is concentrated only in the opening frame, with the rest being clean substrate. One of the purest teaching chapters in the corpus.*

Three images occur in the elect person.

The first is the spiritual image, which is the new man, which the Light Mind shall form in him; and it goes into him and dwells in him.

The second image is the remnant and remainder of the new man, which is the psychic image that is bound in the flesh, as the old man [ ... since the] times of this psychic image.

The other one is the corporeal image [that is] added to them all.

These three images [ ... ] entirely. When it will [ ... ... ] through the nourishment of food. If it shall sink [ ... ... ] down to the corporeal image. [And] the corporeal, [that] is in it, shall divest itself of it and place it in the [ ... ] image. [ ... ] again it rises up and falls to the [ ... ] image; it shall divest itself of the psychical of the enemies, which [ ... ... ] anger. The lust [ ... ... ... ] with it.

Then shall a light virgin come and reveal the spiritual image that is [there], which [is] the new man. That virgin acts as a guide. [She g]oes on before and it is extended to the heights above, and received into this spiritual image. And she [sculpts] it and adorns it with the new man [wi]thin. It is sealed with all the limbs of this light virgin who is present and dwells in the new man.

So, this is [h]ow this living limb shall be [puri]fied and live, the one that comes into the body of [the ri]ghteous one from without through the administration of food of various ki[nds], like this. The living soul shall be clea[n]sed entirely every [d]ay and traverse these three [im]a[ges].

So, it shall divest itself of the body, which is not its own, in the corporeal. It shall also divest itself of the souls that are not its own, these that are mixed with it in the psychical [ ... ... ] anger and desire and [ ... ]-ness and foolishness and envy and strife; and these other wicked teachings that are not its own.

However, in [the] spiritual image itself it shall live and be joined with [lo]ng-sufferingness, the perfection of faith and love that reigns over them all. It is the virgin of light who robes the new man and who shall be cal[l]ed 'the hour of life'. S[he] is the f[irst], but s[h]e is also the last.

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic dialogue frame attribution. 'Once again our enlightener speaks to his disciples' — stereotyped opening with no cosmological content. The dialogue_frame score of 112.5 confirms this is entirely frame vocabulary.
- ¶2: Cosmological topic statement defining the three-degree structure. The institutional score (28.57) fires on 'elect' — the chapter title uses 'righteous Person,' which may preserve the older term. However, the sentence functions as a structural cosmological declaration: it defines the degree system that the teaching will map. Classified as substrate because the statement describes what IS in the cosmic anthropology, not an institutional prescription.
- ¶3: First enumeration unit. Maps the highest image → spiritual → new man → Light Mind's formation. Classic degree-mapping: image-type → cosmic entity → process of formation. Both sides internal to the cosmic system. 'The Light Mind shall form in him' describes emanation/indwelling — substrate process vocabulary.
- ¶4: Second enumeration unit. Maps the middle image → psychic → bound in flesh → old man. The 'new man / old man' pairing is a cosmological-anthropological correspondence describing the degrees of mixture. Lacuna preserves manuscript damage. Both sides internal to the system.
- ¶5: Third enumeration unit. Maps the lowest image → corporeal → 'added to them all.' The corporeal image is the outermost accretion in the degree structure. Brief because it is the lowest degree — the teaching gives more detail to the higher degrees. Completes the three-fold structure.
- ¶6: Process description of purification through the three images. Heavily damaged but the teaching structure is clear: the living soul enters through food, sinks to the corporeal image, divests itself of corporeal accretions, rises to the psychic image, divests itself of psychic enemies (anger, lust). Classic substrate process vocabulary: cosmic mechanism described step by step. Both sides stay within the system — 'enemies,' 'anger,' 'lust' are cosmic-internal psychic forces, not moral exhortation.
- ¶7: Describes the highest stage of the purification process. The light virgin is a cosmic entity who reveals, guides, sculpts, adorns, and seals the soul within the spiritual image. Every action maps cosmic entity → cosmic process: revealing, guiding, sculpting, adorning, sealing. 'Sealed with all the limbs' is body-cosmos correspondence applied to the virgin's cosmic form. Both sides entirely internal to the system.
- ¶8: Recapitulation of the entire process. 'So, this is how' is a summarizing formula but the content is entirely cosmic-process: living limb → purification → food → three images. 'The righteous one' matches the chapter title's term (vs. 'elect' in ¶2), possibly preserving the older substrate term. 'Every day' describes the ongoing cosmic process frequency, not an anchor to the editorial present. 'Administration of food' describes the cosmic mechanism by which living soul enters the body.
- ¶9: Detailed process description of what is stripped at each level. Corporeal: 'the body, which is not its own.' Psychic: anger, desire, foolishness, envy, strife — these are the psychic forces of the dark side, cosmic-internal. 'Wicked teachings' likely renders a cosmological term for dark-side impressions/formations rather than being editorial moralizing, given the systematic context. 'Not its own' repeated — formula for distinguishing what belongs to light vs. dark mixture.
- ¶10: Final stage of the teaching: the soul in the spiritual image is joined with cosmic virtues — long-sufferingness (patience), faith, love. These are the virtues/powers of the Light Mind in the Manichaean system, functioning as cosmic entities rather than moral exhortations. The virgin of light 'robes the new man' — cosmic entity performing cosmic action. 'The hour of life' is a cosmic designation. 'She is the first, but she is also the last' — cosmological statement about the virgin's nature spanning the entire degree structure. No voice shift to exhortation or application. Both sides fully internal to the cosmic system.

## Chapter 115
### The Catechumen asks the Apostle: will Rest come about for Someone who has come out of the Body, if the Saints pray over and make an Alms-offering for him?

*This chapter is dominated by institutional material about prayer for the dead, alms-offerings, and remembrances — classic ecclesiastical practice. The oldest teaching layer survives in two well-preserved cosmological narrative blocks (¶8-14: Mother of Life's supplication and First Man's rescue; ¶17-22: Five Fathers' supplication and Third Ambassador's emanation) plus fragmented third and fourth archetypal episodes (¶25-27: Jesus the Splendour). These emanation narratives describe events entirely within the cosmic system and closely parallel cosmogony found elsewhere in the corpus. The editor repurposed these pre-existing narratives as archetypal precedents for institutional practice, inserting editorial bridge paragraphs ('just as the Mother of Life was granted... so too the saints') and extensive institutional application (four victories of prayer, analogy of the bound relative, catechumen commission). The editorial fatigue pattern is strong: substrate is front-loaded (first half 6.47), institutional accumulates in the second half (7.56). Teaching purity 0.392 reflects the heavy institutional overlay.*

The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life, she claimed and prayed and besought and glorified and praised the first established one, who is the Father. She besought him an entreaty. She claimed of him a request. She received a great gift; she and the many powers with her.

She besought an entreaty on behalf of the First Man, because of how he had come forth and thrown himself down. He became distant from the Mother of Life. He separated from her and gave himself; in great affliction. He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness; in the worlds of Darkness; in the abysses made of fear; in the midst of the demons' ditch; amongst the powers of the devil!

Indeed, he is like a king standing in the midst of his enemies. And when the Mother of Life petitioned and prayed and glorified and praised the Father, the first established one, she did petition him that he might send a power; a protector and redeemer and helper for the son of God who is set in affliction.

⟨p.273⟩ Now, just as she had besought and interceded, so likewise was her prayer fulfilled and received into the presence of the Father of Greatness. He turned and gave to her her entreaty: the great power, the Living Spirit, the giver of ease. The Mother of Life and the Living Spirit came with great power to the borders of the territory. They brought the First Man up from that war and struggle, wherein he is set.

He gave himself as a grace for the Mother of Life. He became estranged and far from her, the one whom his enemies (place) in great affliction, the one who is in the land of Darkness since he became weak.

The Living Spirit grasped and took him in. He freed him. He set him right and gave him ease in the land of rest [...] the Living Spirit, the first giver of ease [...] the will of these three. The welcomer [...] find with the welcomer that [...]

[...] a great power [...] of the loved one [...] the First Man who had been far away. He had become distant. She had besought on his behalf. He was set fast in affliction. (The Living Spirit) went to the place of destruction and weakness, the one that freed and redeemed him and gave him ease from the affliction. He set him right with rest and joy. He has himself become rested, with him for ever.

[...] and they built the universe and completed it with its firmaments, with its wheels, with the ships of light that are in the heights, with its fastenings, with its earths and its walls, with its vessels. They completed the universe and set it right by the fathers of light.

⟨p.274⟩ Then, the Great Spirit stood with the Beloved of the Lights, the Great Builder with the Living Spirit and the First Man. These five stood to prayer; they glorified and praised the Father, the first established one. They besought of him an entreaty. They claimed of him a request, that he might give them the power [...] the leader, a guide to all the things of activity that they had constructed. That he might come and purify the living soul that is set in affliction.

They prayed and besought their Father, and he received from them their prayer [...] he gave them their entreaty and their request [...] he summoned from him [...] which is the Third Ambassador. He, he came from the power of his greatness. He saw the entire soul, how it is entangled in a chain and set fast. It sees the things of activity that his brothers have constructed, that have come from the heights. Again, he saw the living soul, that it is entangled and set fast in a great torture. It is oppressed in the stink of the abyss that is joined with it.

And therefore, he, the Ambassador [...] in his charity. So, he revealed his way, before this underworld that is dug deep down, to free the living soul. He redeemed it from all the fleshes of death wherein it is pained. He gave it ease. He set it right in the place of rest and joy.

And again, he too, the Ambassador, he fulfilled the will of these three; the will of his brothers who had besought the Father for him. They claimed of him a request, together with the [...] of the Father, the one that [...] he gave the entreaty that they had besought of him.

The third is the transferral of the living soul that he had freed. He released it and gave it ease from the affliction. He became king and first to the entire body.

the third entreaty when they besought him the third time [...] displayed his image [...] he hid his image from the [...] he stood firm [...] they revealed [...] the light [...] the First Man [...] mock [...] to him in [...] which is exalted [...] garment [...] they were freed [...] to him [...] their voice [...] the body [...] prayer [...] it [...] beseech [...] the body [...] beseech [...] she besought [...] in [...] be free [...] Ambassador [...] and also [...]

⟨p.276⟩ [...] with the living ones and [...] for ever [...] the first [...] from the [...] he gave him the hope [...]

Also, Jesus the Splendour perfected the will [...] the will [...] they found the light [...] the will of the [...] that he had [...] he gave them the power that is Jesus the Splendour. He granted the will of the elements. [...] for ever. This first [...] he was humbled, he was made free [...] the [...]-ness is displayed to his enemies. He received the hope [...] reveal [...] Jesus the Splendour, the light power [...] and the entreaty of the [...] of light [...]

**Temporal observations:**
- ¶1: Pure formulaic opening: 'Once again, it happened one time while our enlightener is sitting in the midst of the congregation.' Standard Kephalaia session-opening formula with hagiographic attribution.
- ¶2: Catechumen's question about prayer for the dead. 'I beseech you, my master' is standard honorific formula. The question is about institutional practice (efficacy of prayer for the deceased), not cosmic structure. No substrate content to extract.
- ¶3: Continuation of the catechumen's question. Raises the doctrinal tension between intercession and retribution ('each one shall receive retribution according to his deeds'). While doctrinally substantive, this is framed as 'we have heard from you' — recitation of received teaching within the dialogue frame, not systematic cosmic exposition.
- ¶4: Pure frame formula: 'I beseech you my master, that you may instruct me.' 'It is very great and honoured amongst people' references contemporary social valuation — the editorial present.
- ¶5: Opens with dialogue frame ('Then the apostle speaks to him'). Content is institutional teaching about prayer efficacy: 'every elect person, if he beseeches charity in total faith' — describes community practice. 'Our compassionate Father' as addressee of petition. No cosmic-correspondential mapping; this teaches what the elect should do, not what cosmic beings ARE.
- ¶6: Classic editorial bridge: connects cosmic archetype ('the gods first made entreaty of their Father') to institutional present ('the holy church that assumes the flesh is established in prayer and entreaty above all'). One side cosmos, other side contemporary institution. The brief cosmic allusion is not systematic teaching but an editorial summary serving the bridge function.
- ¶7: 'I will instruct you how it is that they shall beseech their entreaty from the first ones' — transitional promise to teach, not teaching itself. 'The first ones' anticipates the cosmic narrative but this paragraph is frame structure.
- ¶8: First emanation narrative begins. The Mother of Life petitions the Father — both sides of the mapping are cosmic beings. 'The Great Spirit, the Mother of Life' and 'the first established one, who is the Father' are systematic cosmological nomenclature. The petition-and-granting is cosmic process (emanation through request).
- ¶9: First Man's descent narrative. All elements within the cosmic system: First Man, Mother of Life, worlds of Darkness, abysses, demons. 'He joined war on earth with the destruction and the grief and the weakness' describes cosmic combat. The five sons' defeat/affliction is core cosmogony.
- ¶10: Continues the First Man narrative. The simile 'like a king standing in the midst of his enemies' is within the cosmic narrative frame. Mother of Life petitions for a cosmic helper. 'Son of God' here = First Man, a cosmic title within the system.
- ¶11: 'Now, just as... so likewise' here narrates the cosmic event resolution — not an editorial bridge to institutional practice. The Father of Greatness emanates the Living Spirit. 'Borders of the territory' is cosmic geography. All actors and locations within the cosmic system. The rescue of the First Man is core cosmogony.
- ¶12: Continues First Man narrative. 'The land of Darkness' is cosmic geography. Describes the First Man's condition before rescue — all within the cosmic system.
- ¶13: The rescue accomplished. 'Land of rest' is cosmic geography. 'The welcomer' is a cosmic figure in the rescue narrative. Heavy lacunae but cosmic content is clear. 'The will of these three' may refer to a triadic cosmic structure.
- ¶14: Fragmented but continues the cosmic rescue narrative. 'Place of destruction and weakness' is cosmic geography. All actors (Living Spirit, First Man, Mother of Life implied) are cosmic beings. The rescue and restoration is cosmic process.
- ¶15: 'See now, I have taught you the first entreaty that the Mother of Life besought by her prayer.' Meta-commentary on the teaching — editorial summary marking the end of the first archetypal narrative. Parallel structure with ¶23.
- ¶16: Classic editorial bridge: 'This also is the case with the prayer that the faithful saints [...] over the one who has been released from his body. They shall be granted their entreaty just as the Mother of Life was given the entreaty.' Maps cosmic archetype (Mother of Life) → institutional practice (saints praying for the dead). One side reaches outside the cosmic system.
- ¶17: Systematic cosmological description of the universe's construction. 'Firmaments, wheels, ships of light, fastenings, earths, walls, vessels' — cosmic architecture nomenclature. 'Fathers of light' are cosmic beings. Both sides of all descriptions stay within the cosmic system. This bridges between the first and second archetypal narratives.
- ¶18: Second emanation narrative. The five great cosmic beings (Great Spirit/Mother of Life, Beloved of the Lights, Great Builder, Living Spirit, First Man) petition the Father. This is systematic emanation hierarchy — all five named, their collective petition described. 'The living soul that is set in affliction' is the cosmic condition requiring the Third Ambassador.
- ¶19: The Third Ambassador's emanation and cosmic perception. 'The stink of the abyss' — sensory quality mapped to cosmic geography. 'Entangled in a chain and set fast' — describes the living soul's cosmic condition. 'His brothers' = the five fathers. All within the cosmic system.
- ¶20: The Ambassador's salvific activity. 'The underworld that is dug deep down,' 'the fleshes of death,' 'place of rest and joy' — cosmic geography and cosmic condition. All within the cosmic system.
- ¶21: Describes the Ambassador fulfilling the cosmic mission requested by his brothers. 'The will of these three' refers to cosmic beings within the hierarchy. Substrate score 0.0 reflects vocabulary pipeline limitations — the content is entirely cosmic process.

- ¶24: 'Now, happen you know this lesson' is editorial formula. Content maps cosmos → institutional practice: 'Each perfect elect and every catechumen who believes... if he beseeches... shall be granted... Just as the entreaty of his fathers was granted.' Institutional language (elect, catechumen, body, faith) with cosmic reference used as justification.
